# NexusBT v1 implementation spec — revision v3.4 (2026-10-28, with the fourth three-reviewer round)

> **Canonical text.** This English text is the canonical spec. The Korean translation is `docs/spec/engine-spec-v3.4.ko.md`; it keeps the same section numbers, ticket ids, error codes, golden ids, property test ids and citations, chapter by chapter, which `tests/docs/test_spec_translation.py` checks. When the two differ in meaning, this English text is right.

> **Public edition.** This file is the repository's public edition of the revision v3.4 spec (private notes, the producer's work plan and personal paths removed). Normative sentences, section numbers, tickets, golden cases and error codes are the same as in the original. Among the bracketed sources, file names such as `review/`, `teardown/`, `sources/`, `research/` and `STORY.md` point to the design record (outside the repository) and are not needed for implementation.

> **Name (defined in this line only).** Engine name = **NexusBT** ("nexus" = the one point where data, backtest, screen and live, and the strengths of other engines meet). Name decision 2026-10-28: Aion → NexusBT (PyPI refused to register `aion`, and the name collides with two active packages that use `import aion`; PyPI `nexusbt` was registered by the user as a 0.0.1 placeholder). Repository = `<owner>/nexusbt`, distribution name = `nexusbt`, import = `import nexusbt as nx` (`nx.` in this document is this abbreviation), config file = `nexusbt.toml`, CLI = `nexusbt`, environment variables = `NEXUSBT_*`, base error class = `NexusBTError`, optional dependencies = `nexusbt[pandas]` and so on. The names of the data standard TDS (`tds.json`, distribution name `nexusbt-tds`, **import name `nexusbt_tds`**, CLI `tds`) belong to the standard and can change separately from the engine name (TDS chapter 13 Q6). In this document, `tds` as in `tds.check(...)` and `tds.value_hash(...)` abbreviates `import nexusbt_tds as tds`.
>
> **Identifier name (defined in this line only).** The stored instrument key is named **`instrument_id`** (in a package's data, the internals, and today's output tables and public API; no alias). The names the user meets, `symbol` for an instrument (the symbol in use on a row's date, 5.3.6), `date` for `session_date` and `prices` for the dataset kind `bars`, come with stage (2), whose first slice builds them (#725 Decision 248; #752, 2026-11-27); stored names stay. v2's `security_id` alias was removed by the three-reviewer consensus: other column names in external data are changed only in the `mapping.yaml` of `tds import csv` (`nx.dataset.from_parquet` was removed 2026-11-24, #752). In code, only the constant `nx.contracts.ID_COLUMN = "instrument_id"` defines this name.
>
> **Status.** Normative spec **v3.4**. **This document is the only basis for implementation**. The input format the engine reads is set by **TDS v1.4** (`engine-data-standard-v1.4.md`; the section numbers "TDS v1.1 x.y" and "TDS v1.2 x.y" in this document are the same in v1.4), and chapter 4 of this document holds only the engine-side reading rules. Public scope: the engine, this spec and TDS are written on the premise that they are public (Apache-2.0). This file is the repository's public edition: the private comment lines about the private producer, the body of section 10.2 and TDS chapter 11 are removed, and the producer's name is replaced by "the reference producer" (the `grep` acceptance test of T0.1). An implementing agent does not invent rules that are not in this document or in TDS. When there is no answer, it uses the recommended defaults of chapter 12; when there are none, it stops and asks.
>
> **Inputs.** `review/consensus.md` (the three-reviewer consensus), `engine-blueprint-by-aspect.md` (below, "the blueprint"; chapter 13 SC, chapter 14 OQ), `engine-data-standard-v1.4.md` (below, "TDS"; chapter 12 D, chapter 13 Q, chapter 11 G and X are a private appendix), `engine-design-2026-10-27.md` (below, "the design"; 9.0 user decisions).
>
> **Priority.** When rules differ, the order is **user decisions (design 9.0 and later decisions) > this spec v3.4 = TDS v1.4 (for the input format, TDS wins over chapter 4 of this spec) > the three-reviewer consensus > the blueprint > the body of the design > sources** (the `sources/` files of the design record, outside the repository). How each conflict was settled is in appendix A (from A-104 for v3).
>
> **Tracing notation.** The bracket at the end of a normative sentence is its source.
> - `[R-xx]`, `[P1]`~`[P10]`: requirements and performance items of design chapter 8 (the performance numbers, P10's tolerance aside, are alerts, not promises, since 2026-11-24: chapter 7). Numbers from `R-56` on were added by this spec (1.4).
> - `[9.0 …]`: the second round of user decisions. Ones like `[Q6]` are the first-round decisions of design 9.1.
> - `[C1]`~`[C10]`, `[11.4.2]`: the Codex review (design chapter 11). `[D1]`~`[D8]` (bracket + D + one digit): E0 decisions.
> - `[SC-xx]`: spec changes of blueprint chapter 13. `[OQ-x]`: open questions of blueprint chapter 14. `[TDS x.y]`: a TDS section. `[TDS-Dxx]`: a difference in TDS chapter 12 (in this document always with the `TDS-` prefix, so it is not confused with the D of E0). `[TDS-Qx]`: a question of TDS chapter 13.
> - `[A-xx]`: conflict-resolution numbers of appendix A. `[INV-xx]`: invariants of 1.3.
> - Appendices D to I (the change summaries), the revision history and the header paragraphs on how each version was made, the review-round tags and the version notes were removed; commit `193e59a` holds them (`git show 193e59a:docs/spec/engine-spec-v3.4.md`).
> - Normative sentences say "does / does not". Explanatory sentences give a source or a reason.
>
> **Notation.** [est.] estimated, [unverified] the original could not be checked. Numeric examples show a shape and are not normative (the normative numbers are only those written as "default" in a table). **v1.x** = the small releases right after the v1 release (M6). **Reserved** = only the name and place are taken; calling it in v1 raises `CapabilityError` (E18). **Private comment** = a note about the private producer (removed in this file, the public edition).

---

> **User decision 2026-10-28:** the spec in Korean (private); public material (TDS, README, quick start, error messages, CONTEXT.md) English canonical + Korean translation; v1 release = develop on synthetic data → pass verification on KR/US real data after the reference producer is complete; PyPI only reserves the name, publishing at v1; the zipline comparison covers the preset scope (OI-20); the hold band is the last ticket of v1 M6 (OI-12; superseded 2026-11-24, #725 Decisions 244: v1, no default width, its place set in the plan); the `security_id` alias removed; the stand-alone three-reviewer review of TDS takes place right before 1.0 is settled. Engine name = NexusBT (settled 2026-10-28; changed from Aion the same day).

> **User decision 2026-10-31 (changes "the spec in Korean (private)" of the 2026-10-28 decision above; amends OI-45):** the spec, like every other public document, is English canonical + Korean translation. "Korean" assumed that the spec was private, but the spec became public by the 2026-10-27 decision, and everything a person reads on the remote repository is written in English, with the translation kept separately. While it is translated, the Korean edition is canonical. The English edition grows chapter by chapter in `docs/spec/engine-spec-v3.4.en.md`, and in the last PR the English edition moves to `docs/spec/engine-spec-v3.4.md` and the Korean edition to `docs/spec/engine-spec-v3.4.ko.md` in one step. Section numbers, ticket ids, error codes, golden numbers, property test ids and citation numbers do not change, and a translation PR changes no rule (a wrong rule is a separate spec PR).

> **User decision 2026-10-28 (changes "v1 long only" of design 9.0 and 9.1 Q2):** the core is symmetric between long and short like a mirror image, through signed quantities and weights (+ long, − short) (zipline-style signed holdings, which futures and options later use as they are). Shorting is **in v1 but off by default** (with `[shorting] enabled = false` the results are bit-identical to the long-only path, test P-SHORT-OFF). Borrow cost, collateral ratio and interest on short proceeds are written in the config by the user, like commissions and taxes. There is no data on borrow availability (everything is assumed borrowable unless the market rules pack forbids it, and the documentation says so). The Korean short-sale ban periods are date rules of the market rules pack. The other user decisions stand.

> **User decision 2026-10-28:** the producer keeps no separate export or conversion layer. The `tds` schema tables of the producer's DB are identical to the TDS datasets; when a build is swapped in, the snapshot folder is written as a TDS package (the 4.1 layout + a `tds.json` with hashes), and the TDS checker is the mandatory check (on failure the build is not swapped in). Year partitions that did not change are hard-linked from the previous snapshot. The engine links by path only (`nx.open(<snapshot path>)` or the path in the config).

> **User decision 2026-10-28 (closing the user questions of the third review round):** (1) **Cash interest**: v1 has `[account] cash_rate_bps` (default 0 = as now), booked on the same accrual as borrow cost (at session start, the previous session's closing balance × calendar days/`day_count`) (5.6.3(1)). (2) **Short ruin**: when the equity (`decision_value`) of a sleeve with shorts falls to 0 or below, that sleeve is marked `ruined` and its new orders stop, and the mandatory obligations (borrow cost, short dividends, terminal obligations, taxes and the like) are recorded only as negative cash; it shows in the output (the header line, `status.json`, E38) (5.6.7). Forced liquidation is still not simulated (cash account; the margin account of the final v3.4 is 5.6.7a). (3) **Snapshot partition reuse**: APFS cloning (`clonefile`) and reflink are used before hard links, stamps and caches check the file identity `(dev, inode, size, mtime_ns)`, and published files are read-only (4.2, TDS 4.1, ADR-0037). The meaning of the decision above (no export layer, linked by path only) stands. (4) **Language**: v1 is Python + numpy/Numba (ADR-0016 stands) with "a seam where Rust can be swapped in" — the hot kernels (window kernels, the session-simulator loop, accounting state) sit behind a narrow interface of Arrow array input and output and columnar state (2.4), and are replaced by a Rust crate (PyO3/maturin) only when M1 and P1~P7 measurements and profiles show that Numba cannot meet the budget, together with a bit-identity test against the Python reference; E4 intraday and tick designs a Rust core and then compares it with a Nautilus bridge (ADR-0016, 11.2; the trigger amended 2026-11-24 by ADR 0054).
>
> **Defaults the user set (reviewer consensus, 2026-10-28)**: pin default `index+root`; no margin loan for longs (feasible region `long_weight + c·short_weight ≤ 1`, a 130/30 book is E16 before the run — from the final v3.4 revision **in the cash account (default) only**; with `[account] type = "margin"`, 5.6.7a); tie key = the fixed salt `sha256("nexusbt-tiebreak-v1" ‖ 0x1F ‖ instrument_id)` (3.7; independent of date and strategy); P-SHORT-OFF = a same-build differential test + value-hash baselines of the four core tables; f64 ratio fitting = ~~the simplest rational within tolerance~~ → removed in v3.4 by user decision (below, 5.6.1); borrow cost = at session start, the previous session's closing quantity × calendar days/`day_count` (seed 365 in both markets, unverified; US corrected to 360); terminal-value basis = three: observed-inclusive, observed-exclusive, policy (5.6.13, consistent with G-55); KR rounding default = the majority option `down` (unverified).

> **User decision 2026-10-28 (after the fourth review round) [R4F-02]:** (1) **Split and stock-dividend ratios must be exact integer ratios**: every corporate action that changes the share count must carry TDS `ratio_num/ratio_den` (TDS v1.4 5.11, check A010 = error). The engine does not infer fractions from f64 (the inference rule of v3.2 and its thresholds are removed). For external data without an exact ratio, only when the user chooses `[corporate_actions] decimal_ratio = "literal"` is the shortest decimal representation of the f64 used as an exact decimal fraction, with a warning and a count in the report (5.6.1). The canonical producer already writes both columns in `tds.corporate_actions`. (2) **`size.kelly` is v1.x**: it is sensitive to the in-window estimate of μ and is left out of v1 (12.1). (3) **ML training inside a validation method is always forward-only**: every validation method (walk-forward, CPCV, segments) uses only the samples before the refit time τ (label availability time + embargo ≤ τ) — the same shape as live operation (5.3.11, 6.3a).
>
> **User decision 2026-10-28 (economic-event investing):** people who invest on economic indicators and corporate events use the same blocks: `Macro.series` (versioned macro series), `Events.<kind>` (event terms), the event schedule `nx.on_event`, `from_parquet(shape=)`; the invariants (time = availability time, determinism, screen = backtest) stand (3.2, 3.7, 5.3.12). **Superseded 2026-11-24 (#752)**: these readers leave; such values are the user's value tables, read as `nx.Item.<name>` (5.3.5) [#725 Decisions 250, 251].
>
> **User decision 2026-10-28 (before round 4):** (1) **CPCV in v1**: walk-forward, purged k-fold, CPCV paths, CSCV (PBO), DSR/PSR/MinTRL and automatic attempt records run inside the v1 engine, with real fills and accounting for each path. (2) **Minimum scope of the ML factor block in v1**: `nx.ml.factor(model, features, label, train=rolling|expanding, refit, embargo)`, the training window enforced by the engine (label availability time + embargo ≤ refit time), the fit/predict interface only (sklearn an optional dependency), predictions and fits recorded, model objects not saved (no pickle). MDA feature importance is v1.x. (3) **Validation accounts**: walk-forward = one account carried through, CPCV = fresh capital for each path and carried through within a path, simple segments = fresh capital for each segment. (4) **One entry point** (added the same day): validation is not a new function but a `method=` value of the familiar `nx.backtest(strategy, start=, end=, method="linear")`. The default `"linear"` is the current single linear run as it is; with `method="walk_forward" | "cpcv" | "cscv"` (or an options object such as `method=nx.splits.cpcv(n_groups=6, k_test=2, embargo=5)`), the engine runs trading for each fold and path and combines them into the same `Run` and the same report (path distribution, PBO, DSR/PSR, attempt record). The first screen shows the linear result and the method's distribution side by side. The CLI too: `nexusbt backtest my.py --method cpcv`. To keep the public surface small, a separate `nx.validate` is **not provided** (6.3a).

## 0. The design story (user 2026-10-28; original `STORY.md`)

**Trade with event accuracy, compute with vector speed. The user only snaps in blocks.**
(English for public documents: *Event-driven where it matters, vectorized where it scales. You just snap the blocks together.*)

**Why build it.** Whatever the engine, quant researchers hit similar walls (our own Korean and US factor research on zipline-reloaded + a 2026-10 source teardown of some 30 existing engines and 5,005 bug reports). (1) **Fitting the data to the engine leaves no time for research** (bundles, dump_bin, each engine's own folder format; point-in-time financials and delistings left to the user's loader). (2) **Fast means inaccurate and accurate means slow** (US, 3,175 names: vbt PRO 0.87 s, zipline 111 s, backtrader 506 s; high-dimensional factor strategies are hard to test). (3) **My own strategy is hard to assemble** (callbacks and engine internals; sizing and risk-management techniques are hard to swap in). (4) **When it is wrong, I cannot tell where** (errors that do not separate data, code and config; silent failures; future information suspected only when results look too good). (5) **ML-built factors are hard to test** (training outside the engine → predictions back in as data; training-time correctness is hard to check). (6) **One backtest cannot catch overfitting** (walk-forward, CPCV, PBO and DSR are manual work outside the engine). Closed screener services are easy, but I cannot put in my own data or code, and I cannot check their rules.

| Engine | Strong at | Which of the six above it hits |
|---|---|---|
| zipline-reloaded | Pipeline panel computation | 1·2·3·4·5·6, maintenance-only development |
| backtrader | an intuitive event engine, many order types | 2 (very slow)·4·6, development stopped |
| vectorbt (PRO) | overwhelming speed, mass parameter search, segment-splitting tools | 2 (real-world rules left to the user)·5, commercial limits, paid |
| QuantConnect LEAN | the widest real-world model | 1·2 (heavy)·6, accounting bugs such as split quantity loss |
| NautilusTrader | a Rust core, the same code as live trading | 1, no equity corporate actions, dividends or delistings (tick-centred) |
| Qlib | ML factor research, rolling retraining | 1 (its own format)·4, simple fills and accounting, China-centred |
| bt · QSTrader · Backtesting.py · PyBroker | stacking blocks, an easy start, walk-forward (PyBroker) | 2 (shallow realism)·4, development stopped, licence limits |
| pysystemtrade | volatility-target sizing, hold band | futures only |
| Portfolio123 · Genport · Quantus | screener = strategy, easy screens | closed services (no own data or code, rules cannot be checked) |

In the end, today one picks one of **fast factor research** (vectorbt, Qlib), **accurate trading** (LEAN, Nautilus) or **an easy screener** (Portfolio123, domestic services) and loses the rest, and **ML factors and statistically trustworthy validation** remain manual work outside the engine on every side. No engine gives all of the following in one place.

- a way to put in Korean and US equity panels with point-in-time financials and delistings as they are
- realistic fills and accounting that include the Korean market rules
- an open engine where my factors, ML models, sizing and risk management plug in as blocks
- validation inside the engine that measures overfitting over many paths, not one backtest
- today's screen and live operation from the same file as the backtest

NexusBT brings these five into one engine. The strengths taken from each engine and the weaknesses fixed are in `teardown/MATRIX.md`.

**What it aims for.**
- **The trading environment closest to reality**: trading with event-driven accuracy (fills at the next open after the decision, the adverse side when the order inside a daily bar is unknown, volume caps, upper and lower price limits, halts, tick sizes, costs and taxes, corporate actions, delistings, short borrow costs, exchange rates, reconciliation with a real ledger) — the account moves in event order every trading day.
- **Large panels, fast**: factors, filters and classifiers over all names × the whole period as arrays at once (the same term once, only the columns and dates needed, a year-chunk cache); the simulator moves one step per trading day, but takes all names of that day together.
- **The user need not know this split**: a strategy is made by plugging blocks into ten slots (schedule · universe · signal · construction · sizing · risk · decide · execution · exits · costs), and my own factor, sizing or risk management becomes a block with one function.
- **The same file to the end**: factor validation → backtest → today's screen → live operation are one strategy file, and the data is one snapshot path.
- **ML factors are blocks too**, **validated many times, not once** (walk-forward, CPCV, PBO and DSR inside the engine), **know at once when it is wrong** (what, where, why and how to fix it + whether data, code, config or engine; future information blocked before computing).

```
 snapshot path ──▶ [vector computation] Pipeline: factors · filters · classifiers (all names × period, at once)
                         ▼
               [blocks] schedule · universe · signal · construction · sizing · risk  ── target weights
                         ▼
               [event accuracy] every trading day: orders → fills → costs and taxes → corporate actions → account (all names of that day as arrays)
                         ▼
               results · today's screen · live ledger      ── the same strategy file
```

Intraday and tick (E4) need a true event loop, so then the same blocks run on a Rust core (or a NautilusTrader bridge) (ADR-0016, 11.2). **What it does not do**: shortcuts that make results look good (filling at the same close, future information, survivors only), structures usable only by someone who knows the engine's internals (mandatory callbacks, mandatory format conversion), giving up accuracy for speed (when the two collide, accuracy comes first, and speed comes from a faster implementation checked bit for bit against the reference implementation).

This chapter is explanation; the norms start at chapter 1. The sizing block, the block contract table, high-dimensional workloads, the ML factor block, the in-engine validation design and the `layer:` line of errors are round 4 topics (`review/round4-topics.md`) and are not yet in the v3.2 norms (v3.2's walk-forward and CPCV splitters are still reserved, 6.3).

---

## 1. v1 scope, terms, invariants

### 1.1 What v1 includes / does not include

**Strategy styles in one sentence** (user decision 2026-11-03): v1 supports systematic strategies that decide at a session close and execute at the next open, with a per-decision user block (`decide`, 3.7) called once per decision session, which may read the account [#725 Decisions 236, replacing 2026-11-03's "imperative per-bar handlers are not v1"]; discretionary intraday decisions and tick-level events are not v1 (the boundary is the "may read" table of the ten slots in 3.7, E41; chapter 11 names the extension points).

| Area | In v1 | Not in v1 (when) |
|---|---|---|
| Assets and frequency | Daily equity bars. Market names, calendars and currencies are read from the data (`tds.json markets`), and **supported markets = markets with a market rules pack** (v1 = `KR` (XKRX) and `US` (XNYS)). A market without a pack is E18; with `[markets] allow_untested = true` it runs on the generic pack, marked "untested market rules" | minutes, ticks, quotes, crypto, futures, options (E4 and E5, design only in chapter 11; TDS holds no schema for them: such kinds may be added later, equities first [#725 Decisions 252, 253]) [9.0 Q35, R-46] |
| Input | TDS v1.4 packages (`tds.json` + Parquet). The data required is **computed from the features the graph uses** (4.3; no fixed list of profiles). Check results come in three: declared / check level / engine-supported. `tds:minimal-daily` (adjusted prices only) serves the **analysis paths** only (`evaluate`, `run_pipeline`, `screen(factor)`), and there a Filter or an output that reads an adjusted column follows 4.3 (E18) [#725 Decisions 218]; an economic backtest needs raw prices + events (E18). The first public path is `tds import csv` + `tds manifest build` (M1). Missing columns get the fallbacks of 4.5 (announced and recorded) [SC-24, R-66, R-67] | an engine-side data ingestion step (ingest), reading DuckDB directly, importing from zipline, LEAN, Nautilus or DBN (v1.x, "planned" in TDS chapter 9) |
| Terms | Factor / Filter / Classifier, an explicit `nx.Pipeline`, operators with zipline names and 15 built-in factors (12 built in M1; the three return-series kernels of #725 Decisions 246 are built by T2.19), window-style user functions `@nx.factor/filter/classifier(window=…)` [R-03, R-05, Codex 11.4.4]. Three-valued Filters (missing = unknown), market-level series terms `nx.Market.series` ([V31-13]), percentile ranks and weighted composites with an explicit missing-value policy (`pct_rank`, `nx.ops.composite`), Korean trading-status terms `Trad.*` | panel-style user functions (`style="panel"`), period windows ("30d"), built-in cross-sectional regression and `Illiquidity` factors, and beta, correlation or regression against anything but a market series (after v1; against a market series they are v1, 3.4) [C8, R-47, OQ-15; #725 Decisions 246]; transforms with fitted state (`fit_segment`, reserved) [SC-19] — from v3.3 the only learning term is `nx.ml.factor` (5.3.11) |
| Market cross-sections | per-market cross-sections (default), cross-market cross-section operations `across_markets=True` (final stage only) [A-03] | feeding a cross-market cross-section back into a window |
| Financials and other values | the user's value tables, read as given as `nx.Item.<name>` (5.3.5) [9.0 Q24, R-63; #725 Decisions 205, 219, 250] | choosing fiscal periods, TTM, source priority or restatements inside the engine (the user's, documented, not checked [#725 Decisions 229]) |
| Portfolio | pure-function target construction: `top`, `bottom` (the short side, when shorting is on), the long–short book `nx.long_short`, **signed weights** (+ long, − short), net and gross exposure caps, weighting equal / value / inverse_vol, `max_weight`, weights within a sleeve, a tie-breaking key (`tiebreak`, default hash), **the sizing slot `sizing=`** (pure functions, market information at the decision only: `size.equal`, `value`, `inverse_vol`, `score`, `erc`, `vol_parity`, `stop_risk` + the modifiers `vol_target`, `cash_floor`, user `@nx.sizer`; `kelly` is v1.x [R4F-02]; 5.5.1a), **the risk list slot `risk=[...]`** (toward zero only: `group_caps`, `regime`, `vol_cap`, `liquidity_cap`, fitting to the exposure caps, the account-read-only `drawdown_brake`, `portfolio_stop`, `no_reentry`, user `@nx.risk_rule`; 5.5.2 and 5.5.2a), user construction blocks `@nx.portfolio` [C6, SC-01] | the target-state recursive hold band `top(n, hold=m)` (its norms are defined now in 5.5.3; it is implemented in T7.1 and is in v1, off by default, with no default width [#725 Decisions 244]) [SC-02, OQ-1]; optimisation `nx.portfolio.optimize`, `nx.risk.ledoit_wolf` (reserved, after v1) [SC-22]; an `on_rebalance` callback that relies on holdings [A-10]; buy/hold/sell asymmetry and turnover caps (v1.x, [V31-31]); correlation-shock and VaR overlays, the ATR version `size.stop_risk(atr=)`, the per-order notional cap `[execution] max_order_value_pct_nav` (v1.x) |
| Orders and fills | market, limit, stop and stop-limit, time in force day/GTC, partial fills under a volume cap, pending orders, a persistent trigger state, quantity conservation (`requested = filled + cancelled + remaining`), the exit-order life cycle (created right after the entry fill, resized at every holding change, no expiry), conservative rules for daily OHLC, intraday sale proceeds not used for intraday buys in the same bar, the quantity buyable under the real cost function, recording per fill the decision resolution, rule, decision price and arrival price [9.0 orders, R-56, SC-11]. Order prices snapped to the price grid (tick sizes), price comparisons in integers of the market minimum unit, a cap from that session's actual volume, minimum trade thresholds and weight bands. Short-sale and cover orders mirror the same rules (the adverse side; in all three fill stages in risk-reducing order, and of two orders that cross the sign, the second only after the reducing one is done). The time stop `exits.time_stop(n)` (the third OCO barrier) | intraday path order (only with minute or tick data, E4), simulating call-auction queues, daily average-price (VWAP) fills (v1.x, [V31-32]), simulating the US uptick rule (Rule 201) |
| Account | signed holdings (long–short symmetric; shorting off by default; when on, short proceeds, collateral, borrow cost and short-dividend payments), the margin account (`[account] type = "margin"`, off by default: one requirement formula, loan interest, a close-of-session margin call → a proportional reduction at the next open, 5.6.7a), integer share arithmetic (rational ratios), stale valuation prices that reflect corporate actions, per-component rounding rules (market rules pack), external deposits and withdrawals in the ledger (time-weighted return; the ledger's CAGR is a TWR index too) [V31-19], cash interest `[account] cash_rate_bps` (default 0), the short-sleeve ruin mark `ruined` (new orders stop, mandatory obligations as negative cash), separately funded sleeves per market, multi-currency valuation, money booked in integer minor units and balanced by the NAV and P&L bridge, decision sizing only from values knowable at the decision (delisting recoveries included), the immediate-settlement research model (`[settlement] model = "immediate"`, a reserved key) [9.0 Q23, 11.4.1, SC-18] | moving money between sleeves, leverage above the gross exposure cap, long margin loans in the cash account (default) (a book outside the feasible region `long_weight + c·short_weight ≤ 1` is E16 before the run), forced liquidation for a collateral shortfall in the cash account (for the margin account `type = "margin"` both are in v1 through 5.6.7a), data on borrowable quantities and borrow fees, T+1/T+2 settlement models [OQ-13], periodic-contribution scenarios in backtests [V31-19] |
| Corporate actions | **one resolver** that takes a reference time (amendments, cancellations, late terms, 5.3.3a), shared by adjustment, accounting and checks. Splits, reverse splits, consolidations (capital reductions), bonus issues (stock dividends, free share issues), cash dividends (booking entitlement, receivable, payment and amendment), return of capital, stock mergers and mixed mergers, cash mergers, terminal value on delisting, a lapse policy for the rights of paid-in capital increases [11.4.3] | automatic handling of spinoffs (fails without data), exchange tender offers, selling or exercising rights |
| Costs | the provisional bp profile (default), commission and tax tables by country and period + ½ spread + the general impact `Y·σ^a3·(q/ADV)^a2` (default a2=0.5, a3=1; `realistic`, after the tables are verified), per-share commissions, 0×, 1× and 2× sensitivity [9.0 defaults, Q6, R-61, SC-11] | calibration to real broker trades (TCA calibration later; only the columns in v1). Optional: provisional cost tables by period and liquidity band (a user setting; the default stays fixed bp) [V31-27] |
| Research | `evaluate` (default label = holding return with dividends reinvested, `fwd_return`; `fwd_return_adj` for comparison; three label times; IC, quantiles, turnover, coverage, counts, naive breadth, expected IR, BHY-adjusted p), `backtest`, `screen`, `sweep`, `compare`, `explain` (term values and **strategy decisions**), `check` (static checks, truncation probe, start-date probe), segment splitting (train/validation/test), attempt records, showing PSR, DSR, PBO, MinTRL and the Lo-adjusted Sharpe, capacity scenarios (optional), rebalance-date luck (`offsets`) and rolling-window tables; **validation methods** `backtest(method=…)` (`linear` default, `walk_forward`, `cpcv`, `cscv`; purging by label availability time and the AFML embargo, real fills and accounting for each fold and path, path distributions, PBO, DSR/PSR/MinTRL, automatic attempt records; 6.3a); **ML factors** `nx.ml.factor` (a training window the engine enforces, fit/predict only, `predictions` and `fits` recorded; 5.3.11); the label `nx.labels.triple_barrier` (5.8) [9.0 Q26, R-37~R-39, R-57, SC-04~SC-09, SC-13] | MDA and clustered MDA feature importance, sample-uniqueness weights, Hansen SPA, ONC N_eff (v1.x); meta-labels, Monte Carlo on synthetic data, hyperparameter search inside purged CV (later); the effective number of trials N_eff (v1.x) [SC-10], pass badges, automatic blocking gates [Q7] (cash interest moved into v1 in v3.2 — the account row) |
| Output | tables derived from the event truth (`orders`, `account_events`), cost, sleeve and currency attribution (`attribution.parquet`), exposures (`exposures.parquet`), ecosystem adapters (`Run.to_pyfolio`, `FactorReport.to_alphalens`; pandas an optional dependency), the round-trip trade table `trades.parquet` (long and short), deviation from target and execution quality, benchmark-relative metrics and monthly tables, long, short, gross and net exposure [SC-07, SC-16] | Brinson sector attribution (v1.x), factor attribution (later) |
| Operation | `live` order preview and pre-checks (E36), the paper ledger, ledgers a person writes (`manual:`: real fills and discretionary records, broker fill CSVs) and manual overrides, ledger deposits and withdrawals [V31-19], screen X-Ray, CSV order sheets, ledger reconciliation `reconcile`, the ledger status file `status.json` [R-49, SC-14] | sending orders to a broker (separately, after E3) [Q11, OQ-10] |
| Surface | Python API, a thin CLI, static HTML reports, an English quick start (CSV → check → factor → saved run → rerun) | web server, login, job queue, browser editor [L5] |
| Publishing and distribution | a public repository (Apache-2.0), a clean wheel-install gate, an independent-producer acceptance test, a changelog, a contributing guide | publishing on PyPI (user decision, chapter 12 OI-44) |
| Verification | zipline-reloaded pinned at 3.2.1, only as a comparison reference (in an isolated environment). Order types are compared **only through presets that imitate zipline's semantics**, and our adverse-side OHLC rules are tested with hand-calculated golden cases [9.0 Q35, R-64, D7, SC-12]. The TDS conformance checker (`tds check`) | a fork, `engine="auto"` [C10, R-48] |

### 1.2 Terms (a short table; the glossary that explains them is `GLOSSARY.md`, 9.2)

| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| TDS | NexusBT Data Standard v1 (document edition v1.4, `tds_version = "1.0"`, status = draft → RC → 1.0). The only input format the engine reads (a folder + `tds.json` + Parquet). `engine-data-standard-v1.4.md` |
| Package, snapshot | one immutable folder that follows TDS. It has a `snapshot_id` and a set of three hashes (physical, value, semantic). An export a producer published is a snapshot |
| Hash set (physical / value / semantic) | physical = sha256 of the file bytes. value = sha256 of a language-independent canonical typed serialisation (TDS 4.5). semantic = value hash + normalised resolution metadata (availability rules, parameters, markets, policies, profile versions). A snapshot's identity is its three hashes (physical, value, semantic; TDS 4.5): the run manifest, `DecisionContext`, the ledger and `rerun` identify a snapshot by its **semantic hash**. The **cache keys** (L0, L1; 5.3.9) do not hold the semantic hash: they hold the **file sha256** of each partition the node reads, the per-dataset resolution-metadata hash, the corporate-action chain hash and the hash of the axis rows each block depends on, so a partition unchanged between two snapshots still hits |
| Profile | a TDS bundle a package declares it follows, written with a version (`tds:equity-daily-core@1.0`). It is only a conformance claim; what the engine can do is set by **capability** (4.3) |
| Capability result set | declared / checked up to a level (verified@manifest, schema, full) / supported by this engine. What was not checked is not called passing |
| Fallback | the defined behaviour of the engine when a profile or column is missing. Always left in the warning `DataProfileWarning` (E30) and in the manifest's `assumptions` (4.5) |
| Edition | removed 2026-11-27 (#752): the engine reads no edition (no kind it reads has one since the tick step), and `[data] edition` left; TDS's `editions` field left with #752's 12e (TDS 4.2) |
| Market | a key of TDS `markets` (for example `KR`, `US`). Each market has one calendar, currency and time zone. Market names are not hard-coded in engine code |
| Market rules pack | `resources/markets/<MARKET>.toml`: that market's price limits, open tolerance, provisional bp, ½-spread floor and tradability rule version. v1 packs = KR, US. Only markets with a pack are "supported markets" |
| Session | one trading day of a market calendar. `(calendar_id, session_date)` with `ts_open`, `ts_close` |
| Market axis | the session sequence of one market. Market terms are computed on this axis |
| Session cutoff | the time the term values of market-axis row s are based on = `ready_m(s) = ts_close(s) + Δ_m` (5.1.2) |
| Availability time (`ts_available`) | the UTC instant an observation can first be used. TDS gives it as a column or a derivation rule |
| Decision | one time a sleeve sets its target. Recorded as a `DecisionContext` |
| Information cutoff (`decision_ts`) | the last UTC time of information a decision may use. An observation is used only when `ts_available <= decision_ts` |
| Reference clock (clock) | the setting that fixes the decision time in multi-market strategies: `"<MARKET>_close"` (that market's close; default = the first market when the snapshot's `markets` keys are sorted by UTF-8 bytes, `KR_close` for a KR+US package), `market_local` (`utc:HH:MM` reserved). `KR_close` and a daily bar mean the regular-session close; after-market trades are not included. For KR this close is the KRX regular-session close: trades on NXT (Nextrade, the alternative trading system) are not included in the close, the OHLC, the volume or `value_traded`, even those made during regular-session hours (decision 2026-11-01) |
| Sleeve | an account compartment funded separately for one market. Cash in local currency, no transfers between sleeves |
| Base currency (base_ccy) | the currency of combined reports. It changes valuation only, not information time |
| Term | Factor (number), Filter (true/false), Classifier (category). A graph node |
| Registered kernel | a computation kernel the engine provides (built-in factors, operators). The numeric rules (5.3.10) and the promise of bit determinism apply to it. User window functions are not registered kernels |
| Eligibility / tradability | eligibility = can it be chosen at the decision (universe). tradability = can it fill in the execution session (state machine). A name halted at the decision can still be chosen if it is eligible; the state machine blocks the fill [SC-23] |
| Canonical target | the `(instrument_id, target_weight)` table one decision produces. What must be the same in screen and backtest |
| Target stage | the stages that build the canonical target: construction (`construction`, `portfolio=`) → sizing (`sizing=`) → risk (the market-only rules of `risk=`). None of them takes the account state; account-reading risk rules come after them, in the final-target layer (5.5.2a) [SC-01] |
| Target state, anchor | the canonical target set of the previous decision (the recursive input) and the decision where that recursion starts. Used by the hold band (T7.1, in v1) [SC-02] |
| Order intent | share-quantity orders computed from the final target + the account state |
| Run | one `evaluate/backtest/screen/sweep/live`. A `runs/<run_id>/` folder |
| study | the group in which attempts are counted. Default = the strategy (or factor) file name. Stored with a stable `study_id` (UUID) |
| Candidate | a distinct (graph hash, parameters, config, segment) combination within a study. It has identity only |
| Observation | the statistics from running one candidate once (snapshot, period, Sharpe, skewness, kurtosis, NW t). Running again adds an observation and does not overwrite one |
| Cohort | a set of comparable observations (same study, kind, segment, label, horizon, frequency). DSR's N and V[SR] and BHY's m are counted within one cohort only, and the report keeps `cohort_sha256` |
| Segment | a user-named period: `train`, `validation`, `test` and so on |
| Exposure | the record of which segments a run computed performance on. (A different word from the portfolio exposure table `exposures.parquet`, which is "holdings exposure") |
| Value tables (`values`, `market_values`, `symbol_values`) | the user's values outside trading, rows of four words in three shapes, read as `nx.Item.<name>` (5.3.5); they replace `fundamentals_state` and the other removed kinds [#725 Decisions 219, 250] |
| Reconcile | replaying the paper ledger **with the snapshots the ledger recorded** to find the first difference. Comparison with the latest data is `restate=True` (restatement) [SC-14] |
| Resolver | the function that takes a reference time and resolves corporate-action amendments, cancellations and late terms (separating an event's existence time from its terms time, 5.3.3a) into one version per event and four effects (price factor, share factor, entitlement, settlement). Adjustment, accounting and checks use only this (5.3.3a) |
| Price grid | the price points laid out at the tick size of that market, date and price band. Order prices snap to the grid on the conservative side, and price comparisons use integers of the market minimum unit (KR 1 won, US 0.0001 dollars) — not tick counts that differ by price band (5.6.4a) |
| Three-valued Filter | true, false, unknown (missing input). `~`·`&`·`|` follow the Kleene rules; at the final use (universe, selection, mask) unknown = false (5.4.1). The only explicit replacement is `Filter.fillna(bool)` |
| Signed holding and weight | long = positive, short = negative. Gross exposure = Σ|w|, net exposure = Σw. With shorting off, every target weight ≥ 0 |
| Phase | the full order of events within a session: `session_start < open < intraday < close_fill < close_mark < decision < session_end` |
| Ruined sleeve (`ruined`) | a sleeve with shorts whose `decision_value ≤ 0`. That sleeve creates no new order intents and books only the mandatory obligations (5.6.7) |
| Rule estimate | a rule value (`short_exit_estimate` and the like) used to put a short's obligation of unknown amount into decision sizing. It is not a cap, and in `nav_economic` it is replaced by the realised amount (5.6.9) |

### 1.3 Invariants (tested in every milestone)

| # | Invariant | Test |
|---|---|---|
| INV-01 | **Information time**: every observation x a decision d uses has `ts_available(x) <= decision_ts(d)`; a date-only value's `ts_available` is the freeze time of the row it sits in (5.1.4) [#725 Decisions 230 to 233]. This holds not only for signal inputs but also for **the account inputs that size orders** (valuation prices, receivables of known amount, terminal recoveries, exchange rates) and for the NAV series the account rules read (`nav_decision`, 5.6.9) [11.4.1, C1]. A corporate action is visible from its existence time and, before its terms time, is an entitlement of unknown amount (5.3.3a). **Fill causality**: fill prices and cost components use only values knowable by that fill stage (an open-stage fill price does not depend on that session's H, L, C or volume; cost inputs up to t−1). Fill **quantities** use that session's realised volume as an **after-the-fact feasibility cap** (5.6.6): it is not a decision input, and it is an approximation that can move results in either direction (v3.1's "it only reduces, so it cannot make results better" was false and was removed; announced as an assumption sentence) | property tests P-FUT, P-FILLCAUSAL, hand-calculated G-TIME, G-TIME-LATE, G-42. There is only one exception: a pretrained model's `allow_overlap=True` (5.3.11, declared, marked and recorded by the user). |
| INV-02 | **Future-data invariance**: adding data after time τ to a snapshot leaves the canonical targets and orders of the decisions with `decision_ts <= τ` unchanged [11.8] | P-FUT |
| INV-03 | **Chunk invariance**: in the same environment (same `uv.lock`, same CPU kind), changing the period chunk size or the parallel order leaves the results the same. Chosen names and target weights exactly, **registered-kernel node** values bit for bit, **user-function nodes** as "same window input → same output" (the window inputs themselves do not depend on chunking, so the results are the same too). There is no bit promise across different environments [11.7, SC-03] | P-CHUNK |
| INV-04 | **Index permutation invariance**: changing the order of the in-memory dense index leaves the results bit-identical (cross-section reductions use the sorted index order of 5.3.10) [11.8, SC-03] | P-PERM |
| INV-05 | **Cache invariance**: cache hits and misses (L0 raw blocks, L1 term values) give bit-identical results. The key holds everything the result depends on (file sha256, every version and availability time of the corporate-action chain, the axis rows of the block, propagated input rows, the learning context of learned nodes, the first `session_ord` of nodes that read the absolute session number) (the key of 5.3.9) [C9, SC-15] | P-CACHE |
| INV-06 | **Accounting balance**: money is booked in integer minor units (int64). Every day `nav_economic = cash + Σ holding×valuation price + receivables + unsettled entitlements (realised amounts)`, with zero difference (in minor units). **NAV and P&L bridge**: per currency, every day `NAV_t − NAV_{t−1} = price-change P&L + distributions (dividends, return of capital, cash consideration) + corporate-action cash (fractional shares) − costs + FX conversion and exchange-rate effects + rounding residual`, and the bridge rows close with zero difference (external deposits and withdrawals are the bridge's `external_flows` row — ledger only, 0 in backtests [V31-19]; borrow cost, rebates, cash interest and margin loan interest (`margin_interest`) are the `financing` row). Mandatory obligations (borrow cost, short dividends, terminal obligations, tax payments) are booked as negative cash even when cash is short (`shortfall`), and in the cash account order fills never create negative cash (the margin account's loans are in 5.6.7a; the bridge formula is the same). Valuation prices are on the same corporate-action basis as the holdings at the same time (a stale valuation price on a day without trades is also multiplied by the price factors of later events, 5.6.3). Holdings are signed (short = negative), and the same formula holds for shorts as it is (including the short-dividend payment and borrow-cost rows). v2's "sum of cash flows = NAV change" was a false formula that left out valuation gains and was removed [11.4.3, blueprint chapter 9 item 6] | P-NAV, P-BRIDGE, hand-calculated G-48, G-49, G-54, G-65, G-72, report `reconciled: yes` |
| INV-07 | **No duplicates**: after a rerun or a resume, no event, payment or fill is duplicated [11.8] | P-RESUME |
| INV-08 | **screen = backtest**: the same strategy, snapshot, policies, **anchor** and `DecisionContext` give the same canonical target. With the same account state as well, the same order intents. The target is a function only of the information at the decision and the canonical target state of the previous decisions (the target-state recursion), and does not take the account state [R-13, 11.4.6, SC-02]. For a strategy with a `decide` block (3.7), this is promised up to the Pipeline values; what the block returns, and everything after it, match only with the same account state and the same block state [#725 Decisions 241] | P-PARITY13 |
| INV-09 | **Determinism**: in the same pinned environment, with the same input (the same semantic hash), the output Parquet bytes are identical and the value hashes are identical [R-31, 11.7]. The deterministic path does not depend on wall-clock time, random numbers or hash-set iteration order, so it is the same even in **two new processes** with different `PYTHONHASHSEED` (2.5) | rerun test, P-DET (two new processes) |
| INV-10 | **Resume = one go**: the result of resuming from a state checkpoint = the result of running without a break. Paper-ledger reconciliation (`reconcile`) is its operational version [11.8 M3, SC-14] | P-RESUME, P-RECON |
| INV-11 | **Adjustment agreement**: a registered kernel's shortcut value = the reference implementation's value (relative 1e-12; an algebraically equal transform, so bit identity is not promised) [C2] | P-ADJ |
| INV-12 | **No hidden filling**: missing values propagate, and filling happens only through an explicit operation (`fillna`). The data fallbacks (4.5) are not hidden filling but defined behaviours that are announced and recorded [C7] | unit tests |
| INV-13 | **Fixed numeric order**: sums, means and variances of registered kernels are computed in the defined order of 5.3.10 (windows by ascending row, cross-sections in sorted `instrument_id` order), and in the same environment the fast kernels are bit-identical to that result. Kernels of the **transcendental-function class** (`log`, `exp`, `pow`) call the same compiled element function on the reference and fast paths (bit-identical in the same environment, relative 1e-12 across environments). BLAS, `fastmath` and parallel reductions are not used [SC-03] | P-KERNEL (fast = reference, bits) |
| INV-14 | **Start-date invariance**: moving the run's start date by k sessions leaves the term values and canonical targets of the overlapping decisions bit-identical (every term is a function only of the data in its own window, and schedules are anchored to the calendar, not to the start of the run — `every(n)` is the absolute `session_ord`). For strategies with the target-state recursion on, only with the same anchor [SC-04] | P-START |
| INV-15 | **Metamorphic relations**: under price scaling (M-SCALE), adding names outside the universe (M-DUMMY), a fake split of ratio 1.0 (M-SPLIT-NEUTRAL), changing the base currency (M-CCY) and summing sleeves (M-SLEEVE), the relations of the table in 8.3 hold **within that table's preconditions**. Counterexamples that break the preconditions are tested too [SC-04] | `tests/property/test_metamorphic.py` |
| INV-16 | **Events are the truth**: `positions`, `daily`, `metrics`, `attribution` and `exposures` can be rebuilt from `orders` and `account_events` (+ snapshot prices and exchange rates) and are equal to the rebuilt values [blueprint chapter 11 item 1] | P-DERIVED |
| INV-17 | **Input conformance**: the engine reads only the datasets and files declared in `tds.json` (inside the package folder, with no duplicate paths), and uses only packages that passed the check level declared at open (4.4). The synthetic generator's output passes `tds check --level full` [SC-24, TDS-D21, TDS-D24] | P-TDS, `test_synth_conforms` |
| INV-18 | **Order quantity conservation**: for every order, always `requested_qty = filled_qty + cancelled_qty + remaining_qty` (all ≥ 0; the order quantity is a size and the direction is `side`), and a split rebase is left as an event (`order_rebased`). Exit orders do not expire until the holding is 0. Share counts use integer arithmetic (5.6.1 `floor_shares`, rational ratios), so holdings of integer multiples create no fractional-share cash (long and short alike). A cover quantity does not exceed the short size `|q|` (the excess is `qty_cancelled(over_cover)`) | P-QTY, P-STATE, G-53 |
| INV-19 | **Ledger once**: in the paper ledger, a decision emits its order batch exactly once and a session fills exactly once. After concurrent runs or a forced stop at a commit boundary, it recovers to one valid state | P-LIVE, P-BOOK |
| INV-20 | **Price grid**: every limit and stop price, and the fill prices set from them, lie on that session's price grid (open and close fills take the data value as it is). Price comparisons use integers of the market minimum unit (`[grid] base_unit`) — not tick counts that differ by price band; an observed bar price that does not fit that integer is not moved, and only that bar is compared in floating point (`off_grid_bar`) | P-GRID, G-57 |
| INV-21 | **Sleeve independence**: once the targets are set, sleeves are independent of each other — replaying each sleeve separately = replaying them together (exception: `[taxes] pay = "cash"` spanning several markets) | P-SLEEVE-ISO |
| INV-22 | **Shorting off = long only**: with `[shorting] enabled = false` (default), the exposure-cap stage is not called, there are 0 short events and collateral entries, and the value hashes of the four core tables (`targets`, `orders`, `account_events`, `daily`) equal the baseline from before the shorting tickets. Even when it is on, with no negative targets and `max_gross ≥ 1`, `max_net ⊇ [0, 1]`, the value hashes of all tables equal those of an off run of the same build (borrow cost 0, collateral computed only when there are shorts; if the user gives narrower exposure caps, longs can shrink too, so they are not the same) | P-SHORT-OFF (differential + baseline) |
| INV-23 | **Risk off = canonical**: without account-reading risk rules (`reads_account=True`), the account layer (5.5.2a) is not called, `targets.final_weight` is bit-identical to `target_weight` (the canonical target), and the `risk_state` table is empty. Even with account rules, if they always return `m = 1`, the value hashes of all the output tables (`targets`, `orders`, `fills`, `account_events`, `daily`, `bridge`, `coverage`, `skips`) **except** the following equal those of a run without rules: `risk_state`, the `step LIKE 'account:%'` rows of `waterfall`, the manifest's `graph_hash`, `settings_hash` and `run_id` (the rule order goes into `graph_hash`, 14.5), and the "risk order" line of the report header. The sizing slot and the market-only risk rules do not take the account, so they are part of the canonical target and INV-08 holds as it is; the result of account rules (`final_weight`) is screen = backtest, like order intents, only when the account state is the same too. With a `decide` block (3.7), `decided_weight` takes the place of `target_weight` in this invariant: without account rules, `final_weight` is bit-identical to `decided_weight` | P-RISK-OFF, P-SIZE-PURE, P-RISK-SHRINK |

### 1.4 Requirement numbers added by this spec

| Number | Requirement | Source |
|---|---|---|
| R-56 | v1 implements market, limit, stop and stop-limit orders, day/GTC, partial fills under a volume cap, pending orders and the exit-order life cycle (5.6.2a). On daily bars it decides by the conservative OHLC rules (5.6.4) and **is tested with hand-calculated golden cases (G-02~G-09, G-31~G-33, G-37~G-41)**. Comparison tests against zipline cover only the order types of the preset that imitates zipline's trigger behaviour (`preset="zipline"`) (8.1 L2-d) | 9.0 orders and advanced options, SC-12, A-57 |
| R-57 | There is no future lock. The user sets the train/validation/test segments in `nexusbt.toml [split]`, and a run chooses one with `segment=`. The engine records attempt counts and exposure and shows DSR | 9.0 Q26 |
| R-58 | Micro-caps are included by default and excluded with one config line (`exclude_microcap = true`). No hidden exclusion | 9.0 Q31 |
| R-59 | The benchmark defaults are the KOSPI and S&P 500 total-return indices. If the snapshot has no index instruments, the benchmark is marked "none" and not built | 9.0 Q30 |
| R-60 | When a dividend pay date is missing, the user declares once when the cash arrives: on the ex-date, or a number of calendar days after it; without that declaration the tool stops and asks (5.6.11). The estimate from the usual interval of the country (decision 2026-11-01) was removed 2026-11-24 (#752) [#725 Decisions 214, 215] | 9.0 Q29 |
| R-61 | Until the cost tables are verified, provisional bp costs (`provisional`) are the default and the report shows "provisional costs" | 9.0 Q28 |
| R-62 | Korea and the US are separately funded sleeves. The reference clock stays a setting. Each decision records a UTC `decision_ts` | 9.0 Q23 |
| R-63 | ~~`Fund.*` only looks up intervals of the TDS `fundamentals_state`.~~ Superseded 2026-11-24 (#752): financial values are the user's value tables, read as given (5.3.5). The engine does not rebuild accounting selection rules | 9.0 Q24, C3 |
| R-64 | zipline-reloaded is used, at a pinned version, only as the comparison reference (oracle). The engine's run path does not import zipline | 9.0 Q35, D7 |
| R-65 | The engine is built with the plinth loop: the repository with `/plinth:new-project`, `/implement #N` for each ticket, merged after the required checks pass + a visual check | user requirement (chapter 9) |
| R-66 | The engine's input contract is TDS v1.1 (`tds_version` 1.x). The engine calls the TDS conformance checker (the `nexusbt_tds` package) as a library to check snapshots, and raises a failure as a `SnapshotError` (E14) carrying the TDS error code and (if there is one) the producer hint. Error messages do not include a particular producer's internal tickets | SC-24, TDS-D21 |
| R-67 | Even when a package does not fill every column and dataset (a valid lower-profile package), the engine runs by the fallback table of 4.5, announcing each fallback it uses (E30) and leaving it in the manifest. When a fallback cannot define the result, it fails (E18). A package that breaks the profile it declared is not repaired by fallbacks (E14) | the user's principle "no hidden filling" |
| R-68 | Market, currency, clock, sleeve and index ids are read from the data. Per-market rules are in market rules packs (4.7), and a market without a pack does not run without `allow_untested` | |
| R-69 | The public user's first path: two CSVs → `tds import csv` → `tds check` → `evaluate`/`run_pipeline` → a saved run → `rerun` works by the English quick start, without private producer knowledge | |
| R-70 | Release gate: the public examples run from a clean wheel install, and an independent producer's package passes the `full` check and the consumption test. A "KR/US verified" claim is made only with evidence (a summary) on real data. For every market rule applied (ticks, price limits, bans, rounding), the release evidence lists its source, its effective boundary and `verified` | |

---

## 2. Repository layout, dependency rules, security rules

### 2.1 Repository, public scope, licence, environment

- Repository: `<owner>/<name>` (after the name is settled, chapter 12 OI-05), **public**, plinth `--archetype=library`. Separate from the data producer [design 1 principle 4].
- **Public scope** (user decision 2026-10-27, TDS-Q5 decided): the engine code, this spec, and the data standard TDS with its format and checker **are designed on the premise of public distribution**. What stays private is only the **real data** inside the reference producer (raw data files, the DB, snapshots, vendor data). The user memory "personal research only (no redistribution, not public)" now applies only to the data [A-97].
- **Licence: Apache-2.0** (the repository's `LICENSE`, `license = "Apache-2.0"` in each package's `pyproject.toml`, a `NOTICE` file). Reasons:
  1. A permissive licence with a patent grant gives the lowest barrier to reusing a public engine and standard.
  2. It is the same licence as the comparison reference zipline-reloaded (Apache-2.0), so quoting its code and comments in `tests/parity` raises no compatibility problem.
  3. It is compatible with every core dependency (numpy, numba, scipy BSD; pyarrow, exchange-calendars Apache-2.0; polars MIT; jinja2 BSD) [exchange-calendars licence = Apache License 2.0, checked against the original text of the repository's LICENSE 2026-10-29 ([LICENSE](https://github.com/gerrymanoim/exchange_calendars/blob/master/LICENSE)); T0.1 checks it again at the pinned version].
  4. NautilusTrader (LGPL-3.0) is used in the E4 bridge only as the **optional dependency** `nexusbt[nautilus]`, by importing the installed library without copying its source. LGPL allows such use and puts no obligation on the Apache-2.0 code (it is enough that the user can swap the library; a Python import satisfies this) [est.: not legal advice]. The engine's default install contains no LGPL code.
  5. The list of code not to copy (GPL: Freqtrade, backtrader, pysystemtrade; AGPL: Backtesting.py; non-commercial: RQAlpha; Commons Clause: vectorbt, **PyBroker** (Apache-2.0 + Commons Clause, the first line of its `LICENSE` is "Commons Clause License Condition v1.0"); subscription: vectorbt PRO; paid implementation: mlfinlab) stands. Only ideas are borrowed. MIT code (ported functions of ml4t-diagnostic) keeps the original repository, commit and licence text at the head of the file and is listed in `NOTICE` [Q22].
  6. The development-only dependency hypothesis (MPL-2.0) does not go into the distribution.
- **What does not go into the repository** (public repository rules, enforced by tests — 2.7): reference-producer data, snapshots or any part of them, answer files (fixtures) made from vendor data, producer data paths (absolute paths under the user's home and the like) and internal host names and addresses, secrets. All test data is made only by the synthetic generator (4.8), by `nexusbt_tds.import_csv` from a mapping file in the test's temporary folder, or by `tds/conformance/build.py` for the conformance packages (2.7). Evidence files of measurements and real-data comparisons hold **summary numbers only** (row counts, maximum error, pass or fail, `snapshot_id`), not per-instrument values or prices.
- Python: **3.13** (`.python-version`). Package management `uv` (workspace), lockfile `uv.lock` (required by plinth). The comparison test environment is separate, **Python 3.12** (`tests/parity/`, 8.1).
- Where it runs: the user's compute machine that holds the data (its kind, address and account only in the user's notes outside the repository; no machine is a performance target, and chapter 7's measurements are taken on an 8 GB machine [#725 Decisions 256]). A notebook on a laptop attaches to that machine's Jupyter kernel over SSH. The engine builds no remote-call feature [Q9, A-17]. The data location is taken only from config (`[data] root`) or the environment variable `NEXUSBT_DATA_ROOT`.

### 2.2 Pinned versions (as of the first `uv.lock`)

Policy: every dependency is pinned exactly by `uv.lock`. A version published less than 14 days before locking is not used. Versions are raised by plinth step 9, one at a time, after the required checks pass.

| Package | Version | Use | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| numpy | 2.5.3 | dense block computation | numba 0.68 needs numpy < 2.6 |
| numba | 0.68.0 (llvmlite 0.50.0) | hot loop kernels | `cache=True`, `fastmath=False`, `parallel=False` (5.3.10). The first JIT delay is measured separately (chapter 7) |
| pyarrow | 26.0.0 | Parquet reading and writing, Arrow tables, IPC (L0 and L1 caches) | the only Parquet writer (deterministic writes) |
| polars | 2.0.0 | helper for interval lookups and sorting, user output tables | not used in the computation graph [Q15] |
| scipy | 1.18.1 | normal distribution functions, ranks | |
| jinja2 | 3.1.6 | `report.html` | one page with no external files |
| pandas | optional dependency `nexusbt[pandas]` | `.to_pandas()`, ecosystem adapters (5.9.8) | the latest 2.x/3.x compatible with numpy 2.5 at locking |
| exchange-calendars | 4.13.2 | predicting the next session in `live`, the synthetic generator (the substitute session table of 4.5 F-02 retired 2026-11-23, #705) | for past sessions TDS `calendar_sessions` is canonical [design 5.2] |
| optional dependency `nexusbt[nautilus]` | pinned when E4 starts (A-30) | event bridge | LGPL-3.0, only the name in v1 |
| optional dependency `nexusbt[ml]` | pinned at locking (scikit-learn 1.x, BSD-3-Clause) | ML examples and tests (the models of `nx.ml.factor`) | the engine does not import it (5.3.11). The default wheel runs without sklearn and the ML examples run in an environment with the extra installed — two install checks (`test_clean_wheel_runs_example`, `test_ml_extra_runs_ml_example`) |
| optional dependency `nexusbt[opt]` | after v1 | `nx.portfolio.optimize` (reserved) | cvxpy or Riskfolio-Lib, after the licence is checked [OQ-8] |
| development: pytest 9.1.1, hypothesis (14-day rule), psutil 7.2.2 | | tests, memory measurement | ruff, mypy and coverage versions are set by the plinth template |
| comparison environment: zipline-reloaded 3.2.1, pandas 2.3.3 (requires <3.0), numpy as the lock sets it | | only for `tests/parity/` | not installed in the engine environment [R-64] |

### 2.3 Package layout

The split follows the deep-module principle (a small interface, much implementation). Each package exports only the "public interface" below from its `__init__.py`; the rest are internal modules with a `_` prefix [Codex 11.6, codebase-design]. The repository is a uv workspace with two distributions: the engine `nexusbt` (import `nexusbt`) and the data-standard checker `nexusbt-tds` (import `nexusbt_tds`, CLI `tds`). TDS is a **public document and package versioned separately** from the engine (the document in `docs/tds/`, the package in the `tds/` folder, version = `tds_version` `1.0`, package version the matching `1.0.x`, release status draft → RC → 1.0) [A-98].

```
nexusbt/                                     (public repository, Apache-2.0)
├── LICENSE  NOTICE  pyproject.toml(workspace)  uv.lock  .python-version
├── AGENTS.md  CONTEXT.md  README.md(English)  CONTRIBUTING.md  SECURITY.md  CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md  CHANGELOG.md
├── docs/spec/engine-spec-v3.4.md             (this document; the public edition without private comments, personal information or hosts)
├── docs/tds/tds-v1.4.md                      (the TDS document body, with tds-v1.4.ko.md and tds-examples.md beside it; the public edition of engine-data-standard-v1.4.md without chapter 11 (producer mapping) or personal information)
├── docs/quickstart.md                        (English quick start, tested by 8.5)
├── docs/adr/0001-… .md                       (9.3)
├── tds/                                      ★ data standard (versioned separately)
│   ├── README.md  CHANGELOG.md
│   ├── schemas/<kind>/<MAJOR.MINOR>.json      (machine-readable dataset schema sources, TDS 10.3; `status: stable|experimental`)
│   ├── conformance/<code>/{pass,fail}/        (small passing and failing packages for each code, TDS 8.5)
│   ├── conformance/hash-vectors.json          (language-independent test vectors for the value and semantic hashes, TDS 4.5)
│   ├── pyproject.toml                         (distribution name nexusbt-tds, dependency: pyarrow only)
│   └── src/nexusbt_tds/                       check, hash, manifest, describe, import_csv (no import of nexusbt)
├── src/nexusbt/
│   ├── __init__.py          re-exports only the public surface (3.1)
│   ├── contracts/           the ID constant, engine-side column sets, units, policies, errors, shared frame and decision-context types (no dependencies)
│   ├── config/              reading and validating nexusbt.toml (secret keys forbidden)
│   ├── data/                opening snapshots (calls the tds checker), profiles and fallbacks, market blocks, L0 cache, value tables, calendars, exchange rates
│   ├── pipeline/            term value objects, datasets, operators, built-in factors, Pipeline, hashes, static checks
│   ├── compute/             planning, reference kernels, fast kernels, adjustment, cross-sections, L1 cache, executor
│   ├── portfolio/           scores → canonical target (pure functions, stages: construction → sizing → risk) + the account layer final
│   ├── risk/                money management `size.*` and risk rules `risk.*` (5.5.1a, 5.5.2, 5.5.2a; reserved `ledoit_wolf`)
│   ├── ml/                  the `ml.factor` training node, training windows, `fits` and `predictions` records (5.3.11; does not import sklearn)
│   ├── execution/           orders (columnar order table), fill models, costs, sizing, the daily state machine, presets
│   ├── accounting/          columnar account state, integer minor-unit booking, consuming the corporate-action resolver, entitlements, terminal value, valuation, NAV bridge
│   ├── research/            labels (triple barrier included), IC, quantiles, breadth, statistics (NW, PSR, DSR, PBO, MinTRL, BHY, Lo), segments, splitters and validation methods (6.3a), attempt records, probes
│   ├── artifacts/           run transactions, manifests, hashes, index (SQLite), saved sources, ledgers, reconciliation, attribution and exposure, reports
│   ├── api/                 evaluate/backtest/screen/live/fill/reconcile/sweep/compare/explain/check/describe/runs
│   ├── cli/                 argparse subcommands (a thin layer that calls api)
│   ├── compat/              zipline.py (imitates zipline names, no zipline import), adapters.py (pyfolio and alphalens shapes, pandas optional)
│   ├── bridge/              the E4 event-engine boundary (Protocols and data types only, chapter 11)
│   ├── testing/             the synthetic TDS package generator (4.8), hand-calculation helpers
│   └── resources/           markets/{KR,US,_generic}.toml (market rules packs), costs/default_v1.csv, limits/{kr_price_limits,kr_new_listing_band}_v1.csv, ticks/{kr,us}_tick_table_v1.csv, shorting/kr_short_bans_v1.csv
├── tests/
│   ├── unit/  property/  golden/  docs/  perf/  repo/  (golden/expected/** cannot be changed by an implementation PR, 8.2)
│   └── parity/              its own pyproject.toml and uv.lock (Python 3.12, zipline 3.2.1)
└── bench/                   measurement scripts and result JSON (summaries only)
```

### 2.4 Public interface by module (deep modules)

Every signature uses `from __future__ import annotations` and passes mypy; data types are `@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)`.

| Module | Public interface (only this is called from outside) | What it hides |
|---|---|---|
| `nexusbt_tds` (separate package, `tds` in this document) | `check(path, *, level: Literal["manifest","schema","full"], profiles: Sequence[str] | None = None, datasets: Sequence[str] | None = None, max_errors: int = 1000) -> CheckResult` (`.errors`, `.warnings`, `.profiles: dict[str, ProfileStatus]` — `ProfileStatus(declared: bool, verified_level: Literal["none","manifest","schema","full"], result: Literal["pass","fail","not_checked"])`, `.unchecked: list[str]`, `.to_jsonl()`), `read_manifest(path) -> Manifest`, `value_hash(path, dataset) -> str`, `semantic_hash(path) -> str`, `import_csv(mapping, out) -> Path`, `build_manifest(path) -> Manifest`, `PROFILES`, `SCHEMAS` | the implementation of the check levels, canonical value serialisation and hash computation, reading schema files |
| `contracts` | `ID_COLUMN`, `ENGINE_COLUMNS: Mapping[str, frozenset[str]]` (the columns the engine must read per dataset, 4.6; a column a 4.6 row marks "+ optional" is read when present and is not in it), `Unit`, `Currency`, `Phase` (the stage order of 5.6.3), the policy enums, one `StrEnum` per 3.11 key with two or more closed choices (`DelistingPolicy` `[delisting] policy`, `RightsPolicy` `[corporate_actions] rights`, `UnsupportedPolicy` `[corporate_actions] unsupported`, `DecimalRatioPolicy` `[corporate_actions] decimal_ratio`, `BonusSharesTradablePolicy` `[corporate_actions] bonus_shares_tradable`, `UnknownInclusionPolicy` `[delisting] unknown_inclusion`, `VerifyPolicy` `[data] verify`, `PinsPolicy` `[data] pins`, `FallbacksPolicy` `[data] fallbacks`, `TiebreakPolicy` `[portfolio] tiebreak`, `BandEntriesPolicy` `[portfolio] band_entries`, `AccountTypePolicy` `[account] type`, `OrderTypePolicy` `[execution] order`, `ExecutionAtPolicy` `[execution] at`, `BandModePolicy` `[execution] band_mode`, `LimitFillPolicy` `[execution] limit_fill`, `UnknownTradabilityPolicy` `[execution] unknown_tradability`, `CostProfilePolicy` `[costs] profile`, `TaxesProfilePolicy` `[taxes] profile`, `TaxesPayPolicy` `[taxes] pay`, `TaxesBasisPolicy` `[taxes.groups.<name>] basis`, `LabelEntryPolicy` `[evaluate] label_entry`, `EvaluateTargetPolicy` `[evaluate] target`, `RoundingModePolicy` `[rounding.<MARKET>] mode`; values are the choice strings of 3.11's Validation column as written, and member names follow `Unit`'s rule: upper case, symbols as `_`), `NexusBTError` and its sub-errors (6.6), output schema version constants (`RUN_SCHEMA = "nexusbt-run/2"`, `EVENTS_SCHEMA = "nexusbt-events/1"`, `CHECKPOINT_SCHEMA = "nexusbt-checkpoint/1"`), the shared frame and decision-context types that cross a chain boundary in this table (`FactorFrame`, `TargetFrame`, `TargetState`, `DecisionGrid`, `DecisionContext`, `DecisionInputs`, `SchemaRegistry`, `ResolvedActions`, `SessionInputs`, `MarketView`, the `EventSink` Protocol; a type one module alone uses stays in that module — `ComputePolicy`, `TermCache` in `compute`, `SimConfig` in `execution`, `AccountState` in `accounting`, `PortfolioRule` in `portfolio`, `RiskRule`, `Sizer` in `risk`, `LabelFrame`, `EvalConfig` in `research`, `Snapshot` in `data`) | nothing (value definitions only) |
| `config` | `load_config(path: Path | None = None, overrides: Mapping[str, Any] | None = None, *, markets: Sequence[str] | Mapping[str, str] | None = None) -> Config` (keys that contain a market name are validated against the snapshot's `markets`; a mapping gives each market's currency, for the `base_ccy` default) | toml parsing, merging defaults, validation messages, the secret-key check, reading market rules packs |
| `data` | `open_snapshot(cfg: DataConfig, *, required: CapabilityRequest | None = None) -> Snapshot` | calling the tds checker, deciding capability (declared, checked, supported), fallbacks, reading partitions, column dictionary → column numbers, L0 cache, value-table rows on the market axis (5.3.5), calendars, exchange rates, the corporate-action resolver |
| `data.Snapshot` | `.id`, `.semantic_hash`, `.value_hash`, `.physical_hash`, `.profiles -> dict[str, ProfileStatus]`, `.capabilities -> DataCapabilities`, `.fallbacks -> list[FallbackRecord]`, `.markets -> Mapping[str, MarketInfo]` (calendar, currency, time zone, watermark, rules pack or None), `.calendar(market) -> Calendar`, `.market_block(market, fields, sessions) -> MarketBlock`, `.items(names, market, sessions) -> MarketBlock` (value tables, 5.3.5), `.actions(market, cutoff) -> ResolvedActions` (5.3.3a), `.fx() -> FxSeries`, `.instruments(market) -> pa.Table`, `.explain(instrument_id, field, ts) -> Explanation` | Parquet, partitions, statistics pruning |
| `pipeline` | `Term`, `Factor`, `Filter`, `Classifier`, `Pipeline`, the datasets `Price/Meta/Item`, the `factor/filter/classifier` decorators, the `factors` module, `graph_hash(term) -> str`, `static_check(graph, ctx) -> list[Finding]` | node normalisation, hash computation |
| `compute` | `compute(pipeline: Pipeline, grid: DecisionGrid, snapshot: Snapshot, policy: ComputePolicy, cache: TermCache | None = None) -> FactorFrame` | planning, batching, kernel choice, adjustment, cross-section operations, parallelism |
| `portfolio` | `build_targets(scores: FactorFrame, rule: PortfolioRule, grid: DecisionGrid, *, risk: RiskRule | None = None, prior: TargetState | None = None) -> tuple[TargetFrame, TargetState]` | tie handling, caps, weighting, recursion |
| `risk` | `RiskRule` (Protocol: `apply(targets: TargetFrame, inputs: DecisionInputs) -> TargetFrame`), `none() -> RiskRule` | – |
| `accounting` | `AccountState` (columnar, 5.6.1), `apply_session_start(state, session: SessionInputs, actions: ResolvedActions, policy: AccountingPolicy) -> tuple[AccountState, list[Event]]`, `value(state, marks: Marks, fx: FxView, *, asof: datetime) -> Valuation`, `bridge(prev: Valuation, cur: Valuation, events) -> BridgeRows` | handling entitlements, receivables and corporate actions, integer minor-unit booking |
| `execution` | `simulate(targets: TargetFrame, market: MarketView, config: SimConfig, initial: AccountState, sink: EventSink) -> SimResult`, `step_session(state, orders, session: SessionInputs, config) -> StepResult`, `SimConfig` (every knob of appendix C) | the order state machine, fill rules, costs, sizing |
| `research` | `forward_labels(..., kind: Literal["econ","adj"] = "econ") -> LabelFrame`, `factor_stats(values: FactorFrame, labels: LabelFrame, cfg: EvalConfig) -> FactorStats`, `psr`, `dsr`, `min_trl`, `pbo_cscv`, `bhy_adjust`, `lo_sharpe`, `newey_west_t`, `signal_weight_corr`, `Segments`, `TrialRegistry` (candidates, observations, cohorts), `probe_truncation`, `probe_start` | quantile rules, bandwidth |
| `artifacts` | `RunStore(root).begin(kind, name, study, ...) -> RunTxn`; `RunTxn.write_table(name, table)`, `.capture_sources(paths)`, `.set_manifest(fields)`, `.seal() -> RunRecord`, `.abort()`; `Index(root)` (holds the canonical pins); `BookStore(root).open(name) -> BookTxn` (locking, state version comparison, commit, recovery, 5.9.6); `derive_tables(events, prices, fx) -> DerivedTables` (INV-16); `render_report(run) -> Path` | temporary folders, atomic renames, fsync order, hashes |
| `api` | the functions of 3.8 | coordination |

**A seam where Rust can be swapped in** [ADR-0016, user decision 2026-10-28]: the three hot kernels sit behind a narrow internal interface — `nx.compute._kernels` (window and cross-section kernels: array input → array output, no state), `nx.execution._step` (one step of the session simulator: columnar state + that session's input arrays → new state + event arrays), `nx.accounting._state` (the columnar account state and its booking functions). These three take no Python objects, callbacks or dicts; they exchange only Arrow arrays (or numpy arrays that share their buffers), integer and float scalars and integer codes. The reference implementation is Python (+ Numba), and another implementation (a Rust crate, PyO3/maturin, an optional build) is swapped in only with the same signatures, while `tests/property/test_kernels.py::test_kernel_swap_bit_equal` checks that it is bit-identical to the reference in the same environment. The condition for swapping is in chapter 7 (measurements and profiles). The signature rules are checked by `tests/unit/test_import_rules.py::test_*_seam_signatures_arrow_only` (T1.7b, T3.3, T3.4).

### 2.5 Dependency rules (enforced by tests)

```
tds (separate package; does not import nexusbt)
contracts ← config ← data ← compute ← api ← cli
nexusbt_tds ← data
contracts ← pipeline ← compute
contracts ← pipeline ← compat
contracts ← risk ← portfolio ← api
contracts ← accounting ← execution ← api
contracts ← research ← api
contracts ← artifacts ← api
```

- No import against the direction of an arrow. The import rule is four lines. (1) `contracts` imports nothing, and every module may import it (it is the root of every chain and is not drawn as an arrow). (2) Any other module imports only the modules its own **direct** arrows point to; an indirect path does not allow an import (`compute` → `data`, `pipeline`; `portfolio` → `risk`; `execution` → `accounting`; `data` → `config`, `nexusbt_tds`; `compat` → `pipeline`, so the zipline shim builds the engine's terms (decision 2026-11-19, #251)). (3) `api` may import every engine module (it is the composer); `cli` imports only `api`. (4) `compute` sits on the `data` and `pipeline` chains as the graph draws it; apart from that, only `api` joins chains.
- `src/nexusbt/**` and `tds/src/nexusbt_tds/**` do not import `zipline`, `nautilus_trader`, `duckdb`, `pickle`, `dill`, `cloudpickle`, `joblib` or `shelve` [R-64, design 1 principle 4, SC-27]. `np.load(..., allow_pickle=True)` and `pd.read_pickle` are forbidden too. Enforced by ruff `flake8-tidy-imports.banned-api` and `tests/unit/test_import_rules.py` (reads every import and call through the AST and compares them with the graph above). The "checker reference implementation DuckDB (read-only)" of TDS 8.5 is implemented in this repository with pyarrow only [A-99].
- **No sources of non-determinism**: the decision-path modules (`data`, `compute`, `pipeline`, `portfolio`, `risk`, `accounting`, `execution`, `research`) do not use `datetime.now/utcnow/today`, `time.time/monotonic/perf_counter` (for progress display, and only in `api`), `random`, `numpy.random` (except where a `Generator` made from the probes' `[checks] seed` is taken as an argument), `uuid.uuid1/uuid4`, `os.urandom` or `secrets`.
