Where iteration over a `set`, `frozenset` or `dict` sets the order of events, outputs or sums, it goes through `sorted(...)`. Only `artifacts` makes the run time, `run_id` and `study_id`. Enforced by ruff `banned-api` + the AST test `tests/unit/test_determinism_rules.py`, and P-DET runs in two new processes with different `PYTHONHASHSEED` (Nautilus NT#3914: two runs with the same seed differed by 6% in PnL because of hash iteration order).
- `pipeline` reads no data (value objects and hashes only). The column and capability information needed to build a graph is passed at binding as a **schema registry** (`SchemaRegistry`, a value object) that `api` builds from the snapshot. `portfolio`, `risk` and `research` take only Arrow/numpy arrays.
- User code imports only `nexusbt`, `nexusbt.factors`, `nexusbt.ops`, `nexusbt.costs`, `nexusbt.exits`, `nexusbt.execution`, `nexusbt.portfolio`, `nexusbt.risk`, `nexusbt.dataset`, `nexusbt.compat.zipline` and `nexusbt.compat.adapters` [R-01]. These names are re-exports of `__init__`.

### 2.6 Security rules [SC-27]

- **No pickle**: run outputs, caches (L0, L1), ledger checkpoints and probe results do not use pickle. Tables are Parquet or Arrow IPC, the rest JSON. (Enforced by the forbidden list of 2.5.)
- **No secrets**: if any table of `nexusbt.toml` has a key whose name contains `key`, `secret`, `token` or `password`, it is a `ConfigError` (E16, "secrets do not belong in nexusbt.toml; use environment variables or the OS keychain"). Broker keys come later, only through environment variables or the keychain, by an adapter ADR. This is blocked together with the repository's `ci / secrets` check.
- **Code that runs**: `rerun` runs the run folder's `source/` in a **new process** (5.9.3; so that a module of the same name already imported in the current session does not get in the way). If the run manifest's `environment.machine_id` (= sha256(host unique id)[:16]; the original host name is not kept) differs from the current machine, it refuses with `UntrustedRunError` (E31) and runs only with `nx.rerun(id, trust=True)` / `nexusbt rerun ID --trust`. The host unique id is `/etc/machine-id` or `/var/lib/dbus/machine-id` where one is there, the hardware UUID (`gethostuuid`) on macOS, else the hardware address `uuid.getnode()` reads, but never the random stand-in Python returns when it finds none (the multicast bit set); when none of these can be read, `machine_id` is `"unknown"`, never a made-up value, and `rerun` treats `"unknown"` on either side as another machine (decision 2026-11-20, #635).
- **No data collection**: the engine does not collect data (that is the producer's job).
- **Package path safety**: the engine and the checker do not read `tds.json` paths that point outside the package folder (`..`, absolute paths, symbolic links that lead outside) or a path that appears twice (TDS-M012/M013, E14).

### 2.7 Public repository hygiene (enforced by tests)

`tests/repo/test_public_hygiene.py` (part of the default `ci / test`):
- `::test_no_private_paths` — fails if any text file of the repository contains a macOS home path (starting with `/Users`), a word from the **forbidden-word list outside the repository** (the file the environment variable `NEXUSBT_HYGIENE_WORDS_FILE` points to, or the git-ignored `.hygiene-private.txt`; it holds the user's accounts, hosts, data paths and producer name — so that those words themselves are not written in the public repository), or a private IP (the regular expression `(?<![\d.])(10(\.\d{1,3}){3}|192\.168(\.\d{1,3}){2}|172\.(1[6-9]|2\d|3[01])(\.\d{1,3}){2})(?!\.?\d)` — four-number addresses only; text such as "section 10.2" is not caught) (the repository edition of this spec is the edition without such lines).
- `::test_no_producer_internal_refs` — zero hits in `src/**`, `tds/src/**` and `docs/**` (the public edition) for the producer name of the forbidden-word list, the ticket shape ` X[0-9]+ ` and `.rebuild.lock`.
- `::test_section_numbers_not_ip` — text cases such as "section 10.2", "10.2.3", "172.16" and the five-number "192.168.1.2.3" are not caught by the hygiene test.
- `::test_no_data_files` — fails if it finds `*.parquet`, `*.duckdb` or `*.csv` in the repository (except small hand-written files under `src/nexusbt/resources/**` and `tds/conformance/**`). The data of `tds/conformance/` and `tests/` is made by generation scripts and its hashes must match the generation scripts.
- `::test_fixtures_are_synthetic` — every place in `tests/**` that opens a snapshot (`DataConfig(path=…)`, `nx.open(…)`, a `snapshot=` argument, `tds check` given a path) uses only a path made by `nx.testing.synth.make_snapshot`, or by `nexusbt_tds.import_csv` from a mapping file in the test's temporary folder, a copy or edit of such a package, a folder such a package was written into (a data root) or a path inside it, a package under `tds/conformance/` reached from the repository root, which `tds/tests/test_cli.py::test_committed_packages_match_the_generator` shows to be `tds/conformance/build.py`'s output, or a copy of it, or the package 8.1's parity runner writes with the generator's command (`python -m nexusbt.testing.synth --out`) and names in its manifest; a path that starts from a string literal never passes. It is a static check: it reads how each test's code builds the path, not what any file holds, so it catches a path written by mistake, not code written to get around it; it does not read what a mapping file names or what a test copies into a data root.
- `::test_license_headers` — `LICENSE` is Apache-2.0, and ported files have a source header.
- `::test_tds_public_has_no_producer_names` — `docs/tds/tds-v1.4.md` has no particular producer's name or tickets (TDS chapter 11 is left out of the public edition).

---

## 3. Public API

### 3.1 The import surface [R-01, R-09]

```python
import nexusbt as nx
from nexusbt import Price, Meta, Item, Trad, Market   # datasets (autocompletion, .pyi stubs; Trad, Market [V31-13])
from nexusbt.factors import (Returns, DailyReturns, SimpleMovingAverage, SMA,
    ExponentialWeightedMovingAverage, EWMA, AverageDollarVolume, AverageTradedValue,
    AnnualizedVolatility, RollingStd, VWAP, RSI, MaxDrawdown)
```

Every public name under `nexusbt.` (a name outside this list starts with `_`):

| Kind | Names |
|---|---|
| Terms | `Factor`, `Filter`, `Classifier`, `Pipeline`, `factor`, `filter`, `classifier`, `ops` (`ops.rolling`, `ops.where`, `ops.coalesce`, `ops.composite`), `factors` (the built-in factors of 3.4; 2.5) (decision 2026-11-18, #243) |
| Data | `Price`, `Meta`, `Trad`, `Market` [V31-13], `Dataset`, `universe` (and `Item`, 5.3.5, with the value tables' code) |
| Strategy | `Strategy`, `param`, `monthly`, `weekly`, `daily`, `quarterly`, `annually`, `every`, `top`, `bottom`, `long_short`, `portfolio` (+ the user block decorator `portfolio`), `size` (5.5.1a), `sizer`, `risk` (all the built-in blocks of 5.5.2 and 5.5.2a), `risk_rule`, the user-block types `PortfolioContext`, `SizingContext`, `RiskContext`, `TargetView`, `RiskDecision`, `Scalar`, `execution`, `exits` (including `exits.time_stop`), `ExitRule`, `costs`, `ml` (`ml.factor`, 5.3.11), `labels` (`labels.triple_barrier`, 5.8), `LabelTerm`, `splits` (`holdout`, `walk_forward`, `cpcv`, `cscv`, 6.3a) |
| Functions | `open`, `evaluate`, `backtest`, `screen`, `live`, `fill`, `reconcile`, `sweep`, `compare`, `run_pipeline`, `explain`, `check`, `describe`, `runs`, `rerun`, `book`, `override`, `record_fills`, `cash_flow` [V31-19], `acknowledge`, `preregister`, `config` |
| Results | `FactorReport`, `Run`, `Screen`, `Orders`, `BookUpdate`, `ReconcileReport`, `SweepResult`, `Comparison`, `ValidationResult` (`Run.validation`), `Split`, `PipelineResult`, `Explanation`, `DecisionExplanation`, `CheckReport`, `RunTable`, `Description`, `Preregistration`, `Config`, `Snapshot`, `Book` |
| Errors | `NexusBTError` and the subclasses of 6.6 |
| Types | the names the chapter 3 signatures use — Protocols or frozen dataclasses the user reads or annotates with, not constructs: `Term` (3.5, 3.8), `BoundColumn` (3.3, 3.8), `Param` (3.7), `Schedule` (3.7, 3.8), `PortfolioRule`, `Sizer`, `RiskRule`, `ExecutionRule`, `CostModel` (3.7 `Strategy`), `Window` (3.8 `ml.factor`, `ml.rolling`), `DecisionContext` (1.2, 5.5.1, 5.9.1; it lives in `contracts`, 2.4) |
| Modules | `compat` (`compat.zipline`, `compat.adapters`; 2.5), `testing` (`testing.synth`, for examples and tests; 2.7, 4.8), `__version__` (5.3.9) |
| Reserved (E18 when called) | `portfolio.optimize`, `risk.ledoit_wolf`, `ml.importance` (MDA, v1.x), `size.kelly` (v1.x [R4F-02]) [SC-22]. v3.2's reserved `splits.*`, `labels.triple_barrier`, `exits.time_stop` and `risk.group_caps` were opened into v1 in v3.3 |

Removed 2026-11-24 (#752), and no longer public names: from Data, `Fund`, `Panel`, `Label`, `Macro`, `MacroTerm`, `Events`, `dataset.from_parquet`, `dataset.from_frame` and `universe.index_members`; from Strategy, `on_event`.

**Identifier** [header "Identifier name"]: the stored key is `instrument_id` (no alias), and today's public functions and output tables use it; the names the user meets, `symbol`, `date` and `prices`, come with stage (2) [#725 Decision 248; #752, 2026-11-27]. Other names in external data are changed by the user before the data enters a package.

### 3.2 Datasets and columns

Dataset columns are built from the snapshot's `dictionary/columns` (TDS 4.4), and `Item`'s names from the items of its value tables (5.3.5). `nexusbt stubs` rebuilds `src/nexusbt/_stubs/*.pyi`, and `nexusbt doctor` reports when the snapshot's columns change [R-04, R-22]. A typo is an `AttributeError` (error E7) that suggests three similar names (`difflib.get_close_matches`, cutoff 0.6).

`Price` (fixed columns, daily on the market axis):

| Column | Type | Unit | Value inside a window (as of the computation day t) [R-18, 11.4.3] |
|---|---|---|---|
| `open, high, low, close` | float64 | local currency/share | adjusted to t for splits, consolidations and stock dividends only: `raw[s]·C_split[t]/C_split[s]`. E18 when the bars hold no value of `open/high/low` (4.4 step 3) |
| `close_tr` | float64 | local currency/share | adjusted to t for splits + cash dividends (total-return path): `raw[s]·C_total[t]/C_total[s]`. Dividends whose amount is not available at t's decision time are not included (5.3.4) |
| `close_unadjusted` | float64 | local currency/share | the raw price as it is |
| `volume` | float64 | shares | `vol[s]·Q[t]/Q[s]`, `Q[x] = Π_{e ≤ x} share_factor_e` over `affects = split_and_total` events (the resolver's cumulative share factor, 5.3.3a; 5.3.3). For pure splits it equals `C_split[s]/C_split[t]` |
| `volume_unadjusted` | float64 | shares | raw volume |
| `value_traded` | float64 | local currency | raw value traded (no adjustment needed) |

- Price cells of halted or missing sessions (`obs_status in {"halted","missing"}`) are NaN inside the window. A session without trades (`"no_trade"`) takes `close` when present (the exchange reference price), otherwise NaN, with `volume = 0`.
- A bar whose `obs_status` is null states no state (a row of a `tds:minimal-daily@1.0` package may, TDS 5.5; #725 Decisions 129): its values are read as stored, a `close` it gives is `Price.close` and a value it does not give is NaN; the session is neither hidden nor taken to have traded. A bars file without the column states no state on any row, read the same way (F-03, which made such rows `traded` or `no_trade`, retired 2026-11-23, #705). What asks whether a session traded (5.6) reads `equity-daily-core` and above, which require `obs_status` (4.3).
- `nx.Item.<name>` [#725 Decisions 219, 224]: an item of the package's value tables (5.3.5) as a term, its value at each row by the engine rule of 5.3.5 and the date rule of 5.1.4. An item of the dated per-symbol table is a Factor; a market-wide item spreads as the same value to every instrument of the market and stays out of a cross-section's denominator; an undated per-symbol item may also group (as a Classifier, for example a sector code). `nx.Item.<name>.age()` (Factor): sessions since the item's last row for that instrument, NaN when none; it replaces the event terms (`Events`, `nx.on_event`, `.days_since()`). Item terms have no degree (3.4). They replace `Fund`, `Panel`, `Label` (the user's label path), `Macro`, `Events`, `from_parquet` and `from_frame`, removed 2026-11-24 (#752) [#725 Decisions 250, 251].
- `Meta`: `market`, `currency`, `asset_class` (attributes that are fixed for an instrument and do not change over time), `venue_mic`, `category` (Classifier). A sector or an industry is a numeric undated item (`nx.Item.<name>`, above); `Meta.sector`, `Meta.industry`, `is_financial` and `Meta.<scheme>(level=)` were removed with `classifications` (2026-11-24, #752). **Changeable attributes** (`category`, `venue_mic`) use the current value of `instruments`, with E30 (`F-14 hindsight_attribute`: "current value used for history") and a manifest record when it reaches a decision input. `instruments.delisted_on`, `last_session` and `name` are not exposed through `Meta` (after-the-fact information). v1's `Meta.country` and `Meta.exchange` remain as aliases of `Meta.market` and `Meta.venue_mic`.
- `Trad.<col>`: the columns of TDS `tradability_daily` as terms: `Trad.admin_status` (Classifier; the open enum `normal`, `administrative`, `caution`, `warning`, `liquidation_trading`, unknown values `other`), `Trad.halted` (Filter = `halted_full_day`), `Trad.limit_up_close`, `Trad.limit_down_close` (Filter), `Trad.price_limit_ratio` (Factor). Availability times are TDS `availability_by_column` as they are (prior information `session_open`, the day's outcome `session_close`; 4.6). Without the dataset, a graph that uses `Trad.*` is E18.
- `Market.series(instrument_id: str, column: str = "close") -> Factor` [V31-13]: a term that makes a bar column of one instrument of the snapshot (usually `asset_class = index`, for example `"IDX:KOSPI_TR"`) into a **market-level series** (one value per session). Windows, `shift` and element-wise operations work, and mixed with instrument terms it spreads as the same value to every instrument of that market. It is also used as `inputs=` of built-in factors and user window functions (for example relative strength against the index `Returns(126) - Returns(126, inputs=[Market.series("IDX:KOSPI_TR")])`, the regime filter `Market.series("IDX:KOSPI_TR").latest > SMA(200, inputs=[Market.series("IDX:KOSPI_TR")])`). Its availability rule is the same as `ready_m` (5.1.2) of that instrument's bars. When the series' market differs from the market of the sleeve that uses it, it becomes a term gathered by `row_m(d)` at the decision (5.3.2 (6)) and cannot be the input of a window or `shift` (E17). It does not enter the denominator of cross-section operations (the default domain of 3.3 as it is). A resulting Filter `&`-ed into `universe` becomes a market regime filter (for example "buy only while the index is above its 200-day line" — when false, that decision's universe is empty and the sleeve is in cash). E18 when the instrument is not in the snapshot.
- External data enters a run only inside a package, as value-table rows (5.3.5): `nx.dataset.from_parquet` and `nx.dataset.from_frame`, with their shapes and `known_on`, were removed 2026-11-24 (#752) [#725 Decisions 251].

### 3.3 Terms and operators [R-03, Codex 11.4.4]

Common: every term is a value object, and `hash(term)` and `nx.describe(term)` work on it. Numeric results are float64; a Filter is **three-valued with a value and a validity mask** (true, false, unknown; unknown when an input is missing, unknown = false at the final use, 5.4.1); a Classifier is int32 codes (missing −1) + a code table.

**Factor methods** (zipline names as they are; result type):

| Signature | Result | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `+ - * / ** -x`, `abs()`, `log()`, `log1p()`, `exp()`, `sqrt()` | Factor | element-wise operations. NaN propagates; division by 0 → NaN + a count of the diagnostic `DivisionByZero` [C7]; a result that would be ±inf for another reason → NaN + a count of the diagnostic `InfiniteResult` (5.4.1) |
| `< <= > >= == !=` (with a Factor or a scalar) | Filter | **unknown** when an input is NaN (false at the final use; still unknown after `~`) |
| `rank(method="ordinal", ascending=True, mask=None, groupby=None, across_markets=False)` | Factor | cross-sectional rank 1..n. `method ∈ {"ordinal","average","min","max","dense"}`. `ordinal` ties use the key of `[portfolio] tiebreak` (default the fixed-salt hash, 3.7; `"id"`, the zipline preset and comparisons use ascending `instrument_id`) [11.4.4] |
| `zscore(mask=None, groupby=None, across_markets=False)` | Factor | (x−mean)/population standard deviation (ddof=0). Variance 0 → NaN |
| `demean(mask=None, groupby=None, across_markets=False)` | Factor | x − mean |
| `winsorize(min_percentile, max_percentile, mask=None, groupby=None, across_markets=False)` | Factor | clipped at the quantile bounds (linearly interpolated quantiles); `min_percentile` and `max_percentile` are fractions, `0 ≤ min ≤ max ≤ 1` (1% is 0.01) (decision 2026-11-18, #244) |
| `top(N, mask=None, groupby=None, across_markets=False)` / `bottom(...)` | Filter | at most N by descending (ascending) score + the tie key (3.7) |
| `percentile_between(min_percentile, max_percentile, mask=None, across_markets=False)` | Filter | within percentiles [min, max] (both ends included), on 0..100: `0 ≤ min ≤ max ≤ 100` (decision 2026-11-18, #244) |
| `pct_rank(higher_is_better=True, mask=None, groupby=None, na="drop", across_markets=False)` | Factor | cross-sectional percentile 0~100 (100 = best; ties take the average rank). `na`: `"drop"` (missing is NaN, default), `"bottom"` (missing values are put in as a tie just below the worst value, then computed), `"middle"` (missing cells = 50, the rest from the valid cells only). Other than `drop`, it is an **explicit** fill operation that gives missing cells a value, so it agrees with INV-12 and is left in the manifest and `explain` |
| `quantiles(bins, mask=None, groupby=None, across_markets=False)`, `quartiles()`, `quintiles()`, `deciles()` | Classifier | tie-preserving quantiles (5.4.3) |
| `isnan()`, `notnan()`, `isfinite()`, `isnull()`, `notnull()` | Filter | `isfinite()` answers as `notnan()` does: a Factor's value is finite or NaN (5.4.1); the name is zipline's (ADR 0001) |
| `fillna(value: float | Factor)` | Factor | the only explicit fill [INV-12] |
| `clip(lower=None, upper=None)` | Factor | |
| `shift(k: int)` | Factor | the value k sessions earlier on that instrument's market axis. With `k < 0`, `LookAheadError` (error E1) when the graph is built. `k = 0` is unchanged. With `k ≥ 1`, an input whose degree (3.4) is known and is not 0 is `TermDomainError` (error E17) when the graph is built |
| `to(ccy: str)` | Factor | only for columns whose unit is a currency. Exchange rates available at the decision (5.6.9). `UnitError` (error E5) when the unit is not a currency |
| `if_else(...)` | – | a Filter method (below) |
| `downsample(freq)` | – | not in v1 → `CapabilityError` |

- `mask`: the computation domain of that term. Cells outside the mask give NaN (unknown for a Filter). It does not affect the computation of upstream inputs [11.4.4]. **Default domain** (the cells in a cross-section when there is no mask) = the cells of that market block = `asset_class = equity ∧ is_synthetic = false ∧ listed in that session`. Indices do not enter cross-sections and are read only through the benchmark path and the market-level series `Market.series` (3.2) [V31-13]. The engine does not secretly put the Strategy's `universe` into upstream terms as a mask; when a cross-section operation without a mask is on the Strategy's signal path, the static check gives the notice E34.
- `groupby`: a Classifier. Cells with a missing group (−1) are left out, and their number is counted as the diagnostic `UnknownGroup`, at that term (5.4.1) [11.4.4].
- Cross-section operations are computed **separately per market** (market = an implicit group) [A-03]. With `across_markets=True`, the cross-sections of every market of one decision are gathered and computed together, and that term cannot be the input of a window or `shift` (`TermDomainError`, error E17). A cross-section operation with `across_markets=True` on a currency-unit term is `UnitError` (error E5) — after `.to(ccy)` or on a ratio it works [R-19].

**Filter methods**: `& | ~` (Kleene three-valued: false & unknown = false, true | unknown = true, `~`unknown = unknown, otherwise unknown; the results of `isnan()`, `notnan()` and `notnull()` are always true/false at a cell where the instrument is listed; at a cell where it is not, every term is absent (5.3.1)), `if_else(if_true: Factor, if_false: Factor) -> Factor` (NaN when the condition is unknown), `all(window: int) -> Filter` (true in all of the last window sessions; Kleene — false if even one is false, otherwise unknown if any is unknown), `any(window: int) -> Filter` (true if even one is true, otherwise unknown if any is unknown), `.as_factor() -> Factor` (true 1.0 / false 0.0 / unknown NaN), `fillna(value: bool) -> Filter` (unknown cells to the given value; the only explicit Filter fill, INV-12).

**Classifier methods**: `eq(value) -> Filter`, `element_of(values) -> Filter`, `isnull()`, `notnull()`, `startswith(prefix) -> Filter` (string code tables only). The result of `eq`, `element_of` and `startswith` on the missing code (−1) is **unknown** (`isnull` and `notnull` are always valid at a listed cell, 5.3.1).

**User window functions** [R-05]:

```python
@nx.factor(window: int, inputs: Sequence[BoundColumn | Factor], *, mask: Filter | None = None,
           dtype: Literal["float64"] = "float64",
           adjustment: Literal["degree 0", "degree 1"] | None = None)
def f(close: np.ndarray, *, n: int = 21) -> np.ndarray: ...
# close.shape == (window, n_alive); last row = computation day t; returned shape == (n_alive,)
```

- The decorated name is a Factor. Binding parameters with keyword arguments, as in `f(n=63)`, makes a new Factor (the values go into the graph hash). Its `function` attribute is the plain function, unbound, for running it alone on arrays: bound values are passed by hand, as in `f.function(close, n=63)`, and `f(n=63).function` is the same unbound function, so `f(n=63).function(close)` computes with the function's default `n`, not 63. A call with a positional argument, as in `f(prices)`, is `ConfigError` (error E16), whose message shows the `f.function(...)` call to type instead, with the values that Factor has bound (decision 2026-11-20, #582).
- A `BoundColumn` input window holds values correctly adjusted to t (the table of 3.2). A `Factor` input window holds that factor's values as each row gave them (5.1.2); for a window of two rows or more, a `Factor` input whose degree (3.4) is known and is not 0 is `TermDomainError` (error E17) when the graph is built. The adjustment shortcut is not used for user functions [C2].
- `adjustment` says what the result is under a per-instrument scaling of the inputs (3.4): `"degree 0"` (a ratio, a return) is unchanged, `"degree 1"` (a level) scales by the same constant. Without it the result has no degree and is not refused by E17, as before. Any other value is `ConfigError` (error E16) when the term is built. The engine checks a declaration once for each user term in a run (a function with its bound values), on the first row where the result for some instrument is finite and not 0: it calls the function again on copies of the input windows in which that instrument's prices are multiplied by 2, each input's column multiplied by `2^p` for its degree p in the price factor (3.4; a price column or a level ×2, a return or another degree-0 input unchanged), and compares that instrument's result b with the expected a (the result, times 2 for `"degree 1"`) within `|a − b| ≤ 1e-12·max(1, |a|)`. A declaration that does not hold is `TermDomainError` (error E17) naming the function and the declaration. The extra call changes neither the values nor the graph hash, and the verdict does not depend on how the run is chunked. If no such row comes before the run ends, or an input has no known degree, a degree in the quantity factor or one of unknown size, the check is recorded as skipped (E45, reason `declaration_unchecked`), never passed in silence (decisions 2026-11-19, #246).
- `dtype` takes `"float64"` only, the dtype of a Factor's values; any other value is `ConfigError` (error E16) when the term is built (decision 2026-11-20, #638).
- `col.latest` is a term: its value at row s is fixed at row s (5.1.2). A window that should see each cell as known at t takes the column itself (`inputs=[col]`), not `col.latest`.
- `@nx.filter` returns bool `(n_alive,)`: a cell is unknown when any cell of that instrument's input windows is NaN, and elsewhere the bool the function returned. `@nx.classifier` returns an integer `(n_alive,)`, the codes themselves: −1 is the missing group, and another negative value is `WindowError` (error E8), since no code table has it, and so is a code an int32 cannot hold; a cell outside `mask` or before the window is the missing group. A result of another dtype or shape (a float for either, a bool for a classifier) is `WindowError` (error E8) (decisions 2026-11-20, 2026-11-27, #620). A classifier that returns str, with its code table, comes with #818; until then a str result is E8 as well.
- When a function indexes rows before `window`, numpy raises `IndexError`, and the engine turns it into `WindowError` (error E8) suggesting "`window=<needed value>`" (needed value = the absolute value of the negative index). Other user exceptions show the original exception and the user's file:line as they are [R-42], also those raised in the dry run of 6.5 (5): a function that raises on windows of 1.0 is stopped there (decision 2026-11-19, #246).
- `nx.ops.rolling(x: Factor, window: int, min_periods: int | None = None).{mean,sum,std,min,max,median}()`: rolling operations that always look backward only (`std` with ddof=1; `min_periods` default = window) [design 3.5]. `1 ≤ min_periods ≤ window`; `min_periods` counts the finite cells of a window that is wholly on the axis, and a window that starts before the axis's first row is NaN whatever `min_periods` is (INV-03, INV-14) (decision 2026-11-18, #243).
- `nx.ops.where(cond: Filter, a, b)`, `nx.ops.coalesce(*factors)`: explicit fills.
- `nx.ops.composite(terms: Mapping[Factor, float], *, higher_is_better: Mapping[Factor, bool] | None = None, na: Literal["drop","bottom","middle"] = "drop", mask=None, groupby=None) -> Factor`: turns each term into `pct_rank(na=…)`, takes the weighted sum (divided by the sum of weights) → `pct_rank` again (0~100). It can be nested (another `composite` in a term's place). The behaviour that corresponds to "NA Negative/Neutral" of the Portfolio123 ranking system is kept as a named, explicit operation. The number of instruments kept by filling missing values is `n_na_filled` of `coverage.parquet`.

### 3.4 Built-in factors and the conformance table [Codex 11.4.4, R-03]

Names that are the same as in zipline 3.2.1 follow zipline's missing-value rules and **input meaning**. zipline `USEquityPricing.close` is the value with the adjustment DB (splits + dividends) applied, so the default input of return-type factors is `Price.close_tr`; price-level factors (`SMA`, `EWMA`, `VWAP`, `RollingStd`) default to `Price.close` (split-adjusted only), to fit price-level comparisons and limit-price references [A-39 corrected]. When the rules below differ from zipline in the L1 comparison (8.1, with the same inputs), **the hand-calculated golden cases (T-25, `tests/golden/factors/`) decide**: when the hand calculation agrees with zipline, this table and the code are corrected (ADR); when zipline is wrong, it is written in `tests/parity/KNOWN_DIFFERENCES.md`. It is the same rule as 8.1's "zipline's known bugs are not taken as correct" (v3's sentence "zipline's behaviour is correct" conflicted with 8.1 and was corrected; when two engines have the same mistake, comparison alone cannot catch it — qlib#1228, nautilus#4333, Lean#9713). `W` = the number of window rows, last row = t.

| Name (default input) | Formula | Missing | Minimum observations | ddof | Warm-up | Adjustment class |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Returns(W, inputs=[Price.close_tr])` | `x[-1]/x[0] − 1` | either end NaN → NaN | both ends | – | W−1 | homogeneous of degree 0 (ratio) |
| `DailyReturns()` = `Returns(2)` | | | | | 1 | degree 0 |
| `SimpleMovingAverage(W, inputs=[Price.close])`, alias `SMA` | `nanmean` | NaN ignored, all NaN → NaN | 1 | – | W−1 | homogeneous of degree 1 |
| `ExponentialWeightedMovingAverage(W, decay_rate, inputs)`, `EWMA`; `.from_span/.from_halflife/.from_center_of_mass` | `average(x, weights=decay^(W−1−i))`; `0 < decay_rate ≤ 1`; a finite `span > 1`, `halflife > 0` or `center_of_mass > 0` whose decay rate, as float64 computes it, is in that range; any other value (NaN and ±inf included) is refused when the term is built, error E16 naming the argument passed and its value (decision 2026-11-20, #666) | any NaN → NaN | W | – | W−1 | degree 1 |
| `AverageDollarVolume(W)` | `nansum(close·volume)/W` (zipline's formula) | NaN counted as 0, a file's `volume` null in every row too; bars holding no value of `volume` are E14 or E18 (4.4 step 3) (decisions 2026-11-20, #667; 2026-11-23, #705 Decisions 57, narrowing #688) | 0 | – | W−1 | invariant (value = price × volume) |
| `AverageTradedValue(W, ccy=None)` (NexusBT) | `nanmean(value_traded)`; `.to(ccy)` when `ccy` | NaN ignored | 1 | – | W−1 | invariant |
| `AnnualizedVolatility(W=252, annualization_factor=252, inputs=[Price.close_tr])` | `nanstd(DailyReturns window, ddof=0)·√a` | NaN ignored | 1 | 0 | W | degree 0 |
| `RollingStd(W, inputs)` (NexusBT) | `nanstd(x, ddof=1)` | NaN ignored | 2 | 1 | W−1 | degree 1 |
| `VWAP(W)` | `Σ close·volume / Σ volume` over the window rows whose close and volume are both finite (pairs only) | a row with either NaN is left out of both sums; no pair, or the pairs' volume summing to 0 → NaN (zipline keeps a NaN close's volume in the denominator, 8.1) [#278] | 1 pair | – | W−1 | degree 1 |
| `RSI(W=15, inputs=[Price.close_tr])` | `d=diff(x)`; `up=nanmean(clip(d,0,∞))`; `dn=abs(nanmean(clip(d,−∞,0)))`; `100−100/(1+up/dn)` | as zipline | | | W−1 | degree 0 |
| `MaxDrawdown(W, inputs=[Price.close_tr])` | `max((cummax(x)−x)/cummax(x))` | NaN ignored | 1 | – | W−1 | degree 0 |
| `SimpleBeta(target, regression_length, allowed_missing_percentage=0.25)` (zipline; `target` = a `Market.series(id)` term, the instrument's input `Price.close_tr`) [#725 Decisions 246] | the slope `cov(r, r_m)/var(r_m)` of the instrument's daily returns `r` on the target's `r_m` over the window's pairs | pairs only; more missing pairs than `allowed_missing_percentage` of the window → NaN (zipline) | `regression_length − int(allowed_missing_percentage · regression_length)` pairs, at least 2 | – | `regression_length` | degree 0 |
| `RollingPearsonOfReturns(target, returns_length, correlation_length)` (zipline; `target` as above) [#725 Decisions 246] | Pearson correlation of `Returns(returns_length)` of the instrument and of the target over `correlation_length` rows | as zipline (a NaN in either series → NaN) | `correlation_length` | – | `returns_length + correlation_length − 2` | degree 0 |
| `RollingLinearRegressionOfReturns(target, returns_length, regression_length)` (zipline; `target` as above) [#725 Decisions 246] | least squares `y = alpha + beta·x`, `y` = the instrument's `Returns(returns_length)`, `x` = the target's, over `regression_length` rows; outputs `.alpha`, `.beta`, `.r_value`, `.stderr` | as zipline (a NaN in either series → NaN) | `regression_length` | – | `returns_length + regression_length − 2` | degree 0 |
| `Latest` (= `col.latest`) | `x[-1]` | | | | 0 | the column table |

**The three return-series kernels** [#725 Decisions 246]: `SimpleBeta`, `RollingPearsonOfReturns` and `RollingLinearRegressionOfReturns` take the market series with zipline's names, `target` being a `Market.series(id)` term of the sleeve's market (another market's series cannot enter a window, E17, as for any `Market.series` term). All three are of the arithmetic numeric class (5.3.10: + − × ÷ and sqrt); zipline's `p_value` output is not given, since it needs the t distribution, outside 5.3.10's classes. A factor with several outputs is one graph node, computed once per window; each output (`reg.beta`, `reg.alpha`, `reg.r_value`, `reg.stderr`) is a `Factor` term of its own, and the object itself is not a term (using it as one is E16, asking for another output E7, each naming the four).

Adjustment classes (5.3.3): homogeneous of degree 0 = invariant under multiplication by a per-instrument scalar, degree 1 = scales by the scalar, invariant = the raw value as it is. The class is declared when the kernel is registered and proven by the P-ADJ property test (random scalars). A kernel's shortcut reads the window after the per-row visibility mask of 5.1.2, as the reference does (5.3.3, "Visibility").

**Degree of a term** (read by the refusal of error E17 (3.3, 6.6) and by E18 on a package whose prices are not raw (4.3) [#725 Decisions 218]; decision 2026-11-18, #513). Walking the graph, the build gives each term degree 0, a degree that is not 0, or no degree. A degree is counted in the two factors of 5.3.3, the price factor `C` and the quantity factor `Q`.
- `.latest` of `open`, `high`, `low`, `close` and `close_tr` has degree 1 in the price factor, and `.latest` of `volume` degree 1 in the quantity factor (the columns the table of 3.2 adjusts to t). `.latest` of `close_unadjusted`, `volume_unadjusted` and `value_traded` has degree 0.
- A built-in factor's term: its kernel's class applied to its input. A degree-1 kernel gives its input's degree; `VWAP`: the degree of its price input (the volume is a weight, above and below the line) (decision 2026-11-18, #243); a degree-0 or invariant kernel gives 0 over a column or a degree-0 input, and no degree over an input with no degree; a degree-0 kernel gives 0 over a `Market.series` term too, a column of one instrument that no other instrument's scalar moves, so a return, beta, correlation or regression against the market series has degree 0 [#725 Decisions 246].
- A constant: 0. `-x`, `abs(x)`, `x * c`, `x / c`: as x. `x * y`, `x / y`: degrees of the same factor add and subtract; a result with degree in both the price and the quantity factor has no degree. `x + y`, `x - y`, `x.fillna(y)`: the degree both have, else no degree (a constant y with an x of non-zero degree: the next line). `x ** p`, `x.sqrt()`: x's degree times p, times one half. `x.shift(k)`: as x.
- Any other numeric function of one term of non-zero degree and constants (`log()`, `log1p()`, `exp()`, `x + c`, `clip`, `fillna(c)`) has a degree that is not 0. Two such terms combined (`Price.close.latest.log() - Price.open.latest.log()`) have no degree.
- A Filter or a Classifier is not refused. A cross-sectional result has degree 0 over a degree-0 input, else no degree. A user function's result has degree 0 when it declares `adjustment="degree 0"`, a degree that is not 0 when it declares `"degree 1"`, else no degree (3.3; decision 2026-11-19, #246). `nx.Item` and `Market.series` terms have no degree.

A term these rules give no degree is not refused. (On a package whose prices are not raw, 4.3 says what E18 does with a Filter or an output that reads an adjusted column.)

Every built-in factor is a registered kernel and follows the numeric rules of 5.3.10 (summation order, BLAS and `fastmath` forbidden, the transcendental-function class). The `EWMA` weights `decay^k` are computed once outside the kernel and passed as a constant array; kernels that only divide, such as `Returns` and `RSI`, are of the arithmetic class, and kernels that use logarithms (`log()`, `log1p()`, spread estimates) are of the transcendental class. The table's `nanmean`, `nanstd` and `nansum` are defined as "the sum of the non-NaN values added from the left in ascending row order" (not numpy's pairwise summation order) [SC-03]. Every built-in factor is a function only of the data inside its own window (no infinite recursion; `EWMA` is a finite-window weighted average) — which is why start-date invariance (INV-14) holds [SC-04].

### 3.5 Pipeline and `run_pipeline` [11.4.4, 11.6]

```python
nx.Pipeline(columns: Mapping[str, Term], screen: Filter | None = None)   # the default cross-section domain is in 3.3
nx.run_pipeline(pipeline: Pipeline, *, start: str | date, end: str | date, freq: Literal["daily","weekly","monthly"] = "daily",
                markets: Sequence[str] | None = None, snapshot: str | Snapshot | None = None,   # markets: keys of the snapshot's markets
                save: bool = False) -> PipelineResult
```

- The result `PipelineResult.table`: an Arrow table `(market: str, session_date: date32, decision_ts: timestamp[us,UTC], instrument_id: str, <columns…>)`, sorted by `(session_date, market, instrument_id)`. Rows where `screen` is false or unknown are not there (unknown = false). `.to_polars()`, `.to_pandas()` (optional dependency).
- `PipelineResult.term_diagnostics`: the counts of 5.4.1 per market, session and term, the table 5.4.1 defines.
- `PipelineResult.run_id`: with `save=True`, the id of the run it sealed (`kind="pipeline"`, 3.8); otherwise `None`.
- When `start` is not a session, it is moved per market to the next session and `end` to the previous session, with a notice (error E9) [design 3.5 error E9].
- The decision grid of `freq="daily"` = every session of each market (market_local). With several markets, the per-market rows are stacked.

### 3.6 Universe [design 3.6, R-58]

```python
nx.universe(spec: str | Sequence[str] = "<toml defaults.universe>", *,
            exclude_microcap: bool | None = None, survivorship_ok: str | None = None,
            exclude: Sequence[str] = ()) -> Filter                      #
```

- `spec`: one key of the snapshot's `markets` (`"KR"`), several joined by `+` (`"KR+US"`), `"all"`, or a list of `instrument_id`. A market name not in the snapshot is E16 (suggesting the list of markets present); a market without a rules pack is E18 (4.4). For a list, a survivorship-bias check (error E4 warning): a warning when every instrument of the list is listed in the snapshot's last session and the run period is longer than 3 years. With `survivorship_ok="reason"`, the reason is written in the manifest instead of the warning.
- **Eligibility and index membership are the user's items** (5.3.5), joined with `&`, for example `nx.universe("KR") & (nx.Item.kospi200 > 0)`; a list given on its listing days takes the carry `next listing`, which replaces "a missing row is false" [#725 Decisions 227, 228]. `investable=`, `[universe] eligibility_rule`, `index_members` and their fallback (4.5 F-07) were removed 2026-11-24 (#752) [chapter 12 OI-01, TDS-D16].
- **Exclusion reasons reported** [SC-23]: per decision and market, the universe size, the number removed by `exclude_microcap`, and the number dropped because the signal is not finite are left in `coverage.parquet` (5.9.1). A liquidity threshold is not hidden in the default universe (examples and the `nexusbt new strategy` template keep a line such as `AverageTradedValue(63, ccy="USD") > 1e6` visible).
- Listing existence (`listed_on <= session <= last_session`) always applies.
- `exclude` (default `()` = excludes nothing; the same principle as the decision to include micro-caps by default): values accept only TDS enum names — `admin_status` values (`administrative`, `caution`, `warning`, `liquidation_trading`; that session's `Trad.admin_status`, available at `session_open`) and `category` values (`preferred`, `spac`, `reit`, `etf`, `fund`, `warrant`, `right`, …; `Meta.category`, 3.2). An unknown name is E16 (with the list of allowed names). It shows as a leaf Filter in step 1 of `explain`, and the excluded counts go per reason into `n_excluded_by_reason` of `coverage.parquet`. `[universe] exclude` can give a default.
- `exclude_microcap` (default = `nexusbt.toml [universe] exclude_microcap`, default false): when true, removes the instruments in the bottom `microcap_pct` (default 0.20) of market capitalisation among that session's universe per market; market capitalisation is `close_unadjusted` × the package's share-count item (5.3.5), and without one it is E18 before the run [#725 Decisions 225]. The boundary value is a `percentile` (linear interpolation), and values equal to the boundary stay [Q17, R-58].
### 3.7 Strategy parts [R-07, R-08, design 3.3]

```python
nx.Strategy(*, universe: Filter, signal: Factor, rebalance: Schedule, portfolio: PortfolioRule,
            sizing: Sizer | Sequence[Sizer] | None = None,                                   # money-management slot
            risk: RiskRule | Sequence[RiskRule] | None = None,                               # risk-management list slot
            costs: CostModel | None = None, execution: ExecutionRule | None = None,
            exits: Sequence[ExitRule] = (), hypothesis: str | None = None, name: str | None = None)
```

- A value object; `strategy.graph_hash` (a structural hash of the term graph + the parameters of the parts) [R-07, R-12]. On disk it is kept only as source (5.9.3), never stored as a serialised object (2.6). `name` default = the file name of the module that defines it.
- `sizing` (default `None` = the shorthand `weight=` and `max_weight=` of the construction rule, below): the money-management slot (5.5.1a). For a list, the first is the base sizer and the rest are modifiers (`vol_target`, `cash_floor`), in the order written.
- `risk` (default `None` = no rules = `nx.risk.none()`): the risk-management list slot (5.5.2). One rule or a list, applied one by one **in the order written**; the order goes into `graph_hash` and the manifest [SC-01, OQ-2].
- `strategy.replace(**slots) -> Strategy`: a new value object with one or more slots replaced (the other slots and the name unchanged, with `~{replaced slots}` after the name). It is the basic tool for comparing techniques (`nx.compare`, 3.8). For example: `base.replace(sizing=nx.size.erc())`, `base.replace(risk=[nx.risk.drawdown_brake()])`.
- A block that reads holdings is the `decide` slot below, called once per decision session; the `on_rebalance` callback stays out [#725 Decisions 236, replacing C6 and A-10's exclusion].
- The slots are read in the same order as bt and LEAN: `rebalance` (when) → `universe`, `signal` (what) → `portfolio` (which, on which side) → `sizing` (how much) → `risk` (reduce) → `decide` (the user's change, in place of the sizing output, then through `risk` once) → `execution`, `exits`, `costs` (how). A stage function takes only the previous stage's output (a table) and the information at the decision; the account state only in risk rules with `reads_account=True` (read-only, 5.5.2a), in `decide` (read-only) and in the execution stage [blueprint E-1].

**The block contract of the ten slots**. The user plugs blocks into slots. Trying to read anything outside the table's "may read" is an error before the run (account access is E41; future slots do not exist by construction).

| Slot (argument) | Input → output | May read | Built-in blocks | User blocks |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| schedule (`rebalance=`) | calendar → list of decision sessions | the schedule calendar (5.1.3) | `monthly`, `weekly`, `quarterly`, `annually`, `daily`, `every` (`on_event` removed 2026-11-24, #752; a decision after an event is a Filter on `nx.Item.<name>.age()` [#725 Decisions 224]) | none (v1). Conditional decisions through `risk.regime`, Filters |
| universe (`universe=`) | Pipeline → Filter (decision × instrument, true/false/unknown) | snapshot data up to the decision (5.1.2) | `nx.universe`, `nx.Item` Filters, `Trad.*`, operators | `@nx.filter(window=)` |
| signal (`signal=`) | Pipeline → Factor score | the same | the 15 built-in factors (3.4), `ops.*`, `ops.composite`, `ml.factor`, `nx.Item` terms (5.3.5) | `@nx.factor(window=)`, `nx.ml.factor(...)` (5.3.11) |
| construction (`portfolio=`) | score, universe, previous canonical target state → selection table (id, side, rank) | term values at the decision, the previous decision's `TargetState` (read-only) | `top`, `bottom`, `long_short` | `@nx.portfolio` (3.8) |
| sizing (`sizing=`) | selection table + market window → signed weights, `Σ|w| ≤ 1` per side | only the market window at the decision (prices, value traded, market cap), **no account** | `size.equal`, `value`, `inverse_vol`, `score`, `erc`, `vol_parity`, `stop_risk`, modifiers `vol_target`, `cash_floor` (5.5.1a; `kelly` v1.x [R4F-02]) | `@nx.sizer` (3.8) |
| risk (`risk=[…]`) | weights → weights × m, `m ∈ [0, 1]` (toward zero only) | market information; with `reads_account=True`, the account (up to the decision, read-only) | `risk.group_caps`, `regime`, `vol_cap`, `liquidity_cap`, `drawdown_brake`, `portfolio_stop`, `no_reentry`, fitting to the exposure caps (5.5.2, 5.5.2a) | `@nx.risk_rule(reads_account=…)` (3.8) |
| decide (`decide=`) | canonical target + account → target weights or order intents (cancel and replace of its own orders included), with quantity and holding limits and a record per decision | the account up to the decision (read-only; dividend amounts not yet published are not seen), its own state, the Pipeline columns it declares; never the fill session's volume [#725 Decisions 235, 237] | none (default `None` = off) | `@nx.decide(columns=[…])`, `fn(ctx) -> Decision`, arrays in and out [#725 Decisions 236~240; #752] |
| execution (`execution=`) | final weights + account → order intents | the account (engine) | `execution.market`, `execution.limit` | none (v1; fill semantics are an accounting contract and are not swappable) |
| exits (`exits=[…]`) | fill → a non-expiring exit order per holding | the holding's `avg_cost`, entry session, closes after entry | `stop_loss`, `stop_limit_loss`, `take_profit`, `trailing_stop`, `time_stop` | none (v1) |
| costs (`costs=`) | fill → cost components | the fill, ADV at the decision, user tables | `provisional`, `realistic`, `bps`, `per_share`, `none` | user tables (`provisional(table=)`, `realistic(table=)`) |

**Four block promises**: (1) **No look into the future** — the end of every block's input window is the decision time (INV-01). (2) **Same input, same output** — no randomness or hidden state inside a block; a risk rule that needs state passes it only through the `risk_state` table the engine stores (5.5.2a). (3) **The account from the risk slot on, read-only** — the sizing slot does not take the account, the risk slot takes a read-only view only with `reads_account=True`, and `decide` takes a read-only view. (4) **Effects are visible** — per slot, the change in weight sums, the number of instruments and the reasons go into `waterfall.parquet` and `targets.reason`, and skipped instruments and blocks into `skips.parquet` (6.6 "no silent failures").

**The `decide` block** [#725 Decisions 235~241; #752, the slot's name and `fn(ctx) -> Decision` with arrays in and out]. An `@nx.decide` function is called once per decision session (the schedule slot says when), in 5.6.3's decision step after the canonical target. Its `ctx` holds the sizing slot's output (`pre_risk_weight`) and the canonical target (`target_weight`), the account up to the decision as read-only arrays (NAV series, holding quantities, `avg_cost`, entry sessions, pending and exit orders; a dividend amount not yet published is not seen), its own state, and the Pipeline columns it declares in `columns=`; reading anything else is E41, as for `@nx.risk_rule`. The fill session's volume is never in it: no input of a decision comes from the session that fills it [#725 Decisions 235]. It returns a `Decision`: target weights, or order intents (market or limit, a quantity, the cancel or replace of an order it placed), each with optional quantity and holding limits, and a record for that decision, kept in `decide_log.parquet`. It cannot change how orders fill, cost or settle (5.6). What it returns takes the place of the sizing slot's output and passes, once and in 5.5.2's order, the risk slot's market rules and the exposure-cap fitting, giving the decided target `decided_weight`; then the account layer (5.5.2a: the account rules, `no_reentry` and `drawdown_brake` among them, in the order written), the final check (5.5.2b) and shorting. Each only shrinks it; a short intent or a negative weight while shorting is off is E16 [#725 Decisions 239]. A block that returns `pre_risk_weight` unchanged gives `decided_weight = target_weight`, bit for bit. Its weights, given or read from its intents, keep the bounds the sizing output keeps in that account (5.5.1a, 5.6.7a): in the cash account `Σ|w| ≤ 1` per side and `Σ|w| ≤ G_max`; in the margin account the larger bound 5.6.7a allows `vol_target` and `long_short` (up to `min(max_gross, 1/initial_margin_max)`); beyond them is E41, as for `@nx.sizer` (a negative weight while shorting is off stays E16, below). An order intent is read for these checks as the weight it would leave: every intent, and every order the block placed and left standing, is taken as filled in full at the decision's valuation price `P_i` (5.6.7), a cancelled order as gone, and exit orders are not counted, as for any target; where a check shrinks that weight, the intent's quantity is cut toward zero, never raised. The block's orders are not `CANCELLED(replaced)` at the next decision as rebalance orders are: they stay until they fill, the block cancels them, or their time in force ends. Exit orders, stop-losses among them, are made at fills (5.6.2a) and fill in the session's phases before its decision (5.6.3), so the block reads their result; re-entry bans and the drawdown brake, being account rules, come after the block. Its state is a table the engine gives, keyed by `instrument_id` and read by symbol, kept in the run record and restored equal on a continued run (checkpoint, 5.6.1); it starts and carries with the account, as `risk_state` does (6.3a, trading state). Its source enters `graph_hash` as `@nx.factor`'s does, and check 6 of 6.5 and E8 apply. An output outside this contract (an id outside the universe, a weight or quantity that is not finite, a cancel or replace of an order it did not place) is E41. `waterfall.parquet` gets its rows as `step = 'decide'`.

**Schedules** (decision dates; the reference calendar is 5.1.3). Every schedule is anchored to the calendar and does not depend on the run's start date (INV-14):

| Function | Decision sessions |
|---|---|
| `nx.monthly(day: int = -1)` | the `day`-th session of the month (from 1), counted from the end when negative. The last session when larger than the number of sessions in the month |
| `nx.weekly(weekday: int = -1)` | the `weekday`-th session of the ISO week (same rule) |
| `nx.quarterly(day: int = -1)` | `monthly(day)` of March, June, September and December |
| `nx.annually(month: int = 12, day: int = -1)` | |
| `nx.daily()` | every session |
| `nx.every(n: int, offset: int = 0)` | the sessions whose absolute session number `session_ord` on the schedule calendar (the one in TDS `calendar_sessions` when present, otherwise the number the engine builds with the calendar's first session = 0) satisfies `(session_ord − offset) mod n == 0`. Independent of the run's start date |

A schedule that counts inside a period (`monthly`, `weekly`, `quarterly`, `annually`) decides in a period only when the calendar settles which session the pick is: a pick counted from the end (`day < 0`) needs a session of the calendar after the period, a pick counted from the start (`day > 0`) needs one before it, and a pick that falls back to the period's first or last session, because the period holds fewer sessions than `|day|`, needs both; otherwise the schedule has no decision in that period (`daily()` and `every(n)` count inside no period, and the rule reads the calendar only, never the run's start or end) [decision 2026-11-15, #461, ADR-0043].

**Parameters**: `nx.param(default: T, *, grid: Sequence[T] | None = None, name: str | None = None) -> Param[T]`. Usable wherever an integer or a float goes (window lengths, `top(n)`, `max_weight`, ratios). Without a name, the name is taken from the global variable of the module that built the strategy (`ConfigError` when two names point to the same Param). Values are set by `params={"n": 20}`, the CLI `--set n=20` or `nx.sweep`. The resolved values are left in the manifest `params` [R-08].

**Portfolio** [C6, 11.4.4]:

```python
nx.top(n: int | Param, *, per: Classifier | None = None,
       weight: Literal["equal","value","inverse_vol"] = "equal",
       max_weight: float | None = None, vol_lookback: int = 252, redistribute: bool = False,
       hold: int | Param | None = None, on_missing: Literal["drop","hold"] = "drop",   #
       tiebreak: Literal["hash","id"] = "<toml [portfolio] tiebreak>") -> PortfolioRule   #
nx.bottom(n, **same arguments) -> PortfolioRule          # short side: n by ascending score, negative weights
nx.long_short(long: PortfolioRule, short: PortfolioRule, *, long_weight: float = 0.5, short_weight: float = 0.5,
                max_gross: float | None = None, max_net: tuple[float, float] | None = None) -> PortfolioRule   #
```

- `hold` (hold band, default `None` = off): with `hold=m` (m ≥ n), an instrument that was in the previous decision's canonical target stays while it is within rank m. The norm is 5.5.3 (the target-state recursion). **Until T7.1 is in, a `hold` other than `None` is `CapabilityError` (E18, "available after ticket T7.1"); supported in the v1 release** [SC-02, OQ-1]. There is no default width: the band is on only with the `m` the user gives [#725 Decisions 244].

- **Short selling and signs**: `top` is the long side (weights ≥ 0), `bottom` the short side (the same rule applied by ascending score, then the weights multiplied by −1 — the mirror image of the long side). `long_short(long, short)` builds the two sides separately (sum ≤ 1 per side, the remainder under the `max_weight` cap in cash) and then **only multiplies** the long-side weights by `long_weight` and the short-side weights by `short_weight` — it does not normalise again, so `top(1, max_weight=0.1)` + `long_weight = 0.5` is 0.05 (v3.1's "the long side sum is fitted to `long_weight`" undid the cap and was removed). When the same instrument is on both sides, only the long side keeps it and it is removed from the short side (`reason = conflict_long_kept`), and the remaining shorts are not refitted (that share is cash). **Feasible region**: longs have no margin loan and one short sale reduces `buying_power` by `c·|q|·P` (5.6.7, c = `collateral_ratio`), so in the cash account (default) a book that exceeds `long_weight + c·short_weight ≤ 1` per sleeve (long only = L 1·S 0, short only `bottom` = L 0·S 1) is `ConfigError` before the run (E16, "long-short book needs margin: long_weight + collateral_ratio × short_weight = {x} > 1"; 130/30 = 1.3 + 0.5 × 0.3 = 1.45). The risk stage only shrinks toward zero, so targets during the run do not leave this region. The assumption sentence is "longs are fully cash-funded; no margin loan; short proceeds are restricted as collateral". With `[account] type = "margin"`, the feasible region is 5.6.7a (`L + S ≤ min(max_gross, 1/initial_margin)`). With `[shorting] enabled = false` (default), using `bottom`, `long_short` or a rule that makes negative weights is `ConfigError` before the run (E16, "short targets need [shorting] enabled = true"). In the cash account both rules apply: this feasible region and the gross cap below. When gross exposure `Σ|w|` exceeds `max_gross` (default = `[shorting] max_gross`, 1.0; in the cash account the cap is `G_max = min(1, max_gross)`, 5.5.1a) or net exposure `Σw` leaves `max_net` (default `[shorting] max_net`, the closed interval `[−1, 1]`; it must contain 0, otherwise E16), the risk stage scales proportionally toward zero (5.5.2; never up). Leverage goes only up to the gross exposure cap the user sets.
- Selection: within each sleeve, at most n of the instruments whose `signal` is finite and whose `universe` is true, by descending score (ascending on the short side) + the tie-break key. The tie-break key: `tiebreak = "hash"` (default) = ascending (the first 8 bytes of `sha256("nexusbt-tiebreak-v1" ‖ 0x1F ‖ instrument_id)` as a big-endian unsigned integer, `instrument_id`) — a fixed order per instrument, independent of date, clock, graph, sleeve and snapshot (v3.1's `decision_id` salt reshuffled the order at every decision and made false turnover in strategies with sparse scores), and independent of instrument numbers, so there is no hidden tilt that "always picks the companies with small (old) numbers"; `"id"` = ascending `instrument_id` (the zipline preset, 5.6.15). `n_tied_at_cutoff` of `coverage.parquet` counts the tie group that straddles the cutoff: the number of instruments whose score equals that of the last one selected, when at least one instrument with that score was left out; 0 when the cutoff falls between two different scores. A decision with several cutoffs (one per sleeve and per `per` group) reports the sum of its groups' tie-group sizes. (The Factor methods `.top()`, `.bottom()` and `.rank("ordinal")` follow the same `[portfolio] tiebreak` — Pipeline selection and portfolio selection pick the same instruments; the zipline comparison (8.1 L1-d) runs with `tiebreak = "id"`.) With `per`, n per group (`per=Meta.market` is the same as the sleeve, so it has no effect + a notice).
- **Shorthand**: `weight=`, `max_weight=`, `vol_lookback=` and `redistribute=` are shorthand for `sizing=nx.size.<weight>(max_weight=…, lookback=…, redistribute=…)`. Giving `sizing=` together with any of these four at a non-default value is E16 before the run ("weight given twice: top(weight=...) and sizing=..."). The definitions in the two lines below are exactly the definitions of `size.equal`, `value` and `inverse_vol`.
- Weights before weighting `u_i`: equal = 1, value = market capitalisation at the decision (the package's share-count item, 5.3.5, × raw close; when missing, removed from the selection with E30), inverse_vol = 1/`AnnualizedVolatility(vol_lookback)` (removed from the selection when NaN or 0).
- `on_missing` (meaningful only together with the hold band `hold`): 5.5.3.
- `w_i = u_i / Σu`. With `max_weight`, `w_i ← min(w_i, max_weight)` and the remaining share is cash (`redistribute=False`; not filled from lower ranks). With `redistribute=True`, it is redistributed repeatedly and proportionally to the instruments not at the cap (at most 50 rounds, convergence 1e-12).
- Result: long only, sum ≤ 1 (within the sleeve) [11.4.1]; long–short, `Σ|w| ≤ G_max`, `Σw ∈ max_net`.

**Execution rules** [R-56, 9.0 orders]:

```python
nx.execution.market(at: Literal["open","close"] = "open", *, max_participation: float | None = None,
                    tif: Literal["gtc","day"] = "gtc", max_age_sessions: int | None = None,
                    min_trade_value: float | None = None, min_trade_weight: float | None = None,     #
                    rebalance_band: float | None = None, band_mode: Literal["edge","target"] | None = None) -> ExecutionRule
nx.execution.limit(offset_bps: float = 0.0, *, ref: Literal["decision_close"] = "decision_close",
                   tif: Literal["day","gtc"] = "day", max_participation: float | None = None,
                   max_age_sessions: int | None = None, min_trade_value=None, min_trade_weight=None,
                   rebalance_band=None, band_mode=None) -> ExecutionRule
```

- Default = `market(at="open")`: fills at the open of the first session after the decision (design Q5). `at="close"` is the close of the first session after the decision (used by the zipline preset). There is no "fill at the same close" [11.4.1, A-12].
- `limit`: the buy limit = ref·(1 + offset_bps/1e4) and the sell limit = ref·(1 − offset_bps/1e4) are snapped to that market's price grid on the **conservative side** (buy down, sell up) (5.6.4a). v3's "not rounded to the tick" was removed (fills at prices that do not exist in the Korean tick grid made results slightly better and always broke the comparison with real trades; zipline#2211, freqtrade#13661).
- `min_trade_value`, `min_trade_weight`, `rebalance_band`, `band_mode` (default = toml `[execution]`, all off): filtering out small rebalancing orders (5.6.7).
- `max_participation` default = toml `[execution] max_participation` (0.05), `max_age_sessions` default 20 [11.4.2].

**Exit rules** (non-expiring closing orders attached to each holding — a sell for a long, a buy to cover for a short; the exit orders of one holding are OCO with each other; life cycle in 5.6.2a) [R-56, Q21 → A-11]:

| Function | Order (long holding / short holding) | Price (long / short) |
|---|---|---|
| `nx.exits.stop_loss(pct: float)` | sell stop / cover (buy) stop | `avg_cost·(1 − pct)` / `avg_cost·(1 + pct)` |
| `nx.exits.stop_limit_loss(stop_pct: float, limit_pct: float)` | sell stop-limit / buy stop-limit | long: stop `avg_cost·(1−stop_pct)`, limit `avg_cost·(1−limit_pct)`; short: stop `avg_cost·(1+stop_pct)`, limit `avg_cost·(1+limit_pct)`; `limit_pct >= stop_pct` |
| `nx.exits.take_profit(pct: float)` | sell limit / buy limit | `avg_cost·(1 + pct)` / `avg_cost·(1 − pct)` |
| `nx.exits.trailing_stop(pct: float)` | sell stop / buy stop | `max(closes after entry)·(1 − pct)` / `min(closes after entry)·(1 + pct)`, recomputed after every session close |
| `nx.exits.time_stop(sessions: int)` | market sell / market cover (buy). Created after the close of the session in which the number of holding sessions (first entry fill session = 1, market calendar sessions, halt days included [V31-52]) reaches `sessions`, and placed in the next session's open stage; when halted, carried over as in 5.6.5 | no price (open); OCO with the stop loss and take profit. When the holding grows or shrinks, the entry session stays the first entry (0 → ≠ 0); when the sign changes, the count starts again |

- A stop loss + take profit + `time_stop` tied as the OCO of one holding is the triple barrier. The label `nx.labels.triple_barrier` (v1, 5.8) is built by calling **the same decision function** (5.6.4) and the same session count [SC-20, blueprint E-10].

- Creation, quantity and price basis are in 5.6.2a: created in the same stage right after the entry fill event, quantity = the sellable quantity of the holding at that moment, price basis = the `avg_cost` at that moment; refitted immediately at every event that changes the holding. No expiry (`max_age_sessions = None`); `CANCELLED(position_closed)` when the holding becomes 0. `avg_cost` is the weighted average purchase price on the raw-price basis (a float before minor-unit booking; quantity and price adjusted at splits). (v2's wording "the sellable quantity at the start of that session" contradicted the same-bar stop G-08 and was removed.)
- Exit prices are computed and then snapped to the price grid on the conservative side (a long stop-loss sell stop down, a short stop-loss buy stop up, a take-profit sell limit up and buy limit down; 5.6.4a). The exit orders of a short holding mirror those of a long (price basis = the average short-sale price `avg_cost`), quantity = the size of the short quantity at that moment. They are created right after the short entry (0 → negative) fill, and the trailing reference is `low_water_close = min(closes after entry)`.
- Daily-bar assumption sentence (manifest `assumptions`): "stops fill at the stop price unless the open gaps through it; no further slippage past the stop is modeled".

**Cost models** [R-25, R-61, Q6]:

| Function | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `nx.costs.provisional(table: str | None = None)` | default. Fixed bp per market (`provisional_bps` of the market rules pack, overridden by toml `[costs.provisional_bps] <MARKET>`), "provisional costs" in the report. Given `table` (or toml `[costs] provisional_table`), it uses the row of the user's **period and liquidity-band table** (schema of 4.7: `market, side, valid_from, valid_to, adv_from, adv_to, bps, source`) for that fill date and that instrument's ADV at the decision (a currency amount, t−20..t−1). The default is no table = fixed bp (the user decision "commissions and taxes are user settings", R-61 as it is); "cost by liquidity tier" on the report's cost line [V31-27] |
| `nx.costs.realistic(table: str = "default", *, impact_y: float = 0.5, impact_a2: float = 0.5, impact_a3: float = 1.0)` | commission and tax tables by period and country + a ½-spread estimate + the impact `Y·σ^a3·(q/ADV)^a2` (default = square-root impact, 5.6.8) [SC-11]. When the table coverage check fails, it is blocked with `CostTableError` (error E23), suggesting `provisional()` |
| `nx.costs.bps(buy: float, sell: float, *, by_market: Mapping[str, tuple[float, float]] | None = None)` | fixed bp. Overridden by market key (the snapshot's `markets`), as in `by_market={"KR": (5, 25)}` |
| `nx.costs.per_share(cost: float, min_trade_cost: float = 0.0)` | commission per share (local currency) |
| `nx.costs.none()` | 0 |

### 3.8 Function signatures and returns

Common keywords ("common" in the table below): `snapshot: str | Snapshot | None = None` (default toml), `segment: str | None = None` (default toml `[split] default_segment`), `start`, `end` (only to narrow further inside the segment; `ConfigError` outside it), `study: str | None = None`, `purpose: Literal["research","debug","seed_comparison"] = "research"` (the run kind of 6.2; user decision 2026-11-04, #196), `save: bool = True`. v3.2's reserved argument `splits=` was removed: splitting is a value of `backtest(method=…)` (6.3a; `evaluate` is linear only) [SC-21].

```python
nx.evaluate(term: Factor | Mapping[str, Factor], *, target: str | BoundColumn = "fwd_return",   # "fwd_return" (economic label) | "fwd_return_adj"
            freq: Literal["monthly","weekly","daily"] = "monthly", horizons: Sequence[int | Literal["next"]] = ("next",),
            quantiles: int = 5, universe: Filter | None = None,
            by: Sequence[Literal["market","year","segment"]] = ("market",),
            delisting: Literal["observed_then_policy","observed_only","lo","hi"] | None = None,
            save_signal: bool = False, **common) -> FactorReport

nx.backtest(strategy: Strategy, *, capital: float | None = None, base_ccy: str | None = None,   # ISO 4217; the snapshot must have exchange rates into that currency
            clock: str | None = None,                                                     # "<MARKET>_close" | "market_local"
            sleeves: Mapping[str, float] | None = None, costs: CostModel | None = None,
            delisting: str | None = None, params: Mapping[str, Any] | None = None,
            preset: Literal["realistic","zipline"] = "realistic",
            offsets: int | None = None,                                                   # rebalance-date luck
            scenarios: bool | Sequence[Literal["gross","cost0x","cost2x","delist_lo","delist_hi","ca_exit_lo","ca_exit_hi","capacity"]] = True,   # gross = cost0x (one and the same replay)
            save_signal: bool = False,
            save_detail: bool = False,                                                    # tier-3 outputs, 5.9.1 [#206]
            method: Literal["linear","walk_forward","cpcv","cscv"] | Split = "linear",      # validation method, 6.3a
            grid: Mapping[str, Sequence[Any]] | None = None,                                # candidates chosen per fold (method ≠ linear)
            select: Literal["net_sharpe","cagr","calmar"] = "net_sharpe",
            preview: bool = False, **common) -> Run                                          #

nx.screen(obj: Strategy | Factor | Pipeline, *, asof: str | date | None = None, top: int | None = None,
          params: Mapping[str, Any] | None = None, anchor: str | date | None = None,
          show: Literal["selected","all","held+near"] = "selected", near: int = 10,
          book: Book | str | None = None,
          account: Literal["auto","none","book","backtest"] = "auto", **common) -> Screen   #

nx.live(strategy: Strategy, *, book: Book | str, asof: str | date | None = None,
        params: Mapping[str, Any] | None = None, snapshot: str | Snapshot | None = None) -> Orders
nx.fill(book: Book | str, orders: Orders | None = None, *, at: Literal["next_open"] = "next_open",
        snapshot: str | Snapshot | None = None) -> BookUpdate
nx.reconcile(book: Book | str, *, restate: bool = False, snapshot: str | Snapshot | None = None,
             day: str | date | None = None) -> ReconcileReport   # day=: the order-day record (5.9.7 (5))
                                   # default: replay with the snapshots the ledger recorded. restate=True: restatement comparison with snapshot (default latest)

nx.sweep(strategy: Strategy, *, grid: Mapping[str, Sequence[Any]] | None = None,
         pbo_blocks: int = 16, **backtest_kwargs) -> SweepResult
nx.compare(*items: str | Run | Strategy, metrics: Sequence[str] | None = None, **backtest_kwargs) -> Comparison   # variant table
nx.splits.holdout() | nx.splits.walk_forward(train="5y", test="1y", step="1y", anchored=False, embargo=0)
    | nx.splits.cpcv(n_groups=6, k_test=2, embargo=5, is_scores=None) | nx.splits.cscv(blocks=16) -> Split   # 6.3a; is_scores: "strict" | "approximate" | None [#215]
nx.ml.factor(model, features: Sequence[Factor], label: str | LabelTerm | None = None, *, train: Window | None = None, refit: Schedule | None = None,
             embargo: int = 0, sample_weight: None = None, cross_section: Literal["pooled"] = "pooled",
             score: Literal["p_up", "p_down", "p_up_minus_down"] | None = None,
             name: str | None = None) -> Factor                                               # 5.3.11; model = a factory or a cloneable model (#195)
nx.ml.rolling(length: str | int) | nx.ml.expanding(min_length: str | int) -> Window     # training window (not a split)
nx.labels.triple_barrier(*, take_profit: float, stop_loss: float, max_sessions: int,
                         entry: Literal["next_open"] = "next_open",
                         time_exit: Literal["close", "next_open"] = "close") -> LabelTerm      # 5.8; time_exit (#195)
@nx.portfolio(side: Literal["long","short"] = "long", window: int = 1, inputs: Sequence[Term] = ())   #; "both" is E16
def f(ctx: nx.PortfolioContext) -> Mapping[str, int]: ...      # instrument_id → +1 (long) / −1 (short); rank = score order
@nx.sizer(window: int = 252, inputs: Sequence[Term] = (nx.Price.close,), returns: Literal["units","weights"] = "units")
def f(ctx: nx.SizingContext) -> Mapping[str, float]: ...        # finite u ≥ 0 → the engine normalises per side; for "weights", same sign as the side and Σ|w| ≤ 1 per side, otherwise E41
@nx.risk_rule(reads_account: bool = False, window: int = 1, inputs: Sequence[Term] = ())
def f(ctx: nx.RiskContext, w: nx.TargetView, state: Mapping[str, Scalar]) -> nx.RiskDecision: ...
                                                                # RiskDecision(mult=float | Mapping[id, float], state={...}, reason="...")
nx.explain(obj: Term | Strategy, instrument_id: str, date: str | date, *, snapshot=None,
           params: Mapping[str, Any] | None = None) -> Explanation | DecisionExplanation
nx.check(obj: Term | Pipeline | Strategy, *, probes: int = 0, seed: int = 0, start_probe: bool = False,
         verbose: bool = False) -> CheckReport
nx.describe(obj: object | None = None) -> Description          # None: the list of datasets
nx.runs(*, study: str | None = None, kind: str | None = None, limit: int = 50) -> RunTable
nx.rerun(run_id: str, *, new_snapshot: bool = False, trust: bool = False) -> Run
nx.book(spec: str) -> Book                                       # "paper:<name>" | "manual:<name>"
nx.override(book: Book | str, instrument_id: str, kind: Literal["no_trade","reduce_only","close"], *,
              from_: str | date | None = None, until: str | date | None = None, note: str = "") -> BookUpdate   #
nx.record_fills(book: Book | str, csv: str | Path) -> BookUpdate     # records of real fills and discretionary trades a person made
nx.cash_flow(book: Book | str, amount: float, *, ccy: str, sleeve: str, session: str | date | None = None,
               note: str = "") -> BookUpdate                       # deposit (+) / withdrawal (−) [V31-19]
nx.acknowledge(book: Book | str, divergence_id: str, note: str, *, by: str | None = None) -> BookUpdate   # 5.9.6 rule 3
nx.preregister(strategy: Strategy | Factor, *, metric: Literal["net_sharpe","cagr","ic_mean","dsr"], threshold: float,
               direction: Literal["higher","lower"] = "higher",                    # pass when metric ≥ threshold / ≤ threshold
               segment: str = "validation", start: str | date | None = None, end: str | date | None = None,   # the registered range (6.4)
               judge_at: str | date | Literal["end"] = "end",                       # the fixed judgement date, or the end of the range
               benchmark: str | None = None,                                         # None = the run's default benchmark (R-59)
               params: Mapping[str, Any] | None = None, costs: CostModel | None = None,
               scenarios: bool | Sequence[str] = True, method: str | Split = "linear",
               grid: Mapping[str, Sequence[Any]] | None = None,                                # the conditions of the claim (6.4)
               freq: str | None = None,                                              # factor only: evaluate's freq (a strategy's is its schedule)
               exposed_elsewhere: bool | None = None,   # was the segment seen outside the engine? (6.4)
               note: str = "") -> Preregistration
nx.config() -> Config                                            # the resolved nexusbt.toml
nx.open(path: str | Path) -> Snapshot   # one package path (or a parent folder with `LATEST` → that snapshot); the result can be given to `snapshot=` of every function
```

| Function | Decision grid | Run record | Attempts and exposure |
|---|---|---|---|
| `evaluate` | the last session of `freq` per market (market_local) | `kind="evaluate"` | candidate +1 per factor (for a `Mapping`, a separate candidate and observation per item), segment exposure |
| `backtest` | the strategy schedule, reference clock (5.1.3) | `kind="backtest"` | strategy candidate (+1 when new) + 1 observation, exposure |
| `screen` | one `asof` (default = each market's last snapshot session) | `kind="screen"` | exposure record only (no performance computed) |
| `run_pipeline(save=True)` | the sessions `freq` picks per market from `start` to `end` (3.5, market_local) | `kind="pipeline"` | exposure record only (no performance computed) |
| `live` | the ledger's next decision | `kind="live"` + ledger events | none |
| `sweep` | as `backtest` | parent `kind="sweep"` + `kind="backtest"` per candidate | +1 per candidate |
| `backtest(method≠linear)` | the strategy schedule, per fold and path | one `kind="backtest"` (`method`, folds and paths are sub-tables under that run's `validation/`) | strategy candidate +1 (+1 per `grid` point) + 1 observation; folds and paths are diagnostics (6.2) |
| `backtest(preview=True)` | the last year (within the chosen segment), a small universe | `kind="preview"` | +1 when a new candidate, 1 observation (`fidelity="preview"`; not a DSR performance input) |
| `compare(Strategy…)` | as `backtest` per variant | parent `kind="compare"` + `kind="backtest"` per variant | +1 per variant when it is a new candidate (the base strategy may already be a candidate) + 1 observation |
| `reconcile` | the decisions of the ledger period | not saved; updates the ledger's `status.json` | none |
| `override`, `record_fills`, `cash_flow`, `acknowledge` | – | ledger events (5.9.6) | none [V31-19] |
| `compare(run ids / Run)`, `explain`, `check`, `describe`, `runs` | – | not saved | none |

- `rerun(run_id)`: runs again with the manifest's snapshot (confirmed by the semantic hash; at least `schema` + the file sha256 stamp), source (a new process, 5.9.3), parameters, config and lock, and compares the value hashes. When the snapshot is missing or its semantic hash differs, `ReplayError` (error E15). `new_snapshot=True` is not a rerun: it runs as a "new run" (`kind` the original one, `derived_from=run_id`) and shows the differences [C9, 11.7]. `rerun` returns the `Run` of the new run record it makes (`run_kind = rerun`, 5.9.5; its index row has `derived_from` = the reproduced run's id, as a `new_snapshot=True` run's has, and `run_kind` tells the two apart): `run_id`, `path` and `manifest` are the new run's, and `comparison` is the line comparing its outputs with the reproduced run's, `outputs identical (value, physical)` or each table that differs and by which hash; `comparison` is None on a `Run` that `rerun` did not make. On a `Run` of a kind that computes no performance (`pipeline`, `screen`; 3.8's run-record table), an attribute whose output that kind does not write is None. [#250]
- `screen(strategy)` gives a ranking even on a day that is not a decision day and marks it `scheduled=False`. On such a day `live` places no new rebalancing orders, only exit-rule orders [11.4.6].
- `screen(factor, top=n)`: the top n of one factor per market in the default universe (3.6).
- **Account rule of screen**: `account="auto"` (default) = `"book"` when `book=` is given, otherwise `"none"`. With `"none"`, risk rules that read the account are not evaluated and are set to `m = 1`, the result header and that rule's line say `drawdown_brake: not evaluated (no account) — use --ledger BOOK or --as-backtest`, and the notice E42 is raised (never skipped silently; one line per rule in `skips.parquet`). `"book"` (CLI `--ledger BOOK`) = gives `final_weight` using the ledger state (valuation NAV history, holdings, entry sessions, exit events) as a read-only account; the ledger state version and the snapshot hash go into the manifest. `"backtest"` (CLI `--as-backtest`) = backtests the same strategy from the anchor (`anchor`) to `asof` (not saved, the same determinism) and evaluates with that account (slow; the time taken is shown). The canonical target (`target_weight`) is the same in all three (INV-08).
- **`preview=True`** (CLI `--preview`): period = first `I` = the chosen segment (`segment=`, default train) ∩ explicit `start`/`end` ∩ the snapshot period, then one year back from the last decision of `I` (`[preview] years`, default 1; all of `I` when `I` is shorter) — that result is the interval recorded as exposure; universe = the strategy universe ∩ per market the top `[preview] max_names` (default 200) by 20-session average value traded at the decision; scenarios, probes and capacity off; saved as `kind="preview"`; counted as an attempt (6.2). The first line is "PREVIEW — 1y, top 200 by traded value per market; not a research result". Target: within 5 seconds on synthetic `small` (a measurement reported, not a gate).
- **`compare`**: for run ids and `Run`s, the comparison table as now. Given `Strategy` values, it runs each with `backtest(**backtest_kwargs)` and gives a **variant table**: rows = variants, columns = the names of the slots that differ from the base strategy (the mark of `replace`), metrics (default CAGR, Sharpe, MDD, turnover, average `Σ|w|`, costs), a risk waterfall summary (the average share reduced per slot), DSR (cohort). The same Pipeline values are reused (L1). Variants count as candidates.
