- **User blocks**: an `@nx.portfolio` function gives only the selection (weights belong to the sizing slot). An output id outside the universe or a side value different from `side` is E41. User construction in v1 is **one-sided only** (`side="long"` or `"short"`): two sides are combined with `nx.long_short(my_long, my_short, long_weight=, short_weight=)` and get the L/S budget and the feasible-region check (3.7) as they are; `side="both"` is E16 before the run ("combine two one-sided blocks with nx.long_short"). The output of `@nx.sizer(returns="weights")` must have the same sign as the side and `Σ|w| ≤ 1` per side, otherwise E41. An `@nx.sizer` function takes only `SizingContext` (selected ids, sides, scores, ranks, the `inputs` window arrays `(window, n)` — last row = the decision row, `params`) and has no account attributes. A return that is not finite or is negative is E41. `@nx.risk_rule` takes `RiskContext` (the same windows + `ctx.account` only when `reads_account=True`: the NAV series up to the decision time, holding quantities, `avg_cost`, entry sessions, exit events — all read-only arrays), the view of the current weights `TargetView` and its own state from the previous decision, and gives a `RiskDecision`; `mult` out of range or not finite → E41; touching `ctx.account` with `reads_account=False` → E41 ("this rule reads the account; declare @nx.risk_rule(reads_account=True)"). State values are JSON scalars only, and the engine stores them in `risk_state.parquet`. All three decorators get check 6 of 6.5 (purity, captured constants) and E8 (window) as they are.
- `scenarios`: `True` = the default (`gross` (= `cost0x`, the zero-cost replay **once**), `cost2x`, `delist_lo`, `delist_hi` — 4 replays; plus `ca_exit_lo` — 1 more, `ca_exit_hi` being the base run's value as it is — only when the base run has at least one `ca_unverified_exit`, 5.6.1 [#150]), `False` = none, a list = those names (giving both `gross` and `cost0x` still replays once, and the same series is not stored under two names). `"capacity"` is not in the default (P5 budget) [SC-06, OQ-4]; it is turned on in `sweep`'s report of the final candidate. Every scenario keeps the run's canonical targets (Pipeline and targets reused, never decided again); a strategy with a `decide` block calls it again in each scenario that runs the account layer again, over that scenario's account (3.7, 5.7) [#725 Decisions 241]; a cost, delisting or `ca_exit_lo` scenario runs the account layer and execution again under its own costs and policies, and `capacity` runs execution only (5.7 "What each scenario runs again", #207). `capacity` is defined in 5.7.
- `anchor` (screen): the decision date where the target-state recursion (5.5.3) starts. Default = toml `[portfolio] anchor_date`. Ignored for strategies that do not use the recursion. The anchor of `backtest` is that run's first decision; for `live`, the decision that created the ledger.
- `explain(strategy, id, date)`: shows that instrument's path at that decision (5.9.9): true/false of each universe Filter → score and rank → selection / caps / risk overlays → order intent → (when the snapshot has the execution session) the fill or `blocked(reason)`. It recomputes, so no saved run is needed. On a saved run, `Run.why(id, date)` gives the same shape without recomputing [SC-13].
- `reconcile(book)`: 5.9.7 [SC-14]. The default is the deterministic replay (the snapshots the ledger recorded at each decision and fill); `restate=True` is a "restatement" comparison recomputed with new data, and the report names it so.
- `check(..., start_probe=True)`: also runs the start-date probe (6.5) [SC-04].
- `offsets=k` (default None): shifts the schedule k ways (monthly, quarterly, annual: the `day=1..k`-th session; `every(n)`: `offset=0..k−1`; weekly: `weekday=1..k`), reruns only the targets and the state machine on **the same Pipeline values** (L1 reuse), and shows the minimum, median and maximum of CAGR, Sharpe and MDD on one "rebalance-date luck" line. This is not a new candidate but a **diagnostic** of one candidate (not counted in attempts or cohorts, 6.2). The result is `offsets.parquet`. Like the truncation probe, the extra time is only reported.
- `screen(..., show=…)`: `"selected"` (only the selected instruments, as now), `"held+near"` (target and held instruments + the "just missed" instruments of ranks n+1..n+`near`), `"all"` (the whole universe). `Screen.xray()` gives a table with steps 1~4 of `explain` (5.9.9) spread as vectors on the same rows: a true/false/unknown column per universe leaf Filter, the value and percentile per signal node, a status column (`held` / `buy` / `hold_band` / `sell(reason)` / `near_miss` / `excluded(reason)`). It reuses the Pipeline node values without new computation. CLI `nexusbt screen --show held+near --html`. The columns that show holdings (`held`, `sell(reason)`, whether `hold_band` is held) are filled only when `book=` (a ledger) is given; otherwise those columns are "holdings unavailable" and holdings are never made up (the same for `Screen.xray(book=None)`).

### 3.9 Result objects and output shapes [R-10, R-11]

Every result object gives **the same lines** from `__str__` (terminal) and `_repr_html_` (notebook); table attributes are Arrow tables with `.to_polars()` and `.to_pandas()`. Format rules: ratios `12.3%` (1 decimal), IC and correlations 3 decimals, t, Sharpe and DSR 2 decimals, counts `1,612`, a count next to a ratio `1,612/1,705 (94.5%)`, dates ISO, KRW amounts integers + commas, USD 2 decimals. Work longer than 2 seconds prints a progress line on standard error (`stage · date · time left`) [R-41].

**FactorReport** (attributes: `summary`, `ic`, `quantile_returns`, `coverage`, `turnover`, `by_year`, `delisting`, `hygiene` (`bhy_p`, `m`, `cohort_sha256`, `breadth_naive`, `implied_ir`), `label` (`fwd_return` | `fwd_return_adj`, a definition sentence), `warnings`, `run_id`; methods `decay(horizons)`, `quantiles()`, `report()`, `to_strategy(path)`, `to_alphalens()` (5.9.8)):

```
{expr}        study: {study}   factor trial #{k} in study ({n_candidates} candidates)
data {snapshot_id} · {freq} · {start} → {end} · segment {segment}
family {family6}: studies {s} · candidates explored {n} (factor {a} · strategy {b}) · selection events {e} · segments seen train {a} · validation {b} · test {c}
label {fwd_return: holding return, dividends reinvested at ex-date close, pre-withholding | …}

                              KR                     US
decisions                    {n}                    {n}
names / decision (median)    {n}                    {n}
coverage                     {a}/{b} ({p}%)         …
IC mean ({h})                {ic:.3f}               …
IC t (Newey-West, L={L})     {t:.2f}                …
Q{q}-Q1 / period             {x:.2%}                …
  t                          {t:.2f}                …
monotonic (Q1<…<Q{q})        {k}/{q-1} steps        …
turnover Q{q} / period       {p}%                   …
rank autocorr (mean)         {r:.2f}                …
worst year IC                {year}  {ic:.3f}       …
exits in Q1 (obs/policy/unobs) {a}/{b}/{c} of {n}   …
naive breadth / yr · IR     {br:,} · {ir:.2f}      …   (naive opportunity count, not independent bets)
BHY-adjusted p (m={m})       {p:.3f}                …   (m = candidates recorded in this cohort)

gates (display only)  KR: IC t {t} {≥|<} {gate}   US: …
next   nx.evaluate(f, by=["year"]) · .quantiles() · .decay() · .to_strategy("strategies/x.py")
saved  runs/{run_id}/report.html
```

**Run** (attributes: `run_id`, `path`, `manifest`, `metrics`, `primary`, `diagnostics`, `provenance`, `validation` (6.3a), `daily`, `targets`, `orders`, `fills`, `events`, `positions`, `scenarios`, `attribution`, `exposures`, `warnings`, `comparison` (`rerun` only, #250); methods `report()`, `why(instrument_id, date)`, `returns(ccy=None)`, `to_pyfolio()`):

```
{name}   run {run_id}   study: {study}  candidate #{k} ({N} candidates in study)
data {snapshot_id} · code {graph_hash6} (git {sha}, {clean|dirty}) · params {k=v, …}
period {start} → {end} · segment {segment} · base {base_ccy} · clock {clock} · sleeves KR {w}/US {w}
execution {order} at next {open|close} · costs {profile}{ (provisional) } · markets {KR: rules KR@1 | ZZ: untested market rules}
benchmark {index} ({TR|PR}, {cap|equal|…}-weighted) · portfolio weighting {equal|value|inverse_vol}
{shorting: on · borrow {bp}/yr KR {a} US {b} · collateral {m} · no borrow-availability data; uptick rule not modeled}   (only when shorting is on)
assumptions  {cash: series {name} ({first date}..{last date}) | cash earns {r}%} · dividends pre-withholding · immediate settlement · fill qty capped by {realized session volume (ex-post){; open fills by opening-auction volume} | past volume only (volume_cap = "prior")} · gross = zero-cost replay (includes path effects)   (the cash item per currency: the series form when its `[account] cash_rate` series is used, the constant form when `cash_rate_bps` is; #313, decision 2026-11-10)
{ruined: {sleeve} on {date} — new orders stopped; obligations booked as negative cash}   (only when there is a ruined sleeve)

{primary    {method}{ {n} paths | {n} folds} {start} → {end}{ · online {k}/{n} folds}: {the method's numbers}}   (only when the run has a reference, 6.3a) [#216]
{reference  linear {start} → {end} · params = default{ (outside the grid)}: the table below}   (only when the run has a reference, 6.3a) [#216]
{IS scores  {strict (per split; {f} fits · {p} passes) | approximate — IS returns are sliced from a full-period run; account state entering a training group was shaped by trading in earlier (possibly test) groups | slice, no learned node — {the same sentence} | slice, training precedes the test window — {the same sentence}}}   (only with a `grid` and `cpcv` or `walk_forward`, 6.3a) [#215]
                      net        gross      KR net(KRW)  US net(USD)  benchmark
CAGR                  …          …          …            …            {… | n/a: not in snapshot}
vol                   …
Sharpe                …
max drawdown          …
{end unknown          {n} held · NAV {v} {ccy} · delist_lo {lo} / delist_hi {hi} | n/a (scenarios off)}   (only when the run holds an instrument whose end is not known, 5.6.13) [#725 Decisions 245]
turnover / year       {x:.1f}x
cost drag / year      {p}%  (direct {d}% · path {p2}%; commission · tax · spread · impact) · costs take {s}% of gross P&L   (always shown with turnover, #725 Decisions 244)
cost sensitivity      0x {cagr} · 1x {cagr} · 2x {cagr}
holdings (median)     {n} (KR {a} / US {b}) · sleeve weight now KR {p}% / US {p}% (start 50/50)
{exposure (median)    long {l}% · short {s}% · gross {g}% · net {n}%   borrow cost {c}%/yr · short dividends paid {d}}   (only when there are shorts)
vs benchmark          excess {x}%/yr (arith) · {y}%/yr (geom) · tracking error {te}% · IR {ir:.2f} · excess MDD {m}%
target gap (median)   {g}% (blocked {a} · cash {b} · cap {c} · lot rounding {d} · small/band {e}) · slip vs decision {s} bp
fx contribution       US sleeve {p}%/yr (local {a}%/yr · fx {b}%/yr · cross {c}%/yr)
signal-weight corr   median {tc:.2f} over {n} decisions   (not risk-adjusted)
{capacity: participation cap binds at ≈ {K} {ccy} (x10 CAGR {a}, x100 CAGR {b})}   (only with the "capacity" scenario)

exits while held      {n}: observed {a}, policy {b}, unobserved {c} · scenario lo/hi CAGR {x}/{y}
hypothetical exits   {n}: P&L {x} {ccy} ({p}% of total P&L), causes missing {a} · unverified {b} · disagrees {c}{, short {k}: bound open} · {ca_exit lo/hi CAGR {x}/{y} · MDD {m}/{m2} | ca sensitivity: n/a (scenarios off)} | ca sensitivity: n/a (0 hypothetical exits)   (the last form when n = 0, 5.6.1) [#150]
orders                filled {a} · partial {b} · expired {c} · cancelled {d} (insufficient cash {k}, {amt} {ccy}) · blocked-days: halt {e}, limit {f}, capacity {g} (bar volume {g2}; ψ-bound fills {b}/{n}{; auction-bound open fills {o}/{n}}), unknown-tradability {h}{, short ban {sb}} · suppressed: small {m}, band {n}
{rebalance-date luck  {k} offsets · CAGR min {a} / median {b} / max {c}}   (only with offsets)
dividends             paid {n} · pay date declared {m}/{n} · estimated by the producer {k}/{n}
PSR(0) {x:.2f} · DSR {x:.2f} (N={N} candidates in cohort {cohort6}, V[SR] from cohort) | DSR n/a ({reason}) · MinTRL(95%) {y:.1f}y{ · vs SR*_N {z:.1f}y (selection-adjusted reference)}{ · IID-based: dependence visible (Ljung–Box lags 1–10, p < 0.05; may also reflect changing volatility)}{ · fixed-sample statistic; {n} looks}   [#197] (the last part only when the look number n > 1, 6.2, #269)
{Sharpe (Lo, ρ1={r:.2f}, lags ≤ 10) {x:.2f}}   (only when |ρ1| > 0.1)
family {family6}: studies {s} · candidates explored {n} (factor {a} · strategy {b}) · selection events {e} · segments seen train {a} · validation {b} · test {c}
evaluation {research data | independent final test (look 1) | repeated look on preregistered data (look {n}) | independence not established ({reason}) | {k} predeclared candidates on the final data (look {n}) | exploratory on reused final data (seen {m} times before)} · comparison set: study {study} · {segment} · {kind}, cohort {cohort6}, {N} candidates, with returns {a}/{b}   (6.2) [#196, #197]
warnings {n} (run.warnings) · data fallbacks {k} · reconciled: {yes|NO}
{next   {entry} → docs/guide/choose-by-question.md#{id}{ · …}}   (only when an entry applies) [#218]
```

**Primary, reference, diagnostics and provenance on the first screen** (decision 2026-11-04, #216; the definitions are in 6.3a): a run with no reference (`method="linear"` or `splits.holdout()`) has the metric table as its primary result and prints neither the `primary` nor the `reference` line. With another method the `primary` line comes first and carries the method, its path or fold count and its period; the `reference` line names the linear run, its period and `params = default`, and the metric table under it is that linear run (`Run.metrics`). The PSR, DSR and Lo Sharpe lines and items (2), (4) and (5) of the hygiene box (6.3a) are diagnostics, each naming its series and comparison set (6.3); item (3) of the box, the path band, belongs to the primary result of a CPCV run; the `assumptions`, `family` and `evaluation` lines, the `IS scores` line (how the IS scores of a `grid` were computed: `strict`, `approximate` or `slice`, 6.3a, #215) and items (1), (6), (7) and (8) of the box are provenance. A value that could not be produced prints its failure marker (6.3a), never `nan`, and a value computed on fewer samples than its period holds prints its evaluation period and `n_valid`/`n_total`. `report.html` and a result table written to CSV carry the same four labels (6.3a).

**The simulated-result line** (user decision 2026-11-25, #764): the first screen and `report.html` of a Run and of a FactorReport each carry one line, always, with no setting that turns it off, saying that the result is simulated from past data, that no trade was actually executed, and that it is no statement that the same result will recur.

**The `next` line** (decision 2026-11-04, #218): at most three entries of the guide `docs/guide/choose-by-question.md` (3.10), in the guide's order, each chosen from this fixed table of what the run did, never from the value of a metric: a `grid`, or a strategy cohort with N > 1 candidates → `compared`; an `nx.ml.factor` or a parameter in `params=` with `method="linear"` → `periodic`; the evaluation label `research data` → `unseen`; an E21 notice or `skipped` > 0 → `na`. Each entry is written as the run's fact and the guide anchor, for example `next   you chose among 12 settings on this data → docs/guide/choose-by-question.md#compared`. A fact the engine does not yet produce gives no entry; with no entry there is no line. The line never ranks methods, never says "safe" and never suggests changing the data, period or metric.

The exposure table, risk metrics (drawdown duration, recovery time, worst month), the attribution table, BHY and N_eff (v1.x) are not on the first screen; they go in the "hygiene", "attribution" and "exposures" sections of the `report()` HTML [blueprint chapter 10 risk (1)]. HTML sections: "monthly" (a calendar month × year return table, with the excess over the benchmark at the end of each row), "rolling" (quantile tables of excess returns over 1-year and 3-year windows), "trades" (counts, win rates, average holding periods and MAE/MFE per exit reason), "execution" (the decomposition of target-gap causes, fill price against decision price and arrival price in bp, the count of effective prices outside the bar range), "cost tiers" (costs per liquidity band [V31-27]). Each of these sections sits under the question it answers in the list below.

**`report.html` of a Run: the eight questions** (user decision 2026-11-04). The page opens with the first screen (the Run lines above and the hygiene box of 6.3a, text only). Below it come the sections in this order, one per question an investor asks of a backtest. A section, or a part of one, that is left out keeps its place: its heading and one line `{part}: n/a ({reason})`, so the order never shifts and the reader sees why.

| # | Question | Section id | Charts and tables | Draws from | Left out when |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | How much was made | `nav` | cumulative NAV, net, gross and benchmark, in the base currency; a log axis when every plotted value is above 0, linear otherwise | `daily.parquet` NAV, the gross replay (5.7 gross return), the benchmark (5.7) | never; the benchmark line when the snapshot has no index (`benchmark: n/a (not in snapshot)`) |
| 2 | How much was lost | `drawdown` | underwater drawdown chart; a table of the five largest drawdowns (peak date, trough date, recovery date or "not recovered", depth, drawdown duration and recovery duration in calendar days as 5.7 defines them) | maximum drawdown and drawdown duration (5.7), on the net series of 1 | never; the table has fewer rows when the run has fewer than five drawdowns |
| 3 | When it worked | `monthly` | a month × year return heatmap and, directly below it in the same section, a yearly table (return, volatility, Sharpe, maximum drawdown, benchmark excess); the evaluation segments (6.1) shaded | the monthly table (5.7); the yearly rows use the 5.7 formulas on each calendar year's daily returns | never; a partial month (the run starts or ends inside it), a month with no valuation day and a month with no trade each carry their own mark in the cell |
| 4 | What risk was held | `exposures` | long and short exposure as areas, gross and net exposure as lines; per-currency exposure | `exposures.parquet` (5.7 exposure), 5.6.9 | the short area when the run holds no short; the per-currency chart for a run with one currency |
| 5 | What ate the return | `attribution` | the cost decomposition table (commission, tax, spread, impact, borrow, FX) with direct cost and path effect; the scenario table (cost sensitivity, capacity); the "cost tiers" table; rolling one-year Sharpe and rolling one-year benchmark excess return charts, and the "rolling" quantile table | `attribution.parquet` (T3.14), cost drag and cost sensitivity (5.7); each point of a rolling chart is the 5.7 Sharpe (or the 5.7 arithmetic annual excess return) of the last 252 daily returns, one point per valuation day; the "rolling" table is 5.7 rolling windows | the rolling charts when the run has fewer than 252 daily returns (`rolling 1y: n/a (run shorter than 252 valuation days)`); the excess chart without a benchmark; the borrow row without shorts; the "rolling" table before T6.18 |
| 6 | Could the trades be done | `execution` | the orders line and blocked-day counts; the target-gap breakdown by cause; the "trades" and "execution" tables | the `orders` line (3.9), target gap and execution quality (5.7), `trades.parquet` | never; the "trades" table before `trades.parquet` exists |
| 7 | Was the data enough | `data` | universe size per year, coverage per field, a summary of instruments delisted while held; a per-year table of names excluded for a missing or doubtful exact ratio (names excluded, share of the universe, decisions affected) and the list of `ca_exclusions.parquet` (5.6.1) [#150]; the totals per term of `term_diagnostics` (5.4.1) | `coverage.parquet` (5.9.1; `n_excluded_by_reason` keys `ca_ratio_*` against `n_universe`), `ca_exclusions.parquet`, the `exits while held` and `hypothetical exits` lines; `survivorship.parquet` (universe size per year, delisted and delisted while held, the terminal value's share of NAV with its lo/hi) and `coverage_fields.parquet` (coverage per field: value, stale, not yet published, missing; once, each `freq` value 5.9.1 does not read as a period [#394]), both 5.9.1 [#160] | each part whose table the run does not carry, named in its one line; the exclusion table when the run has no such event |
| 8 | Can it be trusted | `hygiene` | what stands behind the first-screen hygiene box (6.3a, T6.22), not a second copy of it: the recorded-trial scope (study, cohort, `cohort_sha256`, 6.2) and the attempt records; then the assumption lines (3.9) | 6.3a, 6.2, the `assumptions` line | never; before T6.22, the attempts and assumption lines only |

**`report.html` of a FactorReport** shares the page frame (the first screen at the top, the hygiene and assumption lines at the end) and the visual rules below, with its own sections in this order: `summary` (the FactorReport lines), `ic` (IC series), `quantiles` (quantile cumulative returns, with the weighting, rebalance and cost conditions written on the chart), `by_year` (yearly IC bars), `turnover`, `coverage`, `hygiene`, `assumptions`. The same n/a rule applies.

**Metrics per strategy style** (user decision 2026-11-03): a result is judged by the metrics that fit its style, and the 10.0 eye checks read each representative result this way. A long-holding portfolio (long only, monthly or slower; `examples/strategies/mom_kr.py`) is judged by questions 1, 2, 3 and 5 (NAV against the benchmark, drawdown, the yearly table, costs), not by trade win rate. A long-short book (`examples/strategies/long_short_kr.py`) adds question 4 (exposure). A factor (`examples/factors/momentum.py`) is judged by its FactorReport: IC, quantile returns, turnover and coverage.

**Visual rules** (each one a statement a test or a reader can check):
- Order: sections come in the order of the lists above; in a Run report the first chart is the cumulative NAV with the benchmark, and the second is the drawdown.
- One base currency per chart: a chart of money values uses one currency, named in its title; per-sleeve local-currency values get their own chart.
- Units: every axis has a label with its unit (`%`, `KRW`, `USD`, `days`, `x`).
- Losses not by colour alone: a negative value carries a minus sign in every table and heatmap cell, and a loss area (underwater, short exposure) carries a pattern or a labelled zero line as well as its colour.
- No external files: the page references no URL and no other file (no `src` or `href` outside the page, no web font), so every chart renders in a browser with no network.
- Budget: on the synthetic `full_ci` snapshot, `report.html` is at most 5 MB (a test checks the file size) and shows its first chart within 2 s in a current browser with no network (the person checks it in the 10.0 milestone row). The representative reports are the factor example `examples/factors/momentum.py` from M2 and the long-only example `examples/strategies/mom_kr.py` from M3a.
- Plain explanations (design review D19, #219): every diagnostic line and every method line of the first screen and of the `hygiene` section carries in `report.html` a closed expandable part (`<details>`) whose summary is "what this means"; opened, it shows parts 1 and 6 of that tool's card (8.5) in plain words (the question and the trap; how to read the number and what it cannot support), and a second closed part inside it holds parts 3 and 4 (inputs, comparison set, conditions, how the data is split) and the formula. The text is inlined (the rule above), equals the card's text (`tests/docs/test_tool_cards.py::test_report_explanations_match_cards`), and the terminal and `_repr_html_` lines do not change.
- Rendering means: not fixed here. T2.10b chooses among inline SVG from the template, embedded matplotlib images and an inlined partial Plotly bundle after measuring a representative report's size and generation time, and records the choice and the measures in an ADR. Interactive charts, cross-run overlays and PDF are v1.x (`nexusbt[viz]` as an optional extra).

**Screen** (attributes `table`, `scheduled`, `asof`):

```
{name}  screen as of KR {date} close · US {date} close   data {snapshot_id}   {scheduled|not a rebalance day (preview)}
rank  instrument_id   name            mkt  score   {signal terms…}  target_w  flags
…
universe {n} KR / {n} US · selected {a}/{n_KR} KR, {b}/{n_US} US · run {run_id}
```

`flags`: `vendor` (vendor input), `halted`, `limit-up`, `admin` (administrative issue, caution), `short-ban` (a short target during a short-sale ban period).

With `show = "held+near"|"all"`, the column `status` (`held`/`buy`/`hold_band`/`sell(reason)`/`near_miss`/`excluded(reason)`) is added, and `Screen.xray()` is the Arrow table spread out to the leaf Filter and signal node columns.

**Orders** (attribute `table`: `idempotency_key, sleeve, action, instrument_id, name, order_type, qty, limit_price, stop_price, tif, est_price, est_value, reason, check`; `action` ∈ buy / sell / sell_short / buy_to_cover, `check` = the result of the pre-checks):

```
{name} live · decision {decision_id} · as of KR {date} close · book {book} (value {v} {ccy})
action  instrument_id  name   type    qty    est_price   est_value   reason
…
orders {n} (risk-reducing first: sells, covers) · est cost {c} · next rebalance {date} · checks: {ok|n flagged (E36)}
write: orders.to_csv("orders/{date}.csv")   apply to paper book: nx.fill(book, orders)
```

**DecisionExplanation** (`nx.explain(strategy, …)`, `Run.why`): gives the stage table of 5.9.9 as lines, as it is (`universe  Filter a: True · Filter b: False ← dropped here` → `score {x} · rank {r}/{n}` → `selected | not_selected | capped | risk_capped` → `order {side} {qty}` → `fill {qty} @ {px} ({fill_rule}) | blocked({reason})`).

**ReconcileReport** (`nx.reconcile`): `book {name} · decisions {n} · sessions {a}→{b} · mode {replay (recorded snapshots) | restate (snapshot {id})}`, then the lines `targets {match|first diff}`, `orders …`, `fills …`, `nav …`, and a last line `reconciled: yes` or `first divergence {date} {instrument_id} {stage}: book {x} vs replay {y}`. Above the result, a line `acknowledged {n}` with their ids when the replay starts at an acknowledgement, and a line `corrections {n}` with their list when the book has correction rows (5.9.6 rules 2 and 3); with `day=`, the order-day record of 5.9.7 (5).

**SweepResult**, **Comparison**, **RunTable** and **Explanation** follow the shapes of design 3.3, but the DSR line follows the Run rule above and the PBO line is written `PBO (CSCV, {S} blocks, {C} splits) {p:.2f}`. `Explanation` shows, for an item term, the value-table row it read (its date and value) and its age (5.3.5) [R-23]. In v1.x, `Comparison` gains `first_divergence()` (the date, instrument and cause where daily returns first differ: target / fill / cost).

### 3.10 CLI [R-09]

`nexusbt <command>` calls the API of the same name and prints the same text. Exit codes: 0 success, 1 user error (`NexusBTError`), 2 internal error, 3 waiting for data (`DataAvailabilityError`, live only), 4 reconciliation mismatch (`reconciled: NO` of `reconcile`, `outputs differ` of `rerun` without `--new-snapshot`). An error or a warning of 6.6, in the CLI, is printed on standard error in 6.6's shape alone: no Python `file:line: UserWarning:` prefix before it and no source line after it; what the person's own warning filters (`-W`, `PYTHONWARNINGS`) do to a warning, including turning it into an error, is not decided here (#649, #684).

| Command | Arguments |
|---|---|
| `nexusbt init [--data-root PATH] [--demo] [--lang en\|ko] [--new-family REASON]` | `--new-family` in an existing project replaces `[research] family_id` with a new one and records the old one as its parent with the reason (6.2, #196); otherwise writes a commented `nexusbt.toml` template (part 1 "check these" and part 2 "advanced", the two tiers of 3.11; the default English comments are canonical, `--lang ko` is a variant with the same keys and values and only the comments in Korean; the templates are `nexusbt/templates/nexusbt.toml.{en,ko}`, and a test matches their key set with the table of 3.11). With `--demo`, it unpacks the small synthetic KR+US snapshot shipped in the wheel (the `demo` preset of `nx.testing.synth`, a few MB) and writes its path — step 0 of data preparation. Creates `nexusbt.toml`. Without `--data-root`, it uses the environment variable `NEXUSBT_DATA_ROOT`, and without that it explains how to make a synthetic snapshot (no personal paths go into the repository, 2.7) |
| `nexusbt config check [--file PATH]` | checks only the config file and runs nothing: unknown keys, types and ranges (E16) and **contradictions** (margin keys in a cash account, short keys with `[shorting] enabled = false`, groups with `[taxes] profile = "none"`, a `max_net` that does not contain 0, and so on), in plain words in the format of 6.6 as it is (`layer  config`, a fix line). With no problem, one line "config ok: {n} keys set, {m} defaults", exit code 0/1 |
| `nexusbt config show [--all] [--file PATH]` | a table of the values in effect now and their **source** (`default` / `file` / `CLI` / `code` — a value overridden by an argument in the strategy code). By default only the part 1 keys and the keys not at their default; `--all` is the whole table of 3.11. It is the same function as the resolver `load_config` the run uses (the 2.x module table), so the values equal the config written in the manifest |
| `nexusbt doctor [--full]` | snapshot id, semantic hash, the three profile results (declared / check level / supported), items not checked, counts per TDS code, **the list of fallbacks (4.5)**, per market the rules pack (present / untested), period, instrument count and daily row count, column count, free disk, L0/L1 cache sizes, whether the stubs are current. A line with the number of runs whose root pin could not be written, "pins not visible to producer". When the data root has the publishing mark `<root>/.publishing` (a general distribution convention, outside TDS), it shows only "publishing in progress" and says to wait for the new snapshot [blueprint 12.5] |
| `nexusbt new factor NAME` / `nexusbt new strategy NAME [--template T]` / `nexusbt new --list` | creates a file that runs as it is (default template `monthly-topn`). 22 templates (each a runnable example + an output test, 8.5; a template not yet usable is marked in `--list`, for example "(available after T7.1)"): `monthly-topn`, `value-quality-composite`, `momentum-12-1`, `low-vol`, `dividend-yield`, `sector-neutral`, `kr-us-sleeves`, `long-short`, `inverse-vol-weight`, `erc-vol-target`, `drawdown-brake`, `exits-stop-trail`, `buffer-hold`, `custom-factor`, `custom-sizer`, `custom-risk-rule`, `factor-evaluate-only`, `walk-forward-cpcv`, `ml-factor`, `zipline-port`, `event-driven-earnings` (a Filter on `nx.Item.<name>.age()` after earnings announcements + `time_stop`), `macro-regime` (a market-wide item as a regime filter) [#752] |
| `nexusbt evaluate FILE[:VAR]` | `--segment --start --end --freq --quantiles --set k=v` |
| `nexusbt backtest FILE[:VAR]` | `--segment --start --end --set k=v --preset --no-scenarios --scenarios a,b --offsets K --preview --method linear|walk_forward|cpcv|cscv --method-opt k=v --grid k=v1,v2 --save-detail` [`--save-detail` = `save_detail=True`, 5.9.1, #206] |
| `nexusbt screen FILE[:VAR]` | `--asof --top --anchor --show selected|held+near|all --near K --html --ledger BOOK --as-backtest` |
| `nexusbt explain FILE[:VAR] ID DATE` | for a term, the value explanation; for a strategy, the decision path (5.9.9) |
| `nexusbt sweep FILE[:VAR]` | `--grid k=v1,v2` (= `backtest --method cscv --grid`) |
| `nexusbt compare FILE[:VAR]... | RUN_ID...` | variant table (backtests each file) |
| `nexusbt live FILE[:VAR] --book paper:NAME [--fill next_open]` | first fills pending orders (when the data is there), then today's decision. Updates `status.json` at the end |
| `nexusbt reconcile --book BOOK [--day D]` | 5.9.7. Exit code 4 on a mismatch; `--day` prints the order-day record (5.9.7 (5)) |
| `nexusbt book override BOOK ID KIND [--from D] [--until D] [--note T]` | manual instruction |
| `nexusbt book record-fills BOOK CSV` | records of fills a person made (`manual:` ledger) |
| `nexusbt book flow BOOK AMOUNT --ccy C --sleeve M [--session D]` | deposits and withdrawals [V31-19] |
| `nexusbt book acknowledge BOOK DIVERGENCE_ID --note T [--by NAME]` | acknowledges the divergence of a `reconciled: NO`, after which `live` emits sheets again (5.9.6 rule 3) |
| `nexusbt runs [--study S] [--html] [--prune] [--prune-detail]` | `--prune` deletes only run folders and keeps the index records and attempt counts [R-34]; `--prune-detail` deletes only the tier-3 `detail/` folder of each run and records it as pruned (5.9.5, #206) |
| `nexusbt rerun RUN_ID [--new-snapshot] [--trust] [--save-detail]` | a run folder made on another machine is refused without `--trust` (E31); `--save-detail` also regenerates and compares the tier-3 outputs (5.9.4, #206); exit code 4 when the comparison line starts `outputs differ`, or, when the environment differs, says the outputs differ beyond tolerance (#637), and 0 when it starts `outputs identical` or, when the environment differs, says the outputs are within tolerance; with `--new-snapshot`, 0 whatever the comparison says, as that is a new run (3.8) (#655) |
| `nexusbt stubs` | regenerates `.pyi` |
| `nexusbt check FILE[:VAR] [--probes K] [--start-probe]` | |

The data-standard tools are separate — `tds check PATH [--level manifest|schema|full] [--profile P] [--format json]`, `tds hash`, `tds describe`, `tds import csv MAPPING --out PKG`, `tds manifest build PKG` (TDS 8.1, package `nexusbt-tds`; the commands implemented in v1 and the "planned" commands are in the table of TDS 8.1) — and are not mixed into the engine CLI. `nexusbt doctor --full` calls the same checker as a library.

The default VAR of `FILE[:VAR]`: `strategy` for a strategy file; for a factor file, the last Factor defined (when there are several, `ConfigError` asks for the name).

**Help text** (design review D19, #219): the `--help` of `evaluate`, `backtest` and `sweep` describes each `--method` value and each diagnostic option by the user's question first and the tool's name second, in the terms of 3.9 and of the guide `docs/guide/choose-by-question.md` (English canonical, Korean translation; one entry per question, each with the tool that fits, why, what it needs and what it cannot show), for example `--method walk_forward   I will re-choose or retrain periodically: choose on each training window, test on the window after it`. A help line says what the option needs and what it cannot show; it never ranks options by results, never calls one safe, and never makes a research option look required (the plain `backtest FILE` stays complete). `nexusbt backtest --help` ends with the line `which method fits my question? docs/guide/choose-by-question.md`. Decided 2026-11-04 in #218: v1 has no command that prints a guide entry (the guide is the document and this help text), and the Run first screen gets one `next` line like the FactorReport's, which 3.9 gains through #219.

### 3.11 `nexusbt.toml` schema

**Two tiers** [user request 2026-10-28]: every value the user sets goes in this one file (strategy code holds only the strategy itself). **Part 1 "check these"** (values that change results a lot from person to person) = `[data] path`, `root`, `[defaults] capital`, `base_ccy`, `start`, `end`, `[sleeves] weights`, `[universe] exclude_microcap`, `exclude`, `[account] type`, `cash_rate_bps` (+ `initial_margin`, `maintenance_margin`, `debit_rate_bps` with `type = "margin"`), `[shorting] enabled` (+ `max_gross`, `borrow_bps`, `collateral_ratio` when on), `[costs] profile`, `sensitivity`, `provisional_bps`, `[dividends] withholding`, `[taxes] profile` (+ `currency`, `pay`, `pay_month`, `groups` with `custom`), `[execution] at`, `max_participation`, `max_bar_participation`, `min_trade_weight`, `[split] segments`, `default_segment`. **Part 2 "advanced"** = everything else (left empty, the defaults). The tiers are only a display; the validation rules are the same. `nexusbt init` writes a template commented in these two tiers (3.10), and the report header block "settings (tier 1)" prints every part 1 value actually used by the run with its source (`default`/`file`/`CLI`/`code`) (for part 2, only keys not at their default). Every key of the example file `nexusbt.toml.example` (working folder) is in this table (test `tests/docs/test_config_template.py`).

Validation: an unknown key → `ConfigError` (suggesting similar keys). A key whose name contains `key`, `secret`, `token` or `password` → `ConfigError` (2.6). Reserved keys ("reserved" below) accept only the one value allowed in v1; other values are E18. Dates are `"YYYY-MM-DD"` or `"latest"`/`"start"`. "Default" below is the value when the key is not in the file.

| Key | Type | Default | Validation | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `[project] name` | str | the folder name | `[A-Za-z0-9_-]+` | |
| `[research] family_id` | str | written by `nexusbt init` (a new UUID4) | a UUID4; changed only by `nexusbt init --new-family` (6.2) | user decision 2026-11-04, #196 |
| `[data] root` | path | (written by init; otherwise `$NEXUSBT_DATA_ROOT`) | the folder exists and has at least one snapshot folder with `LATEST` or `tds.json` | R-20, 2.7 |
| `[data] snapshot` | str | `"latest"` | `"latest"` or an existing id. `latest` is resolved once per call | 11.7 |
| `[data] path` | path | none | points directly at one package folder (where `tds.json` is). When present, it comes before `root` and `snapshot`. A parent folder is resolved through `LATEST` | |
| `[data] verify` | str | `"manifest"` | manifest / schema / full (TDS check levels, 4.4). `rerun`, `reconcile`, `live` and `fill` use at least `schema` + the file sha256 whatever this value (a package checked once gets the stamp `runs/.cache/verified/<physical_sha256>`; the stamp records `(dev, inode, size, mtime_ns)` per file, and when they differ the sha256 is computed again and the stamp discarded) | C9, TDS 8.2 |
| `[data] require_profiles` | list[str] | `[]` | TDS profile names (`@version` allowed). When empty, **the requirement is computed from the features the graph uses** (4.3); when a list is given, its profiles are required in addition | 4.3 |
| `[data] pins` | str | `"index+root"` | index (canonical pins = `runs/index.sqlite` only) / index+root (default: also written to the data root `PINS/`, with a warning + `retention: unmanaged` when that fails) | 4.2 |
| `[data] fallbacks` | str | `"warn"` | warn (uses the fallbacks of 4.5, with E30) / fail (E14 when a fallback is needed) | R-67 |
| `[data] history_mode` | str | `"economic"` | v1 economic only. `"system"` is `CapabilityError` | 11.5, OI-08 |
| `[defaults] universe` | str | `"all"` | snapshot `markets` keys joined by `+`, or `"all"` (every market of the snapshot that has a rules pack) | |
| `[defaults] base_ccy` | str | the currency of the first market (first of the `markets` keys in UTF-8 byte order) (`"KRW"` for a KR+US package) | ISO 4217; there must be an exchange-rate series from the other markets' currencies into this currency | Q1 |
| `[defaults] capital` | float | `1_000_000_000` | > 0, in base_ccy units | |
| `[defaults] start` | date | `"1995-01-01"` | | |
| `[defaults] end` | date | `"latest"` | ≥ start | |
| `[defaults] clock` | str | `"<first market>_close"` (first market = first of the `markets` keys in UTF-8 byte order; `"KR_close"` for a KR+US package; the resolved value is fixed in the manifest) | `"<MARKET>_close"` (MARKET a key of the snapshot's `markets`) / market_local (`utc:HH:MM` reserved, E18) | 9.0 Q23, Q1, SC-25 |
| `[defaults] preset` | str | `"realistic"` | realistic (zipline is for tests only, forbidden in toml) | R-55 |
| `[split] segments` | table name→[start,end] | `{train=["start","2021-12-31"], validation=["2022-01-01","latest"]}` | names `[a-z0-9_]+`, no overlapping intervals, in order | 9.0 Q26, R-57 |
| `[split] default_segment` | str | `"train"` | a name of segments, or `"all"` | R-57 |
| `[split] embargo_sessions` | int | 0 | ≥ 0; drops decisions **before** the end of a segment (5.8). Different from the splitters' `embargo=` (the AFML meaning: drops training samples **after** the test block) (6.3a) | 11.4.5 |
| `[validate] max_fits` | int | 200 | ≥ 1; when the number of ML fits of `method` exceeds it, the notice E40 before the run (does not block) | |
| `[validate] min_samples` | int | 100 | ≥ 1; E40 when a fold has fewer training samples (6.3a) | |
| `[ml] threads` | int | 1 | ≥ 1; the thread count fixed during training (5.3.11). When not 1, manifest `nondeterministic_threads` | |
| `[preview] years`, `max_names` | int | 1, 200 | ≥ 1 | |
| `[sleeves] weights` | {MARKET: float} | equal weights over the universe's markets ({KR = 0.5, US = 0.5} for KR+US) | keys are the snapshot's `markets` keys, sum 1 ± 1e-9, each ≥ 0. Markets not in the universe are ignored and the rest is rescaled to 1 | 11.4.1 |
| `[markets] allow_untested` | bool | false | when true, runs a market without a rules pack on the generic pack (`_generic.toml`: no price limits, `provisional_bps` must be given by `[costs.provisional_bps] <MARKET>`), with "untested market rules" in the report and manifest | |
| `[universe] exclude_microcap` | bool | false | | 9.0 Q31 |
| `[universe] microcap_pct` | float | 0.20 | (0, 0.5] | Q17 |
| `[universe] exclude` | list[str] | `[]` | the `exclude` names of 3.6 | |
| `[portfolio] anchor_date` | date | `"start"` (= `[defaults] start`) | the screen anchor of the target-state recursion (5.5.3) | SC-02 |
| `[portfolio] tiebreak` | str | `"hash"` | hash / id (5.4.2, 3.7; the zipline preset fixes id) | |
| `[portfolio] band_entries` | str | `"skip"` | skip / buy — whether to newly buy an instrument kept by the hold band but never bought (5.6.7) | |
| `[portfolio] max_missing_decisions` | int | 1 | ≥ 1; the cap on consecutive missing decisions for `on_missing = "hold"` | |
| `[shorting] enabled` | bool | false | when false, negative target weights are E16, and results are bit-identical to the long-only path (INV-22) | |
| `[shorting] max_gross` | float | 1.0 | (0, 3]; cap on gross exposure Σ|w| (within the sleeve). No leverage beyond this value the user sets | |
| `[shorting] max_net` | [float, float] | [−1.0, 1.0] | the closed interval of net exposure Σw; must contain 0 (otherwise E16) | |
| `[shorting] borrow_bps` | {MARKET: float} | 0 in every market | [0, 5000]; the annual borrow cost (bp) charged on the short value. Accrues at session start as the previous session's closing short quantity × the previous session's valuation price × calendar days/`day_count` (a pack key, KR 365 / US 360) (5.6.3(1)) | |
| `[shorting] collateral_ratio` | {MARKET: float} | 0.5 in every market | [0, 2]; short value × (1 + this value) is held as collateral (5.6.7) | |
| `[shorting] short_rebate_bps` | {MARKET: float} | 0 in every market | [0, 2000]; the annual rebate on the valued short position (the same accrual as the borrow cost). v3.1's name `proceeds_rate_bps` was changed because its meaning (proceeds basis) and its formula (valuation basis) differed | |
| `[account] cash_rate_bps` | float or {MARKET: float} | 0 | [0, 2000]; annual interest (bp) on positive cash balances. The same session-start accrual as the borrow cost (only on the previous session's closing `cash_minor > 0`; no interest on negative cash). With 0 and no `[account] cash_rate` series, results are bit-identical to before this key existed, and every table and the report header are bit-identical to before either key existed (#313, decision 2026-11-10). The fallback for a currency with no `[account] cash_rate` series (#313, decision 2026-11-10). Report header "cash earns {r}%" | user decision 2026-10-28 |
| `[account] cash_rate` | {CURRENCY: str} | none | a dated cash-rate series per currency, the value the name of a market-wide item (`market_values`, 5.3.5) [#725 Decisions 219, 251]. Its value is the annual rate in bp, the unit of `cash_rate_bps`. A market's currency is the one TDS `markets` gives it (R-68). A currency with no series uses `cash_rate_bps`. The accrual (5.6.3 (0)) and the risk-free rate `rf_t` of the Sharpe family (5.7) read this series. Report header "cash: series {name} ({first date}..{last date})" (3.9) (#313, decision 2026-11-10) | #313 |
| `[account] type` | `"cash"` \| `"margin"` | `"cash"` | `"cash"` = as now (fills never make cash negative, no long margin loan); `"margin"` = the margin account of 5.6.7a. Writing the margin-only keys below with `"cash"` is E16 ("margin settings need [account] type = \"margin\"") | user decision 2026-10-28 |
| `[account] initial_margin` | {MARKET: float} | rules pack seed (US 0.50, **unverified**); no KR seed | (0, 1]; the initial margin ratio. With `"margin"`, E16 when a run market has no value. For KR the user writes the credit deposit ratio | |
| `[account] maintenance_margin` | {MARKET: float} | rules pack seed (US 0.25, **unverified**); no KR seed | (0, `initial_margin`]; the maintenance margin ratio (for KR the user converts it from the collateral ratio, for example collateral ratio 140% → 1 − 1/1.4 ≈ 0.286). E16 when missing | |
| `[account] debit_rate_bps` | float or {MARKET: float} | 0 | [0, 5000]; annual interest (bp) on the margin loan (the long share of negative cash). The same session-start accrual as the borrow cost (calendar days/`day_count`), event `margin_interest`, bridge `financing` | |
| `[execution] order` | str | `"market"` | market / limit | R-56 |
| `[execution] at` | str | `"open"` | open / close | Q5 |
| `[execution] limit_offset_bps` | float | 0.0 | ≥ −500, ≤ 500 | |
| `[execution] max_participation` | float | 0.05 | (0, 1] | design 3.6 |
| `[execution] max_bar_participation` | float | 0.10 | (0, 1]; cap relative to that session's actual volume (5.6.6) | |
| `[execution] min_trade_value` | float or {MARKET: float} | 0 | ≥ 0, local currency | |
| `[execution] min_trade_weight` | float | 0.0 | [0, 0.1] | |
| `[execution] rebalance_band` | float | off (none) | (0, 1]; a band relative to the target weight | |
| `[execution] band_mode` | str | `"edge"` | edge / target | |
| `[execution] adv_window` | int | 20 | ≥ 5 | 11.4.2 |
| `[execution] adv_min_obs` | int | 10 | 1..adv_window | |
| `[execution] max_order_age_sessions` | int | 20 | ≥ 1 | 11.4.2 |
| `[execution] limit_fill` | str | `"through"` | through (the price must go past the limit) / touch (touching is enough) | 5.6.4 |
| `[execution] kr_limit_tolerance` | float | 0.005 | [0, 0.05] | 5.6.5 |
| `[execution] unknown_tradability` | str | `"assume_tradable"` | assume_tradable / block (both report counts) | 11.4.2 |
| `[execution] cost_reserve_bps` | float | 0.0 | [0, 500]. Shrinks the executable weight in decision sizing by `(1 − reserve)` to leave room for buy costs (5.6.7). Recorded in the manifest | |
| `[execution] intraday_proceeds` | str | `"next_phase"` | fixed (the one value allowed in v1): proceeds of intraday-stage sales are usable from the close stage (5.6.3) | |
| `[costs] profile` | str | `"provisional"` | provisional / realistic / none | 9.0, R-61 |
| `[costs] table` | str | `"default"` | default or a CSV path (schema of 4.7) | R-25 |
| `[costs] sensitivity` | list[float] | `[0, 1, 2]` | contains 1 | Q6, R-38 |
| `[costs.provisional_bps] <MARKET>` | {buy, sell} | the market rules pack value (KR pack {buy=5, sell=25}, US pack {buy=5, sell=5}) | ≥ 0. Required for a market without a pack (`allow_untested`) | 9.0, R-61 |
| `[costs] provisional_table` | path | none | a provisional cost table by period and liquidity band (written by the user, schema of 4.7). Fixed bp when absent | V31-27 |
| `[costs] impact_y` | float | 0.5 | ≥ 0 | research 2.4 |
| `[costs] impact_a2`, `impact_a3` | float | 0.5, 1.0 | (0, 2] | SC-11, Kissell |
| `[costs] min_half_spread_bps` | {MARKET: float} | the market rules pack value (KR 5, US 1) | ≥ 0 | |
| `[costs] max_half_spread_bps` | float | 200 | ≥ min | |
| `[dividends] withholding` | {MARKET: float} | 0.0 in every market | [0, 0.5]. Not applied to events with `corporate_actions.gross_or_net = "net"`. With 0, "dividends pre-withholding" in the report header | A-14 |
| `[corporate_actions] rights` | str | `"lapse"` | lapse / fail | A-15 |
| `[corporate_actions] unsupported` | str | `"fail"` | fail / cash_at_adjustment | 11.4.3 |
| `[corporate_actions] bonus_shares_tradable` | str | `"at_pay_date"` | at_ex / at_pay_date | 11.4.3 |
| `[corporate_actions] decimal_ratio` | str | `"exclude_instrument"` | exclude_instrument / require_exact / literal: what each does is 5.6.1's (default `exclude_instrument`, user decision 2026-10-29); literal is recorded in the manifest `policies.corporate_actions` | |
| `[delisting] unknown_inclusion` | str | `"conservative"` | conservative / error. When it is unknown whether delisting distributions are included (5.6.13): conservative = the reading that makes the sleeve value smaller + E30, error = E13 | |
| `[delisting] policy` | str | `"observed_then_policy"` | observed_then_policy / observed_only / lo / hi | Q18 |
| `[delisting] scenarios` | bool | true | | |
| `[settlement] model` | str | `"immediate"` | reserved: v1 immediate only | SC-18, OQ-13 |
| `[taxes] profile` | str | `"none"` | none / custom. With custom, the user writes the `[taxes.groups.<name>]` below (in the config file, like commissions; the engine has no built-in tax rates). 5.6.11a | SC-17, OI-18 |
| `[taxes] currency` | str | the base currency | the currency taxes are computed and shown in (for example `"KRW"`). Gains and losses in other currencies are converted at that day's valuation exchange rate (5.6.9) | OI-18 |
| `[taxes] pay` | str | `"report"` | report = NAV unchanged, after-tax NAV and tax lines reported separately / cash = deducted from cash on the payment date | OI-18 |
| `[taxes] pay_month` | int | 5 | 1~12. With `pay = "cash"`, paid on the first session of this month of the next year | OI-18 |
| `[taxes.groups.<name>] markets` | [str] | — | the markets in this group (for example `["US"]`). A market belongs to one group only | OI-18 |
| `[taxes.groups.<name>] gains_rate` | float | 0.0 | [0, 0.6]. Multiplied by each year's sum of realised gains and losses (losses offset) − the exemption, when > 0 | OI-18 |
| `[taxes.groups.<name>] annual_exemption` | int | 0 | ≥ 0, in integer minor units of `[taxes] currency` (for example 2,500,000 won) | OI-18 |
| `[taxes.groups.<name>] basis` | str | `"average"` | average (moving-average cost) / fifo | OI-18 |
| `[fx] max_stale_days_decision` | int | 10 | ≥ 0 | 11.5 |
| `[fx] max_stale_days_valuation` | int | 10 | ≥ 0 | |
| `[fx] series` | str or {"<BASE><QUOTE>": "<rate_kind>@<source>"} | `"auto"` | auto = that one when the currency pair has a single `(rate_kind, source)` series; with several, `rate_kind` priority fixing → close → mid; if still several, `ConfigError` shows the list and asks for a choice | TDS-D17 |
| `[benchmarks] <MARKET>` | str | `"auto"` | `"auto"` = that market's `asset_class = index`, `index_kind = TR` instrument when there is exactly one (with several, the one whose `name` equals the market rules pack's `benchmark_hint`; if still several, E16 with the list), or an instrument id, or any symbol of the package, of any `asset_class` (its latest owner, 5.3.6), or a return series the user gives, or `"none"`; an index's rows need no trading fields (4.6) [#725 Decisions 217]. When none, "n/a: not in snapshot" | 9.0 Q30, R-59, TDS-D15, OI-02 |
| `[evaluate] freq`, `quantiles`, `min_pairs`, `label_entry` | | monthly, 5, 20, `"next_open"` | quantiles 2..20, min_pairs ≥ 3, label_entry next_open / close | 11.4.5; `label_entry = "close"` in a graph that reads a value table is refused before the run (E18, 5.1.4) [#752] |
| `[evaluate] target` | str | `"fwd_return"` | fwd_return (economic: reinvested at the ex-date close) / fwd_return_adj (adjusted-price ratio, for comparison with zipline and alphalens) | |
| `[gates] ic_t`, `dsr` | float | 3.0, 0.95 | display only | Q7 |
| `[checks] auto_truncation_probe` | bool | true | | 6.5 |
| `[checks] probes`, `seed` | int | 4, 0 | probes 0..64 | |
| `[checks] start_probe` | bool | false | runs the start-date probe automatically in evaluate/backtest (off by default; an argument in `nx.check`) | SC-04 |
| `[checks] start_probe_shift` | int | 21 | 1..252 sessions | SC-04 |
| `[runs] dir` | path | `"runs"` | | |
| `[runs] cache_max_gb` | float | 20 | ≥ 0, L0 + L1 together. L0 is not deleted right away when a snapshot is deleted; the LRU of this limit deletes it (starting with what no remaining snapshot refers to) | risk 24, SC-15 |
| `[compute] workers` | int | 1 | 1..8 | 11.7 |
| `[compute] threads` | int | 4 | 1..16 | |
| `[compute] memory_budget_gb` | float | 4.0 | ≥ 0.5. One budget for **the whole process tree** (main process + workers); a batch can be smaller than a year (5.3.8) | P7 |
| `[compute] precision` | str | `"float64"` | v1 float64 only | 9.3 Q25 |
| `[live] book_dir`, `orders_dir` | path | `"runs/books"`, `"orders"` | | |
| `[live] max_price_jump` | {MARKET: float} | the rules pack's price limit (KR 0.30), otherwise 0.5 | (0, 5] | |
| `[live] spike_sigma` | float | 8.0 | > 0 | |
| `[live] max_order_weight_mult` | float | 2.0 | ≥ 1; flagged when one order's amount > sleeve value × maximum target weight × this value | |
| `[live] liquidity_floor_pct` | float | 0.10 | [0, 0.5]; flagged when it picks instruments in this bottom share of universe ADV | |
| `[rounding.<MARKET>] <component>` | {mode, unit} | the market rules pack's `[rounding]` | mode half_up / half_even / down, unit = a multiple of the minor unit. User override (like commissions and taxes) | |
| `[report] rolling_windows_years` | list[int] | `[1, 3]` | 1..10 | |

---

## 4. Input data: TDS v1.4 references and the engine-side reading rules

### 4.1 The canonical source and the scope of this chapter [SC-24, R-66]

- **The canonical source of the input format is TDS v1.4** (`engine-data-standard-v1.4.md`; changes from v1.1 are in TDS chapter 16 [V31], from v1.2 in chapter 17 [R3], from v1.3 in chapter 18 [R4F]; in the repository `docs/tds/tds-v1.4.md`, 9.1). Column names, types, nulls, keys, sort order, partitions, availability rules, hashes and check codes are set by TDS, and this spec does not write them again. When this spec and TDS describe the input format differently, TDS is right (the differences from chapter 4 of the v1 spec, TDS-D1~D24, are already reflected throughout this v2; the mapping is appendix B.3).
- This chapter sets only the **engine side**: the capabilities the engine requires (4.3), the checks at open (4.4), the fallbacks when columns are missing (4.5), the reading rules per dataset (4.6), the engine resource files and market rules packs (4.7), and the synthetic generator (4.8).
- Version rules: the engine opens only packages with `tds_version` MAJOR = 1 (otherwise E14 + `TDS-M002`). A higher MINOR of the same MAJOR is opened, but split by **whether it uses features the engine does not know** [TDS 10.3]: unknown columns and **optional** datasets are ignored with a `TDS-M011` warning; unknown values of **descriptive** open enums (`category`, `delist_type`, `reason` and the like) are read as `other`, `asset_class` aside (4.6); unknown values of **closed enums that affect the run** (`kind`, `obs_status`, `exit_value_status`, `status`) and unknown **required resolution rules** (a new `availability` rule name and the like) are `TDS-M014` → E14 ("this engine does not support …"). It does not promise to accept every future 1.x package unconditionally.
- TDS holds no schema for ticks, quotes, futures, options or crypto, nor for the other kinds its drafts once named: such kinds may be added later, equities first (chapter 11) [#725 Decisions 252, 253].
- The engine does not open DuckDB files [R-20, design 1 principle 4]. The engine reads only the files declared in `tds.json` [INV-17].

### 4.2 Location and distribution conventions (outside TDS, producer conventions) [TDS-D22]

- **Linked by path only**: the engine's data source is **one TDS package path** — `nx.open(path)`, `[data] path`, or the root below + `LATEST`. The engine has no reading code specific to a particular producer (DB connections, producer schemas, export steps). When a producer writes the package directly from its build (TDS 4.1 "producer-native package") and reuses unchanged partitions by cloning (APFS `clonefile`, reflink; preferred) or hard links (fallback), the engine side is the same: it only reads the package (it requires no write permission; caches and stamps are on the `runs/` side, pins in the index and the root `PINS/`), and it judges content by the file sha256. When the file sha256 is the same, the L0 cache hits as it is (5.3.9). **File identity** [ADR-0037]: for every file whose sha256 it has checked, the engine writes `(dev, inode, size, mtime_ns)` in the stamp (`runs/.cache/verified/`) and the L0 entry, and on the next open checks with `stat` that it is the same. When it differs (edited in place, a replaced file), it computes the sha256 again and discards that stamp and L0 entry. The identity check is a defence against accidental edits, not a cryptographic proof (`[data] verify = "full"` reads every byte).
- The engine takes the data root as `[data] root` (or `$NEXUSBT_DATA_ROOT`). `<snapshot_id>/tds.json` under the root is one snapshot. `snapshot_id` = the run id of the published build (for example `20261027-143144`) [Q3].
- `LATEST`: a one-line text file whose content = the latest `snapshot_id`. The writer replaces it atomically, temporary file → `rename` [11.9 Q-E3]. `[data] snapshot = "latest"` is resolved once per call.
- **Pins**: the canonical record is the `pins` table of the engine index `runs/index.sqlite` (5.9.5). With `[data] pins = "index+root"` (**default** — with v3.1's default `index`, the producer's retention rules could not see the engine index, so `rerun` and ledger `reconcile` of a run two days old became E15), it also writes an empty file `PINS/<snapshot_id>/<run_or_book_id>` under the data root. A write failure (a read-only root, another producer) is left only as a warning, and the run manifest and the ledger `status.json` record `retention: unmanaged` (it does not claim the snapshot is protected). A ledger pin is not released while the ledger exists. `nexusbt doctor` shows the number of runs and ledgers whose root pin could not be written on a "pins not visible to producer" line. Whether the producer's clean-up respects the root `PINS/` is a producer convention. When the disk is short, the engine caches (L0, L1) are deleted first.
- This convention is a promise on the producer side; another producer may give just one snapshot folder without `LATEST` or `PINS` (then `[data] snapshot` is the folder name). The engine runs even when the data root is read-only.
- The snapshot's hash set = the package `physical_sha256`, `value_sha256` and `semantic_sha256` of TDS 4.5 (a dataset value hash includes the partition columns as path values, TDS-D19). The `snapshot_semantic_hash` of the run manifest and `DecisionContext` is the **semantic hash** (cache keys are set more finely, per partition, 5.3.9): even with the same Parquet, a change in availability rules, fixed-point exponents, policies or market declarations makes it different data.

### 4.3 Capability contract: profiles, check levels, engine support [TDS chapter 7]

A profile is a **conformance claim**, and what the engine does is set by **capability**. The engine requires no fixed list of profiles (v2's requirement of four default profiles is abolished). At open (4.4) it gives three results per package: (a) **declared** — in `tds.json profiles` (with `@version`), (b) **checked** — up to which level (`manifest`/`schema`/`full`) the rules of that profile were confirmed; rules that level cannot see are `not_checked` and are not written as passing, (c) **supported** — whether this engine version implements the feature. Then it computes the datasets and columns needed from **the features the graph uses** ("feature → needs" in the table below), and when something is missing it applies a fallback (4.5) or raises E18. Factor screening that uses only `Price` needs only `instruments` + `bars` (+ `calendar_sessions` and `corporate_actions` when present). Names written in `[data] require_profiles` are required in addition. A feature's dataset is chosen by its `kind`, and for `bars` by `params.freq = 1d` and `params.bar_time = session` as well, never by its place in `datasets[]`: TDS 3.1 lets a package hold several datasets of one kind. A `bars` dataset always declares `bar_time`, and one without it is TDS-M003, E14 at open (TDS 5.5; #705 C5): the engine reads no value in its place, where it once read `session` (#551). A `bars` dataset with `bar_time = utc_day` (TDS 9.4's mapping of `ohlcv-1d`) or `interval` is not a candidate, so a package whose only daily bars are `utc_day` has no daily bars. When no dataset is left, the dataset is missing (the table below). When two or more are left, the engine cannot choose between them: the features that read that dataset are off, and requesting such a feature, or reading that dataset, is E18. The error names every dataset left and gives the fix, keeping only one of them in the package (decisions 2026-11-18, #547 and #551, under the M1 freeze's defect rule: the result depended on the order of `datasets[]`; a feature that reads a dataset it does not need, as `grid` reads `market_rules`, took a fallback when there were two; and a `utc_day` dataset would be read as session bars).

| Feature (graph, call) | Datasets needed | When missing or short |
|---|---|---|
| `Price.*`, `run_pipeline`, `screen(factor)`, `evaluate` (`fwd_return_adj`) | `instruments`, `bars(1d)` | E14 if `bars` has no close; E18 for a term reading a `volume` or `value_traded` it holds no value of (4.4 step 3) |
| raw prices + adjustment (`close`, `close_tr` exact), `evaluate` (`fwd_return`) | the above + `bars.price_basis = raw` + `corporate_actions` (empty table allowed) + `calendar_sessions` | an adjusted-price package (`tds:minimal-daily`): `Price.close` = that value, `close_tr` and `fwd_return` → E18 ("economic labels need raw prices and corporate actions"); a Filter or an output that reads an adjusted column: below |
| `backtest`, strategy `screen`, `live` | the above + `delistings` (empty table allowed), `bars` OHLCV, `obs_status` (from a `status` column or the trading-status declaration, below), `density = dense`, dividend cash dates (`pay_date` or the declared rule, 5.6.11) | 4.5 F-13 (F-03 retired, #705); E18 for `minimal-daily`; E18 naming the declaration when a dividend row has no `pay_date` and no rule is declared (5.6.11) |
| tradability rules | `tradability_daily` | when missing, blocks by `obs_status` only, with E30 |
| `nx.Item.<name>` | the value table holding that item (5.3.5) | E18 [#725 Decisions 219] |
| multi-market sleeves, `.to(ccy)` | `fx_rates` | E18 |
| `weight="value"`, `exclude_microcap` | a share-count item (5.3.5) | E18 [#725 Decisions 225] |
| `Trad.*`, `universe(exclude=[admin values])` | `tradability_daily` (`admin_status` and so on) | E18 |
| `Market.series(id)` | `instruments` (that id) + `bars` | E18 [V31-13] |
| price grid (limit, stop, exit, price limits) | `instruments.tick_size`/`tick_rule_id` → `market_rules` (TDS v1.2 5.27, optional) → rules pack `[grid]` (the `classifications` step left, #752; the US per-instrument assignment from 2027-11-01 has no source yet, #802) | F-15 |
| exact split ratios | `corporate_actions.ratio_num/ratio_den` (TDS v1.4, required for events that change the share count) | when missing, invalid or disagreeing with its f64 column: 5.6.1 (checked at open, 4.4-4; check A010, not E14) |

**Trading status** [#725 Decisions 212, 213; #752]: a user whose price file has no trading status declares once, in the bars dataset's `params`, "volume 0 = no trade"; the declaration enters the semantic hash. The tool then writes `obs_status` `traded` or `no_trade`. A `no_trade` row's `close` is the reference price, and the tool empties its `open`, `high` and `low` and counts the rows it emptied; a volume-0 row whose `open`, `high` or `low` differs from its `close` is not emptied, and the tool asks about it. The check that judges a `no_trade` row's emptied cells is TDS-V004 (added to TDS with #752). An optional `status` column in the price file comes before the declaration. Without either, `obs_status` is not written and `backtest` stays off (the row above).

**Adjusted-only packages** [#725 Decisions 218; #752]: a package whose `bars.price_basis` is not `raw` (total- or split-adjusted, `split_adjusted` included) serves analysis only, as the rows above say. Its adjusted columns (`open`, `high`, `low`, `close`, `close_tr`, `volume`) carry a scale of each instrument's own, the per-instrument scalar of 3.4's adjustment classes, so their level is not the price traded that day and cannot be compared across instruments or with a fixed number. This rule applies only to a Filter or an output column (`run_pipeline`, `screen`) that reads an adjusted column on such a package (the owner, 2026-11-25, #752). When the graph is built, a comparison `x op y` passes only when both sides have a degree (3.4) known as a number and equal in each factor, the price factor and the quantity factor counted apart, since a positive scalar then moves both alike; "a degree that is not 0" with no number, and no degree, equal nothing, not even each other; `&`, `|` and `~` pass when each part does; any other Filter (`top`, `bottom`, `percentile_between`) and an output column pass when their term has degree 0. A non-zero degree, or no degree, is E18, whose message names the term and why, and gives three fixes: write it as a ratio or a return, declare `adjustment="degree 0"` on a user function, or read an unadjusted column (`value_traded`, `close_unadjusted`). Refused: `Price.close.latest > 5000` (degree 1 against 0); a Filter on the close's rank, `Price.close.latest.rank() <= 10` (a cross-sectional result over a degree-1 input has no degree); `Price.close.latest.log() - Price.open.latest.log() > 0` (no degree); `Price.close.latest * Price.volume.latest` as an output (no degree); `Price.close.latest.log() > Price.open.latest + 5` (a degree that is not 0, with no number, on both sides); `Price.close.latest > Price.volume.latest` (degree 1 in the price factor against degree 1 in the quantity factor); `Price.close.latest.log() > Price.open.latest.log()` (no number on either side). Passed: `Price.close.latest > SMA(20)` and `Price.close.latest > SMA(20) * 1.05` (degree 1 in the price factor on both sides); `Price.close.latest > Price.open.latest`, the log comparison rewritten (the same); the log difference rewritten as the ratio `Price.close.latest / Price.open.latest > 1` (degree 0 on both sides). A term reading no adjusted column (`nx.Item`, the unadjusted columns, `value_traded`) is not affected, and 3.4's "A term these rules give no degree is not refused" stays the rule of E17 on every package. When #701 gives a term built by `nx.ops.rolling` a degree, `nx.ops.rolling(Returns(5), 5).mean()` passes here without reopening this decision.

The table below maps profiles to the features above (for reference).

| Profile | Datasets the engine reads | Engine features it turns on | When missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tds:minimal-daily@1.0` | `instruments`, `calendar_sessions` (every daily profile, 2026-11-23, #705), `bars(1d)` (adjusted prices allowed, `price_basis` declared; `close` only, of `equity` and `etf` too, TDS 7) | analysis paths only: `Price.close`, `run_pipeline`, `screen(factor)`, `evaluate(target="fwd_return_adj")` | backtest and economic labels E18 |
| `tds:equity-daily-core@1.0` | `instruments`, `calendar_sessions`, `bars` (freq=1d, raw, dense, `obs_status`; OHLCV for `equity` and `etf`, `close` for `index` and `rate`, TDS 7), `corporate_actions`, `delistings` | `Price.*`, OHLC fill rules, split, dividend and delisting accounting, `backtest`, `screen` | the fallback F-13 of 4.5 (F-02 and F-03 retired: no `calendar_sessions` is E14, 4.4 step 4, and daily bars are dense, TDS 5.5); E14 if `bars` hold no value of close or of a column it requires (4.4 step 3) |

The `research`, `panels`, `pit-fundamentals` and `bitemporal` profiles were removed 2026-11-24 (#752) with the kinds they named; `tradability_daily`, `instrument_symbols`, `fx_rates` and the value tables are read by the features above [#725 Decisions 250 to 254]. TDS holds no intraday, tick, futures, options or crypto profile (4.1).

### 4.4 When a snapshot is opened [TDS chapter 8, TDS-D21]

1. Calls `tds.check(path, level=…)` as a library. The level is `[data] verify`: `manifest` (default; `tds.json`, path safety (M012, M013), file existence, bytes, presence of the profile datasets), `schema` (+ Parquet footers, statistics, partition paths), `full` (+ every row: keys, sort order, references, time, density, factors ↔ events, values, hashes). `rerun`, `reconcile`, `live` and `fill` use at least `schema` + the file sha256 (skipped when there is a stamp and the file identity `(dev, inode, size, mtime_ns)` is the same, computed again when it differs). `nexusbt doctor --full` and `verify="full"` use `full`. Measurement (P1) is based on `manifest`.
2. If there is even one error (severity E), `SnapshotError` (E14): the first error's TDS code, dataset, file, key and hint, the count per code, and, when the optional block `producer.hints` of `tds.json` has a hint for that code, that sentence (TDS 4.2) — the engine does not know a particular producer's ticket numbers. Warnings (W) are left in the run warnings as counts per code. Check A010 (exact ratios, TDS 5.11) is the exception and does not raise E14 here: a missing exact ratio, or one that disagrees with its f64 column (step 4), follows `[corporate_actions] decimal_ratio` (5.6.1), and an invalid one is E13 in step 4, so `verify = "full"` does not stop the whole snapshot.
3. Capability decision (4.3): three results per profile, the data needed by the features the graph uses. A package that **breaks** a profile it declared (a check error; A010 follows step 2) is not repaired by fallbacks: E14. When a feature is missing in a valid lower-profile package, a feature with a 4.5 fallback is substituted with E30 when `[data] fallbacks = "warn"`, and is E14 when `"fail"`. A feature without a fallback is E18 when that feature is called. A bars file without a `close` column is E14 here, whatever markets the run reads, before step 4's market checks (above `manifest`, step 2's E14 comes first, the checker's codes for it, TDS-M008 or TDS-S011, among its counts): no fallback stands in for `close`, and the equity daily profiles require it (TDS 7). Bars are E14 here too when they hold no value of `close`, or of a column a declared profile requires of them (TDS 7: under `tds:equity-daily-core@1.0` and above, `obs_status` of its bars, and `open`, `high`, `low` and `volume` of bars naming an `equity` or `etf` instrument), read as TDS-M008 reads it: in no file, or null in every row of the dataset; the engine judges it from the files' footer statistics at every `verify` level, and reads that one column of a file without them (2026-11-22, #705 Decisions 48 and 50, in place of #698's "a `close` null in every row is data"). A column read so that no declared profile requires (`open`, `high`, `low` or `volume` of a `tds:minimal-daily@1.0` package, or of bars of indices alone, and `value_traded`, which no profile requires; `volume` and `value_traded` since 2026-11-23) does not stop the open; a graph that reads it is E18 once it is built, before any value is computed, naming the column and the profile whose package holds it (2026-11-22, #705 Decisions 48 (c)). A bars file without a column another file of the dataset holds is E14 here, whatever the profile (TDS 3.6, TDS-S011); `open`, `high`, `low`, `volume` or `value_traded` null in every row of all of one market's `market=` files, while the dataset holds it, is E18 when a run over that market reads it; some files of a market (a year) null in every row are data, as a halted session's null is (2026-11-22, #705). Repairing an old format is not the engine's job but the import step's (`tds import`, TDS chapter 9), which makes a new package.
4. Additional engine-side checks (not in the checker; always at engine open): the markets the run needs are in `markets` and **their market rules pack** (4.7) exists — otherwise E18 ("no market rules pack for {m}; supported: {list}; set [markets] allow_untested = true to run with generic rules (recorded)"); every market of the package has its `calendar_id` in `calendar_sessions`, whatever markets the run reads and at every `verify` level, otherwise E14 naming the market and saying to add the sessions or let `tds import csv` build them: every run reads the sessions (2026-11-23, #705 Decisions 59; the engine reads the `calendar_id` column of the calendar's files once); two `calendar_sessions` datasets are 4.3's rule instead (#547): the package opens, what needs the calendar is off, and reading it is E18 naming both; the types of the columns the engine reads (`contracts.ENGINE_COLUMNS`) are the same as the TDS types. **Data quality**: using a dataset whose `pit_quality` is `backfilled`/`mixed` is E30 (naming the dataset) [TDS 6.5]; the row-level quality and the strict edition it served left with `classifications` and the editions (2026-11-26 and 2026-11-27, #752). **Invalid exact ratios**: if the `corporate_actions` of a run market have rows where only one of `ratio_num/ratio_den` is present or either is 0 or below, `AccountingDataError` (E13; the list of event ids) before the run — in TDS v1.4 A010 is an error (E) (5.11), and the engine blocks the same rows with E13 so that it does not compute quantities from a package whose canonical values are invalid. **Disagreeing exact ratios** [#188, decision 2026-11-04]: a row whose `ratio_num/ratio_den` are both present and positive but differ from the f64 column (`share_factor`, or `ratio_new_per_old` for mergers and spin-offs) by more than 1e-12 relative — A010's own threshold, applied here at open — is not E13 by itself: the producer's canonical value is in doubt, so the event is handled as one without an exact ratio and `[corporate_actions] decimal_ratio` decides (5.6.1 "events without an exact ratio"). The engine never uses a disagreeing exact ratio. **Undeclared instrument market** [#567, decision 2026-11-19]: where the engine takes the bars' market from `instruments` (bars without a `market=` partition), a market that `markets` does not declare is E14 with `TDS-M010` at every `verify` level, naming the market and an instrument, since the checker reads that column only from the `schema` level on (TDS 8.2); any other undeclared `instruments.market` is the checker's M010 from `schema` on. When both apply, the E18 of a run market without a rules pack comes first, and this E14 only when no such E18 stops the open (2026-11-22, #705; #580).
5. As a result it builds `Snapshot.profiles` (the three results), `Snapshot.capabilities` (the set of features turned on) and `Snapshot.fallbacks` (the list of fallbacks used: id, dataset, market, count, and `column`, null since no fallback is one column's: F-01 and F-18 are retired and the field stays, 2026-11-23, #705; #604, #693). They are kept as they are in the run manifest's `data.profiles`, `data.capabilities`, `data.unchecked`, `data.fallbacks` and `assumptions`.
6. When a run starts, (snapshot_id, run_id) goes into the index `pins` (with `[data] pins = "index+root"` also the root `PINS/`; a failure is a warning) [11.7 (9)].

### 4.5 Fallbacks [R-67]

The table below is the defined thing the engine does **when a valid package does not fill some column or dataset** (whatever the producer). Every fallback, without exception, is an E30 warning + a manifest record, not a hidden fallback (INV-12). F-01, F-02, F-03, F-04 and F-18 are retired and their numbers not reused (2026-11-22 and 2026-11-23, #705): daily bars are dense (TDS 5.5; a daily `density = sparse` is TDS-M014, E14 at open) and a row whose `obs_status` is null or absent is read as stored (3.2), where F-03 expanded sparse bars and made rows `traded` or `no_trade`; a market without sessions in `calendar_sessions` is E14 at open (4.4 step 4; F-02 built them with `exchange_calendars`); `open`, `high`, `low`, `volume` and `value_traded` without a value are E14 at open where a declared profile requires them, E18 where a term reads them, and E14 where a bars file lacks one another file holds (4.4 step 3); the backtest's participation cap (5.6.6), which reads `volume` without a term, never meets such bars, since a `minimal-daily` package cannot backtest (E18, 4.3) and core requires `volume` (E14); adjustment from the events is a definition, not a fallback (5.3.3), and the engine reads no `cum_factor_*`. Where a row ends in E18, that E18 is a refusal, not a fallback: the feature is not substituted. Each fallback is tested with the synthetic generator's `degrade=` option (4.8). Engine error and warning sentences mention only TDS codes and `producer.hints`. Engine M0~M6 can be finished with synthetic packages (4.8).

| id | What is missing (a gap in TDS) | Engine behaviour | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| F-05 | the `ex_date` of a Korean cash dividend is really the record date | the engine uses `ex_date` as it is (no repair). It counts the KR cash dividends with `ex_date = record_date` and no `x_*_ex_date_method` column and leaves the count in E30 ("dividend ex_date may equal record date for {n} KR events; entitlement may be one session late" + the `fallback:F-05` sentence of `producer.hints` when there is one) and in the manifest, which keeps the count and the key `fallback:F-05`, never the sentence (5.9.3) | `degrade=["kr_ex_is_record"]` |
| F-06, F-07, F-08 | retired 2026-11-24 (#752) with `fundamentals_state`, `eligibility_daily` and `share_counts`; their numbers are not reused [#725 Decisions 251] | — | — |
| F-09 | US dividend amounts are split-adjusted values and the dates (record date, declaration date, pay date) are missing | no pay date → the user's pay-date declaration of 5.6.11 (R-60; the estimation path was removed 2026-11-24, #752) [#725 Decisions 214, 215]. The engine cannot know the amount scale, so it counts per market the `TDS-A004` warnings (cash factor ≠ `1 − D/ref_price`) of the `full` check, and when the count is above 0 writes "US dividend amounts unverified (A004 n)" in the manifest `assumptions` and one warning line in the report. No repair | `degrade=["us_div_split_adjusted_no_dates"]` |
| F-10 | both `corporate_actions.ts_terms_available` and `ts_announced` null | as the TDS rule says, the terms are taken as known at `ts_effective` (no pending handling). E30 + a manifest record. A dataset with such events must have `pit_quality` `backfilled`/`mixed`, and a run that uses it gives E30 (4.4-4) | unit test |
| F-11 | retired 2026-11-24 (#752) with `fundamentals_events`; its number is not reused [#725 Decisions 251] | — | — |
