| F-12 | no benchmark index instruments | "n/a: not in snapshot" and E30; the engine does not build an index (R-59) | T4.6 |
| F-14 | no point-in-time table for changeable instrument attributes (`category`, `venue_mic`) | `Meta.category` and `Meta.venue_mic` use the current values of `instruments`, and when they reach a decision input, E30 "current value used for history" (count) | `tests/unit/pipeline/test_meta.py::test_hindsight_attribute_warned` |
| F-15 | no usable source of 4.3's grid chain for any instrument of a market (5.6.4a): no `instruments.tick_size`, no `instruments.tick_rule_id` that is a `market_rules` `tick_band` rule set, no `tick_band` rows of the market, no `tick_table` or `base_unit` in its pack (2026-11-22, #705; #448, #453) | grid = the currency minor unit (`minor_unit`; KRW 1 won, USD 0.01). E30 "price grid = currency minor unit for {market}" + a manifest record | `degrade=["no_tick_info"]` |
| F-16 | suspected volume unit (TDS-V011 warning, on the daily bars the engine reads, 4.3's dataset choice: the per-instrument, per-year median of value traded / (volume × representative price) outside [0.5, 2]; 2026-11-22, #705; #547, #584) | no repair. Raises the warning to E30 (counts per instrument and year), and marks "volume unit unverified" on the ADV cap, impact cost and capacity lines of that instrument and year. When the bars hold no value of `value_traded`, doctor prints one line "volume unit unverified" [2026-11-23, #705] | `degrade=["volume_in_lots"]` |
| F-13 | an instrument left the market but there is no delisting event row | when `instruments.delisted_on` (or `last_session` before the watermark) is present and there is no `delistings` row, it builds in memory an event with `exit_value_status = unobserved`, `ret_observed = ret_policy = null`, `ret_lo`, `ret_hi` and `policy_id` null, `ref_price` = the last valid raw close and `ref_date` its bar's date, `exit_date` = `delisted_on` (when absent, the session after `last_session`, or `last_session` itself when the calendar holds no later session; TDS 5.13, #747): the row TDS 5.13 describes for an exit whose consideration is not known, read as a package's such row is, its range the engine's assumption (5.6.13). Under the policy `observed_then_policy`, a holding has `ret_policy` null → E13 (5.6.13; fix: `delisting="lo"|"hi"|"observed_only"`) | `degrade=["no_delistings"]` |
| F-17 | no `coverage.by_market.<m>.processed_through_session` (TDS 4.3) for a dataset the strategy reads that has no row per session: an event or interval dataset, `instruments` and the like (a package written before TDS v1.4's 2026-11-12 addition) | `live`'s newest-data check (5.9.6 rule 4) takes the market's `complete_through_session` as that dataset's processed-through session, as rule 4 did before the field (a per-session dataset takes its `last`, a TDS 4.3 definition with no warning). One E30 line per `live` run, naming each such dataset with its market: "no processed_through_session for {dataset} in {market}, …; the market watermark stands for them", + a manifest record [decisions 2026-11-04 (the value) and 2026-11-12 (this row), #192] | T5.12 `test_newest_data_without_processed_through_E30` |

### 4.6 Engine reading rules per dataset

Common:
- **Identifiers**: the key is `instrument_id`. The engine builds an integer index in memory (0..N−1 in `instrument_id` sort order; session numbers in `calendar_sessions` sort order). It does not expect integer keys in the files [A-05]. When `session_ord` is present, whether it equals the engine's numbering is checked only in `full`.
- **Partitions**: partition columns (`market=`, `year=`, `freq=`, `item=`, `name=`) are read from the path (they are not in the files) [TDS-D5, TDS-D20]. The files to read are chosen only from the manifest's `files[].stats` (minimum and maximum of the main columns) and the path values, without opening Parquet footers (P1). The statistics of a `date` column are read as dates (decision 2026-11-16, #480: TDS 4.2 fixes no form for `stats`): `YYYY-MM-DD` and ISO 8601 date-time text give their date (a date-time with a UTC offset widened by a day on its side, since the market's date may be the next or the previous), and a value that is no date leaves the file to be read; the statistics only ever leave a file unread, so no form of them skips a file that holds rows of the block.
- **Availability time**: when a dataset's `availability` is `{"column": "ts_available"}`, the column is used; when it is a rule (TDS 6.2: `ts_end`, `session_open`, `session_close`, `next_session_open(<col>)`, `ts_end+<duration>`), the engine builds the column by the same rule and uses it. After that every lookup uses the one rule of 5.1.2 (`ts_available <= decision_ts`; market-axis rows by `ready_m(s) = ts_close(s) + Δ_m`) [INV-01].
- **Enums**: unknown values of descriptive open enums are `other` (the engine handles `delist_type` and `reason` through the general path). Unknown values of closed enums that affect the run (`kind`, `obs_status`, `exit_value_status`, `status`) are E14 (4.1). `asset_class` is open (TDS 5.1, #705 B3): an instrument of a class the engine has no rules for, a class TDS does not list included, is in no equity universe and its rows are not read into the market block; a run that leaves such instruments out says so, by class and count (the reporting is built in #745). The tools write `asset_class` from a class the user declares once for the package's instruments; mixing equities with ETFs or indices takes an instrument list (`symbol, asset_class`), whose class comes before the declared one, which stays the default for an instrument not on the list [#725 Decisions 216].
- **Edition**: the engine reads no edition. The kinds that had one (`fundamentals_state`, `feature_panels`, `classifications`) were removed 2026-11-24 (#752), and the run setting `[data] edition` with them on 2026-11-27 (#752). TDS's `editions` field and its place in the semantic hash left with #752's 12e (2026-11-27, TDS 4.2, 4.5), and M010 no longer judges an `edition=` path.
- **Market watermark**: sessions after `markets.<m>.complete_through_session` and `complete_through` are not used for fills in `live` and `fill` (5.9.6).

Per dataset (only the columns the engine reads; column definitions are in TDS chapter 5):

| TDS dataset | Columns the engine reads | Engine-side rules |
|---|---|---|
| `instruments` | `instrument_id, asset_class, category, market, venue_mic, calendar_id, currency, issuer_id, name, listed_on, listed_on_censored, last_session, delisted_on, lot_size, multiplier, is_synthetic, index_kind` | **an after-the-fact static table** (TDS 5.1): deciding listing existence (`listed_on`, `last_session` and `delisted_on` only for the listing-period mask and delisting handling), name display, units and multipliers. `delisted_on`, `last_session` and `name` are not exposed as decision inputs, and `category` and `venue_mic` follow the rules of 3.2 and F-14. v1 uses only `asset_class = equity` (+ `index` for benchmarks). Market block = `equity ∧ is_synthetic = false` (3.3). `is_synthetic = true` is not used for fills. When `listed_on_censored = true`, the window warm-up is taken as cut off at the start of the data and counted in the coverage table |
| `instrument_symbols` | `instrument_id, symbol_type, symbol, venue_mic, valid_from, valid_to, ts_known_from, ts_known_to` | display names and ticker history (the `name` of `Screen` and `Orders`; rectangular lookup 5.3.6). The only place of symbol periods, one period at least for every instrument. A symbol written with no date (in code, a setting or a fixed-list universe) means its latest owner; a dated row reads the symbol in use on its date, as a result's `symbol` does (5.3.6) [#725 Decisions 208, 209] |
| `calendar_sessions` | `calendar_id, session_date, ts_open, ts_close, is_half_day` | the only source of times (5.1.1). Price limits and rule versions are not here (TDS-D8) |
| `bars` (freq=1d) | `instrument_id, session_date, open, high, low, close, volume, value_traded, obs_status` (+ the availability rule, always declared, TDS-M003 without one, the usual one `ts_end` = `ts_close`; for a column rule `params.ready_delay`) | before price comparison, bar prices are turned into integers of the market minimum unit (5.6.4a; US `0.6768000000000001` → 6768 × 0.0001); a value that does not fit within 1e-9 is not moved and is counted as `off_grid_bar`. Volume units: F-16. With `price_basis = raw`, every feature. With adjusted prices (`minimal-daily`), analysis paths only (4.3): `Price.close` and `open/high/low` = those values, adjusted `close_tr` and `volume` = the same values (no adjustment), economic labels and backtest E18. A daily bar, like `KR_close`, means the regular-session close; after-market trades are not included (1.2). For KR this close is the KRX regular-session close: trades on NXT (Nextrade, the alternative trading system) are not included in the close, the OHLC, the volume or `value_traded`, even those made during regular-session hours (decision 2026-11-01). The time a row freezes is `ready(s)` of 5.1.2. `shares_out` is not in bars (TDS-D9) |
| `tradability_daily` | `instrument_id, session_date, status_known, halted_full_day, limit_up_close, limit_down_close, locked_up, locked_down, price_limit_ratio, up_limit_price, down_limit_price, reference_price, admin_status` | 5.6.5. The availability times per column group (`params.availability_by_column`, required for this table from TDS v1.1) matter only when used as decision inputs; when exposed as Pipeline terms, the values known at the end of the day (`locked_*`, `halted_full_day`, `limit_*_close`) are `session_close` (the state machine uses the same session's values when executing in that session — the prices and status of the execution session are facts that happened in that session) |
| `corporate_actions` | every column (TDS 5.11) | **only the resolver (5.3.3a) reads it**: it resolves amendments, cancellations, `status`, the existence time and the terms availability time at each reference time, and adjustment (5.3.3, 5.3.4) and accounting (5.6.10~5.6.12) use the result. **Kind mapping**: the table below |
| `delistings` | every column (TDS 5.13; including v1.2 `ret_observed_includes_distributions`) | 5.6.13 (the distribution just before delisting and the terminal value once only; three bases: observed-inclusive, observed-exclusive, policy). Recoveries are not put into decision sizing before `ts_value_available` (an entitlement of unknown amount, INV-01). For labels, an input of `ts_label_available` (5.8) |
| `market_rules` (TDS v1.2 5.27, optional) | `rule_id, market, rule_kind, venue_group, valid_from, valid_to, price_from, price_to, tick_size, limit_ratio, source, verified` | `verified` goes into the release-evidence rule list as it is. `rule_kind = tick_band` is the price grid (5.6.4a), `price_limit` the `pct` source of 5.6.5 (after `tradability_daily`, before the rules pack table). When present it is used before the rules pack table |
| `fx_rates` | `base, quote, rate_kind, obs_date, ts_event, ts_available, rate, source` | one series is chosen by `[fx] series` (3.11). Decision inputs use `ts_available <= decision_ts`, report valuation `ts_event <= valuation time` (5.1.5) [TDS-D17] |
| value tables (`values`, `market_values`, `symbol_values`; `market=` and `item=` partitions, TDS 5.28) | the four words of 5.3.5, and `params.items` (the optional kind and carry per item) | 5.3.5, 5.1.4. They replace `fundamentals_state`, `fundamentals_events`, `classifications`, `eligibility_daily`, `universe_membership`, `feature_panels`, `label_panels` and `share_counts`, removed 2026-11-24 (#752) [#725 Decisions 250, 254] |
| index instruments (`instruments(asset_class=index)` + `bars`) | `close` (= the index value), `index_kind` | benchmarks (5.7). Only `index_kind = TR` as the default benchmark (`"auto"`, 3.11) [TDS-D15]. An index row needs no trading fields; a benchmark may also be any symbol or a return series the user gives [#725 Decisions 217] |
| `dictionary/columns` | all | dataset attributes, stubs, units (3.2) |

**Corporate-action kind → engine handling** [TDS-D11]:

| TDS `kind` | Engine handling (5.6.x) | Price adjustment (5.3.3) |
|---|---|---|
| `split`, `reverse_split`, `capital_reduction` (share consolidation; `ratio_new_per_old` present, `cash_per_share` null) | 5.6.10 ratio ρ = `ratio_num/ratio_den` (canonical, required in TDS v1.4); when missing, `decimal_ratio` decides (5.6.1) — the decimal notation of `ratio_new_per_old` only when the user chose `decimal_ratio = "literal"` (5.6.1) | `price_factor` |
| `stock_dividend`, `bonus_issue` | 5.6.10 ratio ρ = `ratio_num/ratio_den` (canonical, required in TDS v1.4); when missing, `decimal_ratio` decides (5.6.1) — `1 + ratio_new_per_old` only when the user chose `decimal_ratio = "literal"` (5.6.1, G-53); the new shares locked for sale | `price_factor` |
| `capital_reduction` with cash (paid capital reduction: `cash_per_share` present) | 5.6.10 (ρ) + a receivable of cash `q·cash_per_share` (pay date) | `price_factor` |
| `cash_dividend`, `special_dividend`, `return_of_capital` | 5.6.11 (return of capital follows the same entitlement, receivable and payment path; withholding only for `cash_dividend` and `special_dividend`) | `price_factor` (`affects = total_only`) |
| `stock_merger`, `mixed_merger` | 5.6.12 stock merger (mixed = stock + `cash_per_share`) | the old instrument disappears (`price_factor = 1`) |
| `cash_merger` | 5.6.12 cash merger | – |
| `spinoff` | 5.6.12 (handled when `new_instrument_id` and `ratio_new_per_old` are present, otherwise not supported) | `price_factor` |
| `rights_issue` | 5.6.12 rights policy (`lapse`/`fail`); share factor = 1 (holding quantity unchanged) | `price_factor` (TERP) |
| `exchange_offer`, `other` | unsupported events (5.6.12; when held, the policy `unsupported`) | `price_factor` applied when present |
| `name_change`, `par_change` | no accounting effect (event record only). `symbol_change` was removed 2026-11-24 (#752): symbol periods live in `instrument_symbols` only (5.3.6) [#725 Decisions 209] | none (`affects = none`) |

Events whose `price_factor` is null (`factor_method = unavailable`): when an instrument's window crosses that event's effective session e (when the window has a row with s < e ≤ t), the engine leaves the adjusted price cells of the rows before e as NaN and counts the diagnostic `AdjustmentUnavailable` (no hidden 1.0 substitute, INV-12). This "none" also propagates into the cumulative factors as it is: the ratio of `C` across e is none. When held, the policy for unsupported events applies. One `AdjustmentUnavailable` count is one (instrument, event) pair: an event whose `price_factor` is null and whose effective session e some window of that instrument crossed in the run counts once, however many cells or rows it left NaN. The coverage table counts `AdjustmentUnavailable` per instrument and year, and the report lists the event ids.

### 4.7 Engine resource files and market rules packs (engine repository, versioned) [R-25, TDS-D23]

**Market rules pack** `src/nexusbt/resources/markets/<MARKET>.toml` (v1: `KR.toml`, `US.toml`, the generic pack `_generic.toml`). The market name equals the TDS `markets` key, and a pack is used for a market only when its `calendar_ids` include that market's `calendar_id`:

```toml
[pack]
market = "KR"            # TDS markets key
version = 1              # "rules KR@1" in the report and manifest
calendar_ids = ["XKRX"]
currency = "KRW"
minor_unit = 0           # digits of the currency minor unit (USD = 2)
benchmark_hint = "KOSPI" # name hint when [benchmarks] auto finds several
[price_limits]
table = "limits/kr_price_limits_v1.csv"   # when absent, no price-limit rule (US)
open_tolerance = 0.005   # source of the default of [execution] kr_limit_tolerance
[costs]
provisional_bps = {buy = 5, sell = 25}
min_half_spread_bps = 5
table_market_groups = "costs/venue_groups_v1.csv"
[tradability]
unknown_status_before = ""   # US pack "2019-08-23" (describes the status_known=false period)
[grid]                       # price grid
tick_table = "ticks/kr_tick_table_v1.csv"   # tick sizes by period and price band (for membership checks and snapping); when absent F-15 (minor_unit)
base_unit = 1                # integer coordinate for price comparison (KR 1 won; US pack 0.0001 dollars)
[rounding]                   # rounding rule per booking component; KR pack default: commission, tax and withholding down (the majority option, a research default, not a claim about settlement practice), the rest half_up; US pack all half_up
proceeds = {mode = "half_up", unit = 1}
commission = {mode = "down", unit = 1}      # the truncation practice of Korean brokers and tax law [unverified, verified = false] — the user overrides it with [rounding.KR]. Facts checked (2026-10-29): National Treasury Management Act Article 47(1) truncates below 10 won at payment to the treasury; Income Tax Act Article 86 does not collect withholding tax below 1,000 won (except interest income); Samsung Securities truncates credit interest below 1 won. Per-fill tax and commission rounding was not checked against an original source, so the seed stays as it is
tax = {mode = "down", unit = 1}
dividend_withholding = {mode = "down", unit = 1}
cash_in_lieu = {mode = "half_up", unit = 1}
borrow_fee = {mode = "half_up", unit = 1}
[shorting]                   #
allowed = true
ban_table = "shorting/kr_short_bans_v1.csv"  # periods when new short-sale orders are forbidden (covers allowed); the US pack has none
uptick_rule_modeled = false  # US pack: Rule 201 (price-restricted short sales) is not simulated (a documented assumption)
borrow_availability = "assume_all"   # no data on borrowable quantity: everything is assumed borrowable outside ban periods (a manifest assumption)
day_count = 365              # days per year of the borrow, rebate and cash-interest accrual. KR = 365 verified = true (broker credit-interest terms: loan × rate × days/365, 366 in leap years — Korea Investment & Securities and Samsung Securities guides, 2026-10-29); the US pack seed corrected to 360 verified = true (source: IBKR margin interest USD 360 days, borrow fee /360 — broker practice, not regulation, 2026-10-29)
```

- `ticks/<m>_tick_table_v1.csv` (`valid_from, valid_to, venue_group, price_from, price_to, tick, source, verified`): KR seed = from 2023-01-25, common to KOSPI, KOSDAQ and KONEX: under 2,000 won 1 won · 2,000~5,000 won 5 won · 5,000~20,000 won 10 won · 20,000~50,000 won 50 won · 50,000~200,000 won 100 won · 200,000~500,000 won 500 won · 500,000 won and above 1,000 won — `verified = true` (source: [Samsung Securities notice "Change of tick sizes in the domestic securities and derivatives markets"](https://samsungpop.com/ux/kor/customer/notice/notice/noticeViewContent.do?MenuSeqNo=19236), effective 2023-01-25; the original KRX regulation could not be seen because law.krx.co.kr was unreachable, 2026-10-29). Before that, the old per-market table for [2010-10-04, 2023-01-25) (under 1,000 won 1 · under 5,000 5 · under 10,000 10 · under 50,000 50 · under 100,000 100 · under 500,000 500 · 500,000 and above 1,000; KOSDAQ 100 from 50,000 won) — the KOSPI rows and KOSDAQ 50,000~500,000 won at 100 won are `verified = true` (source: [the KRX announcement of 2010-02-08 as republished (Mondo Visione)](https://mondovisione.com/media-and-resources/news/krx-to-lower-tick-size-for-low-priced-stocks-to-1-won-2011124/) — 1 won under 1,000 won from 2010-10-04; the "current" table of the Samsung Securities notice above). For KOSDAQ 500,000 won and above, the Samsung Securities "current" table says 1,000 won, which disagrees with the seed (100) → `verified = false` [unverified]. **Before 2010-10-04**, everything under 5,000 won was 5 won (no 1-won band), and above that the same table [the KRX announcement above]; earlier effective dates are `verified = false` [unverified]. KR `venue_group = ETF` (`instruments.category ∈ {etf, etn}`): 5 won at every price band, kept in the 2023-01-25 reform as well (ETF, ETN, ELW) `verified = true` (source: the Samsung Securities notice above; the start date is unverified). US seed = 0.01 at $1 and above, 0.0001 below $1 (Reg NMS Rule 612(b)(3)) `verified = true` (source: [17 CFR 242.612](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/17/242.612)); however, the 2024 amendment (89 FR 81774) sets $0.005 for instruments at $1 and above whose time-weighted average quoted spread is ≤ $0.015, reclassified every half year, and its compliance date was postponed by an SEC exemptive order to **the first business day of November 2027** ([Release 34-105656](https://www.sec.gov/files/rules/exorders/2026/34-105656.pdf), 2026-06) — this table is right only until then. From that date an instrument's half-yearly assignment has no source since the `classifications` scheme `tick_rule_id` (decision 2026-11-01) left (#752); where it is read from is #802's decision; an instrument without one keeps this table's 0.01, whose grid lies inside the 0.005 grid. Pre-decimalisation rows — 1/8 dollar before 1997-06-24, 1/16 dollar for [1997-06-24, decimalisation date) — are `verified = false` [unverified: the 1/16 date and the finer unit below $1 have only secondary sources]; the decimalisation date is **per market**: NYSE 2001-01-29 (secondary source: [The Daily Record 2001-01-29](https://thedailyrecord.com/2001/01/29/nyse-goes-totally-decimal-8212-finally/)), Nasdaq 2001-04-09 (source: [SEC testimony 2001-05-24](https://www.sec.gov/news/testimony/052401tslu.htm), the deadline of the SEC order of 2000-06-08) — so the Nasdaq `venue_group` is 1/16 also in [2001-01-29, 2001-04-09). Pilot instruments (NYSE from 2000-08-28, Nasdaq from 2001-03-12) are not represented in the table and show up as `off_grid_bar`. The grid table is used only for membership checks and snapping order prices; comparisons use `base_unit` integers (5.6.4a).
- `shorting/kr_short_bans_v1.csv` (`valid_from, valid_to, scope, except_universe_ids, source, verified`): intervals are **half-open** `[valid_from, valid_to)` (end date = the first day it is allowed again). `scope` grammar = `all` / `index:<id>[,<id>…]` (applies when the instrument is a member of at least one on that date) / `classification:<scheme>=<code>`. `except_universe_ids` (a list) removes from the ban the instruments that are members of at least one on that date (the union). Since `classifications` and index membership left the standard (#752), the names in `index:`, `classification:` and `except_universe_ids` are read as the user's value-table items (5.3.5); how a name is matched to an item is asked of the owner in T4.Gt, which applies the ban (not applied yet) [#752, 2026-11-26]. KR seed — all checked against the original Financial Services Commission press releases, `verified = true` (2026-10-29): [2008-10-01, 2009-06-01) `all` (source: the attachment of [FSC 2008-09-30](https://www.fsc.go.kr/no010101/68952) — KOSPI from 10.1 and KOSDAQ from 10.2, so for KOSDAQ on 2008-10-01 the seed is more conservative by one day; [FSC 2009-05-20](https://www.fsc.go.kr/no010101/69237) lifted for non-financial stocks on 6.1); [2009-06-01, 2013-11-14) `classification:is_financial=true` (financial stocks = the financial sectors of KOSPI and KOSDAQ, including banks, securities and insurance; source: [FSC 2013-11-13](https://www.fsc.go.kr/no010101/70903) "lifted from '13.11.14"); [2011-08-10, 2011-11-10) `all` (source: [FSC 2011-08-09](https://www.fsc.go.kr/no010101/70024) 8.10~11.9, [FSC 2011-11-08](https://www.fsc.go.kr/no010101/70123) non-financial stocks lifted on 11.10, financial stocks kept); [2020-03-16, 2021-05-03) `all`; [2021-05-03, 2023-11-06) `all` but with `except_universe_ids = [index:KOSPI200, index:KOSDAQ150]` (partial resumption; source: [FSC 2021-02-03](https://www.fsc.go.kr/no010101/75290), [FSC 2021-04-29](https://www.fsc.go.kr/no010101/75830)); [2023-11-06, 2025-03-31) `all` (KOSPI, KOSDAQ, KONEX; source: [FSC 2023-11-05](https://www.fsc.go.kr/no010101/81013), [FSC 2024-06-13](https://www.fsc.go.kr/no010101/82454) extended to '25.3.30, [FSC 2025-03-21](https://www.fsc.go.kr/no010101/84216) full resumption on 3.31). During the bans, covered short sales by market makers and liquidity providers were allowed (the 2020 and 2023 originals), but they are not the engine's subject. When the package lacks that item, every instrument is taken as banned (no exception), with E30.
- **Rule verification gate**: the release evidence (T6.15, R-70) lists, for every rule row applied in the run (tick tables, price limits, bans, rounding, `day_count`), its source, its effective boundary and `verified`, shows the number of `verified = false` rows as numerator/denominator, and shows the off-grid bars `off_grid_bar` per market and year. A "KR/US verified" claim is made only when every row actually applied is `verified = true`.
- Only what the pack sets differs per market; the state machine and the fill rules (5.6) are the same for every market. The price-limit rule (5.6.5) is turned on only when the pack has `price_limits` (instead of v2's condition `market == KR`).
- A market without a pack is E18 (4.4). With `[markets] allow_untested = true`, `_generic.toml` is used (no price limits, ½-spread floor 5 bp, no `provisional_bps` → `[costs.provisional_bps] <MARKET>` required), with "untested market rules: {market}" in the report header and the manifest `assumptions`.
- A new market pack is added only by a PR that has public sources (the exchange's regulations) and at least one hand-calculated case for that market (chapter 10 v1.x "add market rules packs").

`src/nexusbt/resources/costs/default_v1.csv`:

| Column | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| country | str | market code (TDS `markets` key; the column name is kept for v1 compatibility) |
| market | str | `*`, KOSPI, KOSDAQ, NYSE, NASDAQ (names of price-policy groups, not MICs; the mapping `instruments.venue_mic` → group is `venue_groups_v1.csv` in the same folder) |
| side | buy/sell (a short sale uses the sell row, a cover the buy row) | |
| component | commission / tax / other | other = the US SEC fee, FINRA TAF and the like |
| bps | float | |
| min_amount | float | minimum amount (local currency), 0 when none |
| valid_from, valid_to | date | half-open, empty valid_to = current |
| source | str | URL of the law or broker notice |
| verified | bool | whether a person checked it against the source |

- Coverage check: `realistic` can be used only when run period × run markets × side × (commission, tax) is covered without gaps by `verified = true` rows. Otherwise error E23. The first version holds only `verified = false` seed values (chapter 12 OI-03) [9.0 defaults, R-61].
- `src/nexusbt/resources/limits/kr_price_limits_v1.csv` (`valid_from, valid_to, market, limit_ratio, source, verified`): a fallback table used only when both `tradability_daily.up_limit_price/down_limit_price` and `price_limit_ratio` are null. Seed values (corrected 2026-10-29 — per `market`): KOSPI [1995-04-01 0.06, 1996-11-25 0.08, 1998-03-02 0.12, 1998-12-07 0.15, 2015-06-15 0.30); KOSDAQ 0.12 (start date [unverified]) → 2005-03-28 0.15 → 2015-06-15 0.30; KONEX 0.15 (left out of the 2015-06-15 widening) [design 5.4, research 2.4]. The evidence is all secondary, so `verified = false` stays and only `source` is filled: newspapers of the time (Hankook Ilbo [1996-11-25](https://www.hankookilbo.com/news/article/199611250055411462), [1998-02-12](https://www.hankookilbo.com/news/article/199802120060207540), [2005-03-10](https://www.hankookilbo.com/news/article/200503100043910652)), a summary of the 2015 effective notice ([Crowe 2015-05-29](https://www.crowe.com/kr/news/news20150529_kr)); the original KRX business regulation and its history were not seen. Before 1995-04-01 the limits were **fixed amounts** (amounts per price band), not ratios, and cannot be represented in this table → that period has no `verified = false` row, only "no price-limit information". When a package has the `market_rules` dataset (TDS v1.2 5.27), its `price_limit` rows are used before this table (4.6, 5.6.5) (TDS-Q15). A second table `limits/kr_new_listing_band_v1.csv` (`valid_from, valid_to, market, low_multiple, high_multiple, source, verified`) holds the first-session band of a new listing — KOSPI and KOSDAQ from 2023-06-26, 0.60 to 4.00 of the offer price, `verified = false`, `source` = "press coverage, rule of 2023-06-26; KRX text not obtained"; v1 does not read it (5.6.5, not in the loader's seed list), and the ticket that turns it on needs the offer price in `instruments` (decision 2026-11-06, #310).
- These files are small tables copied by hand from public sources (laws, exchanges, broker notices), not reference-producer data (the allow list of the hygiene test of 2.7).

### 4.8 Synthetic TDS package generator (`nx.testing.synth`) [TDS-D24]

The engine uses only this generator before a real producer package arrives, and in **every test** of the repository, apart from a package a test imports with `nexusbt_tds.import_csv` from a mapping file in its temporary folder (2.7; changed under the M1 freeze's defect rule, #599: 2.7 names this source, a contradiction otherwise). `tiny` is a **hand-set table** (no randomness; the case yaml becomes rows as it is), and `small` and `full` follow the random rules below. The generator writes TDS v1 packages (`tds.json`, hashes, dictionaries included), and its output must pass `tds check --level full` with the profiles it declares (test `test_synth_conforms`, INV-17).

```python
def make_snapshot(root: Path, *, preset: Literal["tiny","small","full","full_ci"] = "small", seed: int = 0,
                  snapshot_id: str = "synth-0001", start: date | None = None, end: date | None = None,
                  markets: Sequence[str] = ("KR", "US"),            # "ZZ" = a test market without a rules pack (XZZZ, currency XTS)
                  n_kr: int | None = None, n_us: int | None = None,
                  events: Sequence[EventSpec] = (), overrides: Mapping[str, Any] | None = None,
                  degrade: Sequence[Literal["no_ohlc",    # no_sessions and sparse_bars left with F-02 and F-03 (#705)
                                            "kr_ex_is_record","us_div_split_adjusted_no_dates",
                                            "no_benchmarks","no_delistings",   # the removed kinds' values left 2026-11-24 (#752)
                                            "no_tick_info","volume_in_lots","late_bars",
                                            "no_volume"]] = (),   # two values from v3.1; late_bars: rule 3a (#354); no_volume: E18 when read (#584, #705)
                  behind: Mapping[str, int] | None = None,   # dataset → sessions its processed-through session lies before the watermark (#192)
                  price_decimals: int | None = None) -> Path   # 8.1's CI fixture: US and ZZ prices to 10^-n (5a, #252)
```

| preset | Markets and instruments | Period | Use |
|---|---|---|---|
| tiny | KR 6, US 6 (fixed names `KR:T00001`…, `US:T00001`…) | 2020-01-02 → 2020-03-31 | hand-calculated golden cases (8.2). Events only through `events` |
| small | KR 120, US 180, with listings and delistings mixed into the period | 2015-01-02 → 2019-12-31 | unit and property tests, CI performance smoke tests |
| full | KR 5,500, US 22,100 instruments ever listed; about KR 2,300 / US 5,700 alive on a day | 1995-01-02 → 2024-12-31 | performance measurement on the compute machine (about 2.7 GB of disk, ten times a run at a tenth of the instruments, #334; run by a person on the compute machine) |
| full_ci | 1/50 of the instruments of full (same rules and period) | 1995-01-02 → 2024-12-31 | testing the full generation path in CI |
| ("ZZ" in markets) | ZZ 6~120 instruments, calendar `XZZZ` (a copy of the XNYS sessions, only the name differs), currency `XTS` (the ISO test currency) | the preset's period | tests that market names are not hard-coded (no rules pack → E18; runs when a test `ZZ.toml` is added) |

- With `degrade=()`, it declares `profiles = ["tds:equity-daily-core@1.0"]`. `producer = {"name": "nexusbt-synth", "hints": {...}}` (a few hint lines for the fallback tests). Each value of `degrade` imitates one gap of the 4.5 table, and the profiles it can no longer keep are dropped from the declaration (`tds check` still passes — it checks only what is declared). For example: `no_ohlc` → `open/high/low` null, core declaration dropped; `kr_ex_is_record` → KR cash dividends with `ex_date = record_date`; `us_div_split_adjusted_no_dates` → US dividend amounts divided by the later split factors + null dates.
- Namespaces: `id_namespaces = {"KR": "synthetic KR", "US": "synthetic US", "ZZ": "synthetic test market", "IDX": "synthetic indices"}`. No real instrument codes are used (public repository, 2.7).
- Coverage and watermark: every preset writes each dataset's `coverage` block with the keys of TDS 4.2's example that the dataset has a column for (`instruments`, `ts_min`, `ts_max`, and `by_market` for a dataset with `market=` partitions), and each market's watermark in `markets.<m>` (TDS 3.8): `complete_through_session` is the period's last session and `complete_through` its close. A value internal to this generator, not an engine rule (decision 2026-11-11, #390; under the M1 freeze's defect rule: without it the benchmark package cannot exercise D001, nor T006 on its bars). Every dataset also writes, for every market, `coverage.by_market.<m>.processed_through_session` (TDS 4.3) at that watermark session; `behind={"<dataset>": n}` puts it `n` sessions earlier for that dataset, in every market, so a case can make one dataset lag the watermark (5.9.6 rule 4, 8.4). `behind` takes a written dataset without a row per session (not `bars_1d`, `calendar_sessions` or `tradability_daily`, which would need their rows cut too) and `0 ≤ n` below the market's session count; anything else is `ValueError` (decision 2026-11-12, #192). `behind` also cuts, from that dataset in each market, every row whose availability instant is after the close of the cut session in that market's calendar, the instant TDS 4.3 and its D003 read (an action's 5.11 existence instant, an exit's `ts_exit_known`, a column form's column; a null instant stays; `instruments` and `market_rules` have none, so lose no row), together with what derives from the cut rows: a cut action's move stays in the prices (one the producer has not explained yet), and the generator's record of what moved them follows the cut (#705), and a cut exit leaves its instrument's `delisted_on` null. The cut package passes `tds check --level full` (decision 2026-11-12 (3), #425). With it, M016 is judged on every dataset of every preset, and D003 on each dataset without a `last` that has an instant.

Generation rules (deterministic, `numpy.random.Generator(PCG64(seed))`):
1. Calendars: XKRX and XNYS are built from the sessions of `exchange_calendars` 4.13.2 (at generation only). Times: KR open 00:00Z, close 06:30Z (06:00Z before 2016-08-01), US open 14:30Z/13:30Z, close 21:00Z/20:00Z (US daylight saving applied), half days as they are → `calendar_sessions` (with `session_ord`).
2. Instruments: uniform listing dates, exponentially distributed listing periods (mean 8 years; in `full` KR 30 years and US 11 years, values internal to this generator that give the table's instruments alive on a day as the mean over the period, within about 10%, an early day holding fewer and a late day more — decision 2026-11-10, #334), market proportions as in the table above → `instruments`, `instrument_symbols` (1% of ticker histories change, 0.5% have another id reuse an old ticker).
3. Prices: integer draws in basis points (bp) and `+ − × ÷` only, so that the byte-identical sentence below holds on every platform: `exp` and a normal draw need not round the same everywhere (decision 2026-11-03, #128). Here `//` is floor division and `/` exact division. Each instrument draws a volatility `sigma`, a uniform integer from 100 to 400 bp (1% to 4%). With `half = sigma × 1732 // 1000` (about √3·sigma, so the step's sd is sigma) and `mean = 3 + sigma² // 20000` (so the mean log return stays near 0.0003), each session's `step` is a uniform integer on `mean ± half` bp. The first price `p0`, which stands for the close before the first session, is a uniform integer: KR 1,000 to 1,000,000 won, US 200 to 50,000 cents ($2 to $500). Close = `p0` × the running product of the ratios ((10000 + `step`) / 10000), each ratio divided first. Open = previous close × ((10000 + `gap`) / 10000), with `gap` a uniform integer on ± `half // 2`. High = max(open, close) × `w` and low = min(open, close) / `w`, with `w` = (10000 + `k`) / 10000 and `k` a uniform integer from 0 to `half // 2` drawn separately for each, so they enclose the open and close. Volume is a uniform integer from 1,000 to 100,000 shares → `bars` (freq=1d, raw, dense, availability rule `ts_end`; no `cum_factor_*` since 2026-11-22, #705). `value_traded` is the bar's volume × (high + low + close) / 3, taken after every event, halt and limit of (4) and (5) has moved the bar, and 0 on a halted bar, so that TDS-V011's R is 1 (a value internal to this generator, decision 2026-11-11, #231); `tiny` writes `value_traded` too, by the same formula over its hand-set volume, high, low and close after its injected events have moved them: a value derived from the hand-set table, not drawn, and computed with `+ × ÷` of IEEE 754 doubles in that order, each correctly rounded, so its bytes are the same on every platform (added under the M1 freeze's defect rule, #584: once F-18 exists, a `tiny` without `value_traded` is a package with a 4.5 gap that no `degrade=` value asked for, while this section has each gap come from `degrade=` alone).
3a. Late bars (`degrade=["late_bars"]`; added under the M1 freeze's defect rule, #354: without it no property test, P-FUT, P-CHUNK or P-START, has data that reaches the visibility rule of 5.1.2 and 5.3.1): the bars of the preset are written with the column rule (`availability = {"column": "ts_available"}`, no `params.ready_delay`, so `Δ = 0`). Every bar has `ts_available = ready(s)`, the close of its session, except the late ones, which are KR bars only. This is not a gap of the 4.5 table: a late `ts_available` is valid data, so no profile is dropped, and no other value of the package changes. Values internal to this generator, not engine rules (decision 2026-11-11, #354): late are 0.5% of the KR bars that have three sessions of the period after them, chosen without replacement, and the last row of every KR year partition but the last (the overlap tail of a chunk boundary, 5.3.1) when it has three sessions after it; a late bar's `ts_available` is the close of the session one to three sessions after its own (a uniform integer); the draws come from a stream of their own, `PCG64` seeded with `[seed, 354]`, the choice first and then the delays in the order of the bars' rows.
4. Corporate actions: the exact ratio columns `ratio_num/ratio_den` are filled and the f64 column is their quotient (including ratios that are not exact as binary fractions, such as 1:3, 1:7 and a stock dividend of 0.29); 10% of delisting events have a liquidating distribution whose ex-date lies between the last trading day and the exit, and `ret_observed_includes_distributions`; splits at 0.5% a year (2:1, 5:1, 7:1), reverse splits at 0.2% (1:10, 1:3), bonus issues at 0.1%, quarterly cash dividends for 30% of instruments, half of the Korean dividends with `ts_terms_available` 30~60 days after the ex-date, 1% of events amended (`revision_of`; amount and ratio amendments, some after payment), 0.5% cancelled after announcement (`status = cancelled`), `price_factor`, `share_factor`, `factor_method` and `factor_ref_price` filled, except that a cash dividend whose whole-unit amount reaches the previous close (for example a US close below one cent) has `price_factor` null and `factor_method = unavailable`, as TDS 5.11 prescribes, and multiplies no bar (decision 2026-11-10, #357); `resolution_body` (TDS 5.11) from the seed: a year-end cash dividend (ex-date in the year's last quarter) `meeting` with probability 0.8 and `board` with 0.2, any other cash dividend `board`, and 5% of all dividend rows null (decision 2026-11-04, #220); the latest version of each event that is not cancelled multiplies (3)'s raw open, high, low and close from its ex-date by its `price_factor`, and the volume by its `share_factor`. Values internal to this generator, not engine rules: an event is announced (`declared_date`, `ts_announced` at that session's close) 10 sessions before its ex-date, a dividend's `record_date` is the session after the ex-date and its `pay_date` 30 days after its resolution (the ex-date for a share event), an amount amendment adds 5~30 bp to the yield, and the 1% amended and 0.5% cancelled have a floor of one each, with at least one ratio amendment of a share event, so that `small` always holds all three cases → `corporate_actions`.
5. Halts and price limits (Korea): raw prices are rounded to the pack's price grid (some rows get a 1-ULP wobble to test grid snapping), halts on 0.1% of days (some halt periods contain split and dividend effective dates), limit hits on 0.2% of days, `price_limit_ratio` and `up/down_limit_price` (rounded to the grid as TDS 5.10 says) → `tradability_daily` (US `status_known = false` before 2019-08-23). Values internal to this generator, not engine rules (decision 2026-11-04, #220): the grid is the nearest point of `kr_tick_table_v1` (XKRX → the KOSPI bands, XKOS → KOSDAQ, the band chosen by the price before rounding), applied again whenever an event moves the prices, so that a dividend's factor uses a rounded close; the wobble moves 0.1% of the KR closes that lie strictly between low and high up by one ULP; halts take 0.1% of the KR sessions in spans of 1~5 sessions, at least one span from a split's ex-date and one from a cash dividend's (G-54's two cases), and a halted bar has `obs_status = halted`, null OHLC, volume 0 and `halted_full_day = true`; a halted day has `halt_count = 0` (it counts intraday halts); limit hits take 0.2% of the KR sessions after an instrument's last ex-date, never on adjacent sessions, closing at the upper or the lower limit by half, a quarter of them locked all day (O = H = L = C, `locked_up/down`), the close being that session's `up/down_limit_price`, and the bars after it moved by the same ratio (and rounded again) so that every bar trades within its own limits; `price_limit_ratio` comes from `kr_price_limits_v1` (0.15 before 2015-06-15, 0.30 from then), `reference_price` is the last close before the session (over halts) times the ratio of the running split factor (the product of the share events' price factors so far) between the two sessions (an ex-date's base price follows its share events, not its cash dividends), and `up/down_limit_price = reference_price × (1 ± price_limit_ratio)` rounded to the grid inward, the upper limit down and the lower limit up (TDS 5.10; decision 2026-11-05, #287), the three null on a listing's first session; halts, limit hits and wobbles leave alone the close an event's factor uses (the session before an ex-date), the ex-date itself (halts excepted) and the last session of a listing; `admin_status` is `administrative` over the last 60 sessions of 1% of the KR instruments (at least one), `normal` elsewhere; US and ZZ rows have null limit columns and `admin_status`, US rows before 2019-08-23 null in every other column too, and from then on, and on ZZ, `halted_full_day = false` and `halt_count = 0`; `availability_by_column` is `session_open` for the prior information (`price_limit_ratio`, `up/down_limit_price`, `reference_price`, `admin_status`) and `session_close` for the day's results (the halt and limit columns) (TDS 5.10); `tiny` writes no `tradability_daily` yet.
5a. Price decimals (optional): `price_decimals`, an integer from 0 to 9 or `None` (the default, which changes nothing), `small` and `full` only (CLI `--price-decimals`). With `price_decimals = n`, the raw OHLC of every market without a tick grid (US, ZZ) is rounded to the nearest multiple of 10^-n wherever (5) puts KR prices on their grid: when the bars are drawn and again whenever an event moves them, so that a dividend's factor uses a rounded close. KR bars are as without it; the columns computed from prices (a dividend's factor, `value_traded`, a delisting's `ref_price`) take the rounded prices, and the package passes `tds check --level full` as without it. `n = 3` is the CI fixture of 8.1, whose zipline bundle stores a price as an integer × 1000. Values internal to this generator, not engine rules (decision 2026-11-19, #252).
6. Exits: a `delistings` row at the end of every listing (policy shares: observed 50%, estimated 30%, unobserved 20%; half of the observed have `ts_value_available` 20~90 days after the exit), matching `instruments.delisted_on`. Values internal to this generator, not engine rules (decision 2026-11-04, #220): `exit_date` is the session after the last trading day, two sessions after it for (4)'s liquidating distribution, whose ex-date is the session between (a `return_of_capital`, 20~80% of the last close, `total_only`, announced with the exit at `ts_exit_known`, `pay_date` 30 days after its ex-date), on 10% of the exits with at least one; `ts_exit_known` is the close of the session 10 before the exit, or of the first listed session when the listing is shorter; `ref_date` and `ref_price` are the last trading day and its raw close; `ret_observed` (observed only) is a uniform integer from −5,000 to +3,000 bp; `ret_policy` is `ret_observed` when observed, a uniform integer from −5,000 to 0 bp when estimated, and −0.30 when unobserved, except the first unobserved row of each market, left null so that `small` holds 5.6.13's E13 case; `ret_lo = −1` and `ret_hi = 0` on every row (as F-13); `policy_id = exit-value-v1`; `pay_date` 30 days after the exit for the observed, else null; `delist_type` `acquisition_cash` (observed), `performance` (estimated), `unknown` (unobserved), `liquidation` with a distribution; `ret_observed_includes_distributions` true or false by half on the rows with a distribution, null on the others; with `corporate_actions` and `delistings` both present the generator declares `tds:equity-daily-core@1.0` beside `tds:minimal-daily@1.0`.
7. to 11. Share counts, financials, classifications and index membership, eligibility, panels: removed 2026-11-24 (#752) with `share_counts`, `fundamentals_events`, `fundamentals_state`, `classifications`, `universe_membership`, `eligibility_daily`, `feature_panels` and `label_panels` [decision 2026-11-05, #220]. The generator's small value tables (5.3.5, TDS 5.28), in `small` only (`tiny` and `full` write none): `values` `shares_out` (`kind: share_count`; each quarter-end, in that date's share basis), `eps` (`kind: per_share`, `carry: 63`; each quarter-end) and `in_index` (`carry: next_listing`; value 1 on each quarter's first session for about a third of the listed equities), `market_values` `cash_rate` (in bp a year, as `[account] cash_rate` reads it, 3.11; each month's first session) and `symbol_values` `sector` (a code from 1 to 10). These are names of example data only, not names the engine expects; their draws come from a stream of their own, `PCG64` seeded with `[seed, 752]`, so no other dataset's rows change [#725 Decisions 250, 261; the owner, 2026-11-27, #752].
11a. Market rules (optional): `market_rules` (two versions of the KR tick table, price limits); `degrade=["volume_in_lots"]` divides KR volume by 100. Values internal to this generator, not engine rules (decision 2026-11-11, #231): `market_rules` is written in `small` and `full` when the package has KR (`tiny` writes none; its prices are on no grid), its rows copied from the pack's resources with their `source` and `verified`: rule set `kr-tick`, the `tick_band` rows of `kr_tick_table_v1` that have a start date (the 2010-10-04 KOSPI and KOSDAQ tables and the 2023-01-25 table of group `*`; the rows before 2010-10-04 have no start date, which TDS 5.27 requires, so a price before that day has no band and V012 does not compare it), and rule set `kr-price-limit`, the KOSPI and KOSDAQ rows of `kr_price_limits_v1`; every KR instrument has `instruments.tick_rule_id = kr-tick`, US and ZZ instruments null (their prices are not rounded), and no instrument a `tick_size`; `degrade=["no_tick_info"]` (F-15) writes no `market_rules` and no `tick_rule_id` column, changes no price and drops no profile; `volume_in_lots` writes KR volume in whole lots of 100 shares, `floor(volume / 100)`, the odd shares dropped, leaves `value_traded` as it was and drops no profile (V011 is a warning). `degrade=["no_volume"]` (E18 for a term reading `volume` or `value_traded`, #705; added under the M1 freeze's defect rule, #584) writes the bars without the `volume` and `value_traded` columns and drops the core declaration (TDS 7: OHLCV).
12. Exchange rates: daily `fx_rates` for each pair of market currencies (`rate_kind = fixing`, one series; USDKRW for KR+US), published with a 1-session delay, a 2-session delay once a week.
13. Benchmarks: two cap-weighted synthetic TR indices (`IDX:KOSPI_TR`, `IDX:SPX_TR`; built only inside the synthetic data) → `instruments(asset_class=index)` + `bars`.

`EventSpec` (event injection): a dict that uses the column names of a TDS dataset (`corporate_actions`, `delistings`, `tradability_daily`) as they are, such as `{"kind": "split", "instrument_id": "KR:T00001", "ex_date": "2020-02-03", "ratio_new_per_old": 2.0}`, plus a `"dataset"` key. When there is an injected event, no random event is made on the same date (for that instrument). The generator's output must be byte-identical for the same arguments (test `test_synth_deterministic`).

`python -m nexusbt.testing.synth --preset P --out DIR [--markets KR,US,ZZ] [--degrade a,b] --validate` runs `tds check --level full` after generating and prints the row counts per dataset, the package `value_sha256` and `semantic_sha256`, and the three profile results. With `--validate` it also prints "exact ratios: n, distributions before delisting: k, off-grid rows: m" (the `corporate_actions` rows with `ratio_num`; the cash distributions whose ex-date is after their instrument's last trading day; the prices TDS-V012 counts, or "no grid information" for a package without `market_rules`) and, unless the checker says TDS-V011 was not checked (no bar with `value_traded`, volume, high, low and close), "TDS-V011 warnings: n" (#231).

---

## 5. Normative semantics

### 5.1 Time and "as of"

#### 5.1.1 Representation of time [11.4.1]
- Every time inside the engine is UTC, as integer microseconds (`datetime64[us]`). At the Python boundary it is a tz-aware `datetime(UTC)`. The local date (`session_date`) is a label.
- The times of session s are only `ts_open(s)` and `ts_close(s)` of `calendar_sessions` (the engine does not compute time-zone rules).

#### 5.1.2 The one rule of availability [C1, R-17, INV-01]
- An observation x can be used at decision d only when `ts_available(x) <= decision_ts(d)`. No exceptions (no month-end exception; a currency does not change the time) [11.4.1, Q10]. A date-only value takes the freeze time of its row as its `ts_available` (5.1.4): the user's date says it was known before that market's next session opens, which is a daily decision's **information cutoff**, and that decision's orders fill in that market no earlier [#725 Decisions 230, 231]. `decision_ts` stays the instant the decision is taken and recorded (5.1.3).
- **The reference time of market-axis values**: row s of market m freezes at the **freeze time** `ready_m(s) = ts_close(s) + Δ_m`. `Δ_m` is a constant set by the data: 0 when the bars' availability rule is `ts_end`, that duration when it is `ts_end+<duration>`, and the dataset's `params.ready_delay` when it is a column rule (`{"column": "ts_available"}`) (TDS v1.1; 0 when absent). Daily bars under the rule `session_close` take 0 as well, the same instant as `ts_end` on a daily bar (TDS 5.5), each bar's `ts_available` being its session's `ts_close`; any other rule on bars, `session_open` (a bar used before it is complete) among them, gives no constant and is E14, where the bars' availability is first read (decision 2026-11-16, #460). The term values of row s use only information usable at `cutoff(s) = ready_m(s)`. An instrument whose bar has `ts_available > ready_m(s)` is missing in the term values of row s (NaN, counted in the diagnostic `LateBar`) — the values of a row that has already frozen are not changed by data that arrives late, on any rerun and with any later snapshot; where that bar is kept and which reader sees it is the next bullet. That a frozen row does not change holds between snapshots that hold the same bars for the rows read. A bar that a later snapshot replaced is not late data but another history of that row: bars carry no versions in v1 (TDS 5.5). With the same `ts_available` the row shows the replacement's value; with one later than `ready_m(s)` the cell is NaN and counted in `LateBar`; a run is reproduced from the snapshot it recorded (`manifest.json`, 5.9.2) (decision 2026-11-17, #494). When decision d reads values of market m, it uses the latest row `s = row_m(d)` with `ready_m(s) <= decision_ts(d)`. Because `ready` is a declared constant and not a data value, deleting the data after τ does not change the row choice (INV-02). Since `decision_ts >= cutoff(s)`, INV-01 holds.
- **Three times, storage and visibility** [decision 2026-11-04]: an observation has three times — the **observed time** (the session s it belongs to), the **available time** (its `ts_available`) and the **reference time** of a reader (the time up to which that reader may know data; the same reference time the resolver takes, 5.3.3a). The block keeps the observation of session s in row s together with its `ts_available` (5.3.1); an observation of s is never placed in row s+1. A term that reads column cells sees, at row t, a cell (for a window term, each cell of its window) only when that cell's `ts_available <= ready_m(t)`. A term that reads another term's values (`shift`, a window over a term, an element-wise operation) reads the values that term has at each row, each fixed at its own row's `ready_m` (decisions 2026-11-16, #240, and 2026-11-17, #492). No term value of row t therefore holds information later than `ready_m(t)`. So a late bar of s is NaN in row s for the term values, and the window of a later row t whose `ready_m(t)` reaches its `ts_available` reads it at its own position s (G-TIME-LATE, 8.4). A reader with a later reference time — the report and `reconcile` — sees the cell as stored. A block query policy that reads with the snapshot's whole knowledge (`latest`) is not in v1.
- So a user's value table belongs to its package's one market (#725 Decision 223) and reaches only that market's decisions; a cross-market term cannot read one (E17), since another market's sleeve may fill before the value's market has closed [A-02].

#### 5.1.3 Decision time: the reference clock and sleeves [9.0 Q23, R-62, Q1]

| `clock` | Calendar of the decision schedule | `decision_ts` | Row each sleeve reads |
|---|---|---|---|
| `"<M>_close"` (multi-market default = the first market when the snapshot's `markets` keys are sorted by UTF-8 bytes — it does not rely on JSON object order, and the resolved value is fixed in the manifest; `KR_close` for KR+US) | the calendar of market M | `ts_close(M, d) + Δ_M` | M: d. Another market m: the last session with `ready_m(s) <= decision_ts` (for example, at `KR_close` US is usually d−1, at `US_close` KR is usually the same day d) |
| `market_local` | each sleeve its own calendar | `ready(d)` of that sleeve's market | its own market's d |

- A strategy with only one market is `market_local` whatever the `clock` (no notice).
- Each decision builds a `DecisionContext`:

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class DecisionContext:
    decision_id: str            # f"{sleeve_id}:{session_date:%Y%m%d}:{clock}"
    snapshot_id: str
    snapshot_semantic_hash: str  # TDS 4.5 semantic hash
    sleeve_id: str              # market key (TDS markets)
    calendar_id: str            # schedule calendar
    session_date: date          # session date of the schedule calendar
    decision_ts: datetime       # UTC
    clock: str
    availability_policy: str    # tds.json policies.availability_default (+ per-dataset rules in the manifest)
    scheduled: bool             # is the decision on the schedule (False for a screen preview)
    anchor_decision_id: str | None  # starting point of the target-state recursion (None when the recursion is not used) [SC-02]
```

- **Execution session**: an order from decision d can first fill in the first session of that sleeve's market with `ts_open > decision_ts` [11.4.1]. At `KR_close` the US sleeve fills at the US open of the same date, and **when there is no cross-market term** this is the same information and the same fill as `market_local`'s "US d−1 close decision → fill at the d open". The two modes differ in the schedule calendar (whose market's month end is the month end) and in the information of cross-market terms (`across_markets`, the exchange-rate time of `.to(ccy)`).
- Even when the decision schedule falls on a holiday of that sleeve's market (for example `KR_close`, a Korean holiday on a day the US trades), the decision follows the schedule calendar. The US sleeve decides only on Korean session dates.

#### 5.1.4 Date-only values [11.4.1; #725 Decisions 223, 230 to 235; ADR 0047]
A value that carries only a date D, a row of the user's value tables, belongs to the package's one market, declared once, and D is that market's local date (#725 Decision 223). It is read as known before that market's next session after D opens. The engine keeps it in the row of D, or of the first session after D when D is not a session (one day late, never early; where two dates land on one row, the later date's value wins); its `ts_available` is that row's freeze time `ready_m` (5.1.2), so the daily decision at that row's close uses it, truncation (P-FUT, INV-02) cuts it with its row, and 5.1.2's visibility rule needs no exception. That decision's orders fill in that market's next session at the earliest (5.1.3, 5.6.4), so no fill precedes the value; no other market's sleeve reads it (5.1.2).
- **The user's date**: the user writes the date a value was published, an evening publication on its own date; the time of day is not asked and not checked. A value published elsewhere is moved to this market's date by the user (for example a filing at 21:00 New York time on D is dated D+1 in a KR file, KR's close of D having passed).
- **Why one day and not two**: the decision is at the close and its orders fill at the next session, so the day between knowing and trading is already the engine's; dating a value one more session later would count that day twice.
- **Its pair in evaluation**: `evaluate`'s forward returns from `label_entry = "next_open"` (3.11) start where the value is known. A run with `label_entry = "close"` whose graph reads a value table is refused before it starts (E18), since a return from D's close would hold the reaction to a value published that evening.

#### 5.1.5 Availability rules per data

| Data | Value used at a decision |
|---|---|
| bars (`bars`, freq=1d) | the rows up to `row_m(d)` (freeze time `ready_m`, 5.1.2). The window goes back from that row (bar availability time by the declared rule, usually `ts_end` = `ts_close`) |
| value tables (`nx.Item`) | the value of the item's latest row whose date's row is at or before s, by the engine rule and the carry of 5.3.5; a date-only value is in its date's row (5.1.4) [#725 Decisions 221, 227, 230] |
| `instrument_symbols` | two-time-axis rectangular lookup (5.3.6): `ts_known_from <= cutoff(s) < ts_known_to` and `effective_from <= session_date(s) < effective_to` (4.6) |
| `corporate_actions` | only what the resolver (5.3.3a) resolved at `cutoff(s)` (an event is visible from its existence time). Events with unpublished terms: 5.3.4 |
| account inputs (valuation prices, receivables, terminal recoveries) | only values with `ts_available <= decision_ts` are used in decision sizing (5.6.9, INV-01) |
| `fx_rates` | decision inputs (`.to()`, funding wait): the last observation with `ts_available <= decision_ts`; beyond `max_stale_days_decision`, `.to()` is NaN + a warning, and the funding wait is `FxStaleError` (error E20). Valuation for reports: `ts_event <= valuation time` (after the fact allowed, not a decision input) [11.5] |
| market-level series `Market.series(id)` | up to `row_{m'}(d)` of that instrument's market m' (the same `ready` as bars). If m' = the sleeve market, the same market axis; otherwise gathered at the decision (5.3.2 (6)) [V31-13] |
| `Trad.*` | `availability_by_column`: prior information `session_open` (from that session's decision on), the day's outcome `session_close` |

#### 5.1.6 Aligning start and end [11.4.1]
`start` is aligned forward on the schedule calendar (the first session on or after that date), `end` backward (the last session on or before that date). The run period is the intersection with the segment (6.1). There is no forced liquidation at the end (5.6.14).

### 5.2 Summary of decision and execution

```
session t (market m), phase order session_start < open < intraday < close_fill < close_mark < decision < session_end
 ts_open(t) ──► [session_start: resolver effects · payments · terminations] ─► [open: sells → buys → new exit orders] ─► [intraday: sells → buys (intraday sale proceeds not used) → new exit orders (adverse side only)]
            ─► [close_fill: market at=close, sells → buys] ─► [close_mark: close valuation · trailing · expiry] ─► ts_close(t)
                                         │
                       decision_ts(d) = (clock rule, ready included) ─► signal computation → canonical target → final target → order intent (fixed share quantity, only knowable account values)
                                                          → cancel and replace remaining rebalancing orders → valid from the next open ─► [session_end]
```

### 5.3 Pipeline computation

#### 5.3.1 Market axis and blocks [D2, design 4.4.2]
- Per market a dense block `X[m]: (number of sessions, number of instruments) float64`. Instrument axis = the instruments with `asset_class = equity ∧ is_synthetic = false` listed at least once in that chunk's period (sorted by `instrument_id`; index and synthetic instruments are not put in the block and go only through the benchmark path, 3.3). Cells before listing and after delisting are "absent" (value NaN, existence mask False). At such a cell every term's value is absent too, whatever the operation: NaN for a Factor, unknown for a Filter, the missing code −1 for a Classifier. A later row that reads the cell through `shift` or a window reads it as missing: `shift(k)` gives NaN there, and a window holding such cells applies its own rule for NaN (3.4; `nx.ops.rolling`'s `min_periods`; Kleene `all`/`any`) as it does for a listed cell with no value — so `Price.close.latest.isnan().as_factor()` is NaN, not 1.0, before listing (decision 2026-11-19, #563).
- **Storage** [decision 2026-11-04]: next to the value columns the block keeps the `ts_available` of each cell. The observation of session s stays in row s; a late observation is not moved to a later row. Which cells a term that reads the block sees at row t is the visibility rule of 5.1.2 (`ts_available <= ready_m(t)`), applied when a window is read and not when the block is built, so one block serves every row and every reader; the L0 entry (5.3.9) holds the same `ts_available` array.
- Chunk = a session range (by default a year; smaller than 1 year when the memory budget is short; 5.3.8). Before its first row a chunk reads the rows the plan of 5.3.2 gives for its decisions (the overlap); they may lie in more than one earlier chunk. For a graph with a single window and no `shift` this is at most the tail `W_max − 1` rows of the previous chunk (decision 2026-11-16, #486: windows and shifts add up along a path of the graph, 5.3.2 (2)).
- A block builds the `instrument_id → column number` and `session_ord → row number` mappings in memory. Since the files have no integer keys, the Parquet dictionary index is turned into block column numbers once (a small table the size of the dictionary), and `session_date` gets its row number with `searchsorted` [A-05].

#### 5.3.2 Plan [R-14, 11.7]
1. From the decision grid (schedule × sleeve), find for each market the set of rows needed, `R_m = {row_m(d)}`.
2. Follow the graph from top to bottom, propagating the row set each term needs: if a window-W term needs row set R, its input is `⋃_{r∈R} [r−W+1, r]`; for `shift(k)`, `R − k`. Cross-sectional operations use the same row set [11.7].
3. Pruning: each term computes only the rows it needs. When a daily cross-sectional term is used by a window below it, every daily row that window covers is computed [design 4.4.1, 11.7].
4. Each column is read once, on the rows the plan gives it, and every window reads a slice of that array [design 4.4.2].
5. Terms with the same structural hash share one node. A node is dropped when its reference count reaches 0.
6. `across_markets=True` terms and `Market.series` terms of another market [V31-13]: at each decision the `row_m(d)` values of the markets are gathered and computed. The result is a value on the decision grid and does not return to the market axis. Using such a term as a window or `shift` input is a `TermDomainError` (error E17) when the graph is built.

#### 5.3.3 Adjustment [D3, R-18, C2, INV-11]
Definitions: the price multiplier `m_e` of effective session e = the price factor the resolver (5.3.3a) gave for that event (source TDS `corporate_actions.price_factor`, formula TDS 5.11), `C[x] = Π_{e ≤ x} m_e` (forward cumulative, `C = 1` on the first listed session). The split factor `C_split` is the product over `affects = split_and_total` events only, and the total factor `C_total` the product over all events (`affects ≠ none`). The quantity factor `Q[x] = Π_{e ≤ x} q_e` (`q_e` = the resolver's quantity effect `share_factor`; used for volume, share counts and holding quantities). Volume's `Q` multiplies the `q_e` of `affects = split_and_total` events only, as 5.3.4 names volume among the series quantity events move: a `total_only` or `none` event leaves volume as it is, even when its `share_factor` is null (decision 2026-11-18, #241). Base price `B[s] = raw[s] / C[s]`. `C` is always built from the events; the engine reads no cumulative factor column of a package (TDS K4, 5.5; 2026-11-22, #705). Events with a null `price_factor`: the rule at the end of 4.6.

- **Reference meaning (ground truth)**: the value at s ≤ t in the window of computation day t = `raw[s] · Π_{s < e ≤ t, e available} m_e`. When every event up to t is available, `= raw[s]·C[t]/C[s] = B[s]·C[t]`.
- **Reference implementation** (`compute._kernels_ref.adjusted_window`): for each row t, the window `B[t−W+1..t] · C[t]` (prices, split or total factor), and volume `vol[s]·Q[t]/Q[s]`. For cells with a pending event of 5.3.4 or an event whose resolved version differs by reference time (amendments, cancellations), the product is computed directly from the resolver's event list. User window functions always receive this window for `BoundColumn` inputs; a `Factor` input is read as that factor's values at each row (3.3).
- **Registered-kernel shortcuts**: a kernel declares its adjustment class (the table of 3.4).
  - Homogeneous of degree 0: the result computed on `B` (or the volume version of `B`) = the ground truth. No multiplication per row.
  - Homogeneous of degree 1: row t of the result computed on `B` is multiplied by `C[t]` (for volume, the result computed on the base `vol[s]/Q[s]` is multiplied by `Q[t]`, as the 3.2 `volume` row says).
  - Invariant: computed on raw values. A cell before an event whose `price_factor` is none, when the window crosses that event, is still NaN, as in the reference (4.6, INV-12) (decision 2026-11-18, #241; checked by `tests/property/test_adjust.py::test_P_ADJ_kernel_classes`).
  - **Visibility**: every shortcut reads the window after the per-row visibility mask of 5.1.2: a cell whose `ts_available` is later than `ready_m(t)` is NaN to the shortcut exactly as it is to the reference implementation, so the shortcut equals the reference on data with late cells too (decision 2026-11-17, #241; checked by `tests/property/test_adjust.py::test_P_ADJ_kernel_classes`, whose random late cells are NaN in both paths).
  - Anything else (thresholds, nonlinear mixing, price-level ranks): no shortcut. Computed on the reference path.
- **Proof obligation**: for each registered kernel, the P-ADJ property test (random events, random per-instrument scalars) checks shortcut = reference implementation (relative 1e-12; an algebraically equal transform is not promised to be bit-identical). A kernel that does not pass cannot be registered.

#### 5.3.3a Corporate-action resolver (resolver) [TDS v1.1 5.11]

Adjustment (5.3.3, 5.3.4), share counts (5.3.5a), accounting (5.6.10~5.6.13) and ground truth (5.8) all read events through the one `Snapshot.actions(market, cutoff) -> ResolvedActions`. The resolver is a pure function, and the same `(snapshot semantic hash, market, cutoff)` gives the same result.

1. **Chains**: events are tied into chains by following `revision_of` (the root of a chain = the event without `revision_of`, chain id = the root's `event_id`). A cycle is TDS-A007 (E14).
2. **The version at a reference time**: the **existence time** of version r, `ts_known(r)` = the earliest non-null value of `ts_announced` and `ts_terms_available` (`ts_effective` when both are null — the 4.5 F-10 rule), and the **terms time** `ts_terms(r) = coalesce(ts_terms_available, ts_known(r))`. The version used at reference time c = among the versions with `ts_known(r) <= c`, the one with the latest `ts_known` (on a tie, the last in ascending `event_id`). When there is no such version, the chain is "unknown" at c. v3.1's `ts_pub = coalesce(ts_terms_available, …)` hid an event with late terms entirely until its terms were published, so the pending branch of 4 and G-20 (an entitlement of unknown amount) could not be reached. `ts_effective` is used only when both other times are missing (if an event announced later than its effective time were visible from the effective moment, that would be future information).
3. **Status**: when the chosen version has `status = cancelled`, there is no effect at c. When `announced` and `ts_effective <= c`, no effect + E30 ("effective event still announced", counted). When `effective`, it has effect.
4. **Waiting for terms**: when the chosen version has `ts_terms(r) > c` (existence known but terms unpublished), the price effect waits as in 5.3.4, the entitlement is an **entitlement of unknown amount** (the quantity fixed to the holding at the start of the effective session — the entitlement or obligation remains even if the holding is sold or covered after that), and the quantity effect follows the rule of 6. Amounts already booked from an earlier version are left as they are and corrected by the correction entry of 7 in the first session after the terms are published.
5. **Four effects** (from the one chosen version):
   - Price factor `m_e`: `price_factor` ("none" when null, 4.6).
   - Quantity factor `q_e`: `share_factor` is used **explicitly** (split r → r, stock dividend b → 1 + b, rights and cash events → 1, a merger extinguishes the old instrument's holding + the new instrument's `ratio_new_per_old`). When `share_factor` is null, it is built by the `ratio_new_per_old` rule (TDS 5.11) only for split-like kinds (`split`, `reverse_split`, `stock_dividend`, `bonus_issue`, `capital_reduction`), and for other kinds it is not inferred as the reciprocal of the price factor (in that case the quantity effect is "none" → an unsupported event if held; when the ratio columns are missing, 5.6.1's `decimal_ratio` decides first).
   - Entitlement: cash distribution (`cash_per_share`, currency, `gross_or_net`), the new instrument and ratio to be received.
   - Settlement: `pay_date` (`pay_date_status`), the user's declaration when it is missing (5.6.11).
6. **Timing of effects**: price and quantity effects from `ts_effective` (the start of effective session e). But when the terms of a quantity-changing event (`affects = split_and_total`) are published after it takes effect (TDS-A008 warning), accounting changes the quantity at the start of the first session after the terms are published (orders in between use the old quantity), and the price adjustment goes in from publication on, as in 5.3.4.
7. **Correction of amounts already booked**: when accounting booked a receivable or cash at reference time c1 from version r1, and at a later reference time c2 (> c1) the resolver gives a different version r2 (a different amount or ratio, or `cancelled`), accounting makes a **correction entry** at the start of the first session after c2 (`account_events.kind = action_correction`, amount = r2 effect − r1 effect, in integer minor units; a receivable correction before payment, a cash correction after payment). Past entries are not changed (events are canonical, INV-16).
8. **A001** (retired 2026-11-22, #705): the checker called the same resolver with `c = +∞` and compared the package's `cum_factor_*` ratios, columns that have left TDS (TDS 5.5). Whether the synthetic generator's prices agree with its events is compared with this resolver in the generator's tests (4.8).
9. **Tests**: `tds/tests/test_actions.py::test_resolver_at_cutoffs` (the resolver is `nexusbt_tds.actions`, T0.15a; engine-side consumption is `tests/unit/compute/test_adjust.py` and golden cases) — an amended split ratio, a dividend cancelled after announcement, an amount corrected after payment, a split + cash dividend on the same day, a lapsed right (quantity unchanged), and a missing factor, each at three reference times (before first publication, after the first version, after the amendment); a version whose existence is known and whose terms are late (`::test_late_terms_revision_visible_at_ex_date`), an event with an announcement time not visible first at the effective moment (`::test_existence_time_ignores_effective_when_announced`). Hand-calculated G-43~G-47, a G-20 variant.
10. **Events learned late**: when the `ts_known` of a chain's first version is after effective session e (an after-the-fact filing), accounting adds the entitlements and obligations for the **holding at the start of e** (recomputed from the canonical events `account_events`) at the start of the first session after `ts_known` (past entries are not changed, INV-16). Price adjustment from after `ts_terms`, as in 5.3.4.

#### 5.3.4 Events whose terms are published late [11.4.3 (5)]
When the terms time `ts_terms` of the resolved version (5.3.3a) of event e is `> cutoff(t)`, the window of row t does not include e (those cells take the reference path). For cash distributions (`affects = total_only`) `close_tr` is affected; for quantity events (`split_and_total`, rare, A008 warning) `close`, `close_tr` and `volume`. The `C` shortcut is used only for cells with "no pending event". The engine builds a pending mask of `(instrument, row)` for each market block. Account handling is 5.6.10~5.6.11.

#### 5.3.5 Value tables (`nx.Item.<name>`) [R-63, C3; #725 Decisions 219 to 229; ADR 0048]
Every value outside trading is the user's, used as given [#725 Decisions 204, 205]. It reaches the engine in three value tables of four words (`symbol`, `date`, `item`, `value`), one dataset kind each, partitioned by `market=` and then `item=` (TDS 5.28). The four words are the names the user meets; a package stores `symbol` as `instrument_id` (TDS K1, 4.6) and `item` as the `item=` partition (#725 Decision 248; #752):
- `values`: per symbol and date (`symbol, date, item, value`); `market_values`: market-wide by date (`date, item, value`); `symbol_values`: per symbol, no date (`symbol, item, value`). Key cells are never empty, an item lives in one table only, values are numbers, and there is no entry for labels [#725 Decisions 219].
- **Dates**: a value file belongs to the package's one market and its dates are that market's local dates; how a date becomes available is 5.1.4 [#725 Decisions 223].
- **The engine rule**: a value dated D is used from D's row (the first session after D when D is not a session; where two dates land on one session, the later wins) until the item's next row for that symbol; an empty value ends it [#725 Decisions 221].
- **Stops**: the tools stop on an unknown symbol, a key given twice, an item name outside the naming rule (TDS 5.28), an item in two tables, or an empty key cell; they check nothing else about the values [#725 Decisions 222].
- **Two optional declarations per item**, in the dataset's `params.items` (TDS 5.28 gives their words): a **kind**, per-share or share count, which the engine scales by its split ratios after D (the value is in D's share basis; on an ex-date it is post-split; a non-session date is matched at its own date), and which a value the vendor already restated does not take; a **carry**: to the end (default), N sessions, none, or next listing (a date with any row of the item is a listing day, and an instrument without a row that day is empty from it), which replaces "a missing row means false" [#725 Decisions 225 to 228].
- **Reading**: `nx.Item.<name>` (3.2). A market-wide item stays out of a cross-section's denominator; an undated item may group. `nx.Item.<name>.age()` is the number of sessions since the item's last row for that instrument, NaN when none; it replaces the event terms [#725 Decisions 224]. It keeps counting from that last row after a carry ends and the value is empty. A market-wide item's `.age()` counts from that item's last row and is the same for every instrument [#752].
- **The label**: a result gives, per item read, the last value's date and the age of the values read, with no fixed threshold. Date correctness, restatements, consolidated or separate statements, sums and units are the user's, documented and not checked [#725 Decisions 229].

#### 5.3.5a Share counts and market capitalisation [TDS-D9, TDS-Q4]
- A share count is a `values` item declared of kind share count (5.3.5) [#725 Decisions 225]; `Price.shares_out`, `Price.market_cap` and `share_counts` were removed 2026-11-24 (#752).
- Where a block needs market capitalisation (`weight="value"`, `exclude_microcap`), it is `close_unadjusted[s]` × that item at row s. The item read at row s is in row s's share basis (5.3.5's kind scaling applied up to s), so it pairs with `close_unadjusted[s]`. Without such an item it is E18 before the run; two items declared share count are E18 before the run, naming both.

#### 5.3.6 Symbol periods — rectangular lookup [#725 Decisions 206 to 211; ADR 0050]
`Panel`, `classifications` and `universe_membership`, which this section also served, were removed 2026-11-24 (#752); their values are the value tables of 5.3.5.

**Identity and symbols** (the tools' work; the user gives symbols):
- **The key**: the stored key stays `instrument_id` (4.6). The tools write it as `<MIC>:<ticker>_<YYYYMMDD>`, from the earliest symbol and its `list_date`, else the date of the instrument's first bar. A symbol change that merges no two instruments changes no key; a merge keeps the earlier-listed instrument's key; the same input gives the same key.
