Extending a history back without `list_date` may move a key, and the build report names it. There is no ledger of keys across packages [#725 Decisions 206, 207].
- **Symbol periods** live in `instrument_symbols` only, and the tools write one period at least for every instrument, also one whose symbol never changed. `symbol_changes.csv` and `securities.csv` are inputs of the tools, not dataset kinds. Rows given under an old symbol and dated before the change move to its instrument. `corporate_actions` has no `symbol_change` (4.6) [#725 Decisions 208, 209].
- **Which symbol** (built with stage (2)'s first slice; today's result tables hold `instrument_id`, the header line "Identifier name"): a result table's `symbol` is the symbol in use on the row's date, read from `instrument_symbols`; a symbol with no date (a row of `symbol_values`, a symbol written in code or in a setting) means its latest owner. A result may add the optional column `latest_symbol` [#725 Decisions 209; #752].
- **Reuse**: an instrument list with listing periods (`symbol, list_date, delist_date`, the periods not overlapping) splits one symbol into two instruments. The periods only assign ownership: `delistings` keeps the delisting, and where the periods and the delistings disagree the tool stops. Without listing periods the tool asks only on a long price gap; it asks nothing about symbol changes the user did not declare [#725 Decisions 210, 211].

**The two-time-axis table** `instrument_symbols` uses the **rectangular lookup**. For each identifying key `K` = `(instrument_id, symbol_type)`, the answer to query `(cutoff c, date q)` = the row with `ts_known_from <= c < ts_known_to` **and** `effective_from <= q < effective_to`. There must be exactly one (two or more is TDS-T012, E14 in `full` at open; E14 when met during a run that was opened at the `manifest` level), and none means missing. v2's method of choosing the one row with the largest `ts_known_from` and then checking effectiveness is **not used** (a future-effective row announced in advance hides the currently effective row).

Implementation: for each `K`, the rows are kept as an array sorted by `ts_known_from`; for each query c, the candidate rows meeting the known condition (usually 1~3) are gathered with an interval index (the `ts_known_from <= c` prefix + filtering `ts_known_to > c`) and then filtered by the effective condition. It is not expanded per day.

Fixed examples (tests T-18 and T-19, `tests/unit/data/test_intervals.py`):

| # | Rows (effective / known) | Query (c, q) | Answer |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 announced in advance | A: effective [2019-01-01, ∞) known [2018-12-01, ∞); B: effective [2020-03-20, ∞) known [2020-03-06, ∞), and version A', in which A's `effective_to` changed to 2020-03-20 as known from 2020-03-06 | (2020-03-10, 2020-03-10) | A' (currently effective). B meets the known condition but is not yet effective |
| 2 late discovery | C: effective [2015-06-01, ∞) known [2017-01-01, ∞) | (2016-01-01, 2016-01-01) | none (the old code's row if there is one) |
| 3 amendment | D: effective [2018-01-01, ∞) known [2018-01-02, 2019-05-01); D': the same effective period, only the code differs, known [2019-05-01, ∞) | (2019-06-01, 2018-06-01) | D' (the record believed at that time) |

`history_mode` is in the lookup function's signature (`history_mode="economic"`), and its only v1 value is economic [blueprint chapter 3].

#### 5.3.7 Running user window functions [R-05, design 4.4.3]
- For each row t (only the rows needed after pruning), the input window `(W, N_alive)` is built: from `sliding_window_view(B, W, axis=0)[t]` (no copy), only the alive columns are taken with `np.take` and multiplied by `C[t]` (the product is a new array — the original is safe even if the user modifies it). The set of alive columns is recomputed only at rows where the existence mask changes.
- A return that is not `(N_alive,)` or has a different dtype is a `TermOutputError` (a sub-message of error E8).
- The function must be pure. For a function that reads global arrays or files, the static check leaves an `ImpureFunctionWarning` (error E24) and turns off that node's L1 cache; for constant-like globals (int, float, str, bool, tuple), the value goes into the node hash (6.5, 5.3.9).

#### 5.3.8 Chunks, parallelism, determinism [R-27, 11.7, INV-03]
- By default 1 computation process, numpy threads `[compute] threads`. Chunk size = the largest session range that does not exceed the memory budget (`memory_budget_gb`, **one budget for the whole process tree**: divided by the number of workers when `workers > 1`): a year is tried first, and when even 1 year exceeds it, it is split smaller into quarters, months, then by halving the session count (the 1-year minimum rule is abolished). Estimated bytes = rows × columns × 8 × (maximum number of nodes alive at once + 2). When even one session row exceeds the budget, `ConfigError` (E16, layer config, "memory_budget_gb too small for {n} instruments").
- `workers > 1` is process parallelism by chunk, and it can be turned on only after the chunk-invariance test (P-CHUNK) passes. Results are joined in chunk order.
- Reductions (sums, means) follow the fixed order of 5.3.10. Numba kernels are `parallel=False` (v1). Random numbers are not used (probes use `[checks] seed`).
- **Sleeve independence and column batching** [INV-21]: once the targets (`TargetFrame`) are set, fills and accounting are independent between sleeves (no cash movement between sleeves, 5.6.9). So the runner may batch sleeves, scenarios (κ, delisting lo/hi, capacity multiples, `offsets`) and sweep candidates as **extra columns of the same session loop** (separate state per column, prices and event resolution once) (vectorbt's grouped-column method). The result bits must equal those of separate runs (P-SLEEVE-ISO, P-DET). Exception: a `[taxes] pay = "cash"` batch spanning several markets synchronises every year at the payment session.
- Scenario replays (gross, cost0x, …, capacity) and the execution of sweep candidates are independent of each other, so they can run as parallel processes when `workers > 1`, and results are merged in scenario-name order and `candidate_key` order. Default `workers = 1` (OQ-14).
- **What may run in parallel** (decision 2026-11-04, #152): an item of work runs in parallel with others when it reads only immutable inputs and writes a new object — chunks, fits, grid candidates, sweep candidates, CPCV paths, holdout segments, scenarios. It runs in sequence when it reads state another item writes — the sessions of one account, the test windows of a walk-forward account, the updates of a model that learns online. Being a fit does not make work sequential, and being a fold does not make it parallel; the state it reads decides (6.3a "State per run, fold and path"). Results are merged by key (`fit_id`, `candidate_key`, path number, scenario name, chunk order), never by completion order, and seeds come from `run_seed:fit_id` (5.3.11), so completion order cannot change a seed, a selection, a tie-break or an output order. Shared inputs (snapshot blocks, L0 and L1 entries, Pipeline values) are read-only. With `workers > 1` each worker runs `[compute] threads = 1` and `[ml] threads` as configured, and the manifest records `workers` and both thread counts; the memory budget stays one budget for the process tree.

#### 5.3.9 Cache [R-12, C9, INV-05, SC-15]
There are three layers: **L0** the raw block cache, **L1** the term value cache, **L2** the run outputs (`runs/`, 5.9).
- **L0** (implemented in M6, key rule fixed from M1): dense `float64` matrices per market × column × year (including the existence mask, `obs_status` codes, the per-cell `ts_available` of 5.3.1, `C_split`, `C_total` and `Q`), kept as uncompressed Arrow IPC files in `<runs.dir>/.cache/l0/`. Key = sha256(canonical JSON{**the resolution metadata hash of that dataset** (that dataset's `kind`, `schema_version`, `params`, `availability`, `pit_quality`, `key` and column units in `tds.json`, the package `policies`, and only the **stable fields** of `markets` — that market's `calendar_id`, `currency`, `timezone`; the watermark `complete_through*` is not included (it changes every day and made the hit rate 0 in v3.1)), **the file sha256 of the files of that (market, year) partition** (`files[].sha256`, and for a file that declares none, which TDS 4.2 allows, the sha256 the engine computes from its bytes (decision 2026-11-16, #480: hashing nothing would let changed bytes keep the key, INV-05); the optional `value_sha256` is at the H004 warning level, so a wrong one does not block, and it is not used as identity), **the corporate-action chain hash of that market** (for every chain with at least one version whose effective session `ex_date` (TDS 5.11: the session whose open is the default of `ts_effective`) is on or before the end of that year (decision 2026-11-10, #356: the session, as `nexusbt_tds.actions.chain_digest` reads it, not the instant `ts_effective`), the canonical JSON of `event_id`, `instrument_id`, `kind`, `affects`, `ts_known`, `ts_terms`, `status`, `ts_effective` and the four effects of **every version** — an amendment of a middle version or a correction of only a publication time changes the key too; the `C` and `Q` accumulations and the resolution per reference time depend on this), **the axis-row hash of that block** (user decision 2026-11-04: listing periods and sessions set the block's shape, so the key hashes the rows the block depends on, not the sha256 of the axis files, which changes for every year of the market when one row changes. (a) That market's `instruments` rows whose listing period `[listed_on, delisted_on]` (an empty `delisted_on` is open-ended) intersects the year's session range, over the fields the block reads, `instrument_id`, `listed_on`, `delisted_on`, `asset_class` and `is_synthetic`, sorted by `instrument_id`. (b) That market's `calendar_sessions` rows of the year and of the whole previous year, sorted by `session_date`, over every column but `calendar_version` (user decision 2026-11-04: the column is informational and a producer may rewrite it on every publish, TDS 5.4; the engine reads it on no path, so a snapshot that rewrites it on past rows changes no year's key): the previous year stands for the overlap tail of 5.3.1, and taking the whole year rather than `W_max − 1` rows keeps the key independent of the graph; a window longer than a year reaches earlier years through the input L0 keys of L1 below. A calendar without `calendar_sessions` rows does not reach a key: the open refuses it (4.4 step 4; F-02, whose built rows (b) held (#480), retired 2026-11-23, #705). The previous year's `instruments` rows are not in the key: the year's columns come from the instruments whose listing period intersects the year (the rows of (a); 5.3.1), and their existence in the overlap rows follows from fields already hashed. Each row is serialised with the canonical value rules of TDS 4.5), market, column, year (no edition since 2026-11-27, #752), block version `l0-v4`, engine `nexusbt.__version__`}). When the producer reuses an unchanged partition by cloning or hard link (TDS 4.1), the file sha256 is the same, so this key still matches. Even when a new snapshot comes out every day, the L0 of past partitions that did not change still hits, and an axis change misses only the years the table below names (incremental reuse like Qlib's disk cache; unlike Qlib, a past correction changes the file sha256, the chain hash or the axis-row hash and misses automatically). Reads are **read-only memory maps**, and user window functions get only copies (5.3.7). Deleting a snapshot does not delete L0 at once (another snapshot may share it through the same file sha256): beyond the `cache_max_gb` limit, entries referenced by no remaining snapshot are deleted first, then the least recently used. Each entry also records the source file identity `(dev, inode, size, mtime_ns)`, checked on open (4.2). `Snapshot.market_block` hides L0 (no change to the public API).

**What an axis change invalidates** (L0 of that market; every other year keeps its hits; P-CACHE-INCR checks each row):

| Change in the new snapshot | L0 years that miss | Why |
|---|---|---|
| tomorrow's session added (a new `calendar_sessions` row in the current year) | the current year only | the row is in that year's range; no later year has a block yet |
| a new name listed (a new `instruments` row) | the years its listing period touches, from the year of `listed_on` to the year of `delisted_on` or the current year | its row enters the hashed rows of each of those years; a name listed this year misses this year only |
| an old listing date corrected (`listed_on` or `delisted_on` of an existing row) | the years the old period or the new period touches | the row's hashed fields change in each year it was or now is in |
| a session row of year Y corrected, added or removed | Y and Y+1 | the row is in Y's range and in Y+1's previous-year rows (the overlap tail) |
| `calendar_version` rewritten on past rows, nothing else changed | none | the axis-row hash (b) leaves the column out |

- **L1** key = sha256(canonical JSON{node structural hash, the input L0 keys — the hash of the L0 keys of every (market, year) partition reached by the **input row set** with windows and `shift` propagated to the end of the graph (5.3.2) (not only the year of the output rows: the 252-session window of a January row depends on the previous year's partition) — and the computed row mask, all the file sha256 of the datasets without year partitions that the node reads (`fx_rates`, `symbol_values` and so on), except `calendar_sessions`, and except `instruments` for a node that reads only the fields the L0 axis-row hash covers (`instrument_id`, `listed_on`, `delisted_on`, `asset_class`, `is_synthetic`; a node that reads another field, such as `Meta.category` or `Meta.currency`, keeps the `instruments` file sha256), no external inputs (`from_parquet` was removed 2026-11-24, #752 [G-105]), market, the resolved query policy (availability rule, `ready_delay`, adjustment policy, the `fx` series choice), only for a node that reads the absolute `session_ord` (`nx.every`, definition B of 5.3.10) and every node downstream of one, **the `session_ord` of the first session of its input row set**, the per-item declarations of the value tables it reads (5.3.5), precision, kernel semantics version (`kernel_semantics`, ADR-0019 A/B), engine `nexusbt.__version__`, and, only for an `nx.ml.factor` node and every node downstream of one, **the learning context** (user decision 2026-11-04): the `fit_id` and the seed (5.3.11 determinism (1)) of each fit whose predictions reach the node's rows; for `nx.ml.pretrained` the same, its `fit_id` carrying the model identity (name, version, `training_cutoff`, `corpus`)}). **The calendar reaches L1 only through the input L0 keys** (user decision 2026-11-04): the calendar identity `calendar_id` is in each input L0 key's resolution metadata, and the `calendar_sessions` rows the input row set covers, over every column but `calendar_version`, are in the input L0 keys' axis-row hashes, as are the `instruments` rows over the fields that hash covers; the L1 key holds no calendar version and no file sha256 of `calendar_sessions`. So tomorrow's session row misses only the L1 entries whose input rows reach the current year, and a session row of a past year Y corrected misses only those whose input rows reach Y or Y+1; term values whose input rows lie only in earlier years hit. A session row of year Y added or removed misses those too, and renumbers every later session: with a `session_ord` column the later rows change and their years miss in L0; without one, the first `session_ord` below makes the nodes that read the number miss. The first `session_ord` covers what the input years' rows cannot show: when the engine numbers the sessions itself (no `session_ord` column; 3.7 `nx.every`, 4.6), a session added or removed in an earlier year shifts every later number. A node without a learned input carries no learning context and keeps its key. With the learning context, two CPCV combinations whose training samples differ never share an L1 entry of a learned node (5.3.11), and two runs that differ only in `run_seed` do not serve each other's predictions; a cold L1 and a warm L1 give the same validation numbers (P-CACHE-FIT). The `fit_id` in the learning context is the fit's content identity, not derived from the partition keys (decision 2026-11-04, #270): an L1 entry of a learned node is keyed by `fit_id` together with the input L0 keys, not by the partition keys alone, so a partition whose file changed still misses and is recomputed, and a recomputation that yields the same training X and y yields the same `fit_id` and seed, hence the same predictions, and no second fit within the run (5.3.11). Node structural hash = operator name + parameters + input node hashes + the user function's canonical source + **the captured constant global values** (the rule of 6.5). A node that reads a non-constant global does not use the L1 cache (E24).
- Value = an Arrow IPC file of the market axis `(row range, computed row mask, instrument_id, value)` (a Filter node stores the value together with the validity mask — a three-valued round trip). When the computed row mask does not cover all the requested rows, it is not a hit (missing rows are not filled). Chunk placement is not in the key (since chunking does not change results). Writes go to a temporary file → `rename`. When the size exceeds `cache_max_gb`, the least recently used are deleted first. The cache may be deleted at any time. L0 + L1 together do not exceed `cache_max_gb`.

#### 5.3.10 Numeric determinism rules [SC-03, INV-03, INV-04, INV-13]
Registered kernels (built-in factors, operators, cross-sectional operations, statistics) follow the rules below. User window functions are required only to give "same window input → same output" (INV-03).
The bit promise of registered kernels is about chunking, permutation, caching and reruns in **the same environment** (same `uv.lock`, same CPU kind and OS). There is no bit promise between different environments (the tolerance is set by measurement, using the 8.1 L2 criteria).

1. **Window kernels**: output row t is computed only from the values of window `[t−W+1, t]`, as a left fold in ascending row order (`acc = acc + x[r]`, float64) (**ADR-0019 definition A**). Cumulative-sum differences across chunk boundaries (`cumsum[t] − cumsum[t−W]`) and running sums carried from the previous row (add and subtract) are not used (rounding would depend on the chunk start and break INV-03, and rounding traces of observations outside the window would remain and break "a function of the values in the window only" (INV-14)). **The pre-registered alternative definition B** (block partial sums): partial sums of blocks fixed by the absolute session number (`session_ord // 64`) are built as left folds, and a window = the left fold of the two end pieces + the left fold of the inner block sums. It uses only values in the window and anchors to absolute numbers, so chunk and start-date invariance hold bit for bit. If in the M1 bench (T1.24) the computation of all daily rows of `SMA(252)` exceeds the stage budget, it switches to B by ADR (a `kernel_semantics` version bump → the L1 key changes, only the P-KERNEL expected values change).
2. **Fast window kernels** allow only methods bit-identical (in the same environment) to the reference definition: refolding each window (Numba O(W)), block partial sums in the same association order if definition B is adopted, integer counts, and monotone queues for minimum and maximum. O(1) updates of sums, means and variances are not used. For variance, the two-pass definition (mean first, then the sum of squared deviations) is the reference.
2a. **Numeric classes**: the arithmetic class (+ − × ÷ sqrt, min/max, comparisons) is bit-identical in the same environment. For the transcendental class (`log`, `log1p`, `exp`, `pow`), the reference and fast paths use **the same compiled element function** (`nx.compute._elem`, compiled once with Numba; the reference path too calls the compiled version, not `.py_func`), so it is bit-identical in the same environment and relative 1e-12 across environments. Power weights (`decay^k` of `EWMA`) are constant arrays outside the kernel.
3. **Cross-sectional reductions** (mean, standard deviation, sum, quantiles) gather values in **sorted `instrument_id` order**, not memory column order, and then left-fold (sorted index; ADR-0019) [OQ-5]. The exact sum (`math.fsum`) is used only for comparison in reference tests.
4. The reference implementation does not rely on the pairwise summation order of `np.sum`, `np.mean`, `np.nanmean` or `np.add.reduce` (the order changes with array length and alignment). It is written as an explicitly ordered loop (Numba `py_func` or pure Python).
5. Numba uses `fastmath=False`, `parallel=False`, `error_model="numpy"`. Operations that call BLAS (matrix multiplication, 2-D `np.dot`, `lstsq`) are not used in v1 kernels (the bits change with the BLAS thread count and implementation).
6. The later optimizer output (`nx.portfolio.optimize`, reserved) is an exception to bit determinism: the solver name, version and tolerance are written in the manifest, weights are rounded at 1e-10 and small negatives set to 0, and a solver failure is a failure (not replaced by equal weights). Comparisons are bit-exact only with the same solver version on the same machine; otherwise within tolerance [SC-22].
7. Tests: P-KERNEL (fast = reference, bit-identical in the same environment; for the transcendental class, a check that the same element function is used), P-CHUNK (including chunks smaller than 1 year), P-PERM (shuffling memory column order), P-START (INV-14).
8. **Swappable implementations**: other implementations of window and cross-sectional kernels (Numba fast kernels, a Rust crate and so on, the 2.4 seam) keep the definitions of this section (summation order, element functions) as they are and must be bit-identical to the reference in the same environment (`test_kernel_swap_bit_equal`).

#### 5.3.11 ML factor node (`nx.ml.factor`) [user decision 2026-10-28]
- **Place**: the registered special Pipeline node `MLFactor`. Input = feature nodes (Factors with guaranteed timing) + a label node (`label`: `"fwd_return"`, `"fwd_return_adj"`, `labels.triple_barrier(...)`). Output = predictions per decision × instrument (float64), used like an ordinary Factor (`.rank(mask=)`, `signal=`, `nx.evaluate(pred)` — counted as a factor candidate). It is not a window-style user function (the engine manages its state, so it is not subject to the start-date probe E32).
- **Samples**: for each decision d, the rows sorted by (d, `instrument_id`) for which the universe (for a strategy its `universe`, for evaluate `universe=`) is true and all features are finite. Missing features are not filled (INV-12; the user uses `fillna` if needed).
- **Refit schedule**: `refit` (a Schedule, anchored to the calendar, INV-14). Refit time τ_k = the decision time of that session. Predictions use that fit's result for decisions with `τ_k ≤ decision_ts < τ_{k+1}`. Predictions for decisions before the first fit are NaN and are counted in `skips` (reason `ml_no_model`).
- **Training window rule (enforced by the engine)**: the training samples at τ = the (d, i) for which (a) d is in the window — for `train=nx.ml.rolling("5y")`, `decision_ts(d) ∈ (τ − 5y, τ]`; for `nx.ml.expanding(min_length="10y")`, from the first decision in the snapshot where the features are finite up to τ (the first fit after the length 10y is filled) — (b) `embargo` counts sessions: with N = the number of the market session containing `max(ts_label_end, ts_label_available)` (5.8), or of the next session when that time falls between sessions, the `ts_close` of session N + `embargo` is ≤ τ (the numbers of G-92 follow this formula), (c) the label is finite. A gap as long as the label period arises by itself, and the user does not compute a shift. The window is set by the snapshot data, not by the run's start date (decisions before the run start can be training samples too — so moving the start date leaves the fit at the same τ the same, INV-14).
- **E2 ports and E29**: a label node is accepted only at two ports, `evaluate(target=)` and `ml.factor(label=)`. A label that passed this port is used only by the rule above, and the prediction output does not propagate the label marking; reaching it by another path is E2 as now. **Training is always forward-only** (user decision 2026-10-28): in any validation method (walk-forward, CPCV, CSCV, segments), the training samples at τ follow only rules (a)~(c) above — there are no samples after τ (the same shape as live operation; AFML-style "training after the test block" is not used for ML training). In addition, (d) in CPCV, the decisions in that split's **test blocks** (including those before τ), the decisions whose label interval overlaps a test block (purging), and the decisions within `embargo` sessions after a test block's last decision (embargo, 6.3a) are removed — this is where the splitter's `embargo` has meaning in ML. Rule (d) holds for every fit of the split, whichever decision it serves: with strict IS scores (6.3a, #215) the predictions that a split's training decisions are scored on come from these fits too, never from the linear run's fits, which follow (a)~(c) only. Walk-forward does **not** cut the window to the fold's training range (a `refit` inside the test window uses the samples up to that time = a simulation of operation). A setting under which samples overlapping the test block's labels could enter (for example a label that purging cannot stop because `embargo` is shorter than the label period) is E29 before the run — v3.2's reserved check 8 (6.5) becomes concrete with this rule. The report's leakage ledger has one line `ml fits: forward-only (no samples after refit time)`. The two-sided training option (`ml_two_sided`) is v1.x (12.1).
- **Model contract** [decision 2026-11-04, #195]: a trained model has `fit(X, y, sample_weight=None)`, `predict(X)`, `get_params()` and `set_params(**p)`; for a classification label it also has `predict_proba(X)` and the attribute `classes_` (the class values in the column order of `predict_proba`, read after every fit). Label selectors: `label=nx.labels.triple_barrier(...).ret` (default, regression), `.side` (classification), and `evaluate(target=)` takes the same selectors; in v1 classification applies to `.side` only, and every other label is a regression label. The engine does not import scikit-learn (the optional dependency `nexusbt[ml]` is for examples and tests).
- **A new unfitted model for each fit** (clone protocol) [decision 2026-11-04]: `model=` is either (1) a **factory**, a callable with no arguments that returns a new unfitted model, which the engine calls once per fit, or (2) a model object with `__sklearn_clone__()` (every scikit-learn ≥ 1.3 estimator, `Pipeline` included, which returns an unfitted deep copy with the same parameters) or with `clone()`, which the engine calls once per fit. The engine fits only what the factory or the clone returns, never the object passed in, so nothing one fit learned (an imputer's means, a scaler's scale, a PCA basis, a selected feature set) reaches another. An object with neither is E16 before the run ("model {cls} cannot be cloned" / "pass model=lambda: {cls}(...)"); a factory or clone that returns an object this run has already fitted (the same `id`) is E41 ("{node} returned a model already fitted in this run" / "build a new object inside the factory"). v3.4's `type(model)(**model.get_params())` is not used: for a nested model, `get_params()` (default `deep=True`) returns keys such as `scaler__with_mean` that the constructor does not take, and `get_params(deep=False)` returns the inner step objects themselves, which every fit would then share. A pretrained model (below) is never fitted, so it is not cloned.
- **What a fit may learn** [decision 2026-11-04]: every step inside the model's `fit` — imputation, scaling, PCA, feature selection, clustering, early stopping on an inner validation split, inner tuning such as a grid search — sees only that fit's training samples (rules (a)~(d)), because the engine passes only those rows; changing the features of decisions outside the training samples changes no statistic a fit learned. The engine does not see inside the model: its inner splits and tuning are **not purged** by the engine, so labels that overlap in time can sit on both sides of an inner split (a time-ordered inner split with a gap of at least the label period avoids it). The report's leakage ledger adds the line `ml inner: model-internal splits and tuning are not purged by the engine` under `ml fits: forward-only`.
- **Classification and the score** [decision 2026-11-04]: probabilities are matched to classes by the values in `classes_`, never by column position, so a model that orders its classes differently gives the same score. A label declares its classes: `.side` declares (−1, 0, +1). `score=` turns the probabilities into the prediction: `"p_up"` = p(+1), `"p_down"` = p(−1), `"p_up_minus_down"` = p(+1) − p(−1). With three or more declared classes `score` is required, since the engine does not decide what the time-expiry class 0 means (E16 before the run: "label {name} has 3 classes" / "choose score='p_up' | 'p_down' | 'p_up_minus_down'"); for a label declaring two classes, `score=None` is the probability of the larger class value (the v3.4 rule). `score` with a regression label is E16. A declared class that a fit's training samples did not contain has probability 0 (the model has no column for it); a `classes_` value outside the declared classes, or a probability that is not finite, is E41. The output of `predict_proba` is checked after every call, before log-loss and Brier (5.7) or the score read it: shape `(n, len(classes_))`, every value finite and in [0, 1], every row summing to 1 within 1e-6, no duplicate value in `classes_`; a breach is E41 ("{node} returned probabilities outside the contract: {what}" / "check predict_proba: rows must sum to 1 over classes_"), test `tests/unit/ml/test_scoring.py::test_predict_proba_contract` (T6.24) (#310). A training window whose finite labels hold **one class only** is not fitted: the decisions that fit would serve get NaN predictions and the skips reason `ml_single_class`, the run's E40 notice counts such fits, and the previous fit is not carried forward. `score` is part of the node's structural hash (so of its L1 key and of `graph_hash`), not of `fit_id`: two scores over the same training share the fit.
- **Pretrained models** [user decision 2026-10-28]: a model that only predicts without training (time-series foundation models such as TimesFM, Chronos and Moirai, models trained outside) is wrapped with `nx.ml.pretrained(model, *, training_cutoff: str, corpus: str, allow_overlap: bool = False)` and put into `nx.ml.factor(model=...)` (it need not have `fit`; the engine calls only `predict`). **Signature rule** [checked by Sol]: a trained model requires `label`, `train` and `refit` (E16 when missing), and a pretrained model must have all three as `None` (E16 "pretrained model is not trained by the engine" when given); a pretrained model's predictions can still be scored with `evaluate(target=)`. **`training_cutoff` is required** (the last date of the pretraining data; E16 "pretrained model needs training_cutoff" when absent): if the pretraining data contains the test period, that is future information. **Boundary time** [checked by Sol]: take the last session s₀ on or before the `training_cutoff` date in the schedule calendar of the sleeve market, and the `ts_close` of the `embargo`-th session after s₀ is the boundary B (the `ts_close` of s₀ when `embargo` = 0). If there is even one decision with `decision_ts ≤ B`, the run is **blocked** (E48 `PretrainedLeakError`, failing before the run, showing the number of blocked decisions and the first and last dates). The boundary time itself is blocked too. With `allow_overlap=True` it is not blocked: E49 warning + the `pretrained_overlap` marking on those decisions + the report header line "pretrained model {name} saw data up to {cutoff}; {n} decisions before it are marked" and the manifest `assumptions`. In validation (6.3a), overlapping paths and folds are marked separately in the distribution. The model identity (name, version, `training_cutoff`, `corpus`) goes into both `fit_id` and **the strategy's `graph_hash`**, so a change of version, cutoff date or data counts as a new candidate (6.2). The identity is also the pretrained node's learning context in the L1 key (5.3.9); no hash of the model file is taken, since the engine neither reads nor saves model files (2.6), so a user who changes a model's weights gives it a new name or version, or its L1 entries are served as before (user decision 2026-11-04). `allow_overlap=True` is **an explicit exception to INV-01**: declared by the user, allowed only with the manifest `assumptions` and the marking, and never on by default.
- **Determinism**: (1) seed = `int.from_bytes(sha256(f"{run_seed}:{fit_id}")[:4], "big")`, put into every `get_params()` key that is `random_state` or ends in `__random_state` (the steps of a nested model) of the object the factory or clone returned. (2) Threads during fit and predict = `[ml] threads` (default 1): `n_jobs` gets that value if present, and `OMP_NUM_THREADS`, `OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS` and `MKL_NUM_THREADS` are pinned too; when not 1, the manifest's `nondeterministic_threads` and E44. (3) Sample order = (decision_ts, `instrument_id`). (4) Known nondeterministic paths such as GPUs are only marked (E44).
- **Fit id and reuse** (decision 2026-11-04, #270): `fit_id` identifies a fit by the inputs it received, so rows the fit never read leave it unchanged and a correction of a value it read changes it. `fit_id` = the first 16 characters of sha256(canonical JSON{the canonical hash (TDS 4.5 value rules) of the training X and y as passed to the model's outer `fit` — after the engine's masking, in sample order (decision_ts, `instrument_id`), before any preprocessing step inside the model, which the model specification covers —, the training samples (first and last decision, count, hash of the sample keys), the feature node hashes, the label definition hash, the model specification (the class path and the normalised `get_params()` of the object the factory or clone returned, library names and versions), the seed rule, the resolved policies the fit depends on (availability rule, `ready_delay`, adjustment policy, the per-item declarations of 5.3.5)}). The snapshot semantic hash is not in `fit_id`: it is the `fits.parquet` column `snapshot_semantic_hash` and the manifest's snapshot record, as provenance. So a snapshot that only adds rows the fit did not read (later rows of the same year partition included) gives the same `fit_id` and seed, and the same predictions for every decision at or before τ, and a corrected feature or label value of a training sample gives a new `fit_id` and seed and the earlier fit is not reused; its predictions may change and are not required to (P-FUT). Within one run, the same `fit_id` is not fitted again (overlapping walk-forward windows, the same training combination in CPCV). It is not reused between runs (since model objects are not saved).
- **L1 and the learning context** (user decision 2026-11-04): `fit_id` and the fit's seed are in the L1 key of the `nx.ml.factor` node and of every node downstream of it (5.3.9); nodes without a learned input keep their key. Purging and embargo remove different decisions in each CPCV combination, so the training sample keys differ, and **two CPCV combinations never share an L1 entry of a learned node** unless their training samples are the same (then the fit itself is shared, as above). A cold L1 and a warm L1 give the same predictions and validation numbers (P-CACHE-FIT).
- **Records (pickle forbidden, 2.6)**: `predictions.parquet` (decision_id, instrument_id, node, value, fit_id, probs — the class probabilities in the fit's `classes` order, null for regression), `fits.parquet` (fit_id, node, tau, train_first, train_last, n_samples, n_purged, n_embargoed, model_class, params_json, lib_versions, seed, threads, fit_seconds, hash_seconds, pred_sha256, snapshot_semantic_hash, classes — null for regression, score, clone ∈ {`factory`, `sklearn_clone`, `clone`}, provenance_level — below), the manifest's `ml` field. No model files or joblib.
- **Provenance level of a fit** (decision 2026-11-04, #216): `fits.parquet.provenance_level` says, as far as the engine records it (choices made by hand are not seen, 6.2), how far the fit and the choices around it stand outside the decisions it predicts. It has four values, ranked from the lowest to the highest `in-sample` < `model-fit-OOS` < `full-procedure-OOS` < `frozen-policy`, and a fit takes the first that applies in the order (1) to (4); a pretrained model, which is never fitted, has one row under the `fit_id` that carries its identity: (1) `in-sample`: the fit serves a decision inside what it was trained on — a pretrained model run with `allow_overlap=True` that has a `pretrained_overlap` decision; (2) `frozen-policy`: the run's evaluation label is "independent final test (look 1)" (6.2), so the model settings, the parameters and the method were fixed by the preregistration before the data was first seen; (3) `full-procedure-OOS`: the fit serves the test range of a walk-forward fold or of a CPCV split of the run's primary evaluation (6.3a), and every choice the engine made for that range (the `grid` point) read only that fold's training samples; (4) `model-fit-OOS`: every other fit — it is forward-only (no training sample after τ), and the parameters and settings around it were not chosen inside a training range the engine can see; for a pretrained model with no overlap this is the level as declared, since its cutoff is declared and not audited. Every prediction row carries `fit_id`, so the level of a fit's predictions is a join on `fits.parquet`, not a column: no bytes are added per row and the tiers of 5.9.1 stay as they are. The manifest's `ml.provenance_levels` holds the count of fits per level. A fit that the primary evaluation and the linear reference share (the same `fit_id`) has one row, with the level of its use in the primary evaluation, and it is counted once, at that level. The join gives the level of the fit; which evaluation read a prediction is known to the line or score that read it, not to the row. So a score computed from predictions (the IC, log-loss or Brier of an ML factor, a fold's OOS metrics, a path row) names the lowest level among the fits it read, and a line or score of the linear reference of a run that has one names `model-fit-OOS` at most, whatever the join gives: the cap is applied by the role `reference` (6.3a), and the `reference` line says so. A fold's IS metrics are `in-sample` by definition. In a CPCV run whose `grid` was chosen on `approximate` IS scores (6.3a, #215), the choice for a test range read a linear run whose model had seen that range's labels, so condition (3) fails and the fits that serve the split's test ranges are `model-fit-OOS`; with `strict` IS scores condition (3) holds. `ValidationResult.is_scores` says how the IS scores were computed, and the approximation sentence of 6.3a stays beside the level in `Run.provenance` when the mode is not `strict`.
- **rerun**: fits again and compares `pred_sha256` per fit. With threads = 1 they must be equal (E15 "prediction hash differs for fit {id}" when different); otherwise a comparison with relative tolerance 1e-9 + a notice. When a library version differs, `fit_id` differs and the result is "no fit", so the E15 sentence gets "lib {name} {v1} ≠ {v2}; rerun needs the locked environment" appended.
- **Cost**: fit time is outside the chapter 7 budgets (reported on a separate line). The cost of hashing is measured, not assumed (#270): `hash_seconds` (computing `fit_id`) is recorded beside `fit_seconds`, and the chapter 7 result JSON's `fits` block carries the sums of both (T1.24, T6.11). If on the T6.11 workload with an `nx.ml.factor` node (the `ml-factor` example) the sum of `hash_seconds` exceeds 10% of the sum of `fit_seconds`, an ADR adopts the recorded fallback identity in place of the X and y hash: the training sample keys plus, per partition read, the value hash of the rows the fit read. When the number of fits in a run exceeds `[validate] max_fits`, E40 before the run (does not block).
- `nx.explain` explains values and trading decisions (5.9.9); it is **not predictive importance** and says nothing about which inputs carry out-of-sample information. Probability scoring of a classification factor is in v1 (log-loss and Brier, 5.7). MDA feature importance (`ml.importance`) is v1.x (reserved) and the first of the importance tools: permutation inside each split's allowed range, scored on OOS predictions, with the null it tests named. Clustered MDA, sample-uniqueness weighting, MI/VI, denoising and detoning, NCO, meta-labelling and generative models come after that (12.1 RS-04, RS-05; user decision 2026-11-04, #197).

#### 5.3.12 Macro and event terms and the event schedule [user decision 2026-10-28]
Removed 2026-11-24 (#752) [#725 Decisions 224, 250, 251]: macro series and events are market-wide or per-symbol items of the value tables (5.3.5), read on the session axis like any item. `nx.Item.<name>.age()` replaces the event terms (`.days_since()` and the like), and the event schedule `on_event` leaves with its incremental decisions, lifetimes and target rules. A decision after an event is an ordinary schedule (3.7) with an `age()` Filter.

### 5.4 Missing values and cross-sectional operations

#### 5.4.1 Missing-value rules [C7, INV-12]
- Arithmetic propagates NaN. **A Factor's value is a finite number or NaN, at every term** (decision 2026-11-18, #536): where a term's result would be ±inf (`x/0`, an overflow, `log(0)`, a user function's output), that term's value in that cell is NaN, so no term reads an `inf`, and a comparison or `isfinite()` never meets one.
- Inside a built-in factor's kernel (3.4) and a cross-section operation (5.4.2) only an input cell is skipped, never a value the kernel computes, and an overflow is never treated as a missing cell (decision 2026-11-20, #632). The term's value in a cell is a finite number only where that number is the formula's value (3.4, 5.4.2): where ±inf or NaN comes out, and where an overflowed value was divided by, compared against or clipped to and so can no longer give the formula's value (for example `VWAP`'s Σ volume, `zscore`'s standard deviation, `winsorize`'s bounds, `RSI`'s `dn`), it is NaN; an overflow that leaves the formula's value as it is stands (`RSI`'s `up/dn`: 100, as for `dn = 0`; a `MaxDrawdown` ratio of −inf, which a maximum does not take). A division by a zero inside a kernel is NaN whatever the zero's sign. Where 3.4 states the value, it stands: `RSI` with `dn = 0` is 100, `VWAP` with the pairs' volume summing to 0 is NaN, and `AnnualizedVolatility` leaves out a daily return over a zero price, or one that overflows, as `DailyReturns`' own NaN. Each such cell is counted once at the term that computes it: `DivisionByZero` for a zero divisor inside the kernel (`AnnualizedVolatility`'s too, though its value is finite), `InfiniteResult` for an overflow that made the value NaN or left a return out. So a built-in kernel's value does not depend on the sign of a zero in its input.
- A term's value is never −0.0 (decision 2026-11-20, #648): at every term, where a ±inf becomes NaN (above), a −0.0 becomes +0.0 and every other cell keeps its bits, so which of two equal zeros a minimum, a maximum or a clip in the engine's own computation returns, which the library and the CPU choose, never reaches a value; a column's cells read as a window (by a kernel or a user function) are the package's values as they are, a −0.0 included, which are the same in every run and so do not touch INV-03 and INV-04; what a user function does with such a zero inside its own computation (`copysign`, `signbit`) is the function's, and only the −0.0 it returns becomes +0.0.
- Three diagnostics count cells, each cell once, at the term and row that produced it (a term that reads the NaN does not count it again; a node shared by several columns counts once): `DivisionByZero`, the cells of a division whose divisor is 0 and whose dividend is not NaN; `InfiniteResult`, the cells whose result was ±inf for another reason; `UnknownGroup`, the cells in the term's domain with a value that is not NaN whose group is −1, the cells left out for their group alone (3.3 `groupby`; a cell already NaN is left out whatever its group).
- The counts travel with the result as one table, `term_diagnostics`, with the columns `market: str`, `session_date: date32`, `term: str` (as `explain` writes it), `diagnostic: str`, `n: int64`, in that order: one row per (market, session, term, diagnostic) with `n > 0`, sorted by `(session_date, market, term, diagnostic)` as `PipelineResult.table` is (3.5); with nothing to count, it is an empty table with those columns. Until `explain` fixes how it writes a node (T2.11), `term` is the first line `nx.describe` writes for the node, a space, and the first 12 hex digits of its graph hash (5.3.9), as in `Factor div 8e831960ec00`. A cell is counted at the session of the row that produced it, which may be a row read before `start` or between two decisions. Which rows a term is computed at is set by the graph and the decisions (5.3.2), not by the chunks: a term that reads earlier rows of another adds those rows, and their counts. `run_pipeline`'s `PipelineResult.term_diagnostics` carries it, a saved run keeps it as `term_diagnostics.parquet` in the run folder (5.9.1), and the report's question 7 prints its totals per term (3.9). It is not a log line and not a column of `coverage.parquet`.
- Filter: carries values together with a validity mask. A comparison with NaN → unknown. `~f`: the value is inverted and validity stays (unknown stays unknown). `&` and `|`: Kleene three-valued (false & unknown = false, true | unknown = true, the rest unknown). At the final use (`universe`, the candidates of `top`/`bottom`, `mask`, `screen`), unknown = false. So `~(Price.close.latest > 10_000)` does **not include** an instrument without a close because of a halt (v3 included it: the same result as qlib#2119). `isnan()`, `notnan()`, `isfinite()`, `isnull()` and `notnull()` are always valid at a listed cell (5.3.1). `explain` shows unknown as "unknown (input NaN: {column})". The difference from zipline (in zipline `~` turns missing into true) is in `KNOWN_DIFFERENCES.md`. **Operations mixed with unknown**: `all/any(window)` is Kleene (3.3), `as_factor()` turns unknown → NaN, a comparison with a Classifier's missing value (−1) is unknown, `if_else` turns unknown → NaN, and unknown in `Pipeline(screen=)` = false. The only explicit replacement is `Filter.fillna(bool)`. **Trap notice**: during a run, when the unknown ratio of the universe Filter (unknown / listed existing cells) exceeds 50% at a decision and market, `UnknownFilterNotice` (E39) is raised once with the number of decisions and the name of the leaf Filter (for example, before 2019-08-23 in the US, `~Trad.halted` is all unknown because `status_known = false` → the universe is empty; fix `(~Trad.halted).fillna(True)`). The unknown count per leaf Filter is `n_unknown_by_leaf` of `coverage.parquet`.
- The final selection (`top`, portfolio) looks only at finite scores.
- Replacement only through `fillna`, `ops.where` and `ops.coalesce`. The missing-value rules of built-in factors are in the 3.4 table.

#### 5.4.2 Definitions of cross-sectional operations (one decision, one market or the collection of `across_markets`, only cells with `mask` true and a finite value)

| Operation | Definition |
|---|---|
| `rank(method)` | 1..n (5.3.10 order). ordinal ties = the `[portfolio] tiebreak` key (default the fixed salted hash, 3.7; with `"id"`, ascending `instrument_id`). average = mean rank. min/max/dense are the same as scipy `rankdata`. With `ascending=False`, the largest value is 1 |
| `zscore` | `(x − mean)/std(ddof=0)`; std = 0 → all NaN; n < 2 → NaN |
| `demean` | `x − mean` |
| `winsorize(a, b)` | `clip(x, q_a, q_b)`, q = linearly interpolated quantiles (numpy `method="linear"`) |
| `top(N)` | the first N in the order (score descending, tie key ascending). When fewer than N scores are finite, all of them |
| `percentile_between(a, b)` | percentile `p = 100·(rank_average − 1)/(n − 1)` (100 when n=1); `a ≤ p ≤ b` |
| `pct_rank(higher_is_better, na)` | `p = 100·(r − 1)/(n − 1)`, r = mean rank (ascending rank when higher is better), n = number of valid cells (100 when n = 1). `na="bottom"`: missing cells are put in as "one equal value worse than the worst value", increasing n. `na="middle"`: computed on the valid cells, then missing cells = 50 |
| `ops.composite` | per term `pct_rank(na)` → `Σ w_k·p_k / Σ w_k` (over all terms, not only the terms valid in that cell; with `na="drop"`, a cell with any missing term is NaN) → `pct_rank` again |
| `groupby=g` | the definitions above per group. Cells in group −1 (unknown) are NaN/False, with the count in diagnostics |
| (no mask) | cross-section = the default domain (3.3). When a cross-sectional operation without a mask on the Strategy signal path uses a different set from the universe as its denominator, notice E34 ("rank over all listed equities, not the strategy universe; pass mask=universe") |

#### 5.4.3 Tie-preserving quantiles [11.4.5]
For each decision, with the mean rank `r` (1..n, ascending) of the n valid cells, `bucket = min(q, max(1, ceil(r·q/n)))`. Equal scores → the same mean rank → the same quantile. Quantile sizes can differ (not forced to split evenly).

### 5.5 Score → canonical target [C6, 11.4.6, INV-08, SC-01, SC-02]

#### 5.5.1 `TargetFrame`

An Arrow table, sorted by `(decision_id, selected_rank, instrument_id)`:

| Column | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| decision_id | string | |
| sleeve_id | string | |
| decision_ts | timestamp | |
| session_date | date32 | |
| instrument_id | string | |
| score | float64 | |
| selected_rank | int32 | from 1; 0 for held instruments not selected |
| stage | string | `construction` (construction) / `sizing` (sizing) / `risk` (risk, canonical final). The run output `targets.parquet` has only the canonical final (`risk`) rows (with `final_weight` on those rows); with `save_signal=True`, every stage |
| pre_risk_weight | float64 | the weight output by the sizing slot (before the risk slot) |
| target_weight | float64 | the canonical final weight within the sleeve (market information only, INV-08), **signed** (+ long, − short). Long only: sum ≤ 1; long–short: Σ|w| ≤ `max_gross` |
| decided_weight | float64 | with a `decide` block only (else null): what the block returned, through the risk list and the fitting once (3.7, 5.5.2) [#725 Decisions 239] |
| final_weight | float64 | final target = `target_weight` (with a `decide` block, `decided_weight`) × the multipliers of account rules (5.5.2a). Without account rules, bit-identical to that weight [INV-23]. The value order intents (5.6.7) use |
| side | string | `long` / `short` |
| reason | string | selected / selected_short / capped / risk_capped / held_by_band / held_missing_score / conflict_long_kept / not_selected (held but target 0) / `risk:{rule}` (reduced by a market rule) / `account:{rule}` (reduced by an account rule) |

- `build_targets` is a pure function that takes only the information at the decision and the target state of the previous decision (`prior`, 5.5.3) (it does not take the account state). So with the same `DecisionContext` and anchor, screen and backtest give the same table [R-13 corrected, 11.4.6, INV-08].
- The runner appends the "held but target 0" rows from the account state (they are left out when comparing canonical targets).
- The report counts, per decision, "the number of differences between the target set and the held set" (instruments not bought because of a halt or a cash shortfall and so on) (`n_target_not_held` of `coverage.parquet`).

#### 5.5.1a The sizing slot (`sizing=`)

- **Place and contract**: construction (selection) → **sizing** → risk management (5.5.2) → account layer (5.5.2a) → order intent (5.6.7). Input = per sleeve and side the selection table S (id, side, score, rank) + the market window at the decision + parameters. It is a pure function that does not take the account, cash or holdings (inside INV-08; sizing from the account, like backtrader's `cash` or Qlib's `cash × risk_degree`, makes screen ≠ backtest — teardown F lesson 1). Output = per side `Σ|w| ≤ 1` (long ≥ 0; short is the same computation then multiplied by −1). Then multiplication by `long_weight` and `short_weight` of `long_short` (3.7), then the modifiers in the order written. What remains is cash. No leverage: in the cash account both the feasible region `long_weight + c·short_weight ≤ 1` (3.7) and the gross cap apply, and even after the modifiers `Σ|w| ≤ G_max = min(1, max_gross)` (1 for long only; the exposure stage of 5.5.2 scales to `G_max`); in the margin account, only `vol_target` up to `min(max_gross, 1/initial_margin)` (5.6.7a).
- **Notation**: n = the number selected on the side, σ_i = `AnnualizedVolatility(L)` (L daily simple returns of `close_tr` at the decision row, ddof = 1, × √252), Σ (annual) = 252 × the sample covariance (ddof = 1) — rows = the sessions within window L where **all** the selected instruments of that side are valid (listwise deletion). Sums and products are left folds in sorted `instrument_id` order (5.3.10; no BLAS), with `wᵀΣw = Σ_i Σ_j w_i Σ_ij w_j` in lexicographic (i, j) order.
- **Instruments without input** (σ NaN or 0, no market cap, a short window) are removed from that decision's selection and counted in `skips.parquet` as (decision, id, `sizing`, reason) — they are not silently set to 0 (6.6).

| Block | Formula | Parameters (default) |
|---|---|---|
| `size.equal(max_weight=None, redistribute=False)` | `u_i = 1`, `w_i = u_i/Σu`, capped at `max_weight`, the remainder cash (with `redistribute=True`, the iterative redistribution of 3.7) | – |
| `size.value(...)` | `u_i` = market cap at the decision (3.7) | the same |
| `size.inverse_vol(lookback=252, ...)` | `u_i = 1/σ_i` | the same |
| `size.score(transform="rank", ...)` | `"rank"`: `u_i = n + 1 − rank_i` (1 = best); `"raw"`: `u_i = max(s_i, 0)` (on the short side `max(−s_i, 0)`). When all are 0, equal + skips | the same |
| `size.erc(lookback=252, min_obs=0.8, shrink=0.0)` | equal risk with risk budget `b_i = 1/n`, `Σ_δ = (1−δ)Σ + δ·diag(Σ)`, coordinate descent below | `min_obs` = a ratio of L |
| `size.vol_parity(target=0.15, lookback=252)` | `w_i = (target/n)/σ_i`; when `Σw > 1`, all multiplied by `1/Σw` (correlation ignored, the simple version of pysystemtrade) | – |
| `size.stop_risk(risk_pct=0.01, stop_pct=None, max_weight=None)` | `w_i = risk_pct/stop_pct`; without `stop_pct`, the p of the strategy's `exits.stop_loss(p)`, E16 when both are absent; after the `max_weight` cap, multiplied by `1/Σw` when `Σw > 1`. The ATR version is v1.x | – |
| modifier `size.vol_target(target, lookback=63)` (for the margin account's `G_max` and fourth term, 5.6.7a) | `σ_p = √(wᵀΣw)` (the book with both sides combined, 63-day annual covariance), `k = min(target/σ_p, G_max/Σ|w|, max_weight/max|w_i|, 1/(Σw⁺ + c_max·Σ|w⁻|))` (the third term only when there is a cap; the fourth term = keeps the long–short feasible region `L + c·S ≤ 1` (3.7, 5.6.7 short collateral) after the modifier too, `c_max` = the largest `collateral_ratio` among the sleeve's markets, `1/Σw⁺` when there are no shorts), `G_max = min(1, max_gross)`, `w ← k·w`. It grows weights only up to the cap (dropping the hidden leverage of bt's `TargetVol`). When `σ_p` = 0 or cannot be computed, `k = 1` + skips | `target` required |
| modifier `size.cash_floor(c)` | when `Σ|w| > 1 − c`, all multiplied by `(1 − c)/Σ|w|` | `c ∈ [0, 1)` |

- **ERC coordinate descent** (Griveau-Billion, Richard and Roncalli 2013, the formula of ffn's `calc_erc_weights`): start `x_i = (1/σ_i)/Σ_j(1/σ_j)`. One sweep = for each i in sorted `instrument_id` order, `σ_x = √(xᵀΣ_δx)`, `α = Σ_δ,ii`, `β = (Σ_δx)_i − x_i·α`, `γ = −b_i·σ_x`, `x_i ← (−β + √(β² − 4αγ))/(2α)` (the updated x is used immediately for the next coordinate). At the end of a sweep `Δ = Σ_i((x_i − x_i^prev)/Σx)²`; stop when `Δ < 1e-14`, at most 500 sweeps. At the end `w = x/Σx` once (no clipping; dropping the problem of LEAN RP's "clip after normalising", whose sum is not 1). **Fallback**: when (a) it does not converge within 500 sweeps, (b) `α ≤ 0`, NaN or infinite, or (c) after listwise deletion the row count < `min_obs·L`, that decision and side use the `size.inverse_vol` weights (same lookback), with an E43 `SolverFallbackNotice` count + `skips` (reason `erc_fallback:{a|b|c}`). It neither kills the run (ffn) nor hides it (LEAN). When n = 1, w = 1; when Σ is diagonal, the result is proportional to `1/σ` (G-79).
- The sizer's result is part of the canonical target, so its parameters go into `graph_hash` and `settings_hash`. A `top(weight=…)` shorthand and a strategy that states the same sizer explicitly give bit-identical targets (P-RISK-OFF (iii)).

#### 5.5.2 The risk slot (`risk=[…]`) [SC-01, OQ-2]

- **Order**: sizing (5.5.1a) → **the risk rule list** (market-only rules, in the order written) → exposure-cap fitting (only when shorting is on) → canonical final `target_weight` → with a `decide` block (3.7), what it returns, in place of the sizing output, through the same list and fitting once → `decided_weight` → account layer (5.5.2a: account rules, in the order written) → final constraint check (5.5.2b) → order intent (5.6.7). This is the same place as LEAN framework's Portfolio Construction → Risk Management. In the list, account rules must be written **after** all market rules — writing one before is E16 before the run (layer config, fix "account rules run after market rules; move drawdown_brake after vol_cap"; the order is not secretly changed — no silent failures). The report header shows the actual order "risk order: group_caps → vol_cap → [account] drawdown_brake".
- **What one rule means**: per decision it gives a per-instrument multiplier `m_i ∈ [0, 1]` (a missing id = 1), and the engine does `w_i ← m_i·w_i` (sign kept, no redistribution, the reduced share becomes cash). It does not grow a size or change a sign (longs shrink and shorts move toward 0). A multiplier out of range or not finite is E41 (layer your code). Changed rows get `reason = risk:{rule}`, and the per-slot effect goes into `waterfall.parquet` (5.5.2a). "Toward 0" is per instrument, not per risk: in a long–short book, reducing one leg removes part of a hedge and can raise net exposure or `σ_p`; the final constraint check (5.5.2b) records that.
- **Priority** [#151]: market rules in the order written, then account rules in the order written. A rule that reduces never grows a weight, so it cannot break a gross, group or `max_weight` cap an earlier rule met; it can break only the net-exposure bound and `vol_cap` of a long–short book, which 5.5.2b records.
- A market-only rule, like `RiskRule.apply(targets, inputs)`, takes only the table of the canonical target stage and the information at the decision (Pipeline term values, `Meta.*`, `Market.series`) and **does not take the account state** (INV-08 as it is). Account-reading rules are in 5.5.2a.
- **Exposure-cap fitting** (only when `[shorting] enabled = true`; the engine appends it after the list): when shorting is off, this stage is **not called** (v3.1 always called it, and the float sum 1/9 × 9 = 1.0000000000000002 could shrink long-only weights again; INV-22). When on, `G = math.fsum(|w|)`; when `G > max_gross·(1 + 1e-12)`, every weight is multiplied by `max_gross/G` (in the cash account `max_gross` here is `G_max = min(1, max_gross)`, 5.5.1a), and when `N = math.fsum(w)` is outside `max_net` (a closed interval containing 0), only the overflowing side (long or short) is shrunk proportionally to the boundary.

| Block | Reads | Formula | Parameters (default) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `risk.none()` | – | `m = 1` | – |
| `risk.group_caps(by, max, measure="gross")` | market (classification) | for group g, `G_g = Σ_{i∈g}|w_i|` (`|Σ_{i∈g} w_i|` for `"net"`); when `G_g > max`, `m = max/G_g` for i ∈ g; instruments with a missing classification are grouped in one group `"<unknown>"` and the count reported; no redistribution (the proportional reduction of LEAN's `MaximumSectorExposure`) | `by: Classifier`, `max ∈ (0, 1]` |
| `risk.regime(cond, off=0.0, on_unknown="off")` | market (a market-level Filter such as `Market.series`) | when `cond` at the decision is false (when unknown, `"off"`/`"on"` by `on_unknown`), every `m = off` | `off ∈ [0, 1]` |
| `risk.vol_cap(max_vol, lookback=63)` | market | `σ_p = √(wᵀΣw)` (the 5.5.1a definition, with the weights coming into this rule); when `σ_p > max_vol`, every `m = max_vol/σ_p` | – |
| `risk.liquidity_cap(k=0.05, lookback=20, capital="notional")` | market (account for `"nav"`) | `C` = sleeve capital: `"notional"` = `capital` × the sleeve weight, in that market's currency at the decision-time exchange rate (pure); `"nav"` = `decision_value` at the decision (becomes an account rule). `ADV_i = AverageTradedValue(lookback)`. When `|w_i|·C > k·ADV_i`, `m_i = k·ADV_i/(|w_i|·C)`; ADV is computed only when the valid sessions in the window are at least half of `lookback`, otherwise missing (a new listing from 10 sessions on); when ADV is missing, `m_i = 0` + skips | – |
| `risk.drawdown_brake(off=0.20, on=0.10, floor=0.0, mode="brake")` | **account** + shadow index (market) | 5.5.2a | – |
| `risk.portfolio_stop(loss, reset="annual")` | **account** | 5.5.2a | `reset ∈ {annual, monthly, never}` |
| `risk.no_reentry(sessions=21, after=("stop_loss","trailing_stop","time_stop"))` | **account** (exit events) | 5.5.2a | – |

- **Order can change results** (`vol_cap` computes with σ_p after the earlier rules reduced; G-86). The same order gives the same bits. The order goes into `graph_hash`.
- v1.x: correlation shock and VaR overlays (the minimum of pysystemtrade's four risk overlays), a notional cap per order. Later: risk-model-based constraints [SC-22].

#### 5.5.2a The account layer: account-reading risk rules and the final target [INV-23]

- **Place**: after the canonical final `target_weight` (with a `decide` block, after `decided_weight`, 3.7), before order intent (5.6.7). `final_weight_i = w_i × Π_k m_{k,i}`, `w` = `target_weight`, or `decided_weight` with a `decide` block (account rules k multiplied one by one in the order written, sign kept). Without account rules this layer is **not called**, and `w` is copied into `final_weight` as it is (no multiplication, INV-23). A −0.0 in the product and in the risk-slot result is rewritten as +0.0 (`0 × negative`; hash stability). A ruined (`ruined`, 5.6.7) sleeve does not call this layer (no order intents); at the ruin decision, each rule writes a last `risk_state` row `key = "suspended"`, `value = "ruined"`, and the report's rule line shows "suspended at ruin {date}" (the record does not end silently).
- **Reads**: the account view up to the decision time (read-only arrays): the NAV series (daily `nav_decision` of 5.6.9, which at the decision session is `decision_value`; never `nav_economic`), holding quantities, `avg_cost`, first entry session, exit fill events. There are no values after the decision (INV-01).
- **State**: a rule keeps no state in its own object (dropping the rewriting of LEAN's `_portfolioHigh` and backtrader's `p.stake`). It is `(ctx, w, state_{d−1}) → (m, state_d, reason)`, and the engine writes `risk_state.parquet` (decision_id, sleeve_id, rule_index, rule, key, value_json) every decision. Checkpoints (5.6.1) and the ledger (5.9.6) hold this table, so resume = one go (INV-10) and replay hold.
- The hold band's `prior` (5.5.3) uses only canonical targets — the brake does not change the hold band's state.
- **Drawdown brake** `drawdown_brake(off, on, floor, mode)`:
  - NAV drawdown `DD_d = 1 − V_d/P_d`. `V_d` = the sleeve's `decision_value` at decision d (costs, interest and borrowing included), `P_d` = the maximum of daily `nav_decision` since the anchor (or the last re-entry) and `V_d` — so the peak of a decision is the same whether the run is made before or after an amount of that period becomes known.
  - **Shadow index** S (market information and canonical targets only, not the account; with a `decide` block, the canonical target before it, 3.7): `S = 1` at the anchor decision. The canonical target `target_weight` of decision d is bought at the decision session's close and held without costs until the next decision session's close (cash 0%, shorts by their sign); over a graph that reads a value table (5.3.5), it is bought at the next session's open instead and held until the open after the next decision, since a date-only value may be published after that close (5.1.4, as `label_entry = "close"` is refused there). On that entry session t, the previous weights earn the overnight leg and the new ones the session: `S_t = S_{t−1}·(1 + Σ_i w̃_{i,t−1}·r^{on}_{i,t})·(1 + Σ_i w_{i,d}·(C_{i,t}/O_{i,t} − 1))`, `r^{on}` = the total return from the previous close to this open with the session's events applied as `close_tr` applies them (5.3.3), so a cash dividend going ex that session counts as income, not as a fall, and a split moves no value, `O`, `C` = that session's raw open and close; an instrument with no open that session (a market without opens, a halt, a missing bar) has an entry leg of 0 and its `r^{on}` is its close-to-close total return, counted as missing like `r`; the new weights drift from that open; on the other sessions, the daily total returns below [#725 Decisions 234; the owner, 2026-11-25]: `S_t = S_{t−1}·(1 + Σ_i w̃_{i,t−1}·r_{i,t})`, `w̃` = the weights drifted with prices after the decision (after the entry open, for the open entry above), `r` = the daily total return (`close_tr`; missing is 0 + a count). **Delisting**: when an instrument in the shadow is delisted, the last return of that instrument (including −100%) is applied once at the terminal session with the shared terminal-value function of 5.6.13 (the same function as the label `fwd_return` and accounting), and its weight is 0 after that (it vanishes, since cash is 0%); when the terminal value is not yet published at the decision time (after `ts_value_available`), it is left as 0 based on the last `close_tr` + a count and applied at the first decision after publication (INV-01; this prevents a missing 0 from dropping delisting losses and making recovery in a falling market look faster). `DDs_d = 1 − S_d / max_{d' ≤ d} S_{d'}`; when `S_d ≤ 0` (a shadow with short weights), `DDs_d = 1` (no re-entry), left in skips (reason `shadow_ruined`).
  - `mode="brake"` (states normal/braked): in normal, when `DD_d ≥ off`, it becomes braked and **from this decision** `m = floor` (exit orders at the next session open). In braked, when `DDs_d ≤ on`, it returns to normal and from this decision `m = 1`, and at that moment `P := V_d` (the NAV peak is reset; otherwise it would trigger again at once). Why it returns by the shadow and not by NAV: while out in cash, NAV stands still and cannot show the recovery (LEAN resets the peak at once and buys again at the next rebalance, and Nautilus has no such rule — teardown F lesson 4).
  - `mode="linear"` (no state): `m = clip(1 − (DD_d − on)/(off − on), floor, 1)`.
  - E16 unless `0 < on < off < 1`. Report: triggered decisions / decisions, the brake intervals, the number of round trips, `brakes_with_shadow_ok n/m` (the number of triggers where `DDs ≤ on` already held at the triggering decision — to make visible the cases where a drawdown caused by costs, shorts or deviation led to an immediate re-entry that only paid round-trip costs; the re-entry check starts from the decision after the trigger). `risk_state` leaves `peak_source = "reentry"` at re-entry, floats in `value_json` are written in the shortest round-trip notation (`repr`), and NaN or infinity is E41 (so resume = one go holds bit for bit).
- **Portfolio stop** `portfolio_stop(loss, reset)`: `V_ref` of the reference decision (`"annual"` = the first decision of each year, `"monthly"` = the first decision of each month, `"never"` = the anchor); when `V_d/V_ref − 1 ≤ −loss`, stopped, and `m = 0` until the next reference decision.
- **No re-entry** `no_reentry(sessions, after)`: after fill session s in which instrument i reached a holding of 0 through an exit rule in `after`, `m_i = 0` at decisions before market-calendar session `s + sessions` (one block for what LEAN did secretly by deleting Insights).
- **waterfall**: `waterfall.parquet` (decision_id, sleeve_id, step (`construction`|`sizing`|`risk:{rule}`|`exposure`|`account:{rule}`), gross_before, gross_after, net_after, n_changed, n_zeroed). The "risk waterfall" line of the report's first screen: the mean `Σ|w|` per slot and the number of triggered decisions (numerator/denominator).

#### 5.5.2b The final constraint check and residual violations [#151]

- **Check**: at every decision whose risk slot runs (schedule decisions; not exit-only decisions), after the account layer, the engine checks the sleeve's whole `final_weight` book against each constraint the sleeve declares: gross exposure `Σ|w| ≤ G_max`, net exposure `Σw ∈ max_net`, the feasible region (3.7, 5.6.7a), each `group_caps` group, `vol_cap`, `max_weight` and each instrument under `liquidity_cap`, each with the relative tolerance 1e-12 of the exposure-cap fitting (5.5.2). "An order was reduced" and "the final book satisfies its constraints" are two separate facts; this check states the second.
- **Residual violation**: a constraint that fails is kept as it is — no further reduction, no E46, never a silent pass. Each decision writes one skips row per failing constraint, reason `constraint_residual` (constraint, value, limit), until a fill clears it, and the report header shows "constraint residual: {n} decisions ({constraints})". Causes: a later rule broke a bound an earlier one met (5.5.2 priority: net exposure or `vol_cap` of a long–short book, G-109). A reduction by a risk rule whose order is blocked and carried over (5.6.5) is recorded the same way at each decision until its fill, with the rule as the constraint, the instrument's weight at its holding as the value and its `final_weight` as the limit.
- **E46**: the one whole-book case of E46, the event combined book's `k`, was removed with 5.3.12 (2026-11-24, #752); a whole-book violation is never E46 (6.6).

#### 5.5.3 Target-state recursion and the hold band (`top(n, hold=m)`) — normative, in v1 [SC-02, OQ-1, user 2026-10-28; #725 Decisions 244]

v1 fixes the definitions of this section as normative (the INV-08 wording, `DecisionContext.anchor_decision_id`, the `build_targets(..., prior=)` signature) and, until T7.1 is in, raises E18 when `hold` is not `None` (3.7, the E18 row of 6.6). The implementation is T7.1 (user decision 2026-10-28: included in v1). Off by default, with no default width [#725 Decisions 244].

- **Target state** `TargetState_d = (S_long, S_short, missing_streak)`: `S_long` (`S_d` below) = the set of instruments with `target_weight > 0` in decision d's final canonical target, `S_short` = the set with `< 0` (per sleeve, per group when `per=` is given), `missing_streak` = for each instrument kept as `held_missing_score`, the number of consecutive decisions with a missing score. It is not the account holding (independent of fills, halts and cash). Checkpoints (5.6.1), the ledger manifest (5.9.6) and carrying over from `targets.parquet` hold all three (the sets alone cannot replay the missing-score cap).
- **The strategy state record** (decision 2026-11-04, #152): what one decision passes to the next is one record per sleeve with two parts (three with a `decide` block), saved and restored together. Every feature that needs memory (the hold band, event targets with a lifetime, account rules, the `decide` block) reads and writes only this record.
  - `target_state`: one row per instrument with a non-zero canonical target or a non-zero `missing_streak`, key (`sleeve_id`, `instrument_id`), columns `target_weight` (float64, the canonical target in force), `set_by` (the `decision_id` that set the weight), `missing_streak` (int32) (`origin` and `expires_session`, which served event targets, removed 2026-11-24, #752). `TargetState_d` above is a view of it (`S_long`, `S_short` = the rows with weight > 0, < 0).
  - `risk_state`: the rows of `risk_state.parquet` (5.5.2a), key (`sleeve_id`, `rule_index`, `key`), column `value_json`.
  - `decide_state`: the `decide` block's state table (3.7), the rows of `decide_state.parquet`, key (`sleeve_id`, `instrument_id`), the columns the block keeps [#725 Decisions 240].
  - Serialisation: each part is an Arrow table sorted by its key, written to the checkpoint (5.6.1), the ledger manifest (5.9.6) and alongside `targets.parquet`. Restoring it and taking the next decision gives the same canonical and final targets, bit for bit, as not stopping (P-RESUME).
- **Recursion**: `S_d = f(score_d, universe_d, S_{d−1})`. Candidates = instruments with a finite `signal` and a true `universe`; rank = score descending + the tie-break key of 3.7 (`tiebreak`). The short side (`bottom(n, hold=m)`) runs the same recursion separately with score ascending (the mirror of the long side).
- **Missing scores**: with `on_missing = "drop"` (default), an instrument of `S_{d−1}` whose score is not finite is not a candidate and drops out (as in v3; one missing value makes two round-trip trades). With `"hold"`, when `i ∈ S_{d−1}` and `universe_d(i)` is true but only the score is not finite, it stays in `K` with `reason = held_missing_score` (its rank taken as n+1), and it is removed when the number of consecutive missing decisions (`missing_streak`) exceeds `[portfolio] max_missing_decisions` (default 1). A `held_missing_score` instrument stays in K regardless of rank m (even with `hold = n`; the only cap is the consecutive missing count). When that instrument's weighting input (market cap for `value`, volatility for `inverse_vol`) is also missing, the **median** of the weighting inputs `u` of the other instruments selected in that decision on the same side is used (when all are missing, that whole side is equal), left as an E30 count (v3.1's "an equal share of 1" had different units from `u` and could not be mixed). It uses only the target state, so it stays a pure function (INV-08) (PyBroker "a held instrument without a bar keeps its slot", pysystemtrade "keep the previous position when the input is NaN"). (1) Keep `K = {i ∈ S_{d−1} : rank_d(i) ≤ m}` (`reason = held_by_band`, but `selected` if within rank n). (2) Add candidates not in `K` in rank order until `|S_d| = n`. Since `|S_{d−1}| ≤ n`, `|K| ≤ n`. (3) For weights, the sizing slot (5.5.1a; for the shorthand, the weighting rules of 3.7) is applied to `S_d`. The risk stage comes after that. The recursion's `prior` uses only canonical targets and not the account layer (`final_weight`, 5.5.2a).
- **Anchor**: the decision where the recursion starts, `S_{anchor−1} = ∅`. `backtest` = the first decision of that run (fresh capital per segment, so a new anchor per segment; under a validation method, the first decision of each account of the 6.3a state table: the first walk-forward test window, each CPCV path, each `per_fold` fold); `screen` = replays the recursion by running the Pipeline only on decision dates along the schedule from the `anchor` argument (default `[portfolio] anchor_date`) (30 years monthly = 360 cross-sectional computations); `live` = the decision that created the ledger (recorded in the ledger manifest).
- With the same snapshot, `screen` may carry over `S_{d−1}` from a saved `targets.parquet` (from a run with the same strategy hash and anchor) (the values are the same, so the result is the same; a response to the P2 budget).
- **INV-08 wording**: "the same strategy, snapshot, policies, anchor and `DecisionContext` give the same canonical target". With a different anchor they may not be the same, and the report writes the anchor in its header.
- **Hold-band instruments with a zero holding**: the target state is not the account holding, so an instrument that was within rank n at `d−1` but could not buy a single share because of a halt, a cash shortfall or a cap, or that left through a stop loss and now has a holding of 0, stays in `K` (`held_by_band`) if it is at rank n+1..m at `d`. Buying this instrument anew goes against the meaning of the hold band, "sell less of what is already held", so in the execution stage (5.6.7, where the account state is used), with `[portfolio] band_entries = "skip"` (default), no opening order intent is made for an instrument with `reason = held_by_band`, a holding of 0 and no unfilled buy (on the short side, no unfilled short sale) (event `order_suppressed(band_entry)`, that share becomes cash). The target table stays as it is (INV-08). With `"buy"`, it buys as in v3. The count goes on the report's orders line.
- **Plumbing and function**: the strategy state record, its serialisation and the checkpoint are built by T5.16a (M3b, before T3.35), and the ledger-manifest plumbing by M5 ticket T5.16 (decision 2026-11-04, #152; the target-state part is empty for a schedule-only strategy before T7.1), and T7.1 turns on the recursion function and only compares (T7.1 builds the hold band).
- This method meets user decision Q6 ("the hold band is a portfolio option") and removes the reason for deferral in A-10 (relying on holdings is not a pure function) [A-47].

### 5.6 Daily state machine [R-56, 11.4.2, 9.0 orders]

#### 5.6.1 Account state (per sleeve)
`AccountState` is a **column-array structure** (set in T3.3; this shape whether or not Numba is used):
- Money: `cash_minor: int64` (minor units of the local currency, the market rules pack's `minor_unit`; KRW 1 won, USD 1 cent). Every entry (fill amount, cost components, dividends, fractional-share cash, terminal consideration, currency exchange, borrow cost) is rounded to an integer **once**, at the moment of that entry. The rounding method is **that component's rule in the market rules pack `[rounding]`** (mode half_up / half_even / down, unit = a multiple of the minor unit; default half_up·1 = the same as v3; overridden by the user's `[rounding.<MARKET>]`), and the pack version goes into `settings_hash`. `down` is toward smaller size (toward 0). The value before rounding is kept in the event as a float64 `*_unrounded`.
- **Integer share arithmetic**: (1) A ratio is a rational number `ρ = num/den` (positive integers; TDS `ratio_num/ratio_den` is canonical). If the canonical columns are missing, `[corporate_actions] decimal_ratio` decides (the default `exclude_instrument` removes the instrument, below; E13 only with `require_exact`, or with `literal` when there is no decimal to read, below; `"literal"` only when the user chooses it); if either is 0 or negative, or only one of them is present, E13 at open (4.4-4).
