If both are present and positive but differ from the f64 column by more than 1e-12 relative, the exact ratio is in doubt and the event is handled as one without an exact ratio (below; checked at open, 4.4-4) [#188]. **The engine does not infer fractions from f64** (user decision 2026-10-28): from f64 alone one cannot decide whether 0.333 is 1/3 or 333/1000 (Sol's counterexample: if the exact value 0.3333 = 3333/10000, 30,000 shares → 9,999 shares, but inference makes 10,000 shares), and wherever the threshold is set a counterexample remains (0.0476 → 21 shares → 0 shares, 0.124999998 → 8 shares → 0 shares). The one exception is the user's choice `[corporate_actions] decimal_ratio = "literal"`: only for events without an exact ratio, the decimal of `repr(x)` (the Python 3.13 shortest round-trip notation; in exponent notation written out with an integer mantissa and exponent) is used as is as an exact decimal fraction (0.333 → 333/1000, 1.000004 → 250001/250000), leaving the event `ratio_decimal_literal`, an E30 count and the report header line "decimal ratios used literally: n events". An event with neither an exact ratio nor an f64 value to read (the column 4.4-4 compares with is null: `share_factor` and `ratio_new_per_old` both for a split-like kind, `ratio_new_per_old` for the others) has no decimal to use: under `literal` it is E13 at open with the list of event ids (fix: the default `exclude_instrument`, or a package with the ratio) and is not counted [#446]. v3.2's three-step inference rule (rounded repeating decimal, computation noise, decimal as is; thresholds q ≤ 20·1e-8·denominator 10,000) was removed (`sources/engine-spec-v3.3-2026-10-28.md` 5.6.1). (2) `floor_shares(x)` = with `n = round(x)`, n if `|x − n| ≤ 1e-6`, otherwise `floor(x)` (so that the floating-point product `0.57·100000/1000 = 56.99999999999999` does not lose a share). `floor_lot(x) = lot·floor_shares(x/lot)`. (3) Ratio products are in integers: `q' = (|q|·num) div den`, fractional share `frac = ((|q|·num) mod den)/den`, the sign is the sign of q (short = negative); the intermediate product `|q|·num` is computed as a Python integer (or an overflow-checked int64). Results such as LEAN#9882 (16 shares, 1:8 reverse split → 1 share), zipline-reloaded#336 and vectorbt#894, where "one share of an integer-multiple holding becomes fractional-share cash", do not occur.
- **Events without an exact ratio — removing the instrument** [user decision 2026-10-29]: when `decimal_ratio = "exclude_instrument"` (default), an instrument i whose share-changing event e (split, consolidation, bonus issue, stock dividend, capital reduction that is a share consolidation, rights issue with an allotment ratio, spin-off, stock merger, mixed merger; the two qualified kinds need an exact ratio only when they carry `ratio_new_per_old`, as TDS 5.11 A010 reads them [#446]) lacks `ratio_num/ratio_den`, is marked unverified by the producer (reserved, (8)), or has a positive, complete exact ratio that differs from its f64 column by more than 1e-12 relative (4.4-4; decision 2026-11-04, #188) is handled as follows. (1) **Visible time** v(e) = the public time (`ts_announced` → `ts_terms_available` → if neither exists, `ts_effective`; date only, 5.1.4). (2) From the first decision with `decision_ts ≥ v(e)`, i **leaves the universe** of that sleeve (the same rule for screens, evaluation and strategies, screen = backtest). It is not removed before v(e) — filtering in advance by a future event would be future information. (3) If i is held, the target of that decision becomes 0 and it is sold by the ordinary fill rules (bought back if short). (4) If that order cannot fill before `ts_effective` (the ex-date or consolidation effective date), it is treated as closed at the last valid close of the session just before, paying costs (reason `ca_unverified_exit`, manifest `assumptions`). It is not valued at prices after the event. This happens every time a held name's v(e) is `ts_effective` (neither `ts_announced` nor `ts_terms_available`), not only when a fill is blocked: no order of a decision at or after v(e) fills before the event (G-107). (5) Feature values of that instrument whose window crosses event e are NaN (the adjustment factor is unknown; INV-12, no filling). Features that use only the interval before the event are used as they are. (6) Even if no later event with an exact ratio follows event e, i does not come back until the end of the run (v1, a simple rule). (7) The report header line "{n} instruments excluded for missing exact ratios ({m} events; first {date})", an E30 warning, `skips` (reason = the cause of (8): `ca_ratio_missing`, `ca_ratio_unverified` or `ca_ratio_disagrees`), and the list of instruments and events in the manifest `policies.corporate_actions`. (8) **What the exit of (4) is shown as** [#150, decision 2026-11-04]: the default stays the exit of (4), and its effect is always reported. Its trade row carries `exit_reason = ca_unverified_exit` (5.9.1). `ca_exclusions.parquet` (5.9.1) has one row per (instrument, event) excluded by this rule, held or not: the cause (`ca_ratio_missing`; `ca_ratio_unverified` = marked unverified by the producer, **Reserved** (chapter 1 notation): TDS v1.4 has no column a producer could set it with, so it counts 0 until a TDS MINOR adds one, and the report line, `ca_exclusions.parquet` and `skips` keep the value so that their shapes do not change [#446]; `ca_ratio_disagrees` = a positive, complete exact ratio that disagrees with its f64 column, the third case of this rule [#188]), v(e), `ts_effective`, the excluded span (`excluded_from` = the first `decision_ts ≥ v(e)`, `excluded_to` = the end of the run, (6)) and the quantity held at v(e); an exit of (4) adds its price basis (`last_valid_close` and that session), exit price, P&L, costs and `trade_id`. A name not held has `held_qty = 0` and no price; a holding sold by an ordinary fill in (3) has no price basis and names its trade by `trade_id`. The cause is also the `skips` reason and the `coverage.parquet` `n_excluded_by_reason` key. The 3.9 line `hypothetical exits` gives the count of the (4) rows, their summed P&L and costs and its share of the total P&L, and the count per cause; section 7 of the Run's `report.html` gives the per-year table. **Sensitivity**: the scenario `ca_exit_lo` (a default scenario, replayed only when the base run has at least one exit of (4)) keeps the run's targets and values each exit of (4) at 0 (proceeds 0, with the run's cost model applied to that amount); `ca_exit_hi` values them at the last valid close, which is the valuation of the base run, so it is the base run's value as it is and is not replayed (one series, the way `gross` = `cost0x`); a run without such an exit replays nothing for them (a listed `ca_exit` scenario writes no file) and its first-screen line reads `ca sensitivity: n/a (0 hypothetical exits)`, so the skip is visible, and a run with such exits whose scenario list leaves the pair out shows `ca sensitivity: n/a (scenarios off)`; the first screen shows their CAGR and maximum drawdown next to the base, the way `delist_lo`/`delist_hi` work (5.6.13). For a short holding no bound is set (OI-54): both scenarios value it at the last valid close, and the line counts it as `short {k}: bound open`. Removing the names from the universe before v(e) is not a comparison baseline: it uses future information ((2)). `require_exact` gives E13 in this case (for a disagreeing ratio, with the list of event ids, like 4.4-4), and `literal` is as in the table above (for a disagreeing ratio it reads the f64 column, not the exact ratio: the user who chose `literal` chose the decimal column). The instrument removed is the one of the event row (`instrument_id`): for a stock or mixed merger the **old** instrument, the way the list already applies a spin-off to its parent; the new instrument is not touched (its share count does not change and its price history has no event). A `cash_merger` needs no ratio and is not in the list: a missing `cash_per_share` stays E13 (required accounting data missing).
- **Signed holdings**: `qty[int64]` is positive for longs and negative for shorts. A short holding's `avg_cost` is the average short-sale price, and in the place of `high_water_close` a short uses the lowest close since entry (`low_water_close`, updated by `min`, 5.6.3(6)). When shorting is off, `qty ≥ 0` is asserted.
- Holdings (instrument integer index i = the sort position of `instrument_id` in that market block, column length = the number of instruments in the block): `qty[int64]`, `sellable_qty[int64]`, `locked_until_session[int32]` (lock on new stock-dividend shares), `avg_cost[float64]` (raw price, not an entry), `high_water_close[float64]`, `opened_session[int32]`.
- Order table (5.6.2): row = order, columns = `order_seq[int64]`, `instrument_idx[int32]`, `purpose[int8]`, `side[int8]`, `type[int8]`, `at[int8]`, `tif[int8]`, `limit_price[f64]`, `stop_price[f64]`, `max_age_sessions[int32]` (−1 = none), `requested_qty`, `filled_qty`, `cancelled_qty`, `remaining_qty` (int64), `trigger_state[int8]`, `status[int8]`, `oco_group[int64]`, `eligible_from_session[int32]`, `age_sessions[int32]`, `decision_price[f64]`, `decision_id_idx[int32]`. The strings `order_id` and `decision_id` are in a side dictionary table.
- Receivables table: `kind`, `instrument_idx`, `amount_minor[int64]`, `pay_session[int32]`, `estimated[bool]`, `ts_known[int64 us]`. Table of entitlements of unknown amount: `event_chain_id`, `instrument_idx`, `qty[int64]`, `ts_terms_expected`, the after-the-fact amount (for reports, not a decision input).
- `seq` (event sequence number), `state_version` (ledger transaction, 5.9.6), `target_state` (`TargetState = (S_long, S_short, missing_streak)`, 5.5.3; always empty before T7.1), the sleeve state `ruined[bool]`·`ruined_session[int32]`, short collateral `reserved_minor[int64]` (5.6.7), the ledger manual-instruction table (5.9.6).
- **Seam**: this state and the entry functions sit behind the Arrow-array seam (2.4) of `nx.accounting._state`.
- **Checkpoint** = Arrow IPC (uncompressed) of the arrays above + a meta JSON (`schema = "nexusbt-checkpoint/1"`, the last processed session and phase, the snapshot ids, `state_version`). No pickle (2.6). The same state + the same input give the same result [INV-10].

#### 5.6.2 Orders and status

`Order` (one row of the order table): `order_id` (= sha256(decision_id, instrument_id, purpose, seq)[:16]), `decision_id`, `sleeve_id`, `instrument_id`, `purpose` (rebalance / exit:<rule>), `side` (buy / sell / sell_short / buy_to_cover; one order does not cross the holding's sign — an intent to go from long 10 shares → short 5 shares is two orders, `sell 10` + `sell_short 5`, and the opening one fills only after the reducing one is done, 5.6.7), `type` (market / limit / stop / stop_limit), `at` (open/close; market only), `limit_price`, `stop_price`, `tif` (day/gtc), `max_age_sessions` (`int | None`; always None for exit orders), the four quantities `requested_qty`, `filled_qty`, `cancelled_qty`, `remaining_qty` (INV-18: `requested = filled + cancelled + remaining`), `trigger_state` (`untriggered` / `triggered`; stop and stop_limit only), `oco_group` (exit orders), `ts_created`, `eligible_from_session`, `age_sessions`, `status`, `decision_price` (the raw close of the decision row `row_m(d)`; for an exit order, the raw close of the session that created it) [SC-11].

Three things are kept apart: the **life status** (`status`, the table below), the **trigger state** (`trigger_state`) and the **quantity accounting** (the four quantities). A partial cancellation (cash shortfall, split fractional shares) is not a status transition but a quantity event (`qty_cancelled(reason, qty)`), which increases `cancelled_qty` and decreases `remaining_qty`. When `remaining_qty = 0`, the order goes to an end status: `FILLED` if `filled_qty = requested_qty`, otherwise `CANCELLED` (the last reducing reason).

| Status | Entry condition |
|---|---|
| `NEW` | created at a decision (before the fill session) |
| `ACCEPTED` | the start of `eligible_from_session` |
| `TRIGGERED` | the stop condition of a stop or stop_limit is met. From then on **the trigger persists** (even if the price comes back after a partial fill, a cap or a block, it does not return to `untriggered`): a stop's remaining quantity is a market order at the next session's open (price O; 5.6.4, G-37), a stop_limit is like a limit order at Lp |
| `PARTIALLY_FILLED` | partly filled, remaining quantity > 0 |
| `FILLED` | remaining quantity 0 (end) |
| `CANCELLED` | replaced by a new decision (`replaced`), replaced by a newer order sheet of the same decision (`superseded`, 5.9.6 rule 1), sleeve ruin (`ruined`, rebalance orders only), holding gone (`position_closed`), the OCO counterpart brought the holding to 0 (`oco`), delisting (`delisted`), the whole remaining quantity reduced to 0 by quantity events (`insufficient_cash` etc.) (end; `filled_qty` is recorded as it is) |
| `EXPIRED` | end of session for a day order, a **rebalance** GTC reaching `max_age_sessions` (default 20). `purpose = exit:*` orders do not expire (P-STATE forbids the exit → EXPIRED transition) (end) |
| `REJECTED` | quantity 0 after lot rounding, the instrument does not exist in the execution session (end) |

Allowed transitions: NEW→ACCEPTED|CANCELLED|REJECTED, ACCEPTED→TRIGGERED|PARTIALLY_FILLED|FILLED|CANCELLED|EXPIRED, TRIGGERED→PARTIALLY_FILLED|FILLED|CANCELLED|EXPIRED, PARTIALLY_FILLED→PARTIALLY_FILLED|FILLED|CANCELLED|EXPIRED. PARTIALLY_FILLED of a triggered order keeps `trigger_state = triggered`. Any other transition and any INV-18 violation is an `AssertionError` (engine bug). Apart from status, the per-session event `blocked(reason)` (halted / limit_locked / zero_volume / no_open / no_range / capacity_unknown / tradability_unknown_blocked / short_ban / collateral / override / closing_leg_pending) is recorded. The part of a cover beyond the short size is the quantity event `qty_cancelled(over_cover)`. When no order intent is made at all, it is the decision event `order_suppressed(reason)` (small / band / band_entry / override / ruined); when a price was snapped to the grid, the order event `price_rounded` (original value `*_unrounded`). `no_open` = that row's `open` is null, `no_range` = `high` or `low` is null (a row's null; bars without a value of them are 4.4 step 3's).

#### 5.6.2a Life of an exit order [R-56]

1. **Creation**: **right after** the fill event of an entry (a fill that takes the holding from 0 to non-zero — a long buy or a short sale), **within the same phase**, the holding's exit orders (`Strategy(exits=)`) are created. `ts_created` = that fill instant, quantity = the holding's sellable quantity at that instant (for a short, the short size; under the immediate-settlement research model, quantity bought in the same session is sellable too; shares locked from a stock dividend are excluded), price basis = the `avg_cost` at that instant (a trailing stop uses `high_water_close` starting from the fill price, a short `low_water_close`). `eligible_from_session` = that session.
2. **Same-bar participation**: exit orders created after an open-phase entry take part in the intraday phase of the same bar (G-08). Exit orders created after an intraday-phase entry take **only the adverse side** in that bar (long stop loss: fills if `L ≤ Sp`, short stop loss (cover stop): fills if `H ≥ Sp`; a take-profit does not fill in that bar, 5.6.4). Exit orders created after a close-phase entry start from the next session.
3. **Resizing**: at every event that changes the holding (an additional buy fill, a partial sell fill, a split or consolidation, the unlock of new stock-dividend shares), the `requested`/`remaining` of all the holding's exit orders are immediately set to the new sellable quantity (quantity event `exit_resized`, keeping INV-18: an increase raises `requested`, a decrease goes to `cancelled_qty`), and the price basis is recomputed from the new `avg_cost`. A split follows the price ÷ρ rule of 5.6.10.
4. **OCO**: when one exit order fills partly, its sibling orders are **not cancelled** but reduced to the remaining holding (the resizing of 3). When the holding reaches 0, all remaining exit orders are `CANCELLED(position_closed)` (reason `oco` when it ended through OCO). Sibling orders are cancelled only when the remaining holding is 0 (the same for the same-bar rule of 5.6.4).
5. **No expiry**: `max_age_sessions = None`, `tif` is `gtc`. Even when a new decision replaces the rebalance orders, exit orders stay (resizing only).
6. Tests: G-08 (same-bar stop loss after an open entry), G-37 (a partly filled stop loss stays triggered even when the price recovers the next day), G-38 (the remaining holding is protected after a partial OCO), G-39 (a stop loss is still valid after 25 sessions), P-STATE (exit → EXPIRED forbidden), P-QTY.

#### 5.6.3 Session processing order [11.4.2]
The events of all sleeves are processed by a UTC-ordered queue. At the same UTC instant, the **phase order** is `session_start < open < intraday < close_fill < close_mark < decision < session_end`, then sleeve name order (v2's "open < decision < close" contradicted the processing phases and was removed). So a decision always sees the same-instant close fills and valuations up to that instant (at `KR_close`, a KR decision reflects that day's KR close fill result `q_i`, G-50). In session t of market m:

1. **`session_start`** (just before `ts_open(t)`): (0) **Accrual**: with the short quantity q at the end of the previous session t−1 and its `close_mark` valuation price, book the borrow cost `|q|·mark·borrow_bps/1e4 × (date_t − date_{t−1})/day_count` (event `borrow_fee`, leg `financing`) and the rebate `|q|·mark·short_rebate_bps/1e4 × the same days` (event `short_rebate`), and on `cash_minor > 0` at the end of the previous session the cash interest `cash·rate/1e4 × the same days` (event `cash_interest`; `rate` = the value at row t−1 of that currency's `[account] cash_rate` item, a market-wide item read by the engine rule of 5.3.5 and the date rule of 5.1.4; with no such value, or no item, the constant `cash_rate_bps`; #313, decision 2026-11-10) (`day_count` is a pack key, KR 365 / US 360; date = the calendar-day difference of the market calendar's `session_date`). Only nights actually held are charged: a short at the end of Friday pays 3 days at the start of Monday, a short opened on Monday pays 1 day at the start of Tuesday, a short opened intraday and closed the same day pays 0, and on a day covered at the open the previous night's share is still paid. The first session of the run has no accrual. It is a mandatory obligation, so it is booked even when cash is short (5.6.7). (a) The effective corporate actions (5.6.10–5.6.12) of the resolver (5.3.3a, reference time = this instant) — quantity, order and average-price adjustment (`order_rebased` event), entitlement creation, exit-order resizing; (b) entitlements whose amount is public by this instant → receivables (correction entries included); (c) receivables whose pay date has come → cash; (d) terminal events (5.6.13): in the terminal session, order cancellation, holding removal, terminal entitlement creation. The order (0)→(d) is fixed.
2. **`open`**: among orders with `eligible_from_session <= t`, those subject to open fills (market `at=open`, limit, stop and stop_limit orders whose condition the open satisfies, the remaining quantity of triggered stops). **Risk-reducing orders first**: long sells → short covers (`buy_to_cover`) → short sales (`sell_short`) → long buys — this order of the four groups and the `buying_power` allocation (5.6.7) are the same in all three fill phases (open, intraday, close_fill) (with shorting off it is the same as v3's "sells first, then buys"; 5.6.7 cash allocation; open sell proceeds may be used for buys at the same open — the same auction instant, the immediate-settlement research model). Within the same group, (purpose: exit first, rebalance next) → `instrument_id` order. Exit orders are created at every entry fill (5.6.2a).
3. **`intraday`**: the remaining limit, stop and stop_limit orders by the H/L rules (5.6.4). The order of the four groups (long sells → covers → short sales → long buys), within the same group (exit first) → `instrument_id` order. **Intraday sell proceeds are not used for buys in this phase** (the fill instant is unknown, so an afternoon sell cannot pay for a morning buy; `intraday_proceeds = next_phase`, recorded in the manifest under the daily-bar approximation name). Intraday increasing orders (short sales, long buys) stay only within the `buying_power` at the start of this phase — the proceeds and collateral releases of reducing fills in the same phase are not used. Exit orders created after an intraday entry fill judge only the adverse side in the same bar (5.6.2a(2), 5.6.4). OCO conflicts go the adverse way (5.6.4).
4. **`close_fill`**: market `at=close` orders at the close (order of the four groups). Intraday sell proceeds can be used from here on.
5. For every fill, costs are computed once and booked in integer minor units (5.6.8) [11.4.2].
6. **`close_mark`**: holding × raw close. Without a close, the **stale valuation price** = the last valid raw close (session s0) × Π (the price factors `m_e` of events that took effect from after s0 up to this session and that the resolver knows at this time, `C[t]/C[s0]` of 5.3.3), `mark_status = stale_adjusted` (`stale` when there is no event). So a 2:1 split during a halt does not double the NAV (20 shares × 50, G-54), and an ex-dividend date during a halt lowers the price by the price factor, not counting it twice with the receivable (LEAN#9819 MACK case, bt#570). The same value is used by `decision_value`'s `mark` (5.6.9), the target share count's `P_i` (5.6.7) and the terminal entitlement's basis (5.6.13). A short holding multiplies the same valuation price by a negative quantity. Borrow cost, rebates and cash interest accrue from this valuation at the start of the next session ((0) of (1); v3.1's "this session's closing quantity × days since the previous valuation" charged nights not held and missed nights held). Trailing basis update — long `high_water_close = max(high_water_close, close)`, short `low_water_close = min(low_water_close, close)` — and exit-price recomputation, the ruin check (5.6.7), then **expiry**: the remaining part of day orders → EXPIRED. Rebalance GTC orders `age_sessions += 1`, reaching `max_age_sessions` → EXPIRED (exit orders excluded).
7. **`decision`**: if this instant is this sleeve's `decision_ts`, the ruin check (5.6.7) runs again first (before `T_i`), then signal → canonical target → `decide` when set (3.7; what it returns passes the risk list and the fitting once, `decided_weight`) → final target (5.5.2a) → order intents (5.6.7; sizing inputs are only values knowable at `decision_ts`, 5.6.9; a `ruined` sleeve makes no order intents). Remaining rebalance orders are `CANCELLED(replaced)`; exit orders and the `decide` block's orders stay (3.7).
8. **`session_end`**: state recording (a candidate point for a checkpoint).

#### 5.6.4 Daily-bar fill rules [9.0 orders, R-56]
Notation: that session's raw prices `O, H, L, C`. Limit price `Lp`, stop price `Sp`. `through` = for a limit fill the price must pass through the limit (`[execution] limit_fill`, default), `touch` = touching is enough. Principle: inside one bar, all that is known is that O is first and C is last; the order of H and L is unknown. **An unknown order is assumed to be the one adverse to the order owner.** Inside a bar the price is taken to move without breaks (no intraday gaps).

| Order | Open phase | Intraday phase (when not filled at the open) |
|---|---|---|
| Market buy/sell `at=open` | price O | – |
| Market `at=close` | – | price C in the close phase |
| Limit buy Lp | O ≤ Lp → price O | through: L < Lp, touch: L ≤ Lp → price Lp |
| Limit sell Lp | O ≥ Lp → price O | through: H > Lp, touch: H ≥ Lp → price Lp |
| Stop sell Sp | O ≤ Sp → price O (gap) | L ≤ Sp → price Sp |
| Stop buy Sp | O ≥ Sp → price O | H ≥ Sp → price Sp |
| Stop-limit sell (Sp, Lp ≤ Sp) | O ≤ Sp: O ≥ Lp → price O; O < Lp → TRIGGERED, the limit sell Lp rule intraday | L ≤ Sp → TRIGGERED, price Sp (continuous in-bar path assumed: the price at the trigger instant = Sp ≥ Lp, so it can fill at once; the same for `Lp = Sp`). The part left by a cap or a block carries over as TRIGGERED |
| Stop-limit buy (Sp, Lp ≥ Sp) | O ≥ Sp: O ≤ Lp → price O; O > Lp → TRIGGERED, the intraday limit buy Lp rule | H ≥ Sp → TRIGGERED, price Sp (the same boundary rule) |
| Triggered stop (remaining quantity, next session) | price O, like a market order | – |
| Triggered stop_limit (remaining quantity, next session) | the limit Lp rule | the limit Lp rule |

- **Both touched in the same bar**: when the OCO exit orders of one holding (for example stop loss Sp and take-profit Tp) both meet their condition in the intraday phase, only the stop loss (the adverse side) fills. The other one is `CANCELLED(oco)` if the stop loss brings the remaining holding to 0, and if only part fills because of a cap or a block, it is reduced to the remaining holding and kept (5.6.2a(4)). When it is decided in the open phase (O ≤ Sp or O ≥ Tp), the open side comes first.
- **Exit after a same-bar entry**: 5.6.2a(2). For an entry filled at the open, exit orders are valid in that bar's intraday phase. For an entry filled intraday (limit or stop), **only the adverse-side exit** is valid in that bar: a stop loss fills if L ≤ Sp, a take-profit does not fill in that bar.
- **Trigger persists**: for a triggered stop or stop_limit, the part not filled that day stays triggered in the next session too (stop → market at the next open, stop_limit → limit Lp; rebalance orders follow their TIF and `max_age_sessions`, exit orders do not expire).
- **Assumption sentence**: in the manifest `assumptions`, "stops fill at the stop price unless the open gaps through it; continuous intraday path assumed (no intraday jumps)". A stricter model that allows intraday gaps (`stop_limit_fill = close_confirmed`) is an optional knob for v1.x [RV 2.4].
- For fills of limit and stop orders judged on daily bars, `FillWarning` (error E11, a warning) is recorded once in the run warnings: "daily-bar fills for limit/stop orders are approximations; intraday sequencing requires minute/tick data (E4)" [research 2.5].
- When minute or tick data exist (E4), the intraday sequence is judged with that data (chapter 11). The v1 daily-bar rules remain then as the daily-bar mode.
- **Fill record** [SC-11]: for every fill, `fill_resolution` (v1 = `"1d"`; later `"1m"`, `"tick"`), `fill_rule` (`open`, `gap_open` (the open passed the stop price, filled at the open), `limit_open`, `intraday_adverse` (intraday H/L judgement), `close`, `zipline_close` (preset)) and `arrival_price` (the raw open O of the execution session; null if absent) are recorded. A run with mixed judgement resolutions shows "x/n fills judged on daily bars" in the report (a quantification of E11). With `decision_price` and `arrival_price`, implementation shortfall (fill price vs decision price) can be computed after the fact (later TCA).
- If `O` is null, orders due in the open phase are `blocked(no_open)`; if `H` or `L` is null, orders due in the intraday phase are `blocked(no_range)` (0 filled, carried over). If `C` is null with `obs_status = traded`, it is E14 (`TDS-V004`) and never reaches here; since 2026-11-22 so is a null `O`, `H`, `L` or `V` with `obs_status = traded` on an `equity` or `etf` row of bars that a declared profile requires them of (`tds:equity-daily-core@1.0` and above; TDS 5.5, 7, #705).
- **Mirror for shorts**: a short sale (`sell_short`) uses the "sell" rows of the table as they are, and a cover (`buy_to_cover`) the "buy" rows (limit short sale Lp: O ≥ Lp → O, intraday H > Lp → Lp; cover stop Sp: O ≥ Sp → O (gap), intraday H ≥ Sp → Sp). In the OCO of one short holding, when the stop loss (above) and the take-profit (below) are both touched in the same bar, only the stop loss (the adverse side). A short stop loss after a same-bar entry is `H ≥ Sp` (the adverse side). Costs (½ spread, impact) use the same formula regardless of side.

#### 5.6.4a Price grid
- **Grid source** (the first that exists, from the front): instrument `instruments.tick_size` → the `tick_band` of TDS `market_rules` (instrument `tick_rule_id` or market·`venue_group`, valid on that date) → the rules pack `[grid] tick_table` → the currency minor unit (F-15). The `classifications` step that came first left (#752); the per-instrument, half-yearly US assignment of the Rule 612 amendment from 2027-11-01 has no source yet (#802), and an instrument without one keeps the pack's US 0.01. In a price-band table, "the band of that price" is decided by the value before snapping.
- **Snapping order prices**: when an order is made (decision phase, exit-order creation, resizing), the computed Lp and Sp are snapped to the grid in the **conservative direction** — buy limits and cover limits down, sell and short-sale limits up, long sell stops down, short cover stops up, and for a stop-limit each of the two prices. The original value is `price_unrounded`, event `price_rounded`. They are snapped again after a split rebase (`price/ρ`) and a trailing recomputation. If the snapped price is 0 or below, the order is `REJECTED`. Snapping may cross a price-band boundary: when the original value is near a boundary, it goes to the nearest valid grid price in that direction (KR buy 4,997.5 → 4,995, sell 4,997.5 → 5,000). When the rule date changes and the price of a pending GTC order is not on the new grid, it is snapped again in the same direction at `session_start` that day, leaving the event `order_rebased(grid)`.
- **Judgement in integers of the market minimum unit**: every price comparison (limit and stop conditions, near the upper and lower limits, same-bar judgement) is made in integers on one coordinate `units(P) = round(P / base_unit)` (pack `[grid] base_unit`, KR 1 won, US 0.0001 dollars; `|P/base_unit − units| ≤ 1e-9·|P/base_unit|`). v3.1's "the tick count of that price `round(P/tick)`" flipped the order when the two operands were in different price bands, because the denominators differed (4,995/5 = 999 > 5,000/10 = 500). The tick table is used only for checking grid membership and snapping order prices. The bar data's `0.6768000000000001` is 6768 units (US; freqtrade#13661: an exit at the end vanished over a 1-ULP difference). An observed bar price that does not fit a minimum-unit integer within 1e-9 (data wrongly declared as adjusted prices, etc.) is **not moved**; only that bar's comparisons are made in floating point, and an `off_grid_bar` count (market·year, numerator/denominator) is recorded. The fill price is the snapped order price (Lp, Sp) or the data's O or C as is.
- When the price limits `U` and `D` (5.6.5) are made as computed values, they are snapped to the grid too (upper limit down, lower limit up — the conservative direction that widens the blocked side; the data's `up/down_limit_price` as is).
- Assumption sentence: "order prices snapped conservatively to the market price grid ({source})". The zipline preset turns grid snapping off (5.6.15).

#### 5.6.5 Tradability [11.4.2, design 3.6, C4]
Cases where an order cannot fill in that session (0 filled, the order remains and carries over, event `blocked`):

| Condition | What it blocks |
|---|---|
| `bars.obs_status ≠ traded` (halted / missing / no_trade) | everything |
| `tradability_daily.halted_full_day = true` | everything |
| (markets with price limits, rules pack `price_limits`) open near the upper limit: `O ≥ U·(1 − tol)` | open-phase buys |
| (the same) open near the lower limit: `O ≤ D·(1 + tol)` | open-phase sells |
| `locked_up = true` | intraday and close buys |
| `locked_down = true` | intraday and close sells |
| `locked_up` absent (the column missing, or null on the row) and the grid-snapped close at or above the upper limit, `C ≥ U` (decision 2026-11-06, #310) | close buys (`blocked(limit_up)`) |
| `locked_down` absent and `C ≤ D` (the same) | close sells (`blocked(limit_down)`) |
| `status_known = false` and `unknown_tradability = "block"` | everything |
| a ban interval of the rules pack `[shorting] ban_table` (half-open, `scope` grammar, union of `except_universe_ids` — 4.7) | new short sales (`sell_short`) only; covers and long trades allowed |
| ledger manual instruction `no_trade` / `reduce_only` (the increasing side) / `close` (only the reducing side allowed) | as instructed |

- `U`, `D` = `tradability_daily.up_limit_price/down_limit_price`. If absent, `U = ref·(1 + pct)`, `D = ref·(1 − pct)`, `ref` = `tradability_daily.reference_price` (if absent, the previous raw close), `pct` is looked up in the order `tradability_daily.price_limit_ratio` → the `price_limit` of TDS `market_rules` (v1.2, if present) → that market's rules pack `price_limits.table` (KR pack = `kr_price_limits_v1.csv`), and the computed U and D are snapped to the price grid (5.6.4a) in TDS 5.10's directions, U down and D up, so the fallback gives the limit prices the exchange would have published; when the market has no tick table, U and D stay unrounded as computed (the session table has no limit width, TDS-D8). A package without `tradability_daily` blocks only by `obs_status`, without halt or limit rules, and E30. `tol = [execution] kr_limit_tolerance` (default = the rules pack's `open_tolerance`, KR 0.005; a conservative margin that covers tick rounding and approximation; the key name is kept for v1 compatibility) [A-13].
- Whether an open fill is possible is not judged from the close's upper/lower limit mark (`limit_up_close`) [C4].
- When `locked_up`/`locked_down` is absent, a close at the limit takes its place for the close phase only (the engine takes the conservative side where the bar does not say, 5.6.4); an intraday fill is still judged on the bar's range, and the blocks of such sessions are counted as `limit_close_blocks`. Test G-112 (decision 2026-11-06, #310).
- **A new listing's first session** has neither `reference_price` nor a previous raw close, so in v1 it has no `U`/`D` unless `tradability_daily` gives the limit prices themselves (no price-limit rule; `obs_status`, halts and 5.6.6's `blocked(capacity_unknown)` still apply, so an order there fills only where the ADV rule does not bind): the order event of that session (a fill, or the 5.6.6 block) carries `new_listing = true`, and the report counts `new_listing_fills` and `new_listing_blocks`. The KR first-session band of 60% to 400% of the offer price (KRX, from 2023-06-26) is in the KR pack as a `verified = false` row (4.7) that v1 does not read; the ticket that turns it on needs the offer price in `instruments`. Test G-113 (decision 2026-11-06, #310).
- **Liquidation trading** (KR, the sessions before a delisting; no price limit): a session whose `tradability_daily.admin_status = liquidation_trading` has no `U`/`D`, so the open-limit and close-limit rows above do not apply (halts, `obs_status` and the volume cap do), and such fills are counted as `liquidation_trading_fills`; without the column the engine does not infer the period from `delistings` and applies the pack's limits as before. Test G-114 (#310).
- `status_known = false` (US before 2019-08-23) is `assume_tradable` by default, and the number of such fills is reported as `tradability_unknown_fills` [11.4.2].

#### 5.6.6 Volume cap and partial fills [11.4.2, C4]
- `ADV_t` = the mean of the t-based adjusted volume over the sessions among t−20..t−1 that have a bar with `obs_status` `traded` or `no_trade`, a `no_trade` session counting as volume 0 (a session held with no trades); `halted` and `missing` sessions stay out (`adv_window` 20) (#311, decision 2026-11-10). If these sessions are fewer than `adv_min_obs` (10), it is unknown → 0 filled in that session, event `blocked(capacity_unknown)`. The cap below and the impact of 5.6.8 use this `ADV_t`.
- Cap `q_max,t = floor_lot(min(η · ADV_t, ψ · volume_t))`, `η = max_participation` (default 0.05), `ψ = max_bar_participation` (default 0.10), `volume_t` = that session's actual raw volume (in t-based adjusted units). The first term is **an approximation of past-day liquidity, not a measurement of open liquidity** (manifest assumption wording). The second term is a physical cap, "no more can fill than the whole market's volume that day": it stops impossible fills in instruments whose usual ADV is large but which traded very little that day (right after a halt is lifted, thinly traded micro caps — included by default) (Qlib `_clip_amount_by_volume`, the zipline preset's same-bar 10%). That day's volume is **not a decision input but an after-the-fact feasibility cap on fill quantity** (INV-01): the fill session's volume is never an input of the decision behind it [#725 Decisions 235], and it is an approximation that can move results in either direction (it can also avoid bad fills by shrinking fills on low-volume days).
- **Open fills and the opening auction** [#725 Decisions 242; the owner, 2026-11-25]: when the package's bars hold the optional column `open_auction_volume` (TDS 5.5), an open-phase fill's second term is `ψ_open · open_auction_volume_t` instead (a session whose cell is null keeps `ψ · volume_t` for that fill, counted), `ψ_open = [execution] max_open_auction_participation` (default 0.10, a ratio of its own, not ψ). The column has `volume`'s unit and adjustment (shares, brought to t-based units by the quantity factor of 5.3.3); only the fill reads it, never a term or a decision, so it is not a `Price` column and has no row in 3.2's table and no degree in 3.4. Without it, open fills use the whole session's volume, `ψ · volume_t` is only a ceiling, and the label reports the fills it bound. The manifest assumption sentence "fill quantity capped by realized session volume (ex-post bound); full-session volume used for open fills" (its second half "opening-auction volume used for open fills" when the column is given; under `"prior"` below, "fill quantity capped by past volume only"), and in the report's orders line the number of fills bound by ψ / total.
- **Comparison mode** [#725 Decisions 243]: `[execution] volume_cap = "session"` (default, the cap above) or `"prior"`, which keeps only the first term, `q_max,t = floor_lot(η · ADV_t)`, data before the fill session alone; the label says which mode ran and, under `"session"`, how many fills the session-volume term bound, the difference between the two. The two keys join 3.11's table with the code that reads them. In every mode, the binding reason is the fill event's `capacity_bound_reason ∈ {adv, bar_volume, open_auction_volume}`, and the capacity scenarios (5.7) follow the same rule. Hand calculation G-56 (ADV 1,000,000 · that day's volume 2,000 · order 50,000 → 200 shares filled, 49,800 carried over).
- The cap is shared by all orders per (sleeve, instrument, **physical direction**, session) — buy direction = long buys + covers, sell direction = long sells + short sales (each on the same ADV basis; the same in the ×10 and ×100 of the capacity scenarios). Orders that come first in the processing order (5.6.3) use it first.
- The part left over because of the cap stays on the order and carries over to the next session (PARTIALLY_FILLED).

#### 5.6.7 Order quantities and cash [11.4.2]
- **Ruin check** [user decision 2026-10-28]: at the moment the `decision_value` (5.6.9) of a sleeve with shorts becomes 0 or below (checked at every `close_mark` and again just before each decision, before `T_i`; 5.6.3 steps 6 and 7), that sleeve becomes `ruined`, and this is not undone. After that the sleeve makes no new order intents (the decision phase is skipped, `order_suppressed(ruined)`), remaining rebalance orders are `CANCELLED(ruined)`, and exit orders (which only reduce holdings) stay and fill by the "Exit orders and cash" rule below. Mandatory obligations (borrow cost, short dividends, merger cash, terminal obligations, tax payments) keep being booked (negative cash, below). The check comes **before** computing `T_i`, so `V ≤ 0` does not flip the sign of targets. Other sleeves keep running (the combined NAV is the sum). The report header line "ruined {sleeve} on {date}", `ruined` and `warnings` in the ledger's `status.json`, an E38 summary; metrics that assume positive wealth (CAGR, Sharpe, Sortino, MDD, PSR, DSR) are "n/a (ruin)" for that sleeve and the combined series; the NAV series is not cut (a short book can lose more than its starting capital). The cash account (default) does not simulate forced liquidation (the margin account has the margin-call reduction and full liquidation at negative equity of 5.6.7a).
- **Quantities fixed at the decision**: the sleeve decision value `V = decision_value` (5.6.9; only values knowable at the decision). Executable weight `w'_i = w_i·(1 − cost_reserve_bps/1e4)` (`w_i` = the final target `final_weight`, 5.5.2a; without account rules, bit-identical to the canonical target, or to `decided_weight` with a `decide` block, 3.7; an order intent the block returns is made as it returned it, after the checks of 3.7) (default reserve 0, recorded in the manifest). Target share count `T_i = sign(w_i)·floor_lot(|w'_i|·V / P_i)` (integer arithmetic 5.6.1; signed), `P_i` = the raw close of `row_m(d)` (if absent, the stale valuation price of 5.6.3). Order quantity `Δ_i = T_i − q_i` (`T_i = 0` if the holding is not in the target). `Δ_i > 0` buys (covers if short), `< 0` sells (the part where a long goes below 0 is a short sale), 0 means no order. A Δ that crosses the holding's sign is split into two orders (reduce + open the other side), sent together at the same decision. The opening one (short sale or long buy) fills only after the holding has become 0 at the fill instant (short sale `q ≤ 0`, long buy `q ≥ 0`); otherwise it carries over in that phase as `blocked(closing_leg_pending)` (when the reducing one filled only partly because of a cap, a halt or a lock).
- **Filtering small orders**: with values at the decision, (a) if `|Δ_i|·P_i < max(min_trade_value, min_trade_weight·V)`, that order intent is not made (`order_suppressed(small)`); (b) with `rebalance_band = b`, for an instrument that is held and is in the target with the same sign, if `|w_held − w_target| ≤ b·|w_target|` it is not made (`order_suppressed(band)`), and outside it `band_mode = "edge"` (default) trades only up to the band edge `w_target·(1 ± b)` (the nearer side), while `"target"` trades up to the target (pysystemtrade `buffer_trade_to_edge`). **Neither applies to new entries, full exits (target 0) or sign changes.** The target table is unchanged, so INV-08 holds. By default all are off (the same results as v3), and the knobs go into `settings_hash`. In the report's orders line, "suppressed: small {m}, band {n}" (even when off, "orders below 0.1% of sleeve: n (cost x)" is shown). The zipline preset turns them off.
- **Hold-band entry and manual instructions**: `band_entries = "skip"` (5.5.3) and ledger manual instructions (5.9.6) cut order intents here (event `order_suppressed`).
- **Recording the target gap**: at every decision, `rounding_gap = Σ|w'_i| − Σ|T_i|·P_i/V` (intended executable gross allocation − gross allocation after rounding; long–short included, intended cash and cap leftovers are not counted as rounding — v3.1's `rounding_cash_weight = 1 − Σ…` had the wrong meaning for under-invested long books and long–short books), `n_zero_lot_targets` (target > 0 but `T_i = 0`) and the weight gap after rounding `½Σ|w_i − T_i·P_i/V|` are recorded in `coverage.parquet`. If `rounding_gap > 0.05`, `LotRoundingWarning` (E37, number of decisions, median). While waiting, it is not re-matched every day. Splits adjust the quantity [11.4.2].
- **Sellable quantity**: a long sell order's fill does not exceed `sellable_qty` (new stock-dividend shares are locked before the pay date). Short sales do not depend on holdings (no borrowable-quantity data, assumption sentence for the pack's `borrow_availability = "assume_all"`). A cover fill does not exceed the short size `|q|` (the excess is `qty_cancelled(over_cover)`; no long is quietly opened on the other side).
- **Short collateral**: `reserved = Σ_{shorts} |q_i|·mark_i·(1 + collateral_ratio[m])` (valuation point: open phase = that session's O, intraday phase = the value at the start of the phase, close phase = C). Usable cash `buying_power = cash − reserved`. A short-sale fill adds the proceeds `|q|·P` to cash and raises `reserved` by `|q|·P·(1 + c)`, so net `buying_power` falls by `|q|·P·c + costs` — so short sales too fill only within `buying_power`, by the same allocation rule as buys (the actual cost function, proportional scaling): a short-sale candidate's `need_i(q) = round_minor(c·q·P_i) + Σ round_minor(cost component_i(q))` (exactly the change in `buying_power` after the fill). Longs have no margin loan, so the book's feasible region is `long_weight + c·short_weight ≤ 1` (3.7, E16 before the run) — the rule of the cash account (default); with `[account] type = "margin"`, 5.6.7a instead of this section. A cover releases collateral and raises `buying_power` (costs excluded). Long buys only within `buying_power`. When the price moves so that `buying_power < 0` (short prices rise), there is no forced liquidation (no model, assumption sentence); new short sales and long buys are blocked (`blocked(collateral)`), and the number of such sessions and the largest shortfall are reported as `CollateralWarning` (E38). Blocked new risk is not ruin by itself (ruin is `decision_value ≤ 0`). With no shorts, `reserved = 0`, so it is the same as v3 (INV-22).
- **Exit orders and cash** (decision point C; ruined sleeves included): long exits (sells) raise cash, so they fill as they are, and in the same phase they fill **before covers**, so their proceeds are used for covers (the existing order). Covers (short exits included) fill only within usable cash `max(cash, 0)` (INV-06 as it is: order fills do not create or grow negative cash), and exit quantity not bought for lack of cash is **not removed** but carries over as `blocked(cash)` to the next phase or session (keeping the "no expiry" of exit orders, 5.6.2a; only rebalance buys get `qty_cancelled(insufficient_cash)`). When dividends, interest or long sell proceeds come in, it fills in the next phase by the same rule. In the report's orders line, the number of `blocked(cash)` exits and sessions. Test G-94.
- **Buy cash allocation** (the same rule in all three fill phases; the state at the start of a phase is as in 5.6.3): sells (long sells → covers) fill first and cash is updated (covers spend cash but release collateral; covers among themselves are allocated within `max(cash, 0)` by the rule below, and the unbought part of exit covers is `blocked(cash)` above). Then short sales and long buys are allocated within `buying_power` by the same rule. For each buy candidate, fillable quantity `f_i = min(remaining_i, the remaining part of the cap)`. Amounts are computed with the **actual cost function**: `need_i(q) = round_minor(q·O_i) + Σ round_minor(cost component_i(q))` (exactly the formulas of 5.6.8 — minimum commission, nonlinear impact and rounding included; the impact is evaluated with the session's already-filled quantity `Q_before` of the same instrument and side, so sizing and the booked cost agree; #311, decision 2026-11-10). (1) `k = min(1, cash / Σ need_i(f_i))`, `q_i = floor_lot(k·f_i)`. (2) If `Σ need_i(q_i) > cash`, **starting from the candidate with the highest fill ratio `q_i/f_i`** (ties by ascending `instrument_id`), reduce by one unit and recompute `need_i`, repeating until it fits (`need` is monotone in q, so it ends; instead of v2's descending `instrument_id` rule, under which a particular id always lost out). The part not bought for lack of cash (`f_i − q_i`) is removed from the order by the quantity event `qty_cancelled(insufficient_cash)` (`cancelled_qty` increases). Leftover cash is not redistributed to other instruments [design 3.6 "proportional scaling", 11.4.2, fix of A-09]. In the report's orders line, the count and amount of `insufficient_cash`.
- Intraday-phase buys use the same rule within **the cash at the start of that phase** (intraday sell proceeds excluded), and close-phase buys within the cash at that point.
- **Order fills** never take cash (`cash_minor`) below 0 (cash account; except the `zipline` preset, 5.6.15; test G-41; for the margin account, 5.6.7a). **Mandatory obligations** (borrow cost, short dividends, short cash in mergers and spin-offs, terminal obligations, `[taxes] pay = "cash"` payments) are booked as they are even when cash is short and can make cash negative (event column `shortfall = true`, number of sessions and largest shortfall in E38; user decision 2026-10-28) — obligations are not dropped or kept as hidden payables. Negative cash in the cash account bears no interest (assumption sentence "no margin interest on debit balances"; the margin account has `debit_rate_bps`, 5.6.7a). With negative cash, `buying_power < 0`, so new short sales and long buys are blocked. v1 settlement is the **immediate-settlement research model** (sell proceeds are usable in that session at once; it does not claim to match broker cash accounting) [11.4.1]. This assumption is visible in the config too, through the reserved key `[settlement] model = "immediate"`, and is recorded in the manifest's `policies.settlement`. US T+1 and Korean T+2 models are revisited when daily-turnover strategies are used [SC-18, OQ-13].

#### 5.6.7a Margin account (`[account] type = "margin"`) [user decision 2026-10-28]

The default is `"cash"`, and then 5.6.7 holds as it is (output bit-identical, P-MARGIN-OFF). With `"margin"`, the following changes the cash rules of 5.6.7. Counted separately per sleeve (no loans between sleeves).

- **One formula**: equity `E = cash + Σ_i q_i·mark_i` (long +, short −; receivables and unsettled entitlements included = 5.6.9 `decision_value`). Requirement `R(ρ) = Σ_i |q_i|·mark_i·ρ[m_i]` (the same formula for longs and shorts; short proceeds are in cash, so it means the same as Reg T's "150% of short value"). Initial requirement `IM = R(initial_margin)`, maintenance requirement `MM = R(maintenance_margin)`. The cash account's short collateral `reserved` and `collateral_ratio` are not used in the margin account (this formula replaces them; if present in the config, they are ignored with a 1-line notice).
- **Buying power**: new long buys and short sales only as far as they keep `excess = E − IM ≥ 0`: the candidates' `need_i(q) = round_minor(initial_margin[m_i]·q·P_i) + Σ round_minor(cost_i(q))` are allocated within `excess` by the same allocation rule as 5.6.7 (actual cost function, proportional scaling, one unit at a time from the highest fill ratio). And gross exposure `Σ|q|·mark ≤ max_gross·E`. So the gross exposure cap = `min(max_gross, 1/initial_margin_max)`·E (with several markets, conservative with the largest `initial_margin`). Fills can make cash negative (that is the loan). Long sells and covers reduce the requirement, so they always come first (the 5.6.7 order as it is).
- **Feasible region**: 3.7's `long_weight + c·short_weight ≤ 1` (130/30 → E16) applies only to the cash account. In the margin account, exceeding `long_weight + short_weight ≤ min(max_gross, 1/initial_margin_max)` is E16 before the run ("margin book exceeds buying power: L + S = {x} > {cap}"). 130/30 passes with `initial_margin` 0.5 and `max_gross ≥ 1.6`.
- **Loan interest**: loan `debit = max(0, −(cash − Σ_short |q_i|·mark_i))` (short proceeds are taken as not used to repay the long loan — the existing `short_rebate_bps` applies to short proceeds). At session start, the previous session's closing `debit × debit_rate_bps/1e4 × calendar days/day_count` is booked as the event `margin_interest` (a mandatory obligation, negative cash allowed), leg `financing` row (INV-06). Positive cash interest (`cash_rate_bps`) only on the part where `cash − Σshort value > 0`.
- **Margin call**: in the session's close phase (after `close_mark`), if `E < MM`, the event `margin_call` (E, MM, shortfall) is recorded, and in **the next session's open phase** forced reduction orders are sent: all holdings are reduced by the same ratio, keeping the fraction `f = clip((E − Σ_i cost_i(q_i·(1 − E/MM)))/MM, 0, 1)` (on the close valuation, computed once; costs are subtracted in advance so that E ≥ MM after the reduction and the call does not repeat every day); the reduced quantity is `min(|q_i|, ⌈|q_i|·(1 − f)⌉ units)`, **never more than the holding** (the sign does not flip). If `E ≤ 0`, `f = 0` — all holdings are liquidated and that sleeve is marked `ruined` (the same decision point as the ruin rule), risk-reducing groups first (long sells → covers, the 5.6.7 order), market orders, filled without the allocation rule or buying-power check (reduce only); halted instruments carry over per 5.6.5, and the remaining sleeve is not reduced again. Fills are the event `forced_liquidation` (instrument, quantity, price), rebalance orders are `CANCELLED(margin_call)` in that session, exit orders are resized to the remaining holding. Checked again at that session's close (if still short, repeated at the next open). The report header line "margin calls: {n} sessions, forced liquidation {x}" and an E38 summary.
- **Ruin**: the 5.6.7 rule as it is (`decision_value ≤ 0` → `ruined`); in the margin account the same check is made even without shorts when there is a loan. Forced reduction comes after the ruin check (a ruined sleeve gets only exit and forced-reduction orders).
- **Money management**: 5.5.1a's "no leverage `Σ|w| ≤ min(1, max_gross)`" is a cash-account rule. In the margin account, `G_max = min(max_gross, 1/initial_margin_max)`, and the k of `size.vol_target` can exceed 1 up to this `G_max` (the fourth term is `1/(initial_margin_max·Σ|w|)`). Other sizers stay as they are (≤ 1 per side); only `vol_target` and the L and S of `long_short` scale up.
- **Assumption sentence**: "margin account: single requirement R(ρ)=Σ|q|·mark·ρ; margin call checked at close, forced proportional reduction at next open; no intraday margin calls; rules-pack margin seeds unverified".

#### 5.6.8 Costs [R-25, R-61, 11.4.2]
The fill price is always the raw base price (O, C, Lp, Sp). Every cost is a separately recorded **cash deduction** (not put into the price — no double counting) [11.4.2]. Fill amount `N = q·P` (local currency). The sensitivity multiplier κ (0, 1, 2) multiplies every component.

| Profile | Components |
|---|---|
| `provisional` (default) | `provisional = κ·N·b_side/1e4`, `b` = toml `provisional_bps`[market][side] |
| `bps(buy, sell)` | `other = κ·N·b/1e4` |
| `per_share(c, min)` | `commission = κ·max(min, q·c)` |
| `realistic` | `commission = κ·max(min_amount, N·b_comm/1e4)` (table, that date·market·side), `tax = κ·N·b_tax/1e4` (sells), `other = κ·N·b_other/1e4`, `spread = κ·N·hs/1e4`, `impact = κ·P·Y·σ_t^a3·(Q_after·(Q_after/ADV_t)^a2 − Q_before·(Q_before/ADV_t)^a2)` (below; default a2 = 0.5, a3 = 1 → square-root impact `Y·σ·√(q/ADV)` for a single fill) |
| `none` | 0 |

- `hs` (½ spread, bp) = `clip(½·S_t·1e4, min_half_spread_bps[market], max_half_spread_bps)`. `S_t` = the Abdi–Ranaldo (2017) estimate: for each pair j of consecutive sessions in t−21..t−1, `η_j = (ln H_j + ln L_j)/2`, `c_j = ln C_j`, `s²_j = 4(c_j − η_j)(c_j − η_{j+1})`; `S_t = sqrt(max(mean(s²), 0))` — the paper's equation 10, its monthly-corrected form (the two-day terms averaged over the window first, the root taken once), kept because `test_abdi_ranaldo_known_value` (T3.8) and the `bench/` comparison are written against it; the two-day corrected equation 11 (the root of each `max(s²_j, 0)`, then the mean), which the paper recommends, is a diagnostic alternative in `bench/` (12.1 RS-06; decision 2026-11-06, #310); with fewer than 10 pairs or without H and L (null in those rows), `hs = min_half_spread_bps`, and the number of such fills is counted as `spread_floor_fills`. The Corwin–Schultz and Roll estimates are not used for engine costs, only for diagnostic comparison in `bench/` [blueprint chapter 8 item 4].
- **Impact on the session's cumulative quantity** (#311, decision 2026-11-10): a fill pays the marginal part of the session's impact for its instrument and side. `Q_before` = the quantity already filled in the session for the same instrument and side, `Q_after = Q_before + q`, and `P` is the fill's own price (every cost is a separate cash deduction at the fill's price, the first paragraph). A single fill (`Q_before = 0`) gives the former `κ·N·Y·σ_t^a3·(q/ADV_t)^a2` unchanged, and splitting an order does not lower the total: at the same `P`, `σ_t` and `ADV_t`, two fills of 500 pay the same impact as one fill of 1,000 (before each fill's minor-unit rounding below).
- `σ_t` = standard deviation of daily log returns over t−20..t−1 (ddof=1), `Y = impact_y` (0.5), `a2 = impact_a2`, `a3 = impact_a3`. Changing these knobs changes `settings_hash`, making a new candidate (attempt count +1, 6.2). The impact formula is a special form of Kissell I-Star and leaves a path to calibrate it later with live trading records (`decision_price`, `arrival_price`) [SC-11].
- Amount rounding: once per fill amount and per cost component, to an integer of the currency minor unit (`minor_unit`, KRW 1, USD 0.01), by that component's rule in the market rules pack `[rounding]` (default half-up, 5.6.1). The value before rounding is kept in the event too. Cash entries are integer sums and so independent of order [11.4.4]. Nautilus documented that "rounding rules differ by clearing house and exchange" (CME, Eurex), and the truncation practice of Korean taxes and commissions [unverified] is overridden by the user.
- **Effective-price diagnostic**: for every fill, `effective_price = P ± (½ spread + impact)/q` (buy +, sell −) is recorded in the event, and the count and share of those outside that session's [L, H] are given in the report's cost line (the price is not changed; a signal that order sizes are unrealistic. backtrader clips slippage to inside the bar range).
- **Provisional cost table** (optional) [V31-27]: with `provisional(table=…)`, `b_side` is looked up in the table row of (market, side, fill-date interval, ADV-amount interval at the decision); without a row, E23. By default there is no table.
- The profile name is written in the report header, with "(provisional costs)" appended for `provisional` [R-61].
- **The default profile** (user decision 2026-11-25, #764, on the two parts #311 left open): `provisional` stays a fixed bp per market; the ½-spread estimate `hs` and the impact are in `realistic` only, the profile the user chooses; `hs` has no half-tick floor.

#### 5.6.9 Sleeves, currencies, valuation [R-62, 11.4.1, C5]
- **Initial funding**: start time `inception_ts` = the run's first decision `decision_ts` (the earliest across all sleeves). Base-currency capital K is divided by `[sleeves] weights`, and sleeves in other currencies are converted once at the last exchange rate with `ts_available <= inception_ts` (minor-unit rounding). After that there is no cash movement between sleeves [11.4.1].
- Sleeve values (local currency):
  - `decision_value(d) = cash + Σ qty·mark + Σ receivables and payables of known amount` (used for decision sizing; qty is signed). Every value that goes in here is **a value knowable at `decision_ts`**: `mark` = the raw close of `row_m(d)` (if absent, the stale valuation price of 5.6.3; that bar's `ts_available <= decision_ts`), receivables = those whose amount was public before `decision_ts` (dividend `ts_terms_available`, terminal recovery `ts_value_available`, correction entries reflected). Entitlements of unknown amount (pending dividends, terminal entitlements whose recovery is not public) are left out. On the short side, **obligations of unknown amount** (short dividends whose amount is not public, delistings of shorted instruments whose recovery is not public) are not left out but put in as a **rule estimate**: a short dividend is that instrument's previous dividend amount of the same kind (0 if none observed, E30 count), a terminal obligation is `short_exit_estimate = q·ref*·(1 + max(ret_hi, 0))` (q < 0, `ref*` is from 5.6.13; the rule name is recorded in the manifest). Per TDS 5.13, `ret_hi` is a scenario, not an upper bound, so it is not called "the largest burden".
  - `nav_decision` [decision 2026-11-04]: the daily series of that valuation. For every session t of the schedule's calendar, `decision_value`'s formula evaluated as if t were a decision session: the cutoff is the `decision_ts` rule applied to t (the valuation time `v` below), `mark` is the raw close of the row read at that cutoff (or the stale price), receivables are those whose amount was public by that cutoff, entitlements of unknown amount are left out, obligations of unknown amount are the rule estimate, and no after-the-fact amount enters. `decision_value(d)` = `nav_decision` at the decision session of d. The account rules (5.5.2a) read this series, and it is written to `daily.parquet` (5.9.1).
  - `nav_economic = nav_decision − Σ short rule estimates + Σ after-the-fact realised amounts of entitlements and obligations of unknown amount` (signed: long entitlements +, short obligations −) [11.4.3 (5)]. It is **the report series**: used for report metrics and **not read by any rule** (sizing, orders, cash and the account rules read `nav_decision`). An estimate is **replaced** by the after-the-fact amount, not added to (the v3.1 formula added the after-the-fact obligation once more on top of the short estimate: with cash 1,100 and estimate −100, `decision_value` 1,000, and with after-the-fact −80, `nav_economic` = 1,020; v3.1 gave 920). As two series: on every session from the obligation's existence time until the amount −80 is public, `nav_decision` = 1,000 and `nav_economic` = 1,020; from the first session whose cutoff is at or after its publication, the −80 is a payable of known amount in `nav_decision`, it leaves the after-the-fact term, and both are 1,020. After-the-fact amounts are future data and never enter sizing, orders or cash (test G-42, P-FUT).
- **Combined valuation**: valuation time `v` = the same time as the `decision_ts` rule of the schedule's calendar session (with `market_local`, the close of the base-currency market). Combined NAV (base currency) = Σ sleeve `nav_economic` (the last valuation of that sleeve with `ts_close <= v`) × exchange rate (the last observation with `ts_event <= v`; after-the-fact allowed for reports). If the exchange rate is older than `max_stale_days_valuation`, warn and use the last value.
- The drift of country weights (sleeve value / combined value) is recorded and reported every day [11.4.1].
- **Currency attribution** [SC-07, OQ-3]: sleeve s's daily return in the base currency `R_s = (1 + r_local,s)(1 + r_fx,s) − 1` is split exactly into `r_local,s` (local-currency return) + `r_fx,s` (change of the valuation exchange rate, 0 for the base-currency sleeve) + `r_local,s·r_fx,s` (cross term), and the combined return = `Σ_s w_s(previous day's combined NAV weight)·R_s` is built up. Recorded per date in `attribution.parquet` (5.9.1). No approximation (the sum is checked by the test `test_fx_attribution_sums`).
- **Annual linking**: component CAGRs do not add, so annual values are defined as follows. For sleeve s, annual: `L = Π(1 + r_local) − 1`, `F = Π(1 + r_fx) − 1`, cross = `R_year − L − F` (= `L·F` + linking residual; the row sum of the additive table matches the annual return — v3.1's `(1 + R) − (1 + L)(1 + F)` was always 0 when R was linked exactly). Annual contributions of the combined portfolio: the daily contributions `c_{s,k,t} = w_{s,t}·component_{s,k,t}` are summed, and the difference between the daily sum and the annual combined return is shown as one row `linking_residual` (so row sum = annual combined return, exactly). The cost attribution rows are linked the same way.
- **NAV and P&L bridge** (INV-06): a `bridge` row every day per sleeve and currency: `price_pnl` (Σ holding × change in valuation price, including valuation against the fill price; shorts have negative quantities, so a price rise is a loss), `distributions` (entries of dividends, return of capital, cash consideration and fractional-share cash; **short dividend payments are negative**), `action_corrections`, `costs` (per order cost component, negative), `financing` (borrow cost, rebates, cash interest — borrow cost moved from v3.1's `costs`: an account event without an order, so it is not mixed into the execution-cost comparison), `external_flows` (ledger deposits and withdrawals, 0 in backtests) [V31-19], `fx` (currency exchange and exchange-rate effects, only in base-currency aggregation), `rounding` (minor-unit rounding residual) — sum = `nav_economic_t − nav_economic_{t−1}`, difference 0 (minor units). As columns in `daily.parquet`, checked by P-BRIDGE. Tests G-48 (valuation gain without trades: only `price_pnl`, and the bridge closes), G-49 (the bought quantity changes because of commission).

#### 5.6.10 Splits, reverse splits, stock dividends [11.4.3 (3)]
At the start of the effective session (quantity events whose terms are published late, 5.3.3a(6)), ratio ρ = the resolver's share factor `q_e` = the exact ratio `ratio_num/ratio_den` (required in TDS v1.4; if absent, `decimal_ratio` decides (5.6.1), and with the user's choice `decimal_ratio = "literal"` the decimal notation of `share_factor` is used (if absent, only for split-type events, the `ratio_new_per_old` rule — `stock_dividend` and `bonus_issue` are `1 + ratio_new_per_old`); if `ratio_num` or `ratio_den` is 0 or negative, or only one of the two is present, E13 at open (4.4-4), and a positive, complete ratio that disagrees with the f64 column is handled as absent (5.6.1, #188); the kind mapping is the table in 4.6; the cash of a paid capital reduction separately as a receivable):
- Holding `q' = sign(q)·((|q|·num) div den)`; the fractional share `frac = ((|q|·num) mod den)/den` (5.6.1 integer arithmetic) is received as cash `sign(q)·frac·prev_close_raw/ρ` (minor-unit rounding) (`cash_in_lieu`; money paid if short). Examples: 16 shares 1:8 → 2 shares, fraction 0; 7 shares 1:3 → 2 shares + ⅓ share in cash; −7 shares 1:3 → −2 shares − ⅓ share in cash (G-53, G-69).
- `avg_cost' = avg_cost/ρ`, `high_water' = high_water/ρ`.
- Remaining orders: `remaining' = floor_lot(remaining·ρ)` (integer arithmetic), `limit/stop' = price/ρ` snapped to the grid again (5.6.4a). The quantity accounting is recorded as the event `order_rebased(ρ)`, together with `requested`, `filled` and `cancelled` converted by the same ρ (the fractional rounding difference goes to `cancelled_qty`; INV-18 kept). Exit orders are resized per 5.6.2a(3).
- When a stock dividend or bonus issue occurs on a short holding, the short quantity grows by the same ratio (the rights of borrowed shares are repaid to the lender; the lock on new shares does not apply to shorts).
- New stock-dividend shares (`b·q`) are not put into `sellable_qty` until `pay_date` when `bonus_shares_tradable = at_pay_date` (immediately if there is no pay date) [A-16].

#### 5.6.11 Cash dividends [11.4.3 (4)(5)(6), R-60]
- **Entitlement**: created at the start of ex-date session t for the holding q as of the previous close (not the holding on the pay date). The entitlement or obligation quantity is fixed by this holding — even when the terms are published late, and even if it is sold or covered in between (5.3.3a(4)). **If q < 0 (short), it is an obligation**: amount `q·D` (negative, the full pre-tax amount without withholding — a substitute dividend), taken from cash on the pay date through the same entitlement, receivable and payment path (event `dividend_short`). Leaving out short dividends would inflate short returns (#310). The short obligation for a dividend with `gross_or_net = "net"` needs the pre-tax amount: if `[dividends] withholding[market] = w > 0`, restore it as `D_gross = D/(1 − w)` with an E30 count; if w is 0 the pre-tax amount cannot be determined, so `AccountingDataError` (E13, "short dividend obligation needs a gross amount").
- **Amount**: `D` = `cash_per_share` (raw amount, TDS). If the terms time `ts_terms` (5.3.3a) is at or before `ts_effective`, immediately a receivable `q·D·(1 − withholding)` (only for q > 0; withholding 0 when `gross_or_net = "net"`; q < 0 follows the obligation rule above); otherwise it is kept as an entitlement of unknown amount and turned into a receivable at the start of the first session after the publication time.
- **Payment**: cash at the start of the `pay_date` session (`pay_date_status = observed`; a date the producer gave as `estimated` is also used as it is, but counted as `estimated = true`). `pay_date` is optional: without it, the user declares once, in the `params` of `corporate_actions`, when dividend cash arrives — on the ex-date, or a number of calendar days after it; the declaration enters the semantic hash, and the tool writes that date with `pay_date_status = declared` (added to TDS 5.11 with #752). Without the declaration the tool stops and asks; there is no default. A package whose dividend rows lack `pay_date` and that declares no rule (another producer's `pay_date_status = missing` included) opens, and analysis is not blocked; `backtest`, strategy `screen` and `live` are E18 when they start, naming the declaration to add, as a package without trading status leaves `backtest` off (4.3); the engine does not estimate the date (the owner, 2026-11-25, #752). Such a date is paid in the first session on or after it. The dividend is a receivable from the ex-date once its amount is known (before that, an entitlement of unknown amount, as the Amount bullet says) until payment, outside `buying_power` (5.6.7), as any receivable is. The engine's estimate (the median interval of the same country's and kind's events, then a KR event's statutory deadline after `resolution_date`, then `fallback_pay_lag_days`, decision 2026-11-01) and `missing_pay_date = "fail"` were removed 2026-11-24 (#752) [9.0 Q29, R-60, A-08; #725 Decisions 214, 215; ADR 0049]. A row with `pay_date < ex_date` (US due-bill periods, some KR events) is not an error and is not corrected by the resolver: the quantity is still fixed by the holding at the close before the ex-date, so the cash cannot move before that holding is known — it moves at the start of the ex-date session (the pay date used is `max(pay_date, ex_date)`, the same rule as a delisting whose pay date lies before x, 5.6.13), the row is counted as `pay_before_ex` with an E30 warning, and the receivable has no life of its own (receivable and cash in the same session). Test G-111 (#310).
- Withholding `withholding` (default 0) is recorded separately as the event `dividend_tax`. `return_of_capital` is processed by the same path without withholding (the event kind is recorded as it is). Capital-gains tax is 5.6.11a. With the default 0, "dividends pre-withholding" in the report header.
- **Corrections and cancellations**: when the resolver issues a different version (amount correction) or `cancelled` at a later reference time, the correction entry of 5.3.3a(7): before payment, the receivable amount is corrected (`action_correction`); after payment, a cash correction (the same event kind); for a cancellation, a reversing entry of the receivable. The entitlement quantity (the ex-date holding) is not changed. Tests G-43 (amount correction after payment), G-44 (cancellation after announcement).

#### 5.6.11a Tax scenarios [OI-18, SC-17, user decision 2026-10-28]
- **Principle**: taxes, like commissions, **are written by the user in the config file** (3.11 `[taxes]`). The engine has no built-in per-country tax rates or systems and computes the written values as they are. Default `profile = "none"` (no taxes, "pre-tax" in the report header). Transaction taxes (securities transaction tax, SEC fee, etc.) are not here but in the cost table (5.6.8).
- **Dividends**: withholding is `[dividends] withholding` (5.6.11) as it is. Progressive calculations such as comprehensive taxation are not done (written in the report's assumption line).
- **Capital gains**: for every sell fill, realised P&L = fill amount − costs − acquisition cost (`basis`: average = moving average, fifo = first bought first). For shorts, for every cover fill, realised P&L = short-sale proceeds − short-sale costs (in proportion to the covered quantity, **only once**) − cover amount − cover costs (the same basis rule, average short-sale price), and it goes into the same group sum (short gains are taxed too, short losses offset too). P&L is converted to `[taxes] currency` at that day's valuation exchange rate and recorded as the event `realized_gain`. For each year (1/1~12/31) it is summed per group (losses offset within the same year, no carry-forward across years), and `max(0, sum − annual_exemption) × gains_rate` is set as that year's tax (integer minor units, rounding rule 5.6.1).
- **Payment**: with `pay = "report"`, NAV is not changed, and `daily.parquet` gets `nav_after_tax` (the value minus the taxes determined and accumulated up to that day) and `tax.parquet` (year, group, realised P&L, exemption, tax). With `pay = "cash"`, it is taken from the sleeve cash of that group's market at the start of the first session of `pay_month` in the next year (event `tax_payment`; when a group spans several markets, split by realised P&L share; booked as negative cash even when cash is short — a mandatory obligation, 5.6.7). Taxes still unpaid at the end of the run stay in `tax.parquet` as unpaid and are reflected in `nav_after_tax`.
- **Limits (documented)**: loss carry-forward across years, major-shareholder rules, comprehensive taxation, foreign tax credits and tax years that differ by country are not done in v1. When needed, the user approximates them with group tax rates.
- Tests: G-51 (hand calculation of average acquisition cost, exemption and loss offset), G-52 (`pay = "cash"`, deducted in May of the next year).

#### 5.6.12 Mergers, rights, unsupported events [11.4.3 (9)]
- `stock_merger`, `mixed_merger`: at the start of the effective session, holding q of the old instrument → the new instrument `sign(q)·((|q|·num) div den)` (ratio `ratio_num/ratio_den`; if absent, `decimal_ratio` decides, 5.6.1; `literal` uses the decimal notation of `ratio_new_per_old`) + cash `q·cash_per_share` (mixed merger; paid if short) + fractional-share cash (`sign(q)·frac·the new instrument's previous close`). A short holding becomes a short in the new instrument. The average purchase price carries over the total amount. Orders on the old instrument are cancelled. If the new instrument is not in `instruments` or is in another sleeve (another country), `AccountingDataError`. A `delistings` row this event handled (`handled_by_event_id`) does not apply the terminal value again.
- `cash_merger`: at the start of the effective session, a receivable `q·cash_per_share` (on the pay date or in that session), holding removed, orders cancelled.
- `rights_issue` (rights of a rights offering): with `[corporate_actions] rights = "lapse"` (default), the rights are neither exercised nor sold and **the holding quantity is unchanged** (share factor 1); the event `rights_lapsed` (estimated value `q·prev_close·(1 − price_factor)`, not put into the account) is recorded and the report shows the count and the sum of estimated values (G-47). With `"fail"`, `UnsupportedCorporateActionError` (error E12) [A-15].
- `spinoff`: with `new_instrument_id` and `ratio_new_per_old`, it gives `sign(q)·((|q|·num) div den)` shares of the new instrument (if short, a short in the new instrument is created too), and the old holding stays as it is. Until the new instrument's first bar the new holding is **unvalued**: it enters `decision_value`, `nav_decision` and `nav_economic` at 0 (the same exclusion from `decision_value` as a terminal entitlement whose amount is not yet public, 5.6.13; unlike it, no after-the-fact amount enters `nav_economic` either), no price is made from the parent's last price or the ratio, the sessions are counted as `unvalued_holdings` with an E30 count, and the first bar's raw close marks it as any holding (5.6.9), so that day's `price_pnl` carries its whole value. Test G-115 (decision 2026-11-06, #310). Without them, it is an unsupported event.
- `name_change`, `par_change`: no accounting effect, only the event is recorded. `symbol_change` was removed 2026-11-24 (#752): symbol periods live in `instrument_symbols` only (5.3.6) [#725 Decisions 209].
- When an unsupported event (`exchange_offer`, `other`, a spinoff with missing information, etc.) occurs on a **held instrument**, with `unsupported = "fail"` (default) it stops with error E12 (the instrument is not quietly deleted or kept as a fake). With `"cash_at_adjustment"`, cash `q·prev_close·(1 − price_factor)` (if `price_factor` is null this policy cannot be used either, E12) is put in that session and marked with the event `approximated` [11.4.1, 11.4.3].

#### 5.6.13 Delisting [Q18, 11.4.3 (7)(8)]
- The input is TDS `delistings` (without an event row, 4.5 F-13). Terminal session x = the first session in that instrument's calendar on or after `exit_date`. From after `last_trading_date` until before x, the holding is not tradable and is valued at the last valid close (`stale`). When `exit_date = last_trading_date`, which TDS 5.13 gives only when the instrument's calendar holds no session after the last trading date, x is that session: the holding is removed at its start, so the instrument cannot be traded that day, and the terminal value is taken from `ref_price` (the owner, 2026-11-27, #747).
- At the start of x: that instrument's orders `CANCELLED(delisted)`, holding removed, a **terminal entitlement** created (for a short, q < 0, so a **terminal obligation** — the lower the terminal value, the larger the short's gain). The amount is one of **three bases**, depending on what r (table below) is. Two reference prices: `ref_units` = `ref_price` × Π (the price factors of **share-unit events** (split, reverse split, stock dividend, bonus issue, consolidation) that took effect from after `ref_date` until before x — matching the quantity unit only), `ref*` = `ref_units` × Π (the price factors of **cash distributions** in the same interval; the same rule as the stale valuation price of 5.6.3). q is the holding in that unit (splits already reflected by 5.6.10).
  - (a) **Observed, inclusive** (r = `ret_observed`, `ret_observed_includes_distributions = true`): terminal = `q·ref_units·(1 + r)` − (the sum of those cash distributions already booked for that instrument after `last_trading_date`, the same sign rule). The observed return is relative to `ref_price` (TDS 5.13), so distribution factors are not multiplied in, and distributions already booked are subtracted. The result is not cut even if negative (needed to match the inclusive total).
  - (b) **Observed, exclusive** (r = `ret_observed`, flag false): distributions are booked separately per 5.6.11, and terminal = `q·ref_units·(1 + r)` (the observed amount excludes distributions, so the distribution factors are **not multiplied in** — multiplying would reduce it twice by the distributions).
  - (c) **Policy value** (r = `ret_policy`, `ret_lo`, `ret_hi`, or the 0 of `observed_only`): distributions are booked separately, and terminal = `q·ref*·(1 + r)` (the capital left after distributions is the policy's basis).
  - (d) **Observed, inclusion unknown** (flag null and there is an ex-date distribution after `last_trading_date`): no guessing.
