With `[delisting] unknown_inclusion = "conservative"` (default), of the two readings (a) and (b), **the one that makes less sleeve value** is used (long = (a), with the smaller total recovery; short = (b), with the larger total to repay), with an E30 count + the event `inclusion_assumed(conservative)`; counting distributions twice (v3.3's "treated as false": with `r = 0`, 151.00 + 151.30 = 302.30) does not happen for longs. With `"error"`, the economic calculation of that holding and label stops with E13 (Sol's proposal as an option). The majority of the three reviewers (Opus, Fable) said "proceed on the conservative side + warn" and Sol said "stop", so the default is the majority proposal and the stricter one is left to configuration.
  - So an observed 0 (b) and the policy `observed_only`'s 0 (c) give different amounts when there are distributions. This is intended: the vendor's observed value is based on the last trading price, and the policy on the remaining capital.
- **Distributions just before delisting only once**: a cash distribution (`cash_dividend`, `special_dividend`, `return_of_capital`) whose ex-date is after `last_trading_date` and before x is booked **as known at the ex-date** through the entitlement, receivable and payment path of 5.6.11 (the event `distribution_in_terminal` marks the subtracted term of (a)), and an inclusion flag learned later (known time = `ts_value_available`) does not retroactively erase distributions already booked — the subtracted term of (a) fixes the terminal amount (append only, INV-16; v3.1's "if inclusive, do not book distributions separately" made the past be rewritten by a flag not yet known at the ex-date). If the column is null and such a distribution exists, (d) (conservative side + E30, paired with the TDS-A011 warning). G-55 prevents LEAN#9819 (delisting liquidation with liquidation distribution 15.10 + stale price 15.13 → counted twice).
- **When the amount is known**: if r is `ret_observed`, the amount becomes known at `ts_value_available` (if null `ts_exit_known`, and if that is null too, x's `ts_open`, TDS 5.13). Until then the terminal entitlement is an **entitlement of unknown amount** (the same path as a pending dividend: excluded from `decision_value`, the after-the-fact amount only in `nav_economic`), and it becomes a receivable at the start of the first session after the publication time. If r is a policy value (`ret_policy`, `ret_lo`, `ret_hi`, or the 0 of `observed_only`), the engine policy sets that value at x, so it is a receivable of known amount from the start of x. Payment = `max(pay_date, the first session after the amount is published)` (if pay_date is missing or earlier than x, the first possible session on or after x).
- The first valuation session at or after the instant the amount becomes known (the start of x for a policy value) is also the one whose exchange rate converts that amount to the base currency in `survivorship.parquet`, except for an unobserved exit, which converts at the year's last valuation (5.9.1, #407); the ledger still books it in the sleeve's currency in minor units and converts only at valuation (INV-06, 5.6.9).
- Test G-42: even when rows of an October delisting + a December recovery publication are appended to the snapshot, the `targets` and `orders` of the October and November decisions are byte-identical (a fixed case of P-FUT).

| `policy` | observed | estimated / unobserved |
|---|---|---|
| `observed_then_policy` (default) | `ret_observed` | `ret_policy` (error E13 if null) |
| `observed_only` | `ret_observed` | 0 (last price) |
| `lo` | `ret_observed` | `ret_lo` |
| `hi` | `ret_observed` | `ret_hi` |

- **A null range** [#705 Decisions 8; #737]: an `unobserved` row may leave `ret_lo`, `ret_hi` and `policy_id` null (TDS 5.13), and F-13's in-memory event (4.5) leaves them null the same way; the engine reads both alike. It then assumes `ret_lo = −1`, everything lost, and `ret_hi = 0`, which under (c) is the terminal value `q·ref*`, the capital left after distributions, not `ref_price`. The policies `lo` and `hi`, the scenarios `delist_lo` and `delist_hi` and `short_exit_estimate` take these, and the result's labels say that the range of those exits is the engine's assumption, not the producer's. The two values are scenarios, not bounds: a consideration above `ref*` lies outside them. Under `observed_then_policy` such a holding is E13, as above.

- **One shared terminal-value function**: (a)~(d), the policy table and the amount publication time are computed only by the one function `terminal_value(q, held_path, delisting_row, policy, as_of) -> (amount, ts_known, basis)`, and accounting (this section), the label `fwd_return` (5.8), the shadow index (5.5.2a) and the triple-barrier label (5.8) all call this function (no separate formulas; `tests/unit/research/test_labels.py::test_terminal_uses_shared_resolver`). The label path passes as `held_path` the quantity starting from 1 share (splits and stock dividends reflected) and the distributions that path has already received (reinvested); a distribution with no valid price to reinvest at (ex-date after the last trading day) is carried as cash.
- The terminal value is applied only once (when the reference price `ref_price` is a Korean liquidation-trading price that already contains the loss, the reference producer gives `ret_observed = 0`).
- A short holding's terminal obligation of unknown amount is taken in `decision_value` as the rule estimate `short_exit_estimate = q·ref*·(1 + max(ret_hi, 0))`, and replaced in `nav_economic` by the after-the-fact amount (5.6.9).
- The scenarios `delist_lo` and `delist_hi` (included in the default scenarios) rerun the same targets with the `lo` and `hi` policies and report the CAGR range. The policy id (`policy_id`) and the usage count are recorded in the manifest.
- **A held instrument whose end is not known** [#705 G; #737]: when its bars end in `missing` rows to the package's end, with neither `last_session` nor `delisted_on` (TDS 5.5), the default run values it at its last valid close to the end of the run, as any stale mark (E25 after 20 sessions); the engine never assumes a delisting for it, and the result's labels count such holdings and say that their exit is not known, and the run's first screen shows the `delist_lo` to `delist_hi` range next to NAV (3.8, the "end unknown" line), the default valuation unchanged [#725 Decisions 245]; `survivorship.parquet` keeps its columns (such a holding is not a delisting). The scenarios `delist_lo` and `delist_hi` take such a holding into their range: `delist_hi` keeps its last valid close, as the default run does, and `delist_lo` values it at 0 from the session after its last valid close, the policy value −1 of the table above with `ref*` taken at that close. That session is an assumption inside the lower scenario, its "what if it left then", not a delisting: the engine writes it into no package and assumes it in no default run. "A tool never invents a delisting" forbids writing a delisting into the data and assuming one in the default run; a scenario's "what if" is neither.

#### 5.6.14 End of run [11.4.1]
It stops after the close valuation of the last session. No forced liquidation. Remaining orders (`final_open_orders`) and unpaid receivables are written in `metrics.json`.

#### 5.6.15 Preset `zipline` (tests only) [R-55, 8.1]
`preset="zipline"` is used only in `tests/parity` and `nx.backtest(..., preset="zipline")`, and writes "parity preset (not a realistic model)" in the report header. It cannot be set as a default in toml.

| Knob | zipline preset value |
|---|---|
| Fill time and price | the **close** of the session after the decision, price = C·(1 ± 5bp) (buy +, sell −) — FixedBasisPointsSlippage(5, 0.1) |
| Information time | `information_lag_sessions = 1`: the decision's signal row = `row_m(d) − 1` (zipline `before_trading_start` has data up to the previous day), sizing price = the close on the decision day |
| Volume cap | 10% of that bar's volume (sum of all orders on the same instrument), remainder carried over (NeverCancel) |
| Commission | PerShare(0.001, min 0), no currency distinction |
| Tradability | blocks zero volume only. Halt and limit rules off |
| Cash | negative allowed (zipline has no cash constraint) |
| Quantity | like `order_target_percent`, `int(target value/price)` at the close at the decision (truncated toward 0; signed short targets toward 0 too) |
| Delisting | settled at the last price (the same as `observed_only`) |
| Limit and stop trigger and fill | the zipline 3.2.1 way: the trigger is judged on the bar's **close alone** (`check_triggers(close)`), and the fill price is the close plus slippage (`fill_rule = zipline_close`) [in the pinned tag 3.2.1, `finance/slippage.py` lines 169 and 184, the trigger price = `data.current(asset, "close")` was confirmed (2026-10-29); the fill-price side is still an estimate — T3.15b reads `finance/execution.py`, `finance/order.py` (trigger state) and `finance/slippage.py` at the pinned 3.2.1 and confirms or fixes this row; the three-reviewer review confirmed the same direction on upstream main, but that does not replace evidence from the pinned version; OQ-9] |
| Fill record | `fill_resolution = "1d"`, `fill_rule = zipline_close` |
| Dividends | entitlement at the ex-date, cash on the pay date, withholding 0 |
| Cost sensitivity and scenarios | off |
| Tie-breaking | `tiebreak = "id"` (zipline sid order) |
| Price grid and rounding | grid snapping off, rounding half_up |
| Small-order filter and that day's volume cap (ψ) | off (the same-bar 10% replaces them) |
| Shorts | negative holdings allowed (zipline style), borrow cost 0, no collateral constraint (the same meaning as allowing negative cash), short dividends are paid |

### 5.7 Metrics [R-38, 11.4.5]

Valuation series: the combined `nav_economic`, daily (valuation calendar); the per-sleeve local-currency series use the same formulas.

| Metric | Formula |
|---|---|
| Daily return | `r_t = NAV_t / (NAV_{t−1} + F_t) − 1`, `F_t` = external deposits and withdrawals that came in at the start of that session (ledger only; 0 in backtests, so the same as v3's `NAV_t/NAV_{t−1} − 1`). Time-weighted return (TWR) [V31-19] |
| CAGR | `(I_T/I_0)^(365.25/D) − 1`, D = calendar days between the first and last valuation dates, `I` = the TWR cumulative index (`I_t = I_{t−1}·(1 + r_t)`, `I_0 = 1`). In backtests external flows are 0, so it equals `NAV_T/NAV_0`. With the NAV ratio, a ledger's CAGR rose from deposits alone — the growth of account value is separately `account_growth = NAV_T/NAV_0 − 1`. With a ruined sleeve, "n/a (ruin)" (5.6.7) |
| Volatility | `std(r, ddof=1)·√252` (annualisation fixed at 252, recorded in the manifest) |
| Sharpe | `mean(r − rf)/std(r − rf, ddof=1)·√252` on the daily net excess returns `r_t − rf_t`, where `rf_t = rate_t/1e4 × (date_t − date_{t−1})/day_count` is the `[account] cash_rate` series (3.11) as a daily rate, `rate_t` read by the accrual rule of 5.6.3 (0), and the constant `cash_rate_bps` when there is no series. The cash interest stays inside `r_t`; `rf_t` is subtracted. This `rf_t` is the one PSR, DSR and MinTRL use (#313, decision 2026-11-10). If `std = 0`, "n/a (zero variance)": a cash-only strategy with `r_t − rf_t ≡ 0` gives this, not 0, since 0/0 is not a number (#313, decision 2026-11-10) |
| Sortino | `mean(r)/sqrt(mean(min(r,0)²))·√252` |
| Maximum drawdown | `min_t(NAV_t / max_{u≤t} NAV_u − 1)`; the first value of the valuation series = the initial funding K at `inception_ts` (NAV_0), and it counts in the peak calculation (a first-day loss is a drawdown too; qlib#2358). With deposits and withdrawals, the TWR cumulative index instead of NAV |
| Calmar | `CAGR / abs(MDD)`, the CAGR and the maximum drawdown of the rows above over the same series (the report: the whole valuation series; `select="calmar"` in 6.3a: the fold's IS slice). When the maximum drawdown is 0 the value is "n/a (no drawdown)", and as a selection criterion it ranks below every finite value (a series that never fell says nothing about its drawdown risk). Test G-110 (#310) |
| Turnover (one rebalance) | `(½·Σ_i |w_i^after − w_i^before| + ½·|cash^after − cash^before|) / max(gross_before, gross_after, 1)` (weights within the sleeve, cash included; short weights by the difference of signed values; the denominator normalises only long–short books whose gross exposure exceeds 1, and long-only keeps the v3.1 value — "target turnover including residual cash", not the actual fill volume) |
| Turnover / year | sum of turnover / elapsed years (combined by a sleeve-value-weighted mean) |
| Cost drag / year | `CAGR(gross) − CAGR(net)` = direct costs + path effect (the effect of quantities and holdings that differed because of costs). Direct costs = annual mean of the cost-row sum / mean NAV (per component), path effect = the rest. The report writes the two separately |
| Gross return | **one series of the same targets replayed separately with zero costs (κ=0)** (the same series as `cost0x`, replayed only once) — not net return + cumulative costs [11.4.5] |
| Cost sensitivity | CAGR and Sharpe replayed for each κ ∈ `sensitivity` (κ = 0 reuses the gross replay, κ = 1 is the main run) |
| Sub-periods | CAGR, Sharpe and MDD for each 10 years from the start |
| Holding count | median of the number of held instruments on valuation days (per sleeve) |
| Benchmark | a combined series (base currency) that invests in each index with the same initial weights as the sleeves and lets them grow separately. Built on **the same dates at the same valuation time v** as the portfolio valuation series; first value = initial weight × K (the valuation at `inception_ts`), and the first return of both is on the next valuation day (the one-day offset of zipline#1832). The report header writes the index name, TR/PR and weighting method (TDS v1.2 `instruments.index_weighting`; if absent "weighting unknown") together with the portfolio's weighting method (qlib#2315: −4% a year from the difference between an equal-weighted portfolio and a cap-weighted index alone). n/a if absent |
| Relative to benchmark | `excess_t = r_t − r^bench_t` (same valuation calendar), annual excess return both arithmetic (`mean·252`) and geometric (`(Π(1+r)/Π(1+r^b))^(252/T) − 1`) under different names, tracking error `std(excess, ddof=1)·√252`, IR = arithmetic annual excess / tracking error, maximum drawdown of the cumulative excess series. n/a without a benchmark. α·β regression later |
| Monthly table | calendar-month return = the compounding of that month's daily returns `Π(1 + r) − 1` (not the sum), a year × month table, benchmark excess at the end of each row |
| Target gap and execution quality | per decision, `target_gap = ½·Σ_i |w_i^held − w_i^target|` (when that decision's orders are done or just before the next decision, whichever comes first) and its breakdown by cause (by block reason, cash shortfall, volume cap, lot rounding `rounding_gap`, small orders and band, manual instructions); per fill, `slip_vs_decision_bps = side·(fill_price/decision_price − 1)·1e4`, `slip_vs_arrival_bps` (the same form, basis O), reported as median and amount-weighted mean. All derived from the canonical events (INV-16) |
| Exposure | on every valuation day, long, short, gross (Σ|w|) and net (Σw) exposure (within the sleeve), the median on the first screen, per date in `exposures.parquet` |
| Rolling windows and date luck | a table (HTML) of quantiles (5·25·50·75·95%) and counts of the benchmark excess return over 1-year and 3-year windows (start date moved each month); with `offsets=k`, the minimum, median and maximum of CAGR, Sharpe and MDD across the k series |
| PSR(SR*) | `Φ( (SR̂ − SR*)·√(T−1) / sqrt(1 − γ̂₃·SR̂ + (γ̂₄ − 1)/4·SR̂²) )`, SR̂ = daily (non-annualised) Sharpe (ddof=1) of the excess returns `r_t − rf_t` (Sharpe row; #313, decision 2026-11-10), γ̂₃ skewness, γ̂₄ **raw kurtosis (normal = 3; not excess kurtosis)**, both population moments `m_k/m_2^{k/2}` (no bias correction), T = number of daily observations. The implementation allows only the scipy `kurtosis(fisher=False, bias=True)` form, pinned by hand calculation G-60 (putting in excess kurtosis makes the denominator wrong by −0.25·SR̂² for normal returns, so PSR looks larger: LEAN `Statistics.cs:200-214` [est.], vectorbt#872) [Bailey·López de Prado 2012/2014; formula confirmed by three reviewers]. Not computable: T < 30, zero variance, radicand ≤ 0 → "n/a ({reason})" (E21). **Assumption**: the denominator is the variance of SR̂ for serially independent returns with these moments; serial dependence is not in it (the Lo row below changes only the annualised Sharpe shown, not PSR, DSR or MinTRL) (design review 2026-11-04) |
| DSR | `PSR(SR*_N)`, `SR*_N = sqrt(V[SR]) · ((1 − γ)·Φ⁻¹(1 − 1/N) + γ·Φ⁻¹(1 − 1/(N·e)))`, γ = 0.5772156649, N = the number of distinct strategy candidates recorded within the **cohort (6.2)**, V[SR] = the variance (ddof=1) of the daily Sharpe, on excess returns `r_t − rf_t` (#313, decision 2026-11-10), of each candidate's representative observation (the most recent observation) in that cohort. With N = 1, SR* = 0 (DSR = PSR(0)), shown as "(1 candidate)". The report records `cohort_sha256` [C10, 6.3]. It corrects for selection among those N recorded candidates only, under the PSR assumption; selections outside the cohort (other studies, other kinds, earlier observations of the same candidate) and repeated looks at the same data are not in N (6.2) (design review 2026-11-04) |
| MinTRL(SR*, α=0.05) | `1 + (1 − γ̂₃·SR̂ + (γ̂₄ − 1)/4·SR̂²)·(Φ⁻¹(1−α)/(SR̂ − SR*))²` daily observations, divided by 252 for years, with SR̂, γ̂₃ and γ̂₄ of the excess returns `r_t − rf_t` (#313, decision 2026-11-10). SR* = 0: the length this one series needs against zero, with no adjustment for selection among candidates. If `SR̂ ≤ SR*`, "n/a (SR ≤ 0)" [SC-09, Bailey·López de Prado 2012 S-23 unverified]. With N > 1 in the cohort, a second value with SR* = SR*_N of the DSR row, labelled "selection-adjusted reference" ("n/a (SR ≤ SR*_N)" when it does not apply) (user decision 2026-11-04, #197) |
| Autocorrelation and Lo Sharpe | `ρ1` = the first-order autocorrelation of daily net returns. If `|ρ1| > 0.1`, the Lo (2002) corrected annualisation: `SR_ann = SR_daily · 252 / sqrt(252 + 2·Σ_{k=1}^{q}(252 − k)·ρ_k)`, q = 10 (ρ_k is the sample autocorrelation; k > 10 **truncated** to 0 — the original formula is k = 1..251), shown on the first screen as "lags ≤ 10" [SC-09, S-28 unverified]. A correction of the annualisation only, not of uncertainty; with a small `ρ1` and larger `ρ2…ρ10` the line is not shown although the dependence is there. So the HTML "hygiene" section always shows `ρ1…ρ10` against the band ±1.96/√T with the count outside it, and **dependence is visible** when the Ljung–Box Q over lags 1–10 has p < 0.05; then the PSR, DSR and MinTRL line carries the label "IID-based: dependence visible (Ljung–Box lags 1–10, p < 0.05; may also reflect changing volatility)" — the test also reacts to changing volatility, and its nominal 5 % false-alarm rate is measured later by scenario S11 of `docs/design/statistics-verification-plan.md`, not assumed (user decision 2026-11-04, #197). HAC standard errors and the block bootstrap are 12.1 (RS-01, RS-02) |
| Drawdown duration, recovery duration, worst month | calendar days from peak → trough of the maximum drawdown, calendar days of recovery from trough → peak ("not recovered" if it does not recover), the minimum calendar-month return (compounded) [SC-09]. **Recovery = NAV at or above the previous peak** (equality included, compared as minimum-unit integers); peak date = the last day before the trough on which it was at or above the previous peak (backtrader PR #528: counting only "above" as recovery joined two drawdowns together) |
| Signal–weight correlation `signal_weight_corr` | per decision and sleeve, `c_d = corr( zscore(score_d), w_d − w̄_d )` over all instruments in the universe with finite scores (target 0 included), Pearson correlation, not average ranks, summed in the 5.3.10 order. Reported as the median and count across decisions. There is no risk adjustment, so it is not called the Grinold–Kahn transfer coefficient (TC) (renamed from v2's "transfer coefficient") [SC-08] |
| Currency attribution | annualised contributions (local, FX, cross) of the 5.6.9 formula [SC-07] |
| Capacity ("capacity" scenario) | the same targets replayed with capital ×10 and ×100 (execution only): CAGR, the share of orders hitting the cap (`capacity_bound / number of orders`), mean participation rate (`fill_qty / ADV`). "cap binds at ≈ K" = the capital at the multiple where the share hitting the cap first exceeds 5% (among ×1, ×10, ×100; "> x100" if none) [SC-06]. Under the default profile `provisional` the result says that the cost per unit traded (its bp) does not grow with order size and that the limit shows only through the volume caps (5.6.6) (user decision 2026-11-25, #764) |
| N_eff, DSR(N_eff) | v1.x [SC-10]: the number of clusters from grouping the daily net-return correlation matrix of study candidates with distance `1 − ρ`, average-linkage hierarchical clustering + maximum silhouette (the method name is recorded). DSR by default keeps N [OQ-6]. It counts clusters only: the cluster-level return series and the Sharpe variance across clusters are not defined here, and it is a different method from ONC (OI-17), so the two are not named as one. Before T7.2 is built, this row defines the cluster-level return series, the Sharpe variance estimate across clusters and the alignment of candidates with different periods and frequencies (#197) |
| Log-loss and Brier (ML classification factor) [#197] | on `evaluate` of an `nx.ml.factor` with a classification label, over the scored predictions (each decision × instrument whose decision is not among its fit's training samples; the others are counted, not scored): **log-loss** `= −mean ln max(p(y), 1e-15)`, p(y) = the probability the prediction's `probs` give the realised class y, over the label's declared classes (a declared class absent from a fit has probability 0, 5.3.11); **Brier** on the chosen `score=` `= mean (s − t)²`, t = 1[y = +1] for `p_up`, 1[y = −1] for `p_down`, y for `p_up_minus_down`, 1[y = larger class] for two classes with `score=None`. For `p_up_minus_down` this is the mean squared error of the score against y ∈ {−1, 0, +1}, not the classic Brier score; the name is kept for the one line, and this sentence is the reader's warning. FactorReport line `log-loss {x:.3f} · Brier ({score}) {y:.3f} · scored {a}/{b} (model fit out of sample)` |

**What each scenario runs again** (decision 2026-11-04, #207): every scenario keeps the run's **canonical** target (`target_weight`, the strategy's decision; the Pipeline and the targets are reused, never decided again) and runs as its own column with its own state (5.3.8). A cost, delisting or `ca_exit_lo` scenario runs the account layer (5.5.2a) and execution (5.6) again under its own costs and policies, so an account rule such as the drawdown brake may act differently under 2× costs, and that difference is part of what the scenario shows. A `decide` block (3.7) reads the account, so it is called again in each such scenario, before its account layer, and the scenario's targets from `decided_weight` on may differ from the base run's; `ca_exit_hi` and `capacity`, which reuse the base run's value or final targets, do not call it [#725 Decisions 241]. In `capacity`, the block's order intents are carried as the weights they leave after the checks, `final_weight`, and scaled with the capital like every target; their quantities and limit prices are not kept, so `capacity` does not replay those orders' fills, even at ×1. Capacity runs execution only, on the final targets of the base run with capital scaled. No scenario keeps a second, simplified ledger (ADR-0041).

| Scenario | Targets decided again | Account layer (5.5.2a) | Execution (5.6) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `gross` (= `cost0x`), each κ of cost sensitivity, `cost2x` | never | yes, under the scenario's costs | yes |
| `delist_lo`, `delist_hi` | never | yes, under the scenario's delisting policy | yes |
| `ca_exit_lo` (only when the base run has a `ca_unverified_exit`, 5.6.1 (8)) [#150] | never | yes, with those exits valued at 0 | yes |
| `ca_exit_hi` | never | no: the base run's value as it is (its valuation of those exits), not replayed | no |
| `capacity` (×10, ×100) | never | no: the base run's final targets, capital scaled | yes |

### 5.8 `evaluate` computation [R-37, 11.4.5]

- **Decision grid**: per market, the last session of `freq` (market_local). Value = the term value of that row. Sample = universe true ∧ factor finite ∧ label finite.
- **Label `fwd_return`** (economic label, default): entry and exit times by `label_entry` (`"next_open"` default: entry = the open of the session after the decision, exit = the open of the session after the next decision; if `horizons` is an integer h, the open of entry + h sessions; with `"close"`, decision close → next decision close; `"close"` in a graph that reads a value table is refused before the run, E18, 5.1.4 [#752]). Value = **the holding return of buying 1 share and holding it with dividends reinvested at the ex-date close** (the CRSP-style total-return convention), taken in time order [2026-11-04]: start with n = 1 share and cash C = 0 at the entry; for each ex-date session e with entry session < e ≤ exit session, in session order, (1) at the start of e the quantity event of e changes the share count, `n ← n·q_e` (the resolver's `q_e`), and the holder at the previous close gains the right to `n·D_e` (`D_e` = the resolver's cash distribution per share; return of capital included); (2) when the share is still held after the close of e — e is before the exit session — the right is reinvested at that close, `n ← n / m^lab_e` with the label-only factor `m^lab_e = P_e / (P_e + D_e)` (`P_e` = the raw close of e); (3) otherwise — the exit is in session e, at its open, inside the bar (a barrier) or at its close — the right is not reinvested but added as cash, `C ← C + n·D_e`, at its full amount whether its pay date is before or after the exit (a receivable at the exit). `R = (n·P_exit + C) / P_entry − 1`. Each right is counted once, as shares when reinvested and as cash otherwise, and the price drop of e is already in `P_e` or `P_exit`, so nothing is counted twice. With no exit in an ex-date session this is the v3.4 product `(Π_{e ∈ (entry, exit]} (q_e / m^lab_e)) · (P_exit / P_entry) − 1`. The value depends only on what it reads: a label that exits at the open of e reads no high, low or close of e. Withholding 0 ("pre-withholding"), rights lapses as price effect only (no rights value), and a distribution whose amount is not available at the label computation time makes that label missing (`no_terms`) without E21. Example: bought at 100, dividend 10, ex-date close 100 → 10% (the adjusted-price ratio convention gives 11.1%) — a zero-cost accounting run under the same conditions as test T-21 (a G-19 variant) gives the same value; exiting at the ex-date open 90 instead gives (90 + 10)/100 − 1 = 0%, whatever that day's close, the same as the 1-share accounting path (sale 90 + dividend receivable 10; T-21 cases, 8.2). If it terminates before the exit, the last term is obtained with the shared function `terminal_value` of 5.6.13 (the same bases (a)~(d) as accounting, inclusive-distribution subtraction, unit adjustment, policy; v3.3's independent formula `ref_price·(1+r)` was removed), and ex-date distributions after the last trading day are not reinvested but added as cash — the same value as the 1-share path of a zero-cost accounting run (G-55 (g)). If the entry open is missing (halt), it is excluded and counted as `no_entry`. If the exit open is missing, it exits at the last valid close and is counted as `stale_exit`.
- **Label `fwd_return_adj`** (for comparison): `R = (X_exit/C_total[exit]) / (X_entry/C_total[entry]) − 1` (adjusted-price ratio; the zipline and alphalens convention — the reinvestment price is the theoretical ex-dividend price, so it differs from the economic holding return). The zipline comparison and the alphalens adapter use this label. The default can be changed with `[evaluate] target`. In a `tds:minimal-daily` package only this one works (4.3).
- **Label times** [SC-20]: every `LabelFrame` has three: `ts_label_start` (the entry instant: with `next_open`, the entry session's `ts_open`; with `close`, the decision session's `ts_close`), `ts_label_end` (the end of the outcome = the exit instant, by the same rule; for a terminal-value exit, the `ts_open` of terminal session x), `ts_label_available` (the first instant that label value can be computed = the maximum of the available times (5.1.2) of every observation the value reads: the entry and exit prices, the close of every ex-date session where a right is reinvested, every bar a triple-barrier path judges, the `ts_terms_available` of every distribution and quantity event in the window, and `ts_value_available` if the terminal recovery is an observed value [2026-11-04]; an observation the value does not read does not move it). The user label path (`Label.*`, TDS `label_panels`) was removed 2026-11-24 (#752); labels are the engine's only.
- **Purging**: a label whose `max(ts_label_end, ts_label_available)` is later than the `ts_close` of the last session of that run segment is dropped (a delisting label whose recovery is published after the segment end leaves that segment; test T-22). The validation methods (6.3a), ML training windows (5.3.11) and triple-barrier labels use the same columns. With `embargo_sessions > 0`, that many decisions before the segment end are dropped too [11.4.5] — a different meaning from the splitter's `embargo=` (the AFML meaning: drop training samples **after** the test block, 6.3a).
- **Label `labels.triple_barrier(take_profit=a, stop_loss=b, max_sessions=h)`** [SC-20]: entry = the open `P_0` of the session after the decision (`no_entry` if halted). Barriers = upper `P_0·(1+a)`, lower `P_0·(1−b)` (raw prices; corporate actions move the barriers by the same 5.6.2a rule as exit orders), time = the close of the h-th session counting the entry session as 1 (the same **session count** as `exits.time_stop`; the end time differs — the label is the h-th close, while the exit order is created after that close → fills at the next open). **`time_exit`** [decision 2026-11-04, #195]: `"close"` (default) is that h-th close; `"next_open"` ends an untouched path at the open of session h + 1, the instant a `time_stop(h)` exit order fills, so the label is the value a strategy with the same exits realises (barriers are judged through session h only, as the exit orders are; when that open is missing, the first later session that has one, as a blocked order carries over (5.6.5), counted `stale_exit`); `ts_label_end` = that session's `ts_open`. `time_exit` is part of the label definition hash (so of `fit_id` and the L1 key). From the entry session, **the 5.6.4 judgement function (the same code)** is called every session: if the open jumps over a barrier, the open; if both are touched in one bar, the adverse side (the lower one). Value `tb_ret` = the `fwd_return` value of the same path with `P_entry = P_0` and `P_exit` = the end price (distributions and quantity events by the same time-ordered rule, so a right whose ex-date is the end session is cash), `tb_side` ∈ {+1 upper, −1 lower, 0 time}; `ts_label_end` = the end session's `ts_close` (`ts_open` for an open gap), `ts_label_available` by the rule above. A delisting ends like the time barrier, with the 5.6.13 terminal value. `evaluate(target=)` and `ml.factor(label=)` take the two columns (`tb_ret`, `tb_side`). **What the label holds** [2026-11-04]: no costs in either `time_exit` mode (a strategy's return differs by its costs, 5.6.8); distributions and quantity events by the `fwd_return` rule; an open that jumps over a barrier ends at that open; both barriers in one bar take the adverse side; barrier widths are fixed fractions of `P_0`. Meta-labels and volatility-width barriers (V31-69) are v1.x. **Why it is in v1**: the judgement function, the session count (`time_stop`), the three label times and purging are already in v1, and the only new thing is "each label has a different end time", which the purging formula handles as it is.
- **IC**: Spearman (average-rank) correlation per decision and market. If valid pairs < `min_pairs` (20), that decision's IC is missing [11.4.5].
- **Newey–West t**: IC series `x_1..x_T` (missing removed), `γ_j = (1/T)Σ_{t=j+1}^{T}(x_t − x̄)(x_{t−j} − x̄)`, `σ² = γ_0 + 2Σ_{j=1}^{L}(1 − j/(L+1))γ_j`, `t = x̄ / sqrt(σ²/T)`. `L = floor(4·(T/100)^(2/9))`, and for overlapping labels (h > 1 decision interval), `L = max(L, h − 1)`. L is written in the report [11.4.5].
- **Quantiles**: the 5.4.3 rule, equal-weighted mean label per quantile. Spread `Q_q − Q_1` mean and NW t. Number of monotone steps = `#{j : mean(Q_{j+1}) > mean(Q_j)}`.
- **Turnover (quantile q)** = the mean of `1 − |S_t ∩ S_{t−1}| / |S_t|`.
- **Rank autocorrelation** (user decision 2026-11-25, #764): per market, the Spearman (average-rank) correlation of the factor's values at a decision and at the decision before it, over the instruments in both decisions' samples (universe true ∧ factor finite; no label is needed), an instrument in only one of the two left out; missing when those instruments are fewer than `min_pairs` (20), and with one decision there is no pair, so it is missing. The report gives its mean (`rank autocorr (mean)`, 3.9) in the `turnover` attribute.
- **Coverage** = `Σ valid / Σ universe` (numerator/denominator together) [R-11].
- **By year and by segment**: the same statistics by the year and segment of the decision date.
- **Terminal counts**: per quantile, the number of labels whose exit was a terminal value, by status.
- **Naive breadth and expected IR** [SC-08]: naive breadth `BR_naive` = (median of valid instruments per decision) × (annual decisions = number of decisions / elapsed years), per market. It is not an estimate of the number of independent bets but a naive opportunity count ("naive opportunity count"). `implied_ir = IC_mean · √BR_naive` (the Grinold–Kahn fundamental law; since it assumes independent bets, a sentence saying to read it as an upper bound is output with it).
- **BHY-adjusted p** [SC-09]: from the NW t values of the representative observations of the m factor candidates in the same **cohort** (observations with the same study, `kind = factor`, segment, label name, horizon and freq; 6.2), two-sided p values `p_i = 2(1 − Φ(|t_i|))` are made, and this candidate's value after the Benjamini–Hochberg–Yekutieli adjustment (`c(m) = Σ_{k=1}^{m} 1/k`, the cumulative minimum from the back of the sorted `p_(i)·m·c(m)/i`, capped at 1). Fixed output of the limitation sentence: "m counts candidates recorded in this cohort only". The HLZ t > 3 hurdle mark stays as it is. With `evaluate(term=Mapping)`, each item is a separate candidate.
- The long−short quantile spread is a diagnostic. To carry it into execution, `long_short(top, bottom)` and `[shorting] enabled = true` (5.5, 5.6).
- `evaluate` too, with `save_signal=True`, leaves `signal.parquet` (the same schema as backtest) (the three-step record of signal table → signal analysis → portfolio analysis) [SC-16]. The public signature of `evaluate` has `save_signal: bool = False` (a keyword outside the "common" of 3.8).

### 5.9 Run records [R-30~R-34, C9, 11.7]

#### 5.9.1 Run id and folder
- `run_id = f"{YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS}-{kind}-{name}-{h6}"`. The time is the local time of the computing machine, `h6` = the first 6 characters of sha256(canonical JSON{graph_hash, params, settings, snapshot_semantic_hash}). If it already exists, `-2`, `-3`.
- Writing: everything is written to `runs/.tmp-<uuid>/`; on success it is `rename`d to `runs/<run_id>/`, the files are made read-only (`chmod a-w`), and `SEALED` (content = the sha256 of `manifest.json`) is written last. On failure the temporary folder is deleted and `status = aborted` is recorded in the index. A write failure is a run failure [R-30, 11.7 (3)(4)].
- At start, (snapshot_id, run_id) is put into the index `pins` (the root `PINS/` when configured, 4.2) [11.7 (9)].
- **Seal recovery**: at every open, among `runs/.tmp-*`, those with `status = running` in the index and no process are deleted and marked `aborted`; if `runs/<id>/` exists without `SEALED` (stopped after the rename and before the seal), `SEALED` is recomputed and written, or, if the content hash differs from the manifest, it is moved to `runs/.aborted-<run_id>/` (kept for inspection, never read as a result) and marked `aborted`, its index `path` pointing there (decision 2026-11-04, #247). Test `test_runstore.py::test_interrupted_seal_recovers` (forced stop at every commit boundary).

```
runs/<run_id>/
  manifest.json            (below)
  metrics.json             summary metrics (the source of the report and the runs table); top level = the linear run, with the blocks evaluation_context, primary, diagnostics, provenance (6.3a) [#216]
  decisions.parquet        DecisionContext rows
  targets.parquet          canonical targets (5.5)
  orders.parquet           order events (canonical columns below) — canonical
  account_events.parquet   cash entries, corporate actions, dividends, terminations, funding, currency exchange, corrections — canonical (schema below)
  positions.parquet        session_date, sleeve, instrument_id, qty(signed), side, mark_price, mark_status(traded|stale|stale_adjusted), value_local, weight
  daily.parquet            valuation_date, scope(ALL/<MARKET>…), ccy, nav_economic, nav_decision, decision_value, cash, positions_value,
                           receivables, unknown_entitlements, ret, cost_commission, cost_tax, cost_spread, cost_impact,
                           cost_other, cost_provisional, sleeve_weight, bridge_price_pnl, bridge_distributions,
                           bridge_action_corrections, bridge_costs, bridge_financing, bridge_external_flows, bridge_fx, bridge_rounding
                           (amount columns are int64 minor units + base-currency float; `bridge_financing` and `bridge_external_flows` are always present from v3.2 (value 0) even without short sales or a ledger —
                           so that the P-SHORT-OFF baseline does not collide with schema changes in later tickets)
  scenarios/{gross,cost2x,delist_lo,delist_hi,ca_exit_lo,capacity_x10,capacity_x100}.parquet   daily series with the same columns (only those turned on; cost0x = the single gross file; ca_exit_hi = the base run's `daily.parquet`, no file)
  attribution.parquet      scope(ALL/<MARKET>…), period(date or year), component(local/fx/cross/commission/tax/spread/impact/other/provisional/path/linking_residual), value_base, value_local  [SC-07]
  exposures.parquet        valuation_date, scope, sleeve_weight, ccy_weight(json: currency→weight), sector(within the sleeve; what it groups by is not fixed here, T4.10 chooses it: the classification scheme it read left with `classifications` [#725 Decisions 250]), size_quintile, top10_weight, n_holdings, cash_weight  [SC-07]
  coverage.parquet         decision_id, year, market, term, n_present, n_universe, n_excluded_by_reason(json), n_microcap_excluded, n_signal_nan, n_target_not_held, n_na_filled, n_tied_at_cutoff, target_gap, gap_by_cause(json), rounding_gap, n_zero_lot_targets, n_suppressed_small, n_suppressed_band, n_band_entry_skipped, n_held_missing_score, n_unknown_by_leaf(json)  [SC-23]
  survivorship.parquet     year, market, n_universe, n_excluded_by_reason(json), n_microcap_excluded, n_delisted, n_delisted_held, n_observed, n_policy, n_unobserved, terminal_value_base, terminal_value_long_base, terminal_value_short_base, terminal_value_lo_base, terminal_value_hi_base, nav_share, nav_share_lo, nav_share_hi  (columns below) [#160, #394]
  coverage_fields.parquet  year, market, dataset, field, freq, required_by_universe, n_universe_rows, n_value, n_stale, n_not_yet_published, n_missing, share_value  (columns below) [#160]
  term_diagnostics.parquet market, session_date, term, diagnostic, n — the counts of 5.4.1, the table it defines
  trades.parquet           trade_id, sleeve, instrument_id, side(long|short), status(closed|open), ts_open, ts_close, qty_max, avg_entry, avg_exit, legs, realized_minor, unrealized_minor(open), costs_minor, distributions_minor, borrow_minor, exit_reason(rebalance|exit:<rule>|delisted|merger|override|ca_unverified_exit [#150]), sessions_held, mae, mfe(computed without the extremes of the entry and exit bars, marked "daily-bar approximation")
  ca_exclusions.parquet    (only when 5.6.1 excluded a name) sleeve_id, instrument_id, event_id, cause(ca_ratio_missing|ca_ratio_unverified|ca_ratio_disagrees), v_e, ts_effective, excluded_from, excluded_to, held_qty, price_basis(last_valid_close|null), basis_session, exit_price, pnl_minor, costs_minor, trade_id(nullable)  [#150]
  offsets.parquet          (only with offsets) offset, cagr, sharpe, mdd, turnover
  waterfall.parquet        change in Σ|w| per decision × slot (5.5.2a)
  risk_state.parquet       account-rule state (5.5.2a; 0 rows without account rules)
  decide_state.parquet     the decide block's state (3.7, 5.5.3; only with a decide block)
  decide_log.parquet       the decide block's record per decision (3.7; only with a decide block)
  skips.parquet            skipped instruments, blocks and rules: decision_id, sleeve_id, stage, instrument_id(nullable), block, reason, n (6.6)
  predictions.parquet      (with ml.factor) 5.3.11
  fits.parquet             (with ml.factor) 5.3.11
  validation/              (method ≠ linear) split.json, folds.parquet, paths.parquet, path_daily/<path>.parquet, selection.parquet, stats.json (6.3a)
                           columns: split.json = {method, params, split_hash, groups[{group, first, last}], splits[{split, test_groups}]}; folds = fold, train_first, train_last, test_first, test_last, selected_params_json, is_sharpe, is_cagr, oos_sharpe, oos_cagr, oos_mdd, n_purged, n_embargoed, n_fits, refit(fixed|online), n_online_fits, status [#216]; paths = path, cagr, sharpe, mdd, turnover, ruined, n_days, status, eval_first, eval_last, n_valid, n_total [#216]; path_daily = session_date, nav, ret_net, ret_gross, group, split; selection = fold, grid_point, candidate_key, is_metric, is_n_decisions, n_purged, n_embargoed, rank; stats.json = distribution, pbo{value, k, n}, psr, dsr, min_trl, degradation{slope, n}, selection_stability
  signal.parquet           (save_signal=True; in evaluate too) decision_id, instrument_id, term, value
  pipeline.parquet         (run_pipeline only) PipelineResult.table, its columns as 3.5 gives them
  candidates.parquet       (sweep only) candidate_key, date, ret
  detail/                  (save_detail=True) tier-3 outputs, below: predictions.parquet of every grid point and every unselected candidate
  source/                  captured source (below)
  report.html
  SEALED
```

**Three tiers of outputs** (decision 2026-11-04, #206). (1) **Always written**: provenance and summaries — `manifest.json`, `fits.parquet` with `pred_sha256`, `validation/split.json`, the `folds`, `paths` and `selection` summaries, `stats.json`, `candidates.parquet` returns, and every table above not named in (2) or (3). (2) **Written by default**: the predictions and series that reached an account — `predictions.parquet` of the linear run and of the selected candidate per fold, and `path_daily/` of every CPCV path. (3) **Written only with `save_detail=True`** (CLI `--save-detail`): `detail/predictions.parquet` with the predictions of every grid point and every unselected candidate (columns as `predictions.parquet` plus `candidate_key`). Whether or not a tier-(3) file is written, the manifest lists it with its value hash and a `written` flag, so `rerun` can regenerate and compare it (5.9.4) and the seal pins its content. No tier is held whole in memory: predictions are written per fit and per chunk as they are produced (chapter 7 design mean 6).

`orders.parquet` canonical columns [11.6]: `event_id, event_kind(created|accepted|triggered|fill|qty_cancelled|exit_resized|order_rebased|price_rounded|status), phase, order_id, decision_id, ts_event, session_date, sleeve_id, instrument_id, purpose, order_type, side, tif, limit_price, stop_price, trigger_state, requested_qty, filled_qty, cancelled_qty, remaining_qty, fill_qty, fill_price, currency, fill_value_minor, commission_minor, tax_minor, spread_cost_minor, impact_cost_minor, other_cost_minor, provisional_cost_minor, cost_unrounded, status, reason, snapshot_id, model_version, fill_resolution, fill_rule, decision_price, arrival_price, price_unrounded, capacity_bound_reason, effective_price, outside_range, slip_vs_decision_bps, slip_vs_arrival_bps` [SC-11]. The `side` values are buy / sell / sell_short / buy_to_cover.

`account_events.parquet` canonical columns (schema `nexusbt-events/1`): `event_id, seq, ts_event, session_date, phase, sleeve_id, instrument_id(nullable), kind(fill_cash|cost|dividend_entitlement|receivable_booked|receivable_paid|action_correction|cash_in_lieu|rights_lapsed|terminal_entitlement|terminal_receivable|funding|fx_conversion|dividend_tax|approximated|ratio_snapped|distribution_in_terminal|external_flow|override_set|override_cleared|user_fill|user_reject|user_trade|dividend_short|borrow_fee|short_rebate|collateral_change|cash_interest|ratio_unsnapped|sleeve_ruined|user_correct) event_chain_id(corporate-action chain, 5.3.3a), amount_minor(int64, signed), currency, amount_unrounded(float64), qty_delta(int64), cash_after_minor, ts_known(the instant the amount became known), shortfall(bool; a mandatory obligation exceeded cash and made it negative), snapshot_id, reason`. A given (sleeve, seq) is unique, and `seq` increases without gaps.

**Was the data enough: two tables** (#160; the record #157, R2). `survivorship.parquet` and `coverage_fields.parquet` answer question 7 of the report (3.9), which summarises them; the Parquet files keep them in full. The engine computes both from what the run already reads (the snapshot, `coverage.parquet`, the canonical events and, for the lo and hi columns, the scenario replays while they run); no new data is required. Both are always written (tier 1 above). T3.14 fixes their schemas together with the other tables and the event schemas of this section, so that no later ticket changes `nexusbt-events/1` for them. `year` is the calendar year of the market-local session date, and each table is sorted by its leading columns.

`survivorship.parquet`, one row per (`year`, `market`) of the run period, also for a year with no delisting, so that a reader sees the universe was the one that existed then and not today's survivors:
- `n_universe`, `n_excluded_by_reason(json)`, `n_microcap_excluded`: the values `coverage.parquet` gives for that market at the year's first decision [SC-23] — the universe size and the exclusion reasons at the time; null when that market has no decision in the year. `coverage.parquet` has a row per `term` of a decision, and `n_universe` is the same on every `term` row of that decision, since the universe does not depend on the term (#394).
- `n_delisted`: the instruments that were in the universe at one or more decisions of the run and whose terminal session x (5.6.13) falls in that year and inside the run period.
- `n_delisted_held`: those of `n_delisted` held, long or short, at the start of x. `n_observed`, `n_policy`, `n_unobserved` split it as the `exits while held` line (3.9) does. A held delisting of a name that was never in the universe (a spun-off share, 5.6.12) is on that line and not in this table.
- `terminal_value_base`: the sum of the terminal amounts (5.6.13, the shared `terminal_value` function) of those held delistings under the run's `[delisting] policy`, in the base currency: the net of longs and shorts. `terminal_value_long_base` and `terminal_value_short_base`: the same sum over the long holdings and over the short holdings (a terminal obligation, so its amount carries the short's sign), with long + short = net; a long and a short of equal terminal value in one year and market net to 0 and still show in these two columns (#394). Each amount is converted to the base currency once, at the valuation where its amount is first known and enters `nav_decision` (#407; `nav_economic` holds the after-the-fact amount from x, 5.6.13): the first valuation session (valuation time `v`, 5.6.9) at or after the instant 5.6.13 makes the amount known (`ts_value_available`, with its fallbacks, for an observed return; the start of x for a policy value), with the report-valuation lookup of `fx_rates` (5.1.5: the last observation with `ts_event <= v`) that the combined valuation of 5.6.9 uses, so `terminal_value_base` is the amount `nav_decision` first saw. Two exits have no such valuation and convert at the last valuation session of x's year inside the run instead: an unobserved exit (`exit_value_status` unobserved, its policy value; counted in `n_unobserved`, which is how the row says so) and an observed amount published after the run's last session. A name's lo and hi amounts convert at the same instant as its amount under the run's policy. `nav_economic` carries the same receivable, or the cash it was paid into, at the rate of the year's last valuation, so when the rate moved between the two valuations `nav_share` differs from a share taken at one rate by that move; the table does not restate it (G-118). `terminal_value_lo_base` and `terminal_value_hi_base`: the same sum in the `delist_lo` and `delist_hi` replays (3.8); null when that scenario is off.
- `nav_share`, `nav_share_lo`, `nav_share_hi`: each sum divided by `nav_economic` (scope `ALL`) of the same replay at the year's last valuation; the lo and hi columns are null with their sums.

`coverage_fields.parquet`, one row per (`year`, `market`, `dataset`, `field`) for every field the strategy read: prices and volume (`bars` columns), each value-table item (`field` = the item), corporate actions, eligibility and every other leaf of the term graph. A field with no value in a year keeps its row, with `share_value` 0 while the year has universe rows, so "30 years of data" reads as the years each field really covers. The grain is the year and the market, not the universe rule (decision 2026-11-11, #160); the rule is in the manifest. A year whose universe is empty at every decision still writes the row, with `n_universe_rows` 0 and `share_value` null (#394).
- `required_by_universe`: true when the universe rule reads the field (an eligibility filter, a price in a liquidity filter, `nx.Item.x > 0`). Such a field is still counted, but the universe rule admits mostly rows that have it, so its `share_value` is high by construction and says little about the snapshot (a stale value or a window with a missing session can still leave a universe row `n_stale` or `n_missing`); the flag tells the reader so (#394).
- `n_universe_rows`: the (decision, instrument) pairs of that year and market whose universe is true. Each pair is in exactly one of four states at its `decision_ts`, so `n_value + n_stale + n_not_yet_published + n_missing = n_universe_rows` (a null `n_stale` counted as 0); this sum does not hold for an event dataset (below).
- `n_value`: the lookup (5.1.5) returns a value and it is not stale. `share_value = n_value / n_universe_rows` (null when the denominator is 0).
- `n_stale`: the lookup returns a value whose availability instant is more than one period of the field's `freq` before `decision_ts`. The availability instant is the one 5.1.5 reads for the dataset, not always `ts_available`: `ts_known_from` for `instrument_symbols`, the bar's `ts_end` for `bars`, the instant `availability_by_column`'s rule gives for `Trad.*`, and `ts_available` where 5.1.5 names it (#394). `freq` is the `freq` of the field's `dictionary/columns` row (TDS 4.4), not a table of the engine's own (decision 2026-11-11, #160). A value-table item has no `freq` and is never called stale: the label gives, per item, the last value's date and the age of the values read instead, with no fixed threshold (5.3.5) [#725 Decisions 229]. The values read as periods are TDS 4.4's `daily` and `monthly` and the three this section adds, `weekly`, `quarterly` and `annual`: `daily` is one session of the market's calendar, and `weekly`, `monthly`, `quarterly` and `annual` are 7 days, 1, 3 and 12 calendar months. With `freq` `event` or null (also when the dictionary has no row for the field) nothing is called stale: `n_stale` is null and such a pair counts in `n_value`. Any other value (a producer's `1d` or `Q`) is treated the same way, and the report's question 7 (3.9) names that value once, so the null is not silent (#394).
- `n_not_yet_published`: the lookup returns nothing, and the snapshot has a row of that instrument and field observed at or before the decision whose `ts_available` is after `decision_ts`.
- `n_missing`: the lookup returns nothing and no such row exists.

What counts as a value depends on the dataset's kind (#394). For a state or daily table (`bars`, a value-table item and the like), a lookup that returns nothing is `n_missing` (or `n_not_yet_published`, as above). For an event dataset (`corporate_actions`), an absent event is not a gap: an instrument with no split in a year is complete, not missing, so the row reports in `n_value` the events read in that year and market, and leaves `n_missing`, `n_stale`, `n_not_yet_published` and `share_value` null, since an event count over (decision, instrument) pairs is not a share.

What a missing value did to the strategy is not in this table: the instruments and decisions it dropped are counted in `skips.parquet` and on the `skipped:` line (6.6).

- **Events are canonical** [INV-16]: `orders` and `account_events` are canonical, and `positions`, `daily`, `metrics`, `attribution` and `exposures` are built from the canonical tables (+ snapshot prices and exchange rates) by `artifacts.derive_tables`. The test `test_derived_from_events` (P-DERIVED) checks rebuilt values = stored values.
- Run outputs are only Parquet, JSON and HTML (no pickle, 2.6).

#### 5.9.2 `manifest.json`
Adds the following to the columns of design 3.8: `schema_version: "nexusbt-run/2"`, `decision_contexts_hash`, `clock`, `sleeves`, `markets{key: {calendar_id, currency, rules_pack: "KR@1" | "untested"}}`, `segment`, `segments_touched`, `snapshot_semantic_hash`, `snapshot_value_hash`, `snapshot_physical_hash`, `policies` (the snapshot's `tds.json` policies + engine policy versions: `fill_model: "daily-ohlc-adverse-v1"`, `cost_model`, `cost_table_sha256`, `delisting`, `dividends`, `corporate_actions`, `settlement`), `sim_config` (**every knob value** of appendix C, defaults included) [SC-11], `data` (`tds_version`, `profiles` (the three results), `capabilities`, `unchecked`, `fallbacks`, check level and warning-code counts, `verified_stamp`; 4.4), `assumptions` (a list of sentences: "immediate settlement research model", "capacity = lagged ADV approximation", "cash earns {r}%", "dividends pre-withholding", "intraday sale proceeds usable from close phase", "fill quantity capped by realized session volume (ex-post bound)", "longs fully cash-funded; no margin loan", "no margin interest on debit balances", "stops fill at the stop price unless the open gaps through it", "untested market rules: …" etc.), `retention` ("managed" | "unmanaged", 4.2), `resolved_defaults`{`clock`, `base_ccy`, `sleeves`}, `candidate_key`, `observation_id`, `cohort_sha256`, `study_id`, `validation{method, split_hash, n_folds, n_paths}` (when method ≠ linear; the hash of P-SPLIT-DET), `trial_counts{runs, candidates, observations, independent_assumed}`, `outputs{file: {physical_sha256, value_sha256}}`, `source{entry, outside}` [#283, #250]: `entry` is the entry file's path under `source/` (5.9.3), null in an interactive session. `outside` maps each module imported during the run whose file is inside the project by 6.5 (6) but outside the project folder (a package installed in editable mode, #577), and is therefore not in `source/`, from its module name to the sha256 of its file at the run: `{"mylib.helpers": "<sha256>"}`; `{}` when there is none. `rerun` hashes the file each name resolves to in its new process and refuses with E15 when one differs, is missing where its name points, or cannot be found by its name, naming the module ("helper module mylib.helpers changed since run {id} and is not kept in source/: it is outside the project folder"). A manifest without `outside` (a run saved before it) is read as "not recorded", not as `{}`: `rerun` runs, and when its outputs differ, on a rerun that is not `new_snapshot=True`, it says that this run's outside helpers were not recorded, also when they differ beyond the tolerance in another environment (5.9.4); `environment{python, platform, cpu(e.g. arm64/Apple M4), machine_id(2.6), uv_lock_sha256, packages{name: version}, numerics{numba_fastmath: false, numba_parallel: false, blas(np.show_config summary), thread_env{OMP_NUM_THREADS, OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS, VECLIB_MAXIMUM_THREADS, NUMBA_NUM_THREADS}}}` [SC-26]. `created_at` is left out of the logical hash [R-31]. The git user name is not in the manifest: it is kept only as the index's `runs.created_by` (5.9.5), a local file (decision 2026-11-12, #163).

#### 5.9.3 Capturing source [11.7 (2), C9]
In `source/`: the entry file, every file inside the project folder among the modules imported during the run (paths kept), `nexusbt.toml`, the resolved parameters `params.json`, a copy of the engine `uv.lock`, the project `uv.lock` (if present), and the results of `git rev-parse HEAD` and `git status --porcelain`. `rerun` runs from this source (not from the files of the current working folder): (1) it starts a **new Python process** and puts `source/` at the front of `sys.path`, so the captured helpers are loaded before same-named modules of the current working folder (modules already imported in a notebook session do not interfere). (2) If the captured `uv.lock` differs from the current environment, it shows "environment differs" and compares value hashes within tolerance (8.1 L2); to rebuild the same environment, `nexusbt rerun ID --env` creates a temporary virtual environment from the captured lock (`uv sync --locked`) and runs inside it. Test `test_rerun.py::test_archived_helpers_override_local`.

**No local path** (decision 2026-11-10, #355; the rule of 2.7 applied to what a run writes): every path the engine itself writes into `manifest.json`, into `source/` or into a verification stamp (`runs/.cache/verified/`, 4.2) is relative — an output to the run folder, a captured file to `source/` (an entry file outside the project folder is copied to `source/_entry/<file name>`), a snapshot file to its package folder, as `tds.json` names it — and everything else that names a place or a machine is an id or a hash (`snapshot_id` and the three snapshot hashes, never the snapshot's location; `machine_id`; `uv_lock_sha256`), with `environment` holding no host name, user name or folder; an absolute path, a host name or an account name can therefore appear only inside what is copied or recorded as the person wrote it (`nexusbt.toml`, the project `uv.lock`, the source files, `params.json`, the lines of `git status --porcelain`), all of it in `source/`, which the next paragraph keeps local; the index (5.9.5) is not a file of a run and this rule does not cover it.

**Shareable and local files** (decision 2026-11-12, #163; the record #157): a person may hand the **shareable** files of a run to someone else: `report.html`, `manifest.json`, `metrics.json`, `SEALED` and every other file and folder of the 5.9.1 tree except `source/` (the Parquet tables, `scenarios/`, `validation/`, `detail/`). `source/` is **local**, and so, outside the run folder, are the index (5.9.5), `runs/.cache/`, `PINS/` and `runs/books/` (5.9.6); `report.html` links no local file. A shareable file holds none of these:
- an absolute path, a home folder or the data root (`[data] root`, `NEXUSBT_DATA_ROOT`): the snapshot is named by its `snapshot_id` and hashes, a file of the run relative to the run folder, and a source file, also in a "your code" error tail (6.6), relative to the project folder as `source/` keeps it (or `_entry/<file name>`);
- a host name or a local user name: `machine_id` stays the hash of 2.6, and the git user name is not written (5.9.2);
- any part of the snapshot's `producer` block (TDS 4.2: name, version, commit): a `producer.hints` sentence appears only as its key (the TDS code or `fallback:<id>`), the sentence itself on the console;
- what a person wrote into a `manual:` book (5.9.6), where an account number or a holder name would sit: the book's name and the `note` of a recorded fill row.

What `rerun` needs of these stays in `source/` (`nexusbt.toml`, `params.json`), and E31 refuses another machine's run in any case (2.6). The values of the strategy's parameters appear as the person wrote them (the report prints `params`); the engine does not inspect them, so a path passed as a parameter is the person's to leave out. Test `tests/repo/test_shareable_outputs.py::test_shareable_files_hold_no_local_values` (T3.14, which gives its planted values).

#### 5.9.4 Hashes and determinism [R-31, INV-09]
- Physical hash = file sha256. Value hash = the canonical value serialisation of TDS 4.5 (using the same function `tds.value_hash`; independent of Arrow version and writer). The "logical comparison" of run outputs is the value hash.
- In the same pinned environment (same `uv.lock`, same machine kind), `rerun` must match down to the physical hashes of the outputs. In a different environment, the logical comparison uses a tolerance (the L2 basis of the table in 8.1). A run without NAV (a pipeline or a screen run) has no L2 row: its tables are compared cell by cell on the same keys within L1-a's `|a−b| ≤ 1e-9 + 1e-9·|b|`, NaN agreeing with NaN. The environment differs when the sha256 of the captured engine or project `uv.lock` differs from the current one, or when the project lock is on one side only (decision 2026-11-20, #637).
- Tier-(3) outputs (5.9.1, #206) are compared by the value hash the manifest records, not by the files present: a `detail/` file that was not written or was pruned is regenerated by `rerun --save-detail` and compared with that hash; without `--save-detail`, `rerun` compares tiers (1) and (2) only and says that tier (3) was not compared.
- Parquet writing: pyarrow, zstd 3, row group 131,072 rows, statistics on, string dictionary, no time in the metadata.

#### 5.9.5 Index `runs/index.sqlite` [R-34, 11.7 (8)]
SQLite (WAL). Append only: `BEFORE UPDATE/DELETE` triggers guard the tables below (allowed: `runs.status` running→sealed|aborted, sealed→pruned, `runs.path` to `runs/.aborted-<run_id>/` together with running→aborted when seal recovery moves the folder (5.9.1, #247), `runs.detail_pruned` 0→1 (#206), filling `pins.ts_released`). The canonical record of pins is this `pins` table (4.2).

| Table | Columns |
|---|---|
| `families` | family_id(UUID) PK, parent_family_id(nullable), reason(`init` / `new family: {text}` / `linked at migration`), ts_created [#196] |
| `studies` | study_id(UUID) PK, name UNIQUE, family_id, ts_created, hypothesis |
| `runs` | run_id PK, kind, run_kind(research/debug/seed_comparison/rerun/preview/failed/pruned, 6.2), family_id, study_id, name, ts_created, created_by, snapshot_id, snapshot_semantic_hash, segment, graph_hash, params_json, settings_hash, candidate_key, conditions_hash (6.4), preregistration_id(nullable: null when no preregistration matched, 6.2), look_number(nullable: the look of the run's segment in the family, 6.2; null on the default segment), status(running/sealed/aborted/pruned), detail_pruned(0/1, tier 3 of 5.9.1), path, metrics_json, derived_from |
| `candidates` | candidate_key PK, study_id, kind(factor/strategy), segment, graph_hash, params_hash, settings_hash, first_run_id (identity only; no statistics columns) |
| `observations` | observation_id PK, run_id, candidate_key, snapshot_semantic_hash, segment, start, end, target(label name; `net_return` for strategies), horizon, freq, n_obs, sr_daily, skew, kurt, nw_t(factors), method(`linear`/`walk_forward`/`cpcv`/`cscv`), split_hash(nullable), fidelity(`full`/`preview`), ts_created |
| `cohorts` | cohort_sha256 PK, study_id, kind, segment, target, horizon, freq, members_json(sorted list of (candidate_key, observation_id)), ts_frozen — when a report produces statistics, the cohort of that moment is frozen and stored |
| `exposures` | run_id, family_id, study_id, segment, start, end |
| `preregistrations` | id PK, family_id, study_id, graph_hash, metric, direction(`higher`/`lower`), threshold, segment, range_start, range_end, judge_at(a date or `end`), benchmark_id(nullable: null = none), conditions_hash, exposed_elsewhere(nullable), note, ts_created (6.4, #269) |
| `selection_events` | event_id PK, family_id, run_id, source(`to_strategy` / `grid` / `preregister`), from_cohort_sha256(nullable), from_count, chosen_candidate_key, segment, ts_created [#196] |
| `pins` | snapshot_id, holder_id, holder_kind(run/book), ts_created, ts_released |
| `books` | book_id PK, kind(paper|manual), ts_created, base_ccy, sleeves_json, definition_sha256 |

**A former column** (2026-11-27, #752): `runs.edition` left with the editions. An index an earlier engine wrote with it still opens and its cells read as stored; the engine never writes it. An index from before families is rebuilt without it, its values kept in the `index.sqlite.before-families` copy (Migration to families, below).

`nexusbt runs --prune` deletes folders and changes only `status = pruned`. Attempt counts do not go down [R-34]. `nexusbt runs --prune-detail` deletes only a run's `detail/` folder (tier 3, 5.9.1) and sets `detail_pruned = 1` on its `runs` row; the rest of the folder, the seal, the status and the attempt counts stay as they are (decision 2026-11-04, #206).

**Migration to families** [#196]: an index without `families` is migrated once when it is first opened: one family is created with reason `linked at migration` and the project's `[research] family_id` (a new one if the toml has none; then every run's warnings carry the line "add `[research] family_id = "{id}"` to nexusbt.toml" until it is written, and the engine never edits the toml itself), and every existing study, run, exposure and preregistration gets that family. Nothing else changes: sealed reports keep their text, cohorts and `cohort_sha256`.

#### 5.9.6 Paper ledger [R-49, 11.8 M5]
- `runs/books/<name>/`: `book.json` (**the unchanging ledger definition**: strategy `graph_hash`, parameter hash, `SimConfig` hash, sleeves, base currency, initial capital, `anchor_decision_id`, engine version, `definition_sha256`), `events/events-<seq>.parquet` (append fragments), `checkpoint-<seq>/` (Arrow IPC of the 5.6.1 arrays + meta JSON), `HEAD` (the last checkpoint name and `state_version`, replaced atomically), `snapshots.parquet` (a record of the `snapshot_id` and semantic hash used at each decision and fill), `book.lock`. Pins of snapshots the ledger used are not released while the ledger exists (4.2).
- **Idempotency key** = sha256(book_id, decision_id, graph_hash, params_hash). `checkpoint_seq` is not put into the key, only kept as an attribute of the order-batch record (v2's key sent the orders of the same decision twice in live → fill → live). The ledger holds the set `decisions_emitted` (decision_id → order batch id). For a decision already emitted, `live` shows that batch again (including what has already filled) and does not make a new one (`IdempotencyNotice` E26) [11.8 M5].
- **Transaction** [INV-19]: `live` and `fill` (1) take an exclusive lock on `book.lock` (`fcntl.flock`; if already held, `BookConflictError` E35, no waiting), (2) read the `state_version` of `HEAD` and check that `book.json`'s `definition_sha256` matches the current strategy, parameters, `SimConfig` and engine version (if not, E35 "book definition changed; create a new book or `nexusbt book migrate`(v1.x)"), (3) write the new event fragment to a temporary file and fsync → rename → the new checkpoint folder the same way → replace `HEAD` atomically with `state_version + 1` (temporary → fsync → `rename` → folder fsync) → `status.json`, (4) release the lock. The commit point is the single `HEAD` replacement.
- **Recovery**: when a ledger is opened, event fragments and checkpoints newer than the checkpoint `HEAD` points to (leftovers of an interrupted transaction) are moved to `orphans/` with a warning. So dying at any commit boundary returns to one valid state (the previous or the next) (test P-BOOK: forced stop at every boundary). If the same order batch came from another ledger or another `state_version`, `fill` rejects it (E35).
- At every decision and fill, the `snapshot_id` and semantic hash used then are recorded in `snapshots.parquet` (the input of the 5.9.7 replay).
- `fill` proceeds only when the order's execution session is within the snapshot watermark (`complete_through_session`). Otherwise `DataAvailabilityError` (error E19, CLI exit code 3) [risk 25].
- `fill` calls `execution.step_session` (the same code as the backtest). A run stopped midway becomes the same ledger when restarted from the `HEAD` checkpoint [INV-10].
- **Manual instruction (override)**: `nx.override(book, id, kind)` records the ledger events `override_set`/`override_cleared` (inside a state_version transaction) and does not change the ledger definition (`definition_sha256`). `no_trade` = no new order intents for that instrument, `reduce_only` = no intents that increase size, `close` = only reducing orders at the next decision, as if the target were 0. The target table is unchanged, and the cut happens at the order-intent step (5.6.7) (`order_suppressed(override)`). `reconcile` uses the recorded instructions as replay input (Close / No trading / Reduce only of pysystemtrade `override.py`). Backtests do not have it. **Relation to pending orders**: when an instruction is set, that instrument's unfilled rebalance orders are cancelled to fit the instruction (`no_trade` → all `CANCELLED(override)`, `reduce_only` and `close` → only increasing orders cancelled); exit orders only reduce holdings, so they stay under every instruction.
- **Ledgers a person writes** `manual:<name>`: `live` emits only the order sheet and does not fill automatically. `nx.record_fills(book, csv)` (columns `event_kind, idempotency_key, order_ref, sheet_id, corrects, instrument_id, side, qty, price, fees, ts, note` — `event_kind ∈ {fill, reject, correct}` (a discretionary trade is `fill` + an empty `order_ref`), `order_ref` = the order sheet's `idempotency_key`, `sheet_id` = the sheet the row was executed from (rule 1 below; empty for a discretionary trade), `corrects` = the key of the row a `correct` row replaces (rule 2; empty otherwise), `idempotency_key` unique within the `manual:<book>:` namespace; a broker fill CSV is converted to this shape and loaded) appends the events `user_fill` (the actual fill of a recommended order; a partial fill is several rows), `user_reject` (the recommendation was not followed — the remaining quantity is cancelled), `user_correct` (a correction of the quantity, price or costs of an earlier fill row, a new key and `corrects`, append only; rule 2) and `user_trade` (a discretionary trade not in the recommendations) in one transaction (above), and runs only the account accounting (5.6.1, the same code). The same key with the same content is ignored (E26); the same key with different content is rejected (E35, "conflicting duplicate"). This corresponds to Portfolio123 Live Strategy's "rewrite with actual fill prices, reject a recommendation, discretionary mode" and backtrader's `add_order_history`.
- **Exceptions of manual daily operation** [decision 2026-11-03]: five rules, so that a paper or `manual:` book and the account a person trades cannot drift apart unnoticed. Rules 1 to 4 are here, rule 5 is 5.9.7 (5); their checks are P-LIVE (8.3) and the newest-data case (8.4).
  1. **A superseded order sheet.** Every sheet `live` emits is the book event `sheet_emitted` `{sheet_id, decision_id, idempotency_key, snapshot_id, semantic_hash, basis_version, supersedes, ts_emitted}`, and every line of the sheet CSV carries `sheet_id` = sha256(idempotency key, the snapshot's semantic hash, `basis_version`), where `basis_version` is the `state_version` of the last book event that changes the account or the instructions this decision's orders are computed from: a deposit or withdrawal, a manual instruction set or cleared, a discretionary trade, or a fill, rejection or correction recorded against another decision's sheet (0 when none). The decision's own `sheet_emitted` events, the rows recorded against its own sheets and events of other kinds (an acknowledgement, another decision's `sheet_emitted`) do not change it. A `live` for a decision already emitted computes `sheet_id` again: the same id shows that sheet again and records nothing (E26); a different id (a corrected snapshot arrived, or the book changed) emits a new sheet with `supersedes` = the old id, computed from the book's current state (fills already recorded against the old sheet stay and are not ordered again), and the old sheet's unfilled remainder becomes `CANCELLED(superseded)`. A sheet whose execution session `fill` has processed (paper books) is closed: `live` shows it (E26) and does not supersede it. `record_fills` refuses a CSV with a `fill` or `reject` row naming a superseded sheet, and records none of its rows (a `correct` row is checked through the row it corrects, so a fill made from an old sheet can still be corrected) (E35: "sheet {old} was superseded by {new} ({reason}); re-execute from {new}, or record these fills as discretionary rows with an empty order_ref"). `status.json` `current_sheets` gives the current `sheet_id` per open decision.
  2. **A fill correction** is a `correct` row with its own new `idempotency_key` and `corrects` = the key of the fill or correction row it replaces, never an edit. The replaced row stays in the book; its `superseded_by` (the key of the row whose `corrects` names it) is derived when the book is read, since event fragments are append only. The account accounting counts only the last row of each chain, so a corrected fill counts once. A row identical to one already in the book (same key, same content) is ignored (E26) before any other check, so loading the same CSV twice records nothing new; otherwise a `corrects` that names a key not in the book, or a row already replaced, is refused (E35). `reconcile` shows the corrections recorded each day as a count and a list (session, instrument, replaced key → new key, the old and new quantity, price and fees).
  3. **After `reconciled: NO`** (paper books; 5.9.7 (4)): `first_divergence` in `status.json` carries `id` = sha256(book, date, `instrument_id`, stage, the two values), so the same divergence found again has the same id. While `last_reconcile.result` is `"NO"` and no `divergence_acknowledged` event names that id, `live` stops before its pending fill and emits no sheet (E35: "book {b} has an unacknowledged divergence {id} ({date} {instrument_id} {stage}); read nexusbt reconcile, then nexusbt book acknowledge {b} {id} --note …"). `nx.acknowledge(book, divergence_id, note, by=None)` and `nexusbt book acknowledge` record the event `divergence_acknowledged` `{divergence_id, note, by, ts}` in one transaction (above): `note` must not be empty, `by` defaults to the OS login name, and an id that is not the current `first_divergence` is E35. The acknowledgement changes no holdings or orders: the next `live` proceeds from the book as it is. It becomes the replay start: a later `reconcile` replays from the book's checkpoint at the acknowledgement's `state_version` instead of from `anchor_decision_id` (5.9.7 (1)) and lists the acknowledged divergences above its result, so a divergence found after that point has a new id and blocks `live` again. There is no `--force`.
  4. **Newest data is a state, not a date.** Before it builds a sheet, `live` checks every dataset the strategy reads, as the 4.3 table gives them for its features, in each market of the decision: the dataset's processed-through session there must be on or after the decision session, in addition to the market watermark. That session is the coverage `by_market.<m>.processed_through_session` (TDS 4.3), except that a per-session dataset (a row for every session: `bars`, `calendar_sessions`, `tradability_daily`) takes the earlier of it and its `by_market.<m>.last`, since a session processed without its row is a missing row [decision 2026-11-12, #192]. Without the field, a per-session dataset takes its `last`, and any other dataset (an event or value dataset such as `corporate_actions`, `delistings` or a value table, whose last row is the last event, and the rest, such as `instruments`) takes the market's `complete_through_session` (TDS 3.8, 4.3; F-17 of 4.5) [decision 2026-11-04, #192]. An event dataset processed through the decision session therefore blocks nothing on a day without an event. When any dataset is short, `live` emits no sheet, records no book event and fails with E19 (exit code 3), with one line for each dataset short of the decision session, "{dataset} for {market} ends at session {s}, before decision session {d} in snapshot {id}", each followed by one indented line for every thing the graph reads from that dataset (an item such as `nx.Item.roa`, an event kind such as `cash_dividend`), "  read by the strategy: {item}", so the person sees what the strategy waits for.
- **Deposits and withdrawals** [V31-19]: `nx.cash_flow(book, amount, ccy=, sleeve=)` increases (deposit) or decreases cash at the start of that session (E16 if a withdrawal exceeds cash). The bridge's `external_flows` row; returns are time-weighted (5.7). Deposits and withdrawals can be put only at the start of a session not yet processed (E16 for a session already processed), and the replay makes the same events again at the same boundary; the ledger's CAGR and MDD use the TWR index, and the growth of account value is `account_growth`. Periodic-contribution scenarios for backtests come later (12.1).
- **Order pre-checks**: after emitting the order sheet, `live` runs non-blocking checks and reports through the order sheet column `check`, `warnings` in `status.json`, and `OrderSanityWarning` (E36): (1) the decision price is outside ±`[live] max_price_jump` of the previous valid close, or the close change is outside `spike_sigma × σ_20` (t−20..t−1) (suspected data error; pysystemtrade `max_price_spike`), (2) one order's amount > sleeve value × largest target weight × `max_order_weight_mult` (the per-instrument maximum amount per order of the Nautilus risk engine), (3) long sell quantity > holding, cover quantity > short size, (4) there was an effective event between decisions, but the change in `decision_value` does not match the event factor, (5) the strategy universe has no traded-value threshold leaf, and recent decisions picked instruments in the bottom `liquidity_floor_pct` of ADV (Genport: "live linking is not available unless low-liquidity instruments are excluded"). When some orders are caught by the checks, the CLI exit code stays 0 and they are only marked (a person looks and enters them).
