- **Status file** [SC-14]: whenever `live`, `fill` or `reconcile` finishes, `runs/books/<name>/status.json` is written atomically (temporary → `rename`): `{"book", "state_version", "last_decision_id", "last_decision_ts", "last_fill_session": {<MARKET>: …}, "watermark": {<MARKET>: …}, "snapshot_id", "open_orders", "current_sheets": {<decision_id>: <sheet_id>}, "warnings", "last_reconcile": {"ts", "result": "yes"|"NO"|"never", "first_divergence": {"id", "date", "instrument_id", "stage", "values"}|null}, "retention": "managed"|"unmanaged", "ruined": {<MARKET>: date|null}, "ts_written"}`. Notifications and scheduled runs (launchd, cron) read this file and the CLI exit codes (0/1/2/3/4) from outside the engine. The engine does not build a scheduler [L5].

#### 5.9.7 Ledger reconciliation `reconcile` [SC-14, INV-10]
1. From the ledger's `anchor_decision_id` (after an acknowledgement, from the book's checkpoint at it; 5.9.6 rule 3) to the last processed session, it replays with the same strategy, parameters, sleeves, initial capital and `SimConfig` (the same code as `backtest`, not saved). **Default (`restate=False`, deterministic replay)**: at every decision and fill it uses the snapshot the ledger recorded (`snapshots.parquet`) as it is — E15 if the snapshot is missing. **`restate=True` (restatement)**: every step is recomputed with one `snapshot=` (default latest), and the report labels it "restatement against newer data" (differences may be due to data corrections).
2. Compared per decision: (a) the canonical target (the final rows of `TargetFrame`, weights exactly), (b) order intents (instrument, side, quantity), (c) fills (session, quantity, price, costs, in currency minor units), (d) per-session `nav_economic` (minor units).
3. If all are equal, `reconciled: yes`. Otherwise it shows the first differing (date, `instrument_id`, stage = targets/orders/fills/nav) and the two values, with exit code 4. Candidate reasons for the difference are shown too: (for a restatement) the snapshot the ledger used differs from the replay snapshot (data correction), a different engine version, ledger events edited by hand.
4. A paper ledger is the same state machine, so the difference must be 0 in a deterministic replay (P-RECON). `reconcile` of a `manual:` ledger produces "model targets vs actual holdings", "model fill prices vs actual fill prices (implementation shortfall bp)" and "the list of discretionary trades" (differences are normal, so exit code 4 is not used). When a broker ledger exists (11.4), the same report becomes TCA (fill price vs model fill price, implementation shortfall against `decision_price` and `arrival_price`). Broker paper accounts (Alpaca, KIS paper, etc.) are not a verification basis because of random partial fills, unreflected dividends and the like [blueprint 12.2, OQ-10].
5. **The order-day record** [decision 2026-11-03]: `nx.reconcile(book, day=D)` and `nexusbt reconcile --book BOOK --day D` print, for the decisions executed on session D, the record that measures the burden of a manual order day: the sheets (`sheet_id`, current or superseded), the number of orders by order type and side, the time from the first sheet's `ts_emitted` to the `ts` of the last fill recorded for D, the exceptions (superseded sheets, rejections, corrections, discretionary trades, orders flagged by E36) and the re-entries (correction rows, and the rows recorded against a sheet that superseded another). The M5 end condition (10.0) takes its representative order day from this record.

#### 5.9.8 Ecosystem adapters (`nx.compat.adapters`, optional pandas dependency) [SC-16]
- **Zero-copy export**: result table objects (`Run`'s `daily`, `trades`, `orders`, `targets`, `attribution`, the table form of its `metrics`, `primary` and `diagnostics` (6.3a, #216), `PipelineResult`, `FactorReport` tables, `Screen.table`) implement the Arrow PyCapsule interface (`__arrow_c_stream__`). So in `pl.from_arrow(run.trades)`, `pd.DataFrame.from_arrow(run.trades)` (pandas ≥ 3.0) and `duckdb.sql("select * from t")`, **Arrow standard types (integer, float, bool, utf8, date32, timestamp[us, UTC], dictionary-encoded) pass without copying**, and types the receiving side does not support are converted by that library's rules (for example the type conversion of Polars `from_arrow`) — zero copy is not promised unconditionally [confirmed by Sol]. The engine does not depend on those libraries. The pandas adapters below keep only ecosystem-specific shapes (pyfolio, alphalens). Users who need a thin layer that converts tables into other libraries' formats can use a tool such as Narwhals (not an engine dependency).
- `Run.returns(ccy: str | None = None) -> pd.Series`: combined daily net returns (default base currency), index = valuation date (`DatetimeIndex`, no tz, name `date`). The input shape of pyfolio and QuantStats.
- `Run.to_pyfolio() -> tuple[pd.Series, pd.DataFrame, pd.DataFrame]`: (returns, positions (instrument columns + `cash`, base-currency values), transactions (`amount`, `price`, `symbol` = `instrument_id`, index = fill instant)).
- `FactorReport.to_alphalens() -> pd.DataFrame`: the alphalens `factor_data` shape (MultiIndex `(date, asset)`, columns `factor`, `factor_quantile`, label column names of the `1D` form from `horizons`).
- The adapters only convert our outputs into those shapes. They do not depend on those packages (tests check only the shape). Without pandas, `CapabilityError` (E18, "install nexusbt[pandas]") instead of `ImportError`.

#### 5.9.9 Decision explanation (`nx.explain(strategy, …)`, `Run.why`) [SC-13]
For decision d (on a day not in the schedule, a preview decision for that date), the path of instrument i is produced as a table of steps:

| Step | Content |
|---|---|
| 1 Universe | listing existence, `exclude_microcap` (threshold and i's market cap), the `&`·`|` tree of the user universe Filter expanded with the true/false of each leaf Filter — "dropped here" where it first became false |
| 2 Score | the value of each node of the signal term tree (top to bottom; if missing, which input is NaN) |
| 3 Rank and selection | rank within the sleeve / number of candidates, whether selected, `per` group, tie handling |
| 4 Construction and risk | `pre_risk_weight`, cap (`capped`), risk overlay (`risk_capped`), final weight, whether it stayed because of the hold band |
| 5 Order intent | `decision_value`, price, `T_i`, holding `q_i`, `Δ_i` (needs the account state, so only in `Run.why` or a ledger; `nx.explain(strategy, …)` shows "needs account state") |
| 6 Execution | the order events of the execution session: fill (quantity, price, `fill_rule`, costs) or `blocked(reason)`, expiry, cancellation |

`nx.explain(strategy, …)` recomputes (steps 1~4, and the tradability judgement of the execution session if the snapshot has that session). `Screen.xray()` is the same table with steps 1~4 spread as vectors over all instruments of that decision (3.8; the holding column only with `book=`). `Run.why(id, date)` produces it from the run outputs (`targets`, `orders`, `account_events`, `coverage`, and `signal` with `save_signal=True`) without recomputation. Without `signal`, steps 1 and 2 show "re-run nx.explain(strategy, …) for filter detail".

---

## 6. Research hygiene and the list of teaching errors

### 6.1 Splitting into segments (no future lock) [9.0 Q26, R-57]
- Segments are set by the user in `nexusbt.toml [split] segments`. Default `train = [start, 2021-12-31]`, `validation = [2022-01-01, latest]`. Adding `test` takes one line (`test = ["2025-01-01", "latest"]`; then pull in the end of validation). Segments do not overlap.
- Run period = the chosen segment (`segment=`, default `default_segment`) ∩ `[start, end]`. `segment="all"` is the union of all segments.
- **Not blocked.** Any segment can be run right away. Instead, (1) each run records the segments it computed in `exposures`, (2) the report shows "segments seen train a · validation b · test c" counted over the **research family** (6.2), so renaming a file or starting a new study does not reset it, and (3) the first time in the family a segment other than the default segment is seen, the notice `SegmentNotice` (error E10) is raised (user decision 2026-11-04, #196). A real future lock (prospective lock) is not built [9.0 Q26, A-07].
- In each segment the backtest starts with new capital (it does not take over the previous segment's account). Labels are purged at the segment end (5.8). Validation methods that split again **inside** a segment (walk-forward, CPCV, CSCV) and their account rules are in 6.3a.

### 6.2 Study, candidates, attempt counts [R-34, R-35, Q19, 9.3 Q27]
- `study` name default = the entry file name (without the extension; for a notebook, the notebook file name; if unknown, `"interactive"`) [Q19]. For a name used for the first time a `study_id` (UUID4) is created, and from then on records are stored under that id. Renaming the file makes a new study (it can be continued with `study="old_name"`) [A-04]. A study is only the cohort grouping; what was tried and seen is kept per research family (next item).
- **Research family** (user decision 2026-11-04, #196): `[research] family_id` (3.11), created once per project by `nexusbt init`, is above every study. No file name, study name or experiment name sets it; an independent new family is made only by `nexusbt init --new-family REASON` (3.10), which records the old family as parent. Per family: exposures (6.1), candidates explored, selection events, preregistrations and looks. DSR's N stays the raw candidate count of the cohort (below); the family's counts are shown beside it as lineage, not added to N.
  - **Selection events** are what the engine sees: `FactorReport.to_strategy` (the factor cohort chosen from, its size, the chosen candidate), a `grid` choice of a validation run (6.3a), and a set of preregistrations (6.4). Choices made by hand elsewhere are not seen; the line says "recorded by the engine".
  - **Evaluation label** of a run on segment S other than the default segment (#196, #269): let P be the family's preregistrations for S made before S's first exposure in the family, n the run's **look number** (the exposures of S in the family up to and including this run, reruns excluded) and m = n − 1. A run **matches** a preregistration p in P when its graph hash and conditions hash (6.4) equal p's and its range starts at p's registered start and ends at p's registered end or later. If the run matches a p in P and every earlier exposure of S came from a run that matched a member of P:
    - with one preregistration and `exposed_elsewhere = False`: n = 1 with the range ending at p's registered end gives "independent final test (look 1)"; n = 1 with a later end gives "independence not established (range differs from the preregistration)"; n > 1, a run that extends the end date included, gives "repeated look on preregistered data (look n)";
    - with `exposed_elsewhere` `None` or `True`: "independence not established (exposure outside the engine not declared / declared)" with its look number;
    - with k > 1 preregistrations: "k predeclared candidates on the final data (look n)".
  - Otherwise the run is never "independent final test": a run whose graph is in P but that matches no p (its conditions hash or range differs) gives "exploratory on reused final data (seen m times before)" for m > 0 and "independence not established (conditions differ from the preregistration)" or "(range differs from the preregistration)" for m = 0; any other run gives "exploratory on reused final data (seen m times before)" for m > 0 and "independence not established (no preregistration before first exposure)" for m = 0. A run on the default segment is "research data". The label describes the data's history; it does not block the run, and no statistic restores an untouched status.
  - **Looks** (#269): an exact rerun (`run_kind = rerun`, 5.9.4) is excluded from the look count, as from m before; it carries the look number and label of the run it reproduces. The snapshot is not part of the match: a run on a corrected snapshot of the registered range is the next look of the same claim (it still matches p, and the count goes on; it is not reset), and each look's snapshot is recorded (`runs.snapshot_id`). A preregistration made after S's first exposure is a new claim (a new row in `preregistrations`; the earlier claim stays as it was): it is not in P and does not reset the look count of the data. `runs.preregistration_id` holds the matched p (null when none matched) and `runs.look_number` holds n (5.9.5); the label of 3.9 is computed from these. On every run with n > 1, whatever its label, the PSR and DSR line of 3.9 and any confidence interval shown carry "fixed-sample statistic; n looks": they are computed as for one fixed sample and are not valid under repeated looks (sequential procedures are RL-02, v1.x).
  - **Run kinds** (`runs.run_kind`): `research` (default), `debug` and `seed_comparison` (the `purpose=` keyword, 3.8), `rerun` (`nexusbt rerun`), `preview` (6.2 below), `failed` (stopped by an error before results) and `pruned` (stopped on purpose before results). They are counted separately in the attempts line; a failed or pruned run is never given returns, and its candidate counts in the denominator of "with returns a/b" of the comparison-set line (3.9).
- `candidate_key = sha256(kind, study_id, graph_hash, canonical params, settings_hash, segment)`. `settings_hash` holds the universe, costs, execution, exits, sleeves, clock and label names, and leaves out the snapshot id and fine adjustments of `start`/`end` (a rerun on new data is not a new candidate) [C10].
- **Candidate / observation / cohort**: a candidate is identity only (`candidates`). One run adds one observation (`observations`: snapshot, period, statistics). Running again with another snapshot or period does not overwrite the earlier observation. When statistics are computed, a **cohort** is fixed: among the observations with the same study, kind, segment, target, horizon and freq, the one most recent observation per candidate. If observations with different periods (`start`/`end`) are mixed, the report warns "mixed periods". The report freezes the cohort at that moment, stores it in `cohorts` and records `cohort_sha256` (the DSR and BHY of a sealed report reproduce unchanged even when more candidates are added later).
- Four counts are recorded and shown separately: the number of runs (`runs`), the number of distinct candidates (`candidates`), the number of observations (`observations`), and the assumed number of independent trials (= the number of candidates in the cohort, stating that it was assumed; N is the recorded candidate count as the user decided, N_eff is a v1.x auxiliary) [11.4.5].
- `evaluate` counts as a factor candidate (`kind = factor`) and does not go into the strategy Sharpe attempts [11.4.5].
- The k series of `backtest(offsets=k)` are a **diagnostic**: not new candidates or observations, not part of the cohort (DSR, BHY), and kept only in `offsets.parquet`.
- **Counting validation methods**: one `backtest(method=…)` = one observation of one strategy candidate (the statistics of the linear run). Each point of a `grid` = a candidate (the same count as `sweep` today). Folds and paths are **diagnostics** (not observations — otherwise N inflates by the number of paths and DSR becomes useless). The model settings of an ML factor (class, hyperparameters) go into `graph_hash`, so each change counts as a new candidate automatically. **A `preview` run is an attempt too**: it computes and shows performance, so it leaves an observation (`fidelity = "preview"`), and if the strategy (`candidate_key`) is seen for the first time, candidates +1 — scanning 100 distinct strategies with preview and running only one in full still makes N 101. Preview observations have short periods, so they are not used as performance inputs of the cohort statistics (DSR, BHY) (they fall under the "mixed periods" warning), but they count in the candidate count N. Repeated previews of the same strategy only add observations. (Follows Sol S04 — Opus and Fable supported "exposure only", but undercounting attempts is the risky side, so the safer rule was chosen.)

### 6.3 When PSR, DSR and PBO are computed [R-38, R-39, 9.3 Q27]
- PSR(0) and DSR: every `backtest` report (the 5.7 formulas). The variance of candidate Sharpes comes from that run's **cohort** (6.2, `kind = strategy`). Cases that cannot be computed give `StatisticUnavailable` (error E21, notice) with the reason: "no return series", "T < 30", "zero variance", "PSR denominator ≤ 0", "N = 1 (PSR(0) shown)", "V[SR] needs ≥ 2 candidates".
- PBO (CSCV): in a run with a `grid` (`sweep` = `backtest(method="cscv", grid=…)`, or the `grid` of another `method`), with that run's candidates only (the declared set of comparable candidates) [11.4.5]:
  1. Matrix `M (T × N)`: each candidate's net daily returns on the same evaluation dates. Only the common dates from **after the first non-cash decision of every candidate** are used (window lengths differ by candidate, so that cash returns of the early warm-up period do not mix into IS/OOS; cash returns after that stay in the sample as they are) [V31-38 minimum]. N ≥ 2, block count S even and ≥ 4, T ≥ 2S. Otherwise n/a.
  2. Drop the first `T mod S` rows and split into S contiguous blocks.
  3. For each combination c of S/2 blocks (12,870 when S=16): IS = the rows of c, OOS = the rest. Per candidate, `SR_IS` and `SR_OOS` (mean/std ddof=1).
  4. `n* = argmax SR_IS` (ties: the first in ascending candidate_key). `ω_c = rank_avg(SR_OOS[n*]) / (N + 1)` (ascending average rank; OOS ties are settled by the average rank), `λ_c = ln(ω_c/(1 − ω_c))`. If a candidate's IS or OOS standard deviation is 0, that candidate's SR in that combination is set to −∞ (IS) or missing (last in the OOS ranking), and the count is reported.
  5. `PBO = #{c : λ_c ≤ 0} / #c`.
  6. The matrix and each candidate's skewness and kurtosis are stored in `candidates.parquet` [R-39].
- Gates (`[gates] ic_t`, `dsr`) are displayed only. They do not block the run [Q7].
- The statistics functions (`research.stats`) are written anew with reference to the DSR, PBO and CSCV of ml4t-diagnostic (MIT) and matched to independent test values (8.3). They do not depend on that package (a ported file carries a source header, 2.1) [Q22].
- Additional statistics (5.7, 5.8): MinTRL and Lo-corrected Sharpe (every `backtest`), transfer coefficient (every `backtest`), breadth, expected IR and BHY (every `evaluate`). N_eff and DSR(N_eff) are v1.x (T7.2): an auxiliary line beside N, and the default basis of judgement is N (user decisions Q19, Q27, kept by #197) [SC-09, SC-10]. The DSR line names the comparison set N counts (3.9). With dependence visible (5.7 autocorrelation row) the PSR, DSR and MinTRL line carries that row's "IID-based" label; with N > 1 MinTRL also gives the selection-adjusted reference against SR*_N (user decision 2026-11-04, #197).
- Splitters: 6.3a. Purging uses the same `max(ts_label_end, ts_label_available)` as 5.8 [SC-21].
- **Diagnostics are a separate axis from `method`** (design review 2026-11-04): the statistics below attach to a linear run as well, and attaching one does not make that run an out-of-sample evaluation. Each line names the series it was computed on (the linear run, the stitched walk-forward OOS series, or one CPCV path). None of them is the probability that a strategy is real or a promise of profit, and none has a pass threshold (gates are display only). A diagnostic whose input is missing or too small returns E21 with its reason and the run goes on; a method the user asked for is never replaced by another. `Run.diagnostics` holds them, one row per statistic, apart from the primary and the reference result (6.3a, #216).

| Diagnostic | Question it answers (null) | Input, unit | Comparison set | Conditions it relies on | Fixed outputs or rerun | E21 reasons |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PSR(0) | is this series' true Sharpe above 0? (SR ≤ 0) | daily net excess returns `r_t − rf_t` (5.7; #313, decision 2026-11-10) of one series | none | independent returns with finite moments (5.7) | fixed returns | no return series, T < 30, zero variance, denominator ≤ 0 |
| DSR | is this Sharpe above the expected best of N null candidates? | daily net excess returns (#313, decision 2026-11-10); N and V[SR] of the cohort | the cohort's N strategy candidates (6.2) | PSR's; V[SR] over representative observations that may have different periods ("mixed periods") | fixed returns and cohort (`cohort_sha256`) | PSR's; N = 1 (PSR(0) shown); V[SR] needs ≥ 2 candidates |
| MinTRL | how many observations does this Sharpe need for PSR(SR*) ≥ 1 − α? | daily net excess returns (#313, decision 2026-11-10) | none (SR* = 0); with N > 1 also the cohort's SR*_N, the selection-adjusted reference | PSR's | fixed returns | SR ≤ 0; SR ≤ SR*_N for the reference |
| Lo Sharpe | annualised Sharpe with autocorrelation up to lag 10 (an estimate, not a test) | daily net returns | none | shown only when the absolute value of ρ1 is above 0.1 | fixed returns | – (not shown) |
| PBO (CSCV) | how often is the IS-best candidate at or below the OOS median? (a property of choosing on this matrix) | T × N daily net returns on common dates | the run's `grid` candidates | N ≥ 2, S even ≥ 4, T ≥ 2S; the combinations overlap and are not independent | fixed matrix; the choice is redone per combination, trading is not | N < 2, T < 2S |
| CPCV path distribution | how much does the result vary across synthetic OOS paths? | daily net returns per path | the paths of one run | paths share data and fits: quantiles are not a confidence interval and the path count is not a sample size | rerun per path (6.3a) | identical paths → 1 path (E40) |
| IC Newey–West t | is this factor's mean IC different from 0? | IC per decision | none | NW with L lags, wider for overlapping labels (5.8) | fixed labels and values | – |
| BHY p | is this IC's p small after adjusting for the cohort's m factor candidates? (false discovery rate, not familywise error) | NW t of each candidate | the cohort's m factor candidates (6.2) | the BY adjustment holds under any dependence | fixed cohort | – |
| Cost sensitivity, scenarios, capacity | how do the same targets fare under other costs or capital? | the run's canonical targets | none | canonical targets are fixed and candidate choice is not redone; cost and delisting scenarios run the account rules and execution again under their own costs, capacity runs execution only (5.7) | replay of fixed canonical targets (5.7) | – |
| Offsets | how much does the result depend on the rebalance date? | k offset series | not candidates (6.2) | – | rerun per offset | – |
| Log-loss, Brier | how good are the class probabilities of this classification factor out of sample? (an estimate, not a test) | predictions' `probs` and the realised classes; Brier on the chosen `score=` (5.7) | none | scored only where the decision is not among its fit's training samples | fixed predictions | regression label; no scored prediction |
| SPA / White reality check | does any candidate beat a fixed benchmark in expected loss? With `loss = −net_return` this compares expected net return, never Sharpe superiority | benchmark losses and a T × K loss matrix | the declared candidates and benchmark | a dependent bootstrap with a stated block length and studentisation | fixed losses | "not supported in v1" (v1.x, 12.1 RS-03) |

### 6.3a Validation methods `backtest(method=…)`: splitters, purging, accounts, statistics [user decision 2026-10-28]

There is one entry point: the familiar `nx.backtest(strategy, start=, end=, method=…)` (CLI `nexusbt backtest FILE --method cpcv`). `method="linear"` (default) is today's single linear run as it is. With another value, the engine runs trading (real fills and accounting) for each fold and path and combines them into the same `Run` and the same report. There is no separate `nx.validate` — two functions that do the same job lengthen the learning path and split the attempt count in two (user decision 2026-10-28). `nx.sweep(strategy, grid=)` remains as a shorthand for `backtest(strategy, method="cscv", grid=)` (the same computation; `SweepResult` is a view of `Run.validation`).

**Principles**: (1) **Time** (INV-01): splitters cut by decision time and label time, not by date; the condition on a sample used in training is the single 5.8 purging formula. (2) **Determinism** (INV-03): the split list is a pure function of (the decision grid inside the segment, the splitter parameters) only, with no randomness (`split_hash`). (3) **screen = backtest**: folds and paths are not a new execution path — they reuse the same Pipeline values (L1) and rerun only the targets and the state machine (the same way as `offsets` and `scenarios`). (4) **Not blocking** (R-57): any method runs, and every fold, path and candidate is recorded and shown on the first screen.

**Scope**: splitters cut only the decision grid D inside the chosen segment (`segment=`, default `train`) ∩ `[start, end]`. So the `test` segment remains a holdout.

| `method` | Definition | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `"linear"` | no split = the single linear run of v3.2 | default value |
| `splits.holdout()` | the toml segments as they are (new capital per segment, 6.1). A value object with the same meaning as `linear` | – |
| `"walk_forward"` / `splits.walk_forward(train, test, step, anchored=False, embargo=0)` | start of the first test window `t_1` = the first decision of D + `train`; test window k = `[t_k, t_k + test)`, `t_{k+1} = t_k + step`; training window = `[t_k − train, t_k)` (`[first of D, t_k)` with `anchored=True`). With the carried account (default), only `step == test` (neither an overlap nor a gap can carry an account; otherwise E16); with `account="per_fold"`, `step ≥ test`. Lengths are a decision count (int) or `"5y"`·`"6m"` (converted to sessions of the reference clock and recorded). `embargo` = the market sessions additionally left empty between the end of training and the start of the test | `train="5y", test="1y", step="1y"` |
| `"cpcv"` / `splits.cpcv(n_groups, k_test, embargo, is_scores=None)` | splits D into `n_groups` contiguous groups (equal decision counts; the remainder goes one each to the groups from the front). For each of the C(`n_groups`, k) splits, test = the chosen k groups, training = the samples of the rest minus those removed by purging and embargo. Path count `φ = k·C(n_groups,k)/n_groups`; path p = for each group g, the p-th of "the splits that tested g" (split number = lexicographic order of the combination), joined together. `is_scores` names how the IS scores of a `grid` are computed (below, #215) | `n_groups=6, k_test=2, embargo=5` (15 splits, 5 paths); `is_scores=None` = `strict` with a learned node and a `grid` |
| `"cscv"` / `splits.cscv(blocks)` | does not rerun trading per split: the same-date net daily return matrix of the `grid` candidates is split into blocks for PBO (the 6.3 procedure). Without `grid`, E16 | `blocks=16` |

**Purging and embargo** (common to all splitters): a training sample (d, i) is removed (1) if its label interval `[ts_label_start, max(ts_label_end, ts_label_available)]` overlaps the interval of any test block `[first decision time, the latest max(ts_label_end, ts_label_available) over every sample (d, i) of the block]`, both ends included (**purging**, by label publication time — a delisting label whose recovery was published late overlaps that much longer; the research conclusion is that no engine purges by publication time). The block ends at its latest label, not at its last decision's label (user decision 2026-11-04, #149): labels end and become available each at their own time (5.8; triple-barrier stops differ per sample), so an earlier test decision's label can end or be published after the last decision's, and a training sample that overlaps only that label is purged too. A training sample is also removed (2) if its decision lies within `embargo` market sessions after the last test decision's `decision_ts` of a test block — not after the block's end in (1), so a late-published label widens the purge but does not move the embargo (**embargo**, AFML 7.4; it has meaning only for the grid IS performance of CPCV, where there are training decisions after a test block, and, in ML training, after a test block before τ — ML training is always forward-only, 5.3.11). This `embargo` has a different meaning from `[split] embargo_sessions` (drops decisions **before** a segment end, 5.8) and is named differently. Each fold records `n_purged` and `n_embargoed`; a sample caught by both is counted once, in `n_purged`, so their sum is the number of samples removed. "Training samples" are used in two places: ML factor training (5.3.11; forward-only) and the IS performance of `grid` selection. **The return series of IS performance: the slice** (walk-forward always; CPCV in the modes `approximate` and `slice` below): for each grid point, **one linear run over all of D** (= that candidate's observation, the same run as 6.2; the same Pipeline values) is run, and a fold's IS performance = its daily net returns cut to the holding periods of the training decisions (decision → fill of the next decision), from which the purging and embargo above (the same function) remove the decision intervals that overlap a test block or fall within `embargo` after it. In the slice the state machine is not run separately per split × grid. Assumption sentence: "IS returns are sliced from a full-period run; account state entering a training group was shaped by trading in earlier (possibly test) groups". `selection.parquet` holds, per fold and grid point, the IS sample decision count, the purged count and the embargoed count, and (#215) `is_scores` and `n_blocks` (the number of contiguous training blocks replayed; null for a slice).

**Strict and approximate IS scores in CPCV** (decision 2026-11-04, #215; design review D04, the leak found in #149). The slice reads, for every split, one linear run whose learned nodes were trained by 5.3.11 (a)~(c) without (d). A model fitted after a test group has then read that group's labels, its predictions reach the IS returns of a later training group, and the parameter chosen for the test group can change when only the test group's labels change (8.2 G-116). The account that enters a training group was also shaped by trading in an earlier test group. A **learned node** here is an `nx.ml.factor` with a trained model; a pretrained model (5.3.11) is never fitted and reads no label, so it is not one. A strategy has a learned node when the graph of any of its `grid` points has one. `splits.cpcv(..., is_scores=)` (CLI `--method-opt is_scores=approximate`) names how the IS scores of a `grid` are computed, and the mode used is recorded:

| Mode | When | IS returns of a split and grid point | `IS scores` line (3.9) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `strict` | the default (`is_scores=None`) when the strategy has a learned node and a `grid`; also when asked with `is_scores="strict"` for a `grid` with no learned node | from that split's own fits and a replay of each contiguous training block as its own account (rules 1 to 5 below) | `strict (per split; {f} fits · {p} passes)` |
| `approximate` | only when asked: `is_scores="approximate"` with a learned node and a `grid` | the slice | `approximate — ` and the assumption sentence |
| `slice` | the default when there is a `grid` and no learned node (no learning reads a test label there); `is_scores="approximate"` with no learned node is recorded as this too; not a value the user passes | the slice | `slice, no learned node — ` and the assumption sentence |

Where no IS score is computed per fold — no `grid`, `linear`, `splits.holdout()`, and `cscv`, which reruns nothing per split — `ValidationResult.is_scores` is null and no `IS scores` line is printed; an `is_scores` argument to `cpcv` with no `grid` is E16 ("is_scores needs a grid"). `is_scores` is a parameter of `cpcv` only. Walk-forward keeps the slice and records `slice`, with the line `slice, training precedes the test window — ` and the assumption sentence: a fold's training window ends before its test window starts, so neither that window's labels nor the trading in it reach the fold's IS scores, and the account that enters the training window is still the linear run's. So `slice` always means "sliced from the linear run", and the line says why no test label reaches it. The IS metrics of a fold and `degradation` (below) read the fold's IS series of the recorded mode; in `strict` mode that series carries the entry cost of each block (rule 4). A run whose mode is `approximate` or `slice` is never called strict out-of-sample: the line above, `ValidationResult.is_scores`, `evaluation_context.is_scores` in `metrics.json` and the approximations of `Run.provenance` carry the mode and the assumption sentence, and for `approximate` the provenance level of the fits is capped (5.3.11). In `strict` mode the assumption sentence is not printed, because the state at every block start is stated.

Strict mode, for a split s:

1. **Predictions.** Every fit of s's learned nodes follows 5.3.11 (a)~(d) with s's exclusion mask (the decisions of s's test groups, the purged and the embargoed decisions), whichever decision the fit serves. They are the same fits, by `fit_id`, that serve s's test range. The prediction a training decision of s is scored on comes from the fit of s that serves it (`τ_k ≤ decision_ts < τ_{k+1}`), never from a fit of the linear run. A held-out label of s stays out of s's fits and IS scores by this mask even after the moment it would be usable for training (the moment 8.5 scenario 4 names, #271); under walk-forward a label past that moment may feed the declared refits.
2. **Blocks and accounts** (reset at each block; no replay from a warm-up period). The IS decisions of s are the training decisions whose intervals the slice would keep: the decisions of D that are not in a test group of s and whose decision interval the purging and embargo above do not remove (the same function, acting on a decision as a whole, as in the slice). A **contiguous training block** is a maximal run of consecutive decisions of D that are all IS decisions of s. For each grid point, each block is replayed as its own account: an empty book and capital K at the block's first decision, with the target-state recursion and the account rules anchored there: it starts as a `per_fold` fold starts (below). The replay runs through the session in which the decision after the block's last would fill, or to the run's `end` when the block holds the last decision of D; that next decision is not made in this account, so no order is placed and the last holdings are marked, not sold (unlike the boundary of a `per_fold` fold, nothing is traded at a block's end). A block's IS returns are those of `backtest(start=the block's first decision, method="linear")` cut at that session, run with the grid point and with s's predictions (8.3 P-IS-STRICT). No trading state reaches a block from a test group, from another block, from another split or from the linear run.
3. **Warm-up.** Computed values read the data before the block start that their windows need, the prices of an earlier test group included: a feature at a training decision that is built from test-period prices already public at that decision is legitimate (INV-01, INV-14), and strict mode does not remove it. Learned state: the fit that serves the block's first decision may have its τ before the block, and it trains on what 5.3.11 (a)~(d) allow before τ; a decision of the block before s's first fit has a NaN prediction (`ml_no_model`), as in any run. Trading state has no warm-up: holdings, pending and exit orders, the stop basis, `time_stop` counts, `target_state` (the hold band) and `risk_state` (the brake's peak) start at the block's first decision.
4. **Costs.** The costs of building the book from empty at each block's first fills are part of that block's IS returns, as they are what that account paid; nothing is charged at a block's end. A strict IS score therefore carries one entry cost per block where a slice carries none at a block start. IS scores are compared only between the grid points of one split in one mode, and a strict score is not compared with a slice's.
5. **The fold's IS series.** The blocks' daily net returns in date order, each block starting from K, are the fold's IS return series; `select` reads it as below (the CAGR's D spans the removed intervals).

What strict mode does and does not promise: changing only the labels of s's held-out decisions leaves s's training samples, fits (the same training sample keys and the same `pred_sha256`; and the same `fit_id`, since the fit's inputs are unchanged, 5.3.11), IS predictions, IS scores and chosen parameter unchanged (P-IS-STRICT). It does not promise that the OOS profit and loss of s's test range is unchanged, and the model inside a learned node is still not seen by the engine (5.3.11 "What a fit may learn").

Cost of strict mode, stated before the run: **fits**, at most splits × refit times × the number of distinct learned-node specifications among the grid points (1 when the `grid` changes no learned node and none of its inputs), since strict mode reads the fits of every refit time of each split, not only those that serve its test range; equal `fit_id`s are fitted once (5.3.11). **State-machine passes**, splits × grid points × the split's blocks, each over its block only (in sessions, about splits × grid points × (`n_groups` − k)/`n_groups` of D), against one pass over D per grid point for the slice. The fits fall under the `[validate] max_fits` cap like any other fit, and the cap compares the fit count only: above it, E40 before the run (not blocking), whose message gives both planned counts and the mode and whose `fix` line names `is_scores="approximate"` as the cheaper, labelled option. The passes alone never raise E40; the run's result records them as `replays` beside `fits` (chapter 7).

**What is selected**: (a) an ML factor inside the strategy — the 5.3.11 rule (forward-only; in CPCV the test blocks are excluded); (b) with a `grid`, the top parameter by IS performance over the fold's training range (`select`, default net Sharpe) (ties: ascending `candidate_key`) is chosen and applied to that fold's test range ("choose inside the window, apply outside the window"). `select` reads one number of the fold's IS return series (the slice above, as the TWR index `I` of 5.7): `"net_sharpe"` = the Sharpe row, `"cagr"` = the CAGR row with D = the calendar days from the slice's first to its last valuation date (removed intervals spanned, not summed), `"calmar"` = the Calmar row; an "n/a" value ranks below every finite one (#310). With neither, there is nothing to select: walk-forward's stitched OOS series and every table are bit-identical to **`backtest(start=t_1, method="linear")`** (a linear run started fresh with capital K at the first test decision; the INV-14 calendar anchor gives the same targets) (P-WF-STITCH; it is not identical to a cut of a linear run built up from the first decision of D, because entry costs, rounding remainders, the stop basis and the `time_stop` count differ), and CPCV, whose paths are then all identical, is reduced to 1 path after notice E40.

**Accounts** (user decision 2026-10-28):
- **walk-forward: one account carries across the test windows.** Capital K at the start of the first test window; at window boundaries, holdings, cash, pending orders, exit orders, `risk_state`, `target_state` and the `decide` block's state (5.5.3; decision 2026-11-04, #152; #725 Decisions 241) pass over unchanged, and only the next window's targets are built with the new parameters and the new model (no fake turnover or costs at the boundary; the only shape that can be reconciled with a paper ledger). Result = one stitched OOS series.
- **CPCV: new capital per path, carried within the path.** The target of path p = for each group, the `TargetFrame` of the split that tested that group, joined together; the state machine runs once per path (over all of D). At group boundaries only the model and parameters change, and holdings carry over. The path rows of the report always carry the interpretation sentence "paths carry holdings across group boundaries; the model/params that created them change at each boundary — a synthetic OOS path, not one live strategy". A path's target-state recursion (5.5.3), its `risk_state` and its `decide` block's state are anchored at the path's first decision and carried within the path; a path never reads the state of another path or of the linear run, so the hold band starts fresh on each path, and the interpretation sentence adds "hold band and account rules start at the path's first decision" (decision 2026-11-04, #152).
- **Holdout segments: new capital per segment** (as in 6.1).
- Only the diagnostic option `method=splits.walk_forward(..., account="per_fold")` (new capital per fold) is provided; the default is the decision above. In a `per_fold` fold every trading-state item of the table below starts empty with capital K, and the anchor of the target-state recursion and of the account rules (5.5.3, 5.5.2a) is the fold's first decision (decision 2026-11-04, #152).
- Scenarios (`cost2x` and so on) and capacity run only on the linear run (to prevent a path count × scenario count explosion); paths get only net returns and `gross`.

**State per run, fold and path** (decision 2026-11-04, #152). Four kinds of state, each with one lifecycle:

| State | Created | Shared | Carried in order | Reset |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Computed values (term values, Pipeline outputs, L0 and L1 entries) | once per node structural hash, input keys and rows | read-only, across runs, candidates, folds, paths and scenarios | never: a value does not depend on what was computed before it (INV-14) | never; a cache entry is dropped, not reset |
| Learned state (scaler, selected features, model, covariance, chosen grid point) | per fit (`fit_id`), a new object every time (5.3.11) | only between uses with the same `fit_id` and seed (5.3.9) | from one refit to the next only through what the declared window reads; no model object passes to another fit | at every fit |
| Trading state (cash, holdings, cost basis, pending and exit orders, stop basis, `time_stop` counts, `risk_state`, `target_state`, the `decide` block's state [#725 Decisions 241]) | at each account start: the linear run, the first walk-forward test window, each CPCV path, each holdout segment, each `per_fold` fold, each contiguous training block of a strict IS replay (#215) | never between accounts | session by session within one account | at the next account start |
| Research state (study, candidates, attempts, cohort, segment exposure, selections, versions) | per study (6.2) | across all runs of the study | appended in run order | never inside a study |

Warm-up: computed values read the data before the start that their windows need (INV-14); learned state may train on samples before the start, within the 5.3.11 window; trading state has none — an account starts empty with capital K at its first decision (P-WF-STITCH). Where the engine cannot say which state was reset or rebuilt at a boundary (the IS returns sliced from the linear run above: the modes `approximate` and `slice`), the report does not call the result strict out-of-sample and keeps that interpretation sentence; in `strict` mode every training block starts from an empty account, so the state is stated (#215).

**Side by side with the linear result**: when `method ≠ "linear"`, the engine also runs a **linear run** with the same period, the same settings and the strategy's default parameters (`params=`) (the same Pipeline values; extra cost = one pass of the state machine). The headline metrics of `Run` (`Run.metrics`) and the top level of `metrics.json` are the linear run, labelled the **reference**, and the method's results are in `Run.validation` (`ValidationResult`) and the `validation/` folder, reached as the **primary** result through `Run.primary` (below). The first screen of the report shows the two on two labelled lines, the primary first, **with their periods** (linear = all of D, walk-forward OOS = `[t_1, end)`), in the form 3.9 prints: "primary    cpcv 5 paths {start} → {end}: Sharpe min/median/max · worst MDD" and "reference  linear {start} → {end} · params = default: the table below", the table being the linear run's CAGR, Sharpe and MDD; PBO p (k/n) is a diagnostic and stays in the hygiene box. If the default parameters are a point outside the grid, the reference line says so. With `method="linear"`, `Run.validation` is `None`.

**Primary, reference, diagnostics, provenance** (decision 2026-11-04, #216; design review D09). Every number of a result is of one of four kinds, and `Run`, `ValidationResult`, `metrics.json`, the first screen, `report.html` and a table written to CSV name the kind in the same words:

| Kind | What it is | Python | `metrics.json` |
|---|---|---|---|
| primary | the evaluation the user asked for with `method`, with its period | `Run.primary` | the top level for a run with no reference; otherwise the `primary` block |
| reference | the linear run with default parameters beside a `walk_forward`, `cpcv` or `cscv` evaluation; a `linear` or `splits.holdout()` run has none | `Run.metrics` | the top level, named by `evaluation_context.reference` |
| diagnostics | the statistics of 6.3, each with the series and the comparison set it was computed on | `Run.diagnostics` | `diagnostics` |
| provenance | what the data and the procedure were: data exposure, preregistration, approximations, assumptions | `Run.provenance` | `provenance` |

- **The primary result per method**: `linear` and `splits.holdout()`: the run's metrics (`Run.primary` is the same object as `Run.metrics`). `walk_forward`: the metrics of the stitched OOS series over `[t_1, end)`. `cpcv`: the path distribution (Sharpe minimum, 25, 50, 75 and maximum, the count of complete paths, the worst-path MDD); where one number has to stand for it (a CSV row, a one-line summary) it is the **median over the complete paths**, written "median of n paths", and with no complete path it is the marker `incomplete_path` with "complete paths 0/b". The best path is never the representative result and no single path is picked as the result; the path quantiles are a spread over paths that share data and fits and are never called a confidence interval (6.3). For a `cpcv` run the "CPCV path distribution" row of the 6.3 table is this primary result (`role = primary`) and is not repeated in `Run.diagnostics`; the statistics computed per path (`psr`, `dsr`, `min_trl`) stay diagnostics. That 6.2 calls folds and paths diagnostics is about counting — they are not observations — and does not change. `cscv`: no trading is rerun per split, so the primary result is the candidate table of `SweepResult` (the linear run of each `grid` point, 6.2), and PBO is a diagnostic of choosing among them.
- **Existing fields keep their meaning** (compatibility note): `Run.metrics`, the top level of `metrics.json` and the statistics of the run's observation (6.2) are the linear run as before, with no deprecation; `Run.validation`, every `ValidationResult` field below and the `validation/` files keep their content. Where each old value is now also read: `ValidationResult.oos`, `paths` and `distribution` → `Run.primary`; `psr`, `dsr`, `min_trl`, `pbo`, `degradation` and `selection_stability` → rows of `Run.diagnostics` (the same numbers, with their series and comparison set); `hygiene` and the manifest `assumptions` → `Run.provenance`. `Run.primary`, `Run.diagnostics` and `Run.provenance` are views of the stored values, not a second computation, and no existing name is given a new meaning.
- **`metrics.json`** gains four blocks beside its unchanged top level: `evaluation_context` = {`method`, `primary` (the block that holds the primary result: `"metrics"`, the top level, for a run with no reference; `"primary"` otherwise), `reference` (`"metrics"`, or null for a run with no reference), `primary_period` and `reference_period` (first and last date), `exposure` (the evaluation label of 3.9 with its look number, 6.2), `comparison_set` (the comparison-set line of 3.9), `is_scores` (`"strict"`, `"approximate"`, `"slice"` or null where no IS score is computed per fold; #215)}; `primary` (only when the run has a reference); `diagnostics` (one entry per statistic: `statistic`, `value`, `series`, `comparison_set`, `status`, `n_valid`, `n_total`); `provenance`. The top level keeps its shape and its values as they are written today; the markers and the `n_valid`/`n_total` pairs below are carried by the new blocks and by the table form.
- **`Run.diagnostics`**: one row per statistic of the 6.3 table that the run computed or could not compute, with `series` (`linear`, `walk_forward_oos`, `cpcv_path:{p}`, `grid_matrix`, `ic`), `comparison_set` (none, the cohort with its N, the `grid` candidates, the paths of the run) and `status` (a failure marker below, null when the value is valid).
- **`Run.provenance`**: data exposure (the evaluation label, the look number and the family's segments-seen counts, 6.2), preregistration (the matched claim and its pass or fail line, 6.4, or "none"), approximations (each sentence the result rests on: the IS-scores mode with the IS-slice sentence above when the mode is `approximate` or `slice` (#215), "daily-bar approximation", `label availability assumed at label end`), assumptions (the manifest `assumptions`) and, for a run with ML fits, the count of fits per provenance level (5.3.11).
- **Tables and CSV**: `metrics`, `primary` and `diagnostics` read as a table — and so written to CSV through any table library (5.9.8) — have one row per number with the columns `role` (`primary`, `reference`, `diagnostic`), `method`, `series`, `period_first`, `period_last`, `metric`, `value`, `status`, `n_valid`, `n_total`, `comparison_set`. The rows of `Run.metrics` have `role = primary` for a run with no reference and `reference` otherwise, so a CSV row says what it is without its file name, and every primary row carries the method and its period.

**Online refits in walk-forward** (#216): a fold whose test window holds a refit time τ of an `nx.ml.factor` (τ after the window's first decision `t_k`; walk-forward does not cut the training window to the fold, 5.3.11) has `refit = online` in the fold table, with `n_online_fits`, the fold row of the report says "online (refit on labels confirmed during the test window)", and the `primary` line adds `online {k}/{n} folds` (3.9). Such a fit is forward-only and is not a leak; the label says that the fold's OOS numbers are those of a model updated during the window, not of the model fixed at `t_k`. A fold with no refit inside its window is `fixed`. **What a changed label can reach** (decision 2026-11-04, #271): the label of a decision inside test window k is first usable for training at the `ts_close` of the session `embargo` market sessions after the one that holds its `max(ts_label_end, ts_label_available)` (5.3.11 (b)). Changing only such labels leaves unchanged every fit, IS score and chosen parameter made before the first of those moments; a fit or a selection made at or after it, under the declared refit policy, may differ, and never one made earlier. So the invariance is stated up to that moment, not for the whole window: an early label of window k may feed a refit later in window k, and a label whose usable moment is not inside the window — the label of the window's last decision, when it ends at the next decision — changes no fit inside the window. Strict CPCV differs after that moment: the held-out labels of a split stay out of that split's fits and IS scores by its exclusion mask even once they are usable (above, #215).

**Failure types, not one NaN** (#216): a value that could not be produced is never shown as a bare NaN and never left out without a word (6.6). Each case has its own marker: in the `status` column of the table that holds the value (null when the value is valid), in `metrics.json` (`value: null` with `status`), and printed in the place of the number on the first screen and in `report.html`.

| Marker | Case | Shown as | Record |
|---|---|---|---|
| `incomplete_path` | a CPCV path or the stitched walk-forward series has, in the test range of at least one fold, a decision that should be served by a fit that was not made (`fit_failed`), whatever the strategy then held and whether or not it has other factors; `n_valid` counts the sessions outside the holding periods of those decisions. Such a period is not `cash_no_signal`, which is cash for lack of a signal, not for lack of a model | "incomplete: evaluated {first} → {last}, {n_valid}/{n_total} sessions"; no CAGR, Sharpe or MDD over the dates that remained is shown as that path's result, the path is left out of the distribution, and the distribution says "complete paths a/b" | `paths.status`, `eval_first`, `eval_last`, `n_valid`, `n_total`; for the stitched walk-forward series the same five on `ValidationResult.oos` and in the `primary` block |
| `missing_prediction` | a decision × instrument has no prediction: no model yet, or the fit that should serve it was not made | "predictions {a}/{b}" on the ML line | the `skips` reasons `ml_no_model` and `ml_single_class` (5.3.11); the prediction stays NaN inside the factor (5.4.1) |
| `fit_failed` | a fit was not made: one class in the training labels (5.3.11) | "fits {a}/{b}" with the reason | `folds.status`, the E40 notice |
| `ruined` | a sleeve was ruined (5.6.7) | the `ruined` line with the sleeve and the date (3.9), and the mark in the path row | `paths.ruined`, the `sleeve_ruined` event |
| `unfilled` | an order was not filled, in whole or in part (expired, cancelled, blocked) | the `orders` and `target gap` lines (3.9), never a missing return | the `orders` events (canonical, 5.6.2) |
| `stat_unavailable` | the input of a statistic or of a metric is missing or too small (E21 for the statistics of 6.3; the "n/a" cases of 5.7 for a metric) | "{statistic} n/a ({reason})" | `status` with the reason, in `diagnostics` and in the table form of `metrics` and `primary` |
| `cash_no_signal` | the strategy held cash because no instrument had a signal | **not a failure**: the period stays in the sample as a cash return under the cash interest and cost rules (5.6.3, 5.6.8) and counts in `n_valid` | holdings and `coverage` as now |

Every primary, reference and diagnostic value has an evaluation period and the pair `n_valid`/`n_total` (the valid samples over the samples its period should hold), and both are shown whenever the two counts differ, so an incomplete path is never read as a complete OOS record.

**`ValidationResult`**: `primary`, `reference`, `diagnostics` and `provenance` (the same objects as `Run.primary`, `Run.metrics`, `Run.diagnostics` and `Run.provenance`; #216), then, with their content as before apart from the columns named above, `method`, `split` (the split table, `split_hash`), `folds` (folds: training and test periods, the chosen parameters, IS metrics, OOS metrics, `n_purged`, `n_embargoed`, fit count), `paths` (per-path CAGR, Sharpe, MDD, turnover, `ruined`), `distribution` (path Sharpe minimum, 25, 50, 75, maximum and the path count), `oos` (walk-forward's stitched series), `pbo` (with a `grid`: CSCV PBO; for CPCV also "splits where the IS winner was below the OOS median / splits"), `psr`·`dsr`·`min_trl` (walk-forward: the stitched OOS series; CPCV: computed per path, with the median and minimum; N = the cohort's candidate count (6.2), V[SR] from the cohort), `degradation` (the regression slope of IS Sharpe against OOS Sharpe and the point count), `selection_stability` (the value chosen per fold, number of changes / number of folds), `hygiene` (the four counts of 6.2, `cohort_sha256`), `is_scores` (`"strict"`, `"approximate"`, `"slice"` or `None` where no IS score is computed per fold: how the IS scores of the `grid` were computed, above; #215), `report()`.

**Report "hygiene" box** (first screen, shared by `backtest` and `sweep`; no pass badge, only numbers and thresholds, ratios with numerator/denominator): (1) the attempts line `runs 37 · candidates 12 · observations 19 · assumed independent trials N = 12 (recorded)` + "segments seen: train 30 · validation 2 · test 0"; (2) `DSR 0.71 (N=12) · PSR(0) 0.97 · MinTRL 6.1y vs track 8.0y`; (3) the path distribution band and `paths 5`, worst-path MDD; (4) `PBO 0.38 (4,856 / 12,870 combinations λ ≤ 0)` and the IS–OOS slope; (5) selection stability; (6) leakage ledger: purged/total, embargoed count, whether `label availability assumed at label end` was assumed, ML fit count and minimum sample count, `ml fits: forward-only`, and, for `cpcv` or `walk_forward` with a `grid`, the IS-scores mode in the words of the `IS scores` line of 3.9 (#215); (7) preregistration result; (8) `preview runs: n (k new candidates)`.

**Cost**: rerunning the state machine is cheap (Pipeline reuse). Most of the CPCV cost is ML training, C(`n_groups`, k) × the refit count (× the distinct learned-node specifications of a `grid`, and with strict IS scores the block replays, as counted above; #215); above `[validate] max_fits`, E40 before the run (not blocking). Training time is reported separately, outside the chapter 7 budget.

### 6.4 Preregistration [design 3.9]
`nx.preregister(strategy, metric=, threshold=, segment=, …, exposed_elsewhere=)` only records; it registers a **claim**, not only a graph (#269). Recorded fields (`preregistrations`, 5.9.5):

| Field | Required / default |
|---|---|
| graph hash, family, study, time | taken from the strategy and the project |
| primary metric `metric`, `threshold` | required |
| direction `direction` | `"higher"` (pass when metric ≥ threshold; `"lower"`: ≤) |
| evaluation range: `segment`, `start`, `end` | `segment = "validation"`; `start` and `end` default to the segment's start and end as `[split] segments` (6.1) gives them at registration time, and the resolved dates are recorded |
| judgement date `judge_at` | `"end"` (the claim is judged at the end of the range); a date fixes it earlier |
| benchmark `benchmark` | the run's default benchmark (R-59), recorded by id; null when the snapshot has none (shown "none") |
| conditions hash | computed, below |
| `exposed_elsewhere`, `note` | `None`, `""` |

The **conditions hash** is the sha256 of the canonical form of the run parameters that change the claim: `params=`, the resolved cost settings (`costs=` and `[costs]`), the scenario set (`scenarios=`), the refit or re-selection policy (`method`, a name or a split object, 6.3a, and `grid`) and the decision frequency (the strategy's schedule; for a factor, `evaluate`'s `freq`). An argument left out resolves as a run would resolve it at registration time (the toml and the defaults), and a run computes its own conditions hash the same way (`runs.conditions_hash`). The snapshot is not in it: a corrected snapshot keeps the claim (6.2). An **exact rerun** (`rerun`, 5.9.4) is the same claim and the same look, and adds no look. A run on the registered segment with a **changed condition** has another conditions hash: it is not that claim and is never labelled "independent final test" (6.2).

A report that ran that segment with that graph shows "preregistered {metric} {≥|≤} {threshold}: pass/fail · registered before first {segment} exposure in family: yes/no". `exposed_elsewhere` is the user's statement whether the segment's data was seen outside the engine (`None` = not stated). Several preregistrations for one segment before its first exposure form a predeclared set, recorded as one selection event; the evaluation label of 6.2 follows from them (#196). Registering again after the first exposure is a new claim (6.2) and does not make the segment untouched.

### 6.5 Future-information checks [R-15, R-40, R-51, R-54, C8]

**Structural guarantees** (inputs the engine manages; structure, not checks): the publication time rule (5.1.2), as-of adjustment (5.3.3), the value tables' date rule (5.1.4), label marking propagation. These are objects of proof and are confirmed by tests (INV-01, P-FUT).

**Static checks** (when the graph is built, before heavy computation, within 1 second [P8]):
1. `shift(k < 0)` → `LookAheadError` (E1).
2. Label marking propagation: if a node derived from a label (`labels.triple_barrier`, the label node of 5.3.11) reaches `signal`, `universe`, `mask`, `groupby` or a portfolio input, `LabelLeakError` (E2).
3. Removed 2026-11-24 (#752) with `from_parquet` and `known_on` (E3 retired).
4. A currency-unit cross-section with `across_markets` → `UnitError` (E5).
5. Column names → E7. Window function dry run: called once with a `(W, 3)` array of 1.0 to check the returned shape and dtype and `IndexError` (→ E8). Any other exception the function raises there is raised as it is (3.3, R-42).
6. User function purity and cache dependencies: following `co_names` and closures, helpers inside the project go into the source hash, and among the module globals read, **constant-like** ones (int, float, str, bool, None, and tuples and frozensets of them) **have their values put into the node hash** (`THRESHOLD = 10 → 20` changes the cache key). Reading any other global (arrays, DataFrames, dicts, lists, objects) or opening files (`open`, `np.load`, `pd.read_*`, `pl.read_*`) gives `ImpureFunctionWarning` (E24) and turns off the L1 cache of that node and the nodes below it. A helper wrapped by a decorator (`functools.lru_cache`, `numba.njit` and the like) is followed along the standard `__wrapped__` chain to the plain function it keeps, whose source, its decorators and their arguments included, goes into the hash; a callable of the project from which no plain function is reached gives E24, since its source cannot go into the hash (#572). A file is inside the project unless it is the standard library (a zipped one included: a `.zip` entry of `sys.path` under the interpreter's own folder), a package installed into the environment's library folders, or the engine's own packages (`nexusbt`, `nexusbt_tds`); a package installed in editable mode lies outside those folders, so it is inside and its source goes into the hash, and the same factor then has one graph hash where that package is editable and another where it is installed from a wheel (decision 2026-11-19, #577). Any other callable a user function reads is taken as pure only when it is found again by reference: the module outside the project that it names as its `__module__` holds that same object under its qualified name, its name or the name the function reads it by (`np.add`, `len`, `math.sqrt`, `np.random.rand`), or it is a method bound to an object found so (`np.add.reduce`); every other callable was built while the program ran (`np.vectorize(f)`, `operator.methodcaller("pipe", f)`, `operator.itemgetter(1)`, `functools.partial(f, 2)`) and holds what the hash cannot read, so it is E24 (decision 2026-11-19, #589). A user function under a library decorator that keeps it in `__wrapped__` (`functools.singledispatch`) is read as the project function it keeps, whether it is the term's own function or a helper: its source, the helpers it calls and the constants it reads go into the hash as they would without the decorator; the decorator's wrapper, when it is a Python function, is judged by the rule above without going back into the project function, and when it holds what the hash cannot read (`singledispatch`'s registry of other implementations), the term's function is E24, and the message names the function and the decorator, not a name inside the library (decisions 2026-11-20, #645, #676). A wrapper that is not a Python function is not walked: `functools.lru_cache`'s and `functools.cache`'s (`functools._lru_cache_wrapper`) and `numba.njit`'s dispatcher (a `numba.core.dispatcher.Dispatcher`) are followed along `__wrapped__` as above, as helpers; any other object that keeps a project function in `__wrapped__` (an instance of a library's class, such as `joblib.Memory.cache`'s) was built while the program ran and is E24, the message naming the function that reads it and the class (decision 2026-11-21, #695). A module or a class a user function reads is looked into only when it is found again by its name: `sys.modules` holds the module under its name (for a class, the module its `__module__` names); one loaded without that (`importlib.util.spec_from_file_location` without registering it, `runpy.run_path` under another name, `exec`) is E24, and the message names the class or module and says it cannot be found by its name (decision 2026-11-21, #690).
7. A fixed instrument-list universe → `SurvivorshipWarning` (E4).
8. ML layer: the training window rule of `nx.ml.factor` (5.3.11) and the fold cutting of validation methods (6.3a) are the actual checks. A setting under which test-block labels could enter training gives `FitLeakError` (E29) before the run. Other fitted state transforms (`fit_segment`) remain reserved [SC-19].
9. Data capability: if the data needed by a feature the graph uses (table 4.3) is not in the snapshot's `capabilities`, E18 (E30 if there is a fallback, 4.5).
10. Unmasked cross-section operations: if a cross-section operation on the Strategy signal path (`rank`, `zscore`, `demean`, `winsorize`, `quantiles`, `percentile_between`) has no `mask` and the Strategy `universe` is narrower than the default domain, notice E34.

**Truncation probe** (a diagnostic, not a proof) [C8]. What the probe can catch is only "a read that changes in the truncated view": a function that rolls values inside an already truncated window (`np.roll(close, -5)` inside the window) does not read the future and so is not a target (the expectation of v2 8.4 was mathematically impossible), and a real leak (reading an array over the snapshot's full period through a global, a closure or a file) is watched by the static check E24 and the probe together:
- When: if the graph has user functions and `[checks] auto_truncation_probe = true`, `evaluate`/`backtest` run it once per (graph_hash, snapshot semantic hash) with `probes` (default 4) dates. `nx.check(obj, probes=k)` at any time.
- Method: (1) Split the run's decision grid into k equal parts, and in each part pick one date d by this priority [SC-05]: (a) a decision whose selected set (the instrument set of the canonical target; for a factor, the top-quantile set) differs from the previous decision, (b) a decision at which a selected instrument of that part had a corporate-action effective session, (c) a decision at which an item value of a selected instrument changed (5.3.5); within the same priority by `seed`, and when none of the three exists, at random by `seed`. (2) Build a truncated view of the snapshot: in every table `ts_available <= decision_ts(d)`, for bars `session <= row_m(d)` (filtered reads without copying). (3) On the same computation path, compute only the grid {d}, with enough warm-up rows (the sum of the windows of the whole graph). (4) Compare with the full run's values at d, node by node: selections and targets exactly, numbers `|a − b| ≤ 1e-12·max(1, |a|)`. (5) If they differ, raise `LookAheadError` (E1) with the name of the lowest differing node, the first date and an example instrument. C (the cumulative factor) is based on the first session, so future events do not change past values, and the adjustment reference point causes no false differences.
- The report always states (i) nodes compared / nodes in the graph, (ii) the share of cells whose values were finite on the probe dates (numerator/denominator), (iii) why the dates were picked (a/b/c/random), and the limitation sentence: "probes compare k dates; they cannot detect reads of globals/files that do not change with truncation, or branches not taken on probed dates". The extra time is only reported, with no upper-bound promise [C8, P9 fix, SC-05].

**Start-date probe** (a diagnostic, the user version of INV-14) [SC-04]:
- When: `nx.check(obj, start_probe=True)`, or, with `[checks] start_probe = true`, `evaluate`/`backtest` once per (graph_hash, snapshot semantic hash).
- Method: between a run whose start date is moved `[checks] start_probe_shift` (default 21) sessions earlier and the original run, node values and canonical targets are compared on the overlapping decisions (registered-kernel nodes bit for bit, user-function nodes `|a − b| ≤ 1e-12·max(1, |a|)`, selections and targets exactly). If they differ, `StartDependenceWarning` (E32) with the lowest differing node: it means a user function uses state outside the window (global accumulation, recursion). For a strategy with target-state recursion (v1.x) turned on, the reference point differs, so the target comparison is left out and only term values are compared.

### 6.6 List of teaching errors [R-40, R-42, R-43]

The shape of every error and warning:

```
{ClassName} [{code}] in {user_file}:{line} {term_or_object}
  layer  {data | your code | config | engine bug} — {layer tail}
  what   {what}
  where  {where: instrument/date/column/file}
  why    {why it is a problem}
  fix    {how to fix it: code that can be pasted as is}
  more   {see more: nx.check(...) / nx.describe(...) / document section}
```

**Layer line**. The second line is always `layer`, and it lets the reader know from the sentence alone which layer caused the error (of the engines researched, only Freqtrade and ml4t do this partly, teardown G). Each layer has a fixed tail:
- `layer  data — snapshot {id} / {dataset} / column {c} / producer {p}`, with `tds check PATH --level full` in `more`.
- `layer  your code — {user_file}:{line} in {function}` (for a user block, the block name and slot: `in my_rule (risk slot)`).
- `layer  config — nexusbt.toml [{table}] {key} = {value}` or `argument {name}={value}`, with a suggestion of similar keys.
- `layer  engine bug — please report: nexusbt bugreport {run_id}` (builds a file bundling the reproduction command, the manifest). Invariant violations (INV-*), engine-internal asserts, unexpected exceptions raised in engine code (`EngineBugError`, E46, CLI exit code 2). Only this layer may use engine-internal names.
- When the cause spans two layers, it is written from outside in, like `layer  data → your code` (for example E39 — missing data produces unknowns in a user Filter).
- Per code (+ condition), the layer is a fixed table in `tests/errors/layers.toml` (the same as the "Layers" paragraph below). **Layer golden**: `tests/errors/cases/` triggers the error for each code and condition and checks that it has a `layer` line and a `fix` line and that the layer matches the table (`test_every_code_has_layer_case`).
- **No internal terms**: sentences of the data, your code and config layers (what/where/why/fix) contain no engine module or class names (names under `nexusbt.` starting with `_`, names outside the 3.1 public table, things like `Planner`, `kernel`, `_step`); `tests/errors/test_no_internal_terms.py` checks the sentences of every error case against the table of public names (this stops sentences like zipline's "Can't compute windowed expression…").
- **User function exceptions**: the original exception is raised as it is (class and traceback kept, R-42), and Python `add_note` appends one `layer  your code — …` line and one `fix` line (absorbing LEAN's exception interpreter): for a common exception in the interpretation table, that sentence; otherwise the general sentence `fix    check {function} at {user_file}:{line}; reproduce with nx.explain(strategy, '{id}', '{date}')` (every user exception has a fix line). Interpretation table `errors/hints.toml`: `KeyError` (missing id or column → `ctx.ids`, `nx.describe`), `IndexError` (outside the window → `window=`), `ValueError` shape mismatch (→ return shape `(n_alive,)`), `TypeError` `NoneType` (→ missing-value handling), `ZeroDivisionError`/`FloatingPointError` (→ `np.where`, `fillna`), NaN comparison warnings.
- **No silent failure**: every case where the engine processes less than planned — instruments dropped from selection or sizers for missing inputs, sizer fallbacks (E43), account rules not evaluated (E42), ML predictions without a model, reduced paths (E40), scenarios turned off, optional data not read — is recorded in `skips.parquet` with a reason code (fixed list `skips.toml`; initial v1 list: `missing_input`, `sizer_fallback` (E43), `erc_fallback:{a|b|c}`, `account_not_evaluated` (E42), `ml_no_model`, `paths_reduced` (E40), `scenario_off`, `optional_data_unread`, `rule_not_applicable`, `shadow_ruined`, `constraint_residual` (5.5.2b), `declaration_unchecked` (E45, 3.3 `adjustment`; #246)) and a count, and the report and the CLI first screen always show `skipped: {n} rows in {k} reasons (top: …)` (`skipped: none` when 0). Each reason has a fixed E code, and one without a fixed code is announced with E45. There is no skipping without a warning (bt's `False` without a reason, Qlib recording only and dropping progress). Order rejections and blocks remain, as now, with the `orders` events as the canonical record. Test P-SKIP.
- **What the user can do next** (design review D19, #219): a notice whose cause is a condition of the data or of a method rather than a mistake (E21, E40, E42, E43, E45) says in its `fix` line, in plain words, what the user can do next (a longer history, fewer blocks, the input that is missing, the setting that removes the condition) or that nothing needs doing (`fix    nothing to fix; PSR(0) is shown instead`), and its `more` line names the entry of the guide `docs/guide/choose-by-question.md` that fits (for example `more   docs/guide/choose-by-question.md#na`). The `fix` line never proposes another method in place of the one the user asked for (6.3a) and never proposes changing the data, period or metric until a number looks better.

Blocking principle [R-43 fix, A-01]: (a) future-information errors the user cannot pass by judgement (E1, E2, E29) are blocked (E3 retired 2026-11-24, #752). (b) code, data and config errors whose result is undefined (E5, E7, E8, E12~E20, E23, E31, E35, E41, E46, E48) fail (E47 retired 2026-11-24, #752). Error sentences are in English and do not mention a particular producer's internal tickets or paths (hints only from `producer.hints`). (c) The rest are warnings and notices, do not block, and are kept in the manifest `warnings` and the report. Segments (E10) are not blocked [9.0 Q26].

Layers (`layers.toml`): E1·E2·E4·E5·E7·E8·E17·E24·E29·E32·E34·E41·E49 = your code; E6·E12·E13·E14·E15·E19·E20·E21·E25·E27·E28·E30·E36·E43 = data; E33 = data → engine bug (engine bug if it still differs when run again on the same snapshot); E9·E10·E11·E16·E22·E23·E26·E31·E35·E37·E38·E40·E42·E44 = config; E48 = your code (the same leaking setting as E29); E18 = config (a feature outside v1) or data (data not in the snapshot); E39 = data → your code; E45 = the layer of that skip reason; E46 = engine bug.

| Code | Class | Kind | Condition | Message core (what / fix) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | `LookAheadError` | block | `shift(k<0)`; truncation probe difference | "The value on {d} changed when data after {d} was removed" / "use .shift(k) with k ≥ 1 or nx.ops.rolling(...) on a return or a ratio; for a price level, a built-in factor such as Returns(...) or @nx.factor(window=..., inputs=[Price.close]), which read the column on today's basis" + limitation paragraph |
| E2 | `LabelLeakError` | block | a label node reaches a decision input (outside the `evaluate(target=)` and `ml.factor(label=)` ports, 5.3.11) | "{label} is a forward-looking label" / "remove it from the signal; test it with nx.evaluate(f, target={label})" |
| E3 | `PointInTimeError` | block | retired 2026-11-24 (#752) with `from_parquet` and `known_on`; the number is not reused | — |
| E4 | `SurvivorshipWarning` | warning | a fixed list all currently listed, period > 3 years | "a fixed list of {n} securities is used from {start} to {end}; {k} delisted included" / "nx.universe('KR') & ... .top(n); keep with survivorship_ok='reason'" |
| E5 | `UnitError` | fail | comparing or ranking a currency-unit term across markets | "{col} is in local currency (KRW for KR, USD for US)" / "remove across_markets=True, .to('USD'), or use a ratio" |
| E6 | `CoverageWarning` | warning | valid/universe < 50% in 5% or more of decisions | "the screen keeps {a} of {b} names on {d} ({p}%)", NaN counts per cause column / "accept (coverage table recorded), or start=..." |
| E7 | `ColumnNameError` (subclass of `AttributeError`) | fail | missing column | "Price has no column '{x}'. Did you mean: {a}, {b}?" / "nx.describe(Price)" |
| E8 | `WindowError` | fail | indexing outside the window, wrong return shape or dtype | "{f} reads close[-{k}] but window={W}" / "@nx.factor(window={k}, ...)" |
| E9 | `SessionNotice` | notice | start or end is not a session | "start {x} is not a {m} session; using {y}" / "start='{y}'" |
| E10 | `SegmentNotice` | notice | the first computation in a research family of a segment other than the default segment (6.1) | "this run computes performance on segment '{s}' ({start} → {end}); exposures in family: {n}" / "nx.preregister(...) before looking, if you want a recorded criterion" |
| E11 | `FillWarning` | warning | limit and stop orders judged on daily bars | "daily-bar fills for limit/stop orders are approximations" / "see [execution] fill_model note; use at='open' market orders to avoid" |
| E12 | `UnsupportedCorporateActionError` | fail | an unsupported event on a held instrument (policy fail), an event whose quantity effect cannot be determined (5.3.3a) | "{instrument_id} has a {kind} on {date} while held ({q} shares)" / "[corporate_actions] unsupported = 'cash_at_adjustment' (recorded), or exclude the security from the universe before {date}" |
| E13 | `AccountingDataError` | fail | required accounting data missing (policy value null, no merger target), exact ratio missing, disagreeing with its f64 column (#188) or wrong (4.4-4; fix "ask the producer for ratio_num/ratio_den, or use the default [corporate_actions] decimal_ratio = 'exclude_instrument' (5.6.1 "events without an exact ratio"), or 'literal' (recorded)"; which of these causes stop: 5.6.1 [#446]), whether a delisting distribution is included unknown + `[delisting] unknown_inclusion = "error"` (5.6.13), a short net dividend whose pre-tax amount cannot be determined (5.6.11) | table, key, column / "the snapshot lacks {column} for {key}; choose a policy ({fix}) or use a snapshot that provides it" (+ the `producer.hints` sentence if there is one) |
| E14 | `SnapshotError` | fail | 4.4 check failure (TDS error code), path safety violation, an unsupported required interpretation rule or closed enumeration value (M014), no snapshot, a required profile lacking + `fallbacks = "fail"` | the first TDS code, dataset, file, key and hint, counts per code, the `producer.hints` sentence for that code (if any) / "tds check PATH --level full; nexusbt doctor --full" |
| E15 | `ReplayError` | fail | a rerun dependency (snapshot, source, lock) is missing | "snapshot {id} is not available" / "nx.rerun(id, new_snapshot=True) creates a new run" |
| E16 | `ConfigError` | fail | toml or argument validation failure; a `decide` block's short intent or negative weight while shorting is off (3.7) [#725 Decisions 239] | key, value, allowed values / similar keys |
