| E17 | `TermDomainError` | fail | an `across_markets` term as the input of a window or shift; a term whose degree (3.4) is known and is not 0 as the input of `shift(k ≥ 1)` or of a window of two rows or more; a user function whose `adjustment` declaration does not hold under the scaling check (3.3); a cross-market term reading a value table (5.1.2) [#752] | "{term} is a cross-market term and cannot feed a window"; "{term} reads value table {item} of market {m} across markets"; "Price.close.latest is a split-adjusted price level and cannot feed shift(1)"; "{f} is declared adjustment='degree 0' but its result for {id} went from {a} to {b} when {id}'s prices were scaled by 2" / "remove adjustment=..., or declare 'degree 1' if that is what {f} is"; "Returns(2, inputs=[Price.close]) for a return; @nx.factor(window=2, inputs=[Price.close]) to read the earlier price on today's basis; Price.close_unadjusted.latest.shift(1) for the price as it traded that day" |
| E18 | `CapabilityError` | fail | features outside v1 (`engine="event"`, period windows, `style="panel"`, `history_mode="system"`, `downsample`, reserved names: `hold=` (before T7.1), `size.kelly` [R4F-02], `portfolio.optimize`, `risk.ledoit_wolf`, `ml.importance`, `clock="utc:…"`, other values of `[settlement]`), data features not in the snapshot (4.3, 4.5: an `nx.Item` not in the value tables, `at="open"` in a market without opens, backtest or economic labels on an adjusted-price package, a Filter or an output that 4.3 refuses on a package whose `price_basis` is not `raw`, naming the term, why, and the three fixes of 4.3 [#725 Decisions 218; #752], `backtest`, strategy `screen` or `live` on a package whose dividend rows lack `pay_date` and that declares no cash-date rule (5.6.11) [#752], and so on), a market without a rules pack (without `allow_untested`), `label_entry = "close"` in a graph that reads a value table (5.1.4) [#752] | "{feature} is not available in v1" or "{dataset} not in snapshot; needed for {feature}" or "no market rules pack for {market}; supported: {list}" / alternatives |
| E19 | `DataAvailabilityError` | fail (live) | the execution session is beyond the watermark; a dataset the strategy reads does not cover the decision session (5.9.6 rule 4) | "the execution session {d} for {market} is not in snapshot {id} (complete through {w})" · one line per short dataset: "{dataset} for {market} ends at session {s}, before decision session {d} in snapshot {id}", each followed by "  read by the strategy: {item}" for every item or event kind the graph reads from it / "run again after the next data refresh" |
| E20 | `FxStaleError` | fail (funding), warning (`.to`) | the exchange rate is older than the limit | "latest USDKRW available at {ts} is {k} days old" / "[fx] max_stale_days_decision = {k} (recorded) or use a snapshot with newer fx_rates" |
| E21 | `StatisticUnavailable` | notice | not enough input for DSR or PBO | "DSR n/a ({reason})" / "run longer than 30 sessions or add candidates to the study"; when an engine-produced series is the cause, the second form of the data tail `data — run {id} / series {name} / T = {t} (needs 30)`, with no `tds check` in `more` |
| E22 | `ProvisionalCostWarning` | warning | `provisional` profile | "costs are provisional fixed bps (KR buy 5/sell 25, US 5/5)" / "[costs] profile='realistic' after the cost table is verified" |
| E23 | `CostTableError` | fail | `realistic` but the table coverage is insufficient | list of empty (country, period, side, component) / "use provisional, or add verified rows to costs/*.csv" |
| E24 | `ImpureFunctionWarning` | warning | a user function reads a global array or a file | "{f} reads global '{name}'; the engine cannot check what it contains"; for a function under a library decorator whose wrapper holds what the hash cannot read: "{f} is under {decorator}, whose wrapper holds what the engine cannot check"; for a helper under such a decorator: "{f} reads {helper}, which is under {decorator}, whose wrapper holds what the engine cannot check"; for a class or a module that cannot be found by its name: "{f} reads class {class} as '{name}', whose module '{module}' cannot be found by its name; the engine cannot check what it contains" |
| E25 | `StaleMarkWarning` | warning | a holding is valued at a price more than 20 sessions old | instrument, period / "exclude {id} from the universe, or check trading_status for {id} with tds check (the mark is the last valid close, 5.6.3)" |
| E26 | `IdempotencyNotice` | notice | orders with the same idempotency key (book, decision, graph, params) requested again, with the same `sheet_id` or on a closed sheet (5.9.6 rule 1) | "orders for decision {id} already exist in book {b} (state version {v}, sheet {sheet_id}); showing them" |
| E27 | `TradabilityUnknownWarning` | warning | fills with unknown tradability > 0 | count, period / "[execution] unknown_tradability='block' to see the sensitivity" |
| E28 | `PayDateEstimatedWarning` | warning | an estimated pay date used | count / the dates marked estimated (`pay_date_status`) |
| E29 | `FitLeakError` | block | in an `ml.factor` training window or a validation fold, test-block labels could enter training (5.3.11, 6.3a); (reserved) the fit segment of a fitted state transform overlaps or adjoins the evaluation segment [SC-19] | "{term} is fitted on {fit} which overlaps {segment}" / "set fit_segment='train' and embargo_sessions" |
| E30 | `DataProfileWarning` | warning | a 4.5 fallback used, a dataset-level `backfilled` or `mixed` dataset used, suspect data (F-05, F-09, F-16 volume units), after-the-fact attributes (F-14), late bars (`LateBar`), untested market rules, no grid (F-15), a decimal literal ratio chosen by the user (`ratio_decimal_literal`), pre-tax restoration of a short net dividend, root pin unusable (`retention: unmanaged`), whether a delisting distribution is included unknown, no data on exceptions admitted to a short-sale ban, a dividend whose pay date lies before its ex-date (`pay_before_ex`, 5.6.11), a spun-off holding before its first bar (`unvalued_holdings`, 5.6.12) | fallback id, dataset, market, count (+ `producer.hints`) / "[data] fallbacks='fail' to stop instead" |
| E31 | `UntrustedRunError` | fail | `rerun` of a run folder made on another machine [SC-27] | "run {id} was created on another machine; its source/ will be executed" / "nx.rerun(id, trust=True) after reading source/" |
| E32 | `StartDependenceWarning` | warning | start-date probe difference (6.5) [SC-04] | "{node} on {d} changed when the run started {k} sessions earlier" / "remove state carried across windows (globals, recursion)" |
| E33 | `ReconcileMismatch` | notice (CLI exit code 4) | the first reconciliation difference (5.9.7) [SC-14] | date, instrument, stage, the two values / candidate reasons + "nexusbt reconcile --restate; if the snapshot is identical, nexusbt bugreport {run_id}" |
| E34 | `UnmaskedCrossSectionNotice` | notice | a cross-section operation on the Strategy signal path has no `mask` and the universe is narrower than the default domain (6.5 check 10) | "{term} ranks over all listed equities, not the strategy universe" / "pass mask=universe (or ignore if intended)" |
| E35 | `BookConflictError` | fail | the book lock is held, the book definition (`definition_sha256`) differs from the current strategy and settings, an order batch of another book or state version; fills against a superseded sheet, a correction of an unknown or already replaced row, `live` with an unacknowledged divergence, an acknowledgement of a divergence that is not the current one (5.9.6 rules 1 to 3) | "book {b} is locked by pid {p}" / "book {b} was created for graph {g1}; current strategy is {g2}; create a new book" · "sheet {old} was superseded by {new} ({reason})" / "re-execute from {new}, or record these fills as discretionary rows with an empty order_ref" · "book {b} has an unacknowledged divergence {id}" / "read nexusbt reconcile, then nexusbt book acknowledge {b} {id} --note …" |
| E36 | `OrderSanityWarning` | warning (live) | there are orders caught by the pre-order checks (5.9.6) | "order {id} for {instrument_id}: {check} ({value} vs limit {limit})" / "check the data for {date}; the order is shown, not blocked" |
| E37 | `LotRoundingWarning` | warning | `rounding_gap > 0.05` of a decision (expensive instruments, small capital) | "{n} of {m} decisions leave >5% cash from share rounding (median {p}%; {k} targets got 0 shares)" / "raise capital, lower n, or use max price filters" |
| E38 | `CollateralWarning` | warning | there were sessions in which short collateral was insufficient and new short sales and long buys were blocked (cash account: no forced-liquidation model; margin account in 5.6.7a); sessions in which mandatory obligations made cash negative (`shortfall`); sleeve ruin `ruined` | "collateral short by {amt} {ccy} on {d} sessions (max {x})" · "mandatory debits left cash negative on {d} sessions (min {x})" · "sleeve {m} ruined on {date}: new orders stopped" / "lower [shorting] max_gross or collateral_ratio; forced buy-ins are not modeled" |
| E39 | `UnknownFilterNotice` | notice | during a run, the unknown share of a universe Filter > 50% at a decision and market (5.4.1) | "{p}% of listed names are unknown for universe filter {leaf} on {n} decisions (input NaN: {column})" / "make the fallback explicit: ({leaf}).fillna(True) or .fillna(False)" |
| E40 | `ValidationNotice` | notice | a CPCV with nothing to select reduced to 1 path, the fit count exceeds `[validate] max_fits`, a fold's training samples fewer than `min_samples` (default 100) (6.3a), fits not made because the training labels hold one class (5.3.11) | "cpcv has nothing to fit or select; all paths are identical, showing 1 path" · "{n} model fits and {p} state-machine passes planned (> max_fits {m}){; IS scores {mode}}" (raised on the fit count only; the counts include the fits and block replays of strict IS scores, and with a `grid` the message names the mode; `fix` then adds "or is_scores='approximate' (cheaper, labelled approximate)"; 6.3a, #215) · "{n} fits skipped: one class in the training labels (ml_single_class)" / "add grid= or an ml.factor, or use method='walk_forward'" |
| E41 | `BlockOutputError` | fail | a user block's output is outside its contract: a multiplier outside `[0, 1]` or not finite, a negative or infinite sizer output, a selected id outside the universe, account access by a `reads_account=False` rule, a state value that is not a JSON scalar (3.8, 5.5.2); a `decide` block reading outside its `ctx`, or returning an id outside the universe, a weight or quantity that is not finite, weights beyond the sizing output's bounds, or a cancel or replace of an order it did not place (3.7) [#725 Decisions 237, 238]; a model's `classes_` value outside the label's classes, a `predict_proba` output outside its contract (shape, a value outside [0, 1] or not finite, a row not summing to 1 within 1e-6, a duplicate in `classes_`), a factory or clone that returns a model already fitted in the run (5.3.11) | "{block} returned mult {x} for {id} on {d}; risk rules may only scale toward zero (0 ≤ m ≤ 1)" / "clip in your rule, or declare @nx.risk_rule(reads_account=True)" |
| E42 | `AccountNotEvaluatedNotice` | notice | `screen` meets an account rule without an account (m = 1 is used), or a `decide` block without an account (the block is not called, and the canonical target is shown, 3.7) | "{rule}: not evaluated (no account)" / "use --ledger BOOK or --as-backtest" |
| E43 | `SolverFallbackNotice` | notice | ERC fallback, `vol_target` not computable (5.5.1a) | "erc did not converge on {n} of {m} decisions; inverse_vol used (reasons a/b/c: …)" / "raise lookback, add shrink=0.1, or use size.inverse_vol" |
| E44 | `MLDeterminismWarning` | warning | `[ml] threads ≠ 1`, known non-deterministic models or GPU (5.3.11) | "fits used {k} threads; rerun compares predictions with tolerance 1e-9" / "[ml] threads = 1 for bit-identical reruns" |
| E45 | `SkippedWorkNotice` | notice | a skip reason without a fixed E code (6.6 no silent failure) | "skipped {n} {what} for reason {r}" / reason-code documentation |
| E46 | `EngineBugError` | fail (exit code 2) | invariant violation, engine assert, unexpected exception in engine code; a whole-book constraint violation is never E46, its one case having been removed with 5.3.12 (5.5.2b; #752) [#151] | "this is a NexusBT bug, not your code or data" / "please report: nexusbt bugreport {run_id}" |
| E47 | `DatasetShapeError` | fail | retired 2026-11-24 (#752) with `from_parquet`, `Events` and `Macro`; a value table's shape is stopped by the tools (5.3.5) [#725 Decisions 222]; the number is not reused | — |
| E48 | `PretrainedLeakError` | fail | trying to use predictions for decisions before a pretrained model's `training_cutoff` (+ embargo) (5.3.11) | layer: your code / "model {name} was trained on data up to {cutoff}; decision {d} is before it" / "start after {cutoff+embargo}, or nx.ml.pretrained(..., allow_overlap=True) to mark instead of fail" |
| E49 | `PretrainedOverlapWarning` | warning | overlapping decisions marked with `allow_overlap=True` and the run proceeds (5.3.11) | layer: your code / "{n} decisions use a pretrained model that saw their period" / "read the marked rows before trusting the result" |

- An exception inside a user function raises the original exception as it is (not wrapped in an engine error), and engine frames are hidden so that the first line of the traceback is the user file:line (`__tracebackhide__`; `raise ... from None` is not used) [R-42].

---

## 7. Performance budgets [P1~P11, 11.8]

**Machine** [#725 Decisions 256]: no machine is a target. The measurements are taken on the measuring machine, an 8 GB machine of the maintainer's with a local SSD and macOS, at least 20 GB of free disk, with its usual work running alongside (addresses outside the repository, 2.7); where a ticket says a measurement on synthetic data runs "on the compute machine", it runs on this one; a measurement that needs the real snapshot (T6.28) runs where the snapshot is (2.1), and its numbers are kept as a record beside these. The spec and the docs keep the measured records and say what the engine runs well on; they promise no time on any machine. **Data**: one fixed snapshot (the first real export of stage E1; before that, the synthetic `full` preset `synth-full-0001`). The snapshot id is written in the results.

**"Interactive"** (user decision 2026-11-03): research is interactive when the edit-and-rerun loop is: the user changes a strategy or factor file, runs it again and reads the result, and any call that runs longer than 2 s (P2's first number, kept as this rule) shows a running indicator and can be cancelled. It is not one number; the budgets below are the parts of that loop that can be measured.

**What the numbers mean** [#725 Decisions 257; the owner, 2026-11-25]: each number of P1 to P9 and P11 below is a first estimate, re-written after the first measurement; from then on a measurement worse than the one before it raises an alert, which is a regression to fix. None is a pass/fail gate. P10 stays a correctness gate (table 8.1). Where the engine's behaviour reads a number, that number is a rule, not a budget, and stays: the process-tree memory budget `[compute] memory_budget_gb` (default 4.0, 3.11), by which chunks split (5.3.8); the running indicator after 2 s (above); P8's order, static checks before the heavy computation (6.5); and the pre-registered switch of window kernels from definition A to B, which reads M1's stage budget for `SMA(252)` (ADR 0019, OI-47). `capacity` stays off by default for the time it costs (OI-15), a default, not a gate. The CI smoke thresholds at the end of this chapter are that test's own.

| # | Task | First number (alert) | Target | Measurement definition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | `import nexusbt as nx` + opening and validating `tds.json` (`verify="manifest"`, profile judgement included) | p95 ≤ 1.5 s | 1.0 s | a new process each time, OS cache warm |
| P2 | `nx.screen(strategy)` 252-session window, KR+US | p95 ≤ 2 s | 1 s | warm process, first of 11 runs in the same process discarded |
| P3 | `nx.evaluate(Returns(232).shift(21))` monthly 30 years KR+US | p95 ≤ 5 s (no engine cache) / ≤ 1 s (cache) | 3 s / 0.5 s | new process, cache state stated |
| P4 | per-session IC factor (`freq="daily"`) 30 years US | p95 ≤ 30 s | 20 s | |
| P5 | `nx.backtest` monthly top-N (n=50/sleeve) with costs 30 years KR+US, accounting, 5 scenarios and saving included | p95 ≤ 30 s | **10 s** | new process, no engine cache |
| P5-daily | `nx.backtest` with daily decisions, top-N (n=50/sleeve) with costs, 30 years KR+US, accounting and saving included. The workload holds three things the monthly P5 does not: daily turnover (a rebalance decision every session), a user function in the graph (one window-style `@nx.factor(window=…)`), and partial fills (order sizes that the volume cap of 5.6.6 binds on some sessions, the rest carried over) | measured first, budget set after | measured first, budget set after | new process, no engine cache. The person sets the p95 budget and the target after the first measurement (end of M3a, the table below), and this row is changed then |
| P6 | `nx.sweep` 27 candidates, common terms shared | p95 ≤ 120 s | 60 s | |
| P7 | peak summed RSS of the engine process tree (main + workers) during P5 | ≤ 4 GB | 3 GB | psutil samples every 50 ms, summed over the whole tree. L0 memory-mapped pages count in RSS, so the peak summed **USS (anonymous memory)** is also reported on a separate line, and the alert also reads USS against 4 GB (both reported). The rule the engine follows is `[compute] memory_budget_gb`: if a 1-year chunk would exceed it, it is split smaller (5.3.8) [SC-15] |
| P8 | static checks (6.5) | ≤ 1 s; before heavy computation (a rule, kept) | | from the call to the start of computation |
| P9 | truncation probe | no gate, time reported | | [C8] |
| P10 | zipline comparison tolerance | table 8.1 | | correctness gate |
| P11 | **High-dimensional**: 300 factors (made from about 40 base columns through windows, ranks and composites) × 30 years × KR+US, monthly decisions — (a) `nx.evaluate({300 factors})`, (b) `backtest` of a 300-factor `ops.composite` score (top-N, costs and accounting included), (c) (b) with `method="walk_forward"` + a 3-point `grid` | **target only** (no gate: since 2026-11-24 its first number after T6.26's measurement is an alert, as for the others) | (a) ≤ 300 s, (b) ≤ 60 s, (c) ≤ 180 s; engine tree USS ≤ 6 GB | new process, both without and with the engine cache; bytes of columns read, decision rows computed, node count and shared node count reported alongside |

**Early measurement**: the bench rig (T1.24) comes in at **M1**, and each milestone measures the workloads pinned in the table below, so that the real cost of the structure is known before M3a fixes it rather than at M6. M6 (T6.11) measures every workload, records it and compares it with the earlier measurements; M6's correctness gates stay (10.0) [#725 Decisions 257]. P7 memory is measured as the sum over the **whole process tree** (main + workers). The rule "a stage over 2 times its first number: profile → cause ADR" (under P11 below) applies to every stage measured from M1 on, as soon as that stage has a first number; it asks for a cause, not a rewrite.

| When | Ticket | Snapshot | Workloads |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | T1.24 | `synth-full-m1-0001` (T0.8d: the M1 datasets at `full` scale, about 27,000 instruments and 60 million daily bar rows; a different id from `synth-full-0001`, which also holds the M2 datasets) | P1; opening, block reads, adjustment and cross-sections; `SMA(252)` over all daily rows (definition A; the basis for deciding whether to switch to ADR-0019 B). Each stage cold and warm, with the summed USS of the process tree |
| end of M2 (after T2.8) | T2.14 | `synth-full-m1-0001` | P3, P8 |
| end of M2 (after T2.10a) | T2.24 | `synth-full-m1-0001` | P11 (a) **reduced, M1 columns**: the 300 factors of `bench/fixtures/p11.json` built only from the base columns the M1 datasets carry (price, volume, calendar-derived) through windows, ranks and composites, `nx.evaluate` at monthly decisions on decision rows only; cold and warm, with the P11 reporting columns |
| end of M3a | T3.18 | `full_ci` in CI (1/50 of `full`), `synth-full-0001` on the compute machine | P5 on one market with the state machine, P5-daily on one market, P7; on the compute machine also the **full** P11 (a) (the 40 base columns of `bench/fixtures/p11.json`) |
| end of M4 | T4.9 | `synth-full-0001` | the whole P5 (two sleeves, five scenarios), P5-daily KR+US, P7 |
| M6 | T6.11 | `synth-full-0001` or the first real export | every workload, beside the earlier measurements, and P2b (it needs the L0 cache of T6.12 and the L1 cache of T6.4, both M6) |

**Cold and warm** before M6 use the caches that exist at that milestone: cold = a new process with the Numba cache emptied, warm = a new process with it filled (the OS file cache is warm in both, as in P1). From T6.12 on, cold also deletes the L0 folder (and from T6.4 on, the L1 folder). Warm numbers measured before #144 and #148 (what the L0 and L1 keys hash) are closed carry `provisional: true` in the result JSON.

**Measurement rules** [11.8, 3.10]: `bench/run.py --workload P5 --reps 10 --snapshot <id>`. 10 or more runs, p50/p95/maximum reported, the first-process JIT delay (Numba cache emptied) on a separate line, the engine cache state stated, saving time included, no extrapolation from return arithmetic. The result JSON `bench/results/<YYYYMMDD>-<git sha>-<machine>.json` (`<machine>` as T6.11 says; workload, reps, p50, p95, max, rss_max, uss_max, snapshot_id, cache_state (hit/miss for each of L0/L1, and cold or warm), provisional, jit_first_s, versions, `fixture_sha256`, `stage_seconds{}`, `output_value_sha256{}` (canonical output value hashes — the evidence of a result that is fast yet the same) — summary figures only, no paths or instrument values, 2.7) is committed at the end of a milestone. Per-stage budget (first estimates within P5's first number of 30 seconds [est.]): opening the snapshot 1.5, block reads 0.5 on an L0 hit and 6 on a miss, Pipeline decision rows only 3, targets 0.5, state machine (30 years × 2 sleeves) 8, 4 scenarios (gross = cost0x once) 7, saving and hashing 2 — replaced by a real table from T1.24 (M1) and measured again at the end of M2, M3a and M4 [blueprint 12.3]. The state machine is column arrays (5.6.1), and T6.6 (Numba session step) is done only on the condition its entry states (ADR 0054). When a measurement raises an alert, the bottleneck is found by profiling before the table is changed and the regression is fixed; a Rust implementation follows only ADR 0054's trigger, swapped in through the 2.4 seam and tested for bit identity (ADR-0016) [Q14, 11.9 Q-E4].

**Workload P2b** (not a gate, reported): `screen(strategy)` with L0 and L1 caches warmed by yesterday's snapshot + today's snapshot (only the last year partition changed) — the real situation of a screener used every day. Target ≤ 2 s (the same as P2), reported together with the number of changed partitions and the hit rate (the v3.1 key included `markets`, which holds the watermark, so the hit rate was 0; the v3.2 L0 key has only stable fields). Today's snapshot usually also adds a session row and may list new names; the L0 key hashes axis rows, not axis files, so those changes miss only the years the invalidation table of 5.3.9 names (the current year for both), and "only the last year partition changed" follows from the key (user decision 2026-11-04). For the sleeve and scenario column batches (5.3.8), the two values with them on and off are written in the T6.5 and T6.6 measurements.

**Design means for high-dimensional workloads** (user difficulty 2: strategies with hundreds of factors were hard to test on zipline). To hold P11, existing rules are bundled and two are added:
1. **Sharing the same terms**: nodes with the same structural hash are computed once (5.3.2) — the return, window-mean and rank inputs that 300 factors use in common.
2. **Computing only on decision dates**: a node whose consumers read only decision rows is computed only on decision rows and reads only the window input rows of those (the planner propagates the row mask backwards, 5.3.2). Monthly 30 years = 360 decision rows (instead of about 7,500 daily rows).
3. **Column pruning**: only the columns the graph uses are read from Parquet (by column), and unneeded years are skipped by year partitions and row-group statistics.
4. **Year and market chunk caches**: L0 and L1 (5.3.9); the same L1 values are reused by `offsets`, `scenarios` and validation paths.
5. **Parallelism by market and factor**: workers compute independently per (market, year chunk, node chunk), and the results are gathered in sorted-index order so they are bit-identical (INV-03, INV-04). The worker count and the memory budget are in `[compute]` (5.3.8).
6. **Memory ceiling** (outputs too, decision 2026-11-04, #206: no output tier of 5.9.1 is held whole in memory; predictions are written per fit and per chunk as they are produced): processing is done in year chunks with immediate reduction — `evaluate` reduces to IC and quantile statistics per decision and does not hold the whole factor value matrix (300 × 360 × about 12,000 × 8 B ≈ 10 GB) in memory, and a composite score holds only the 300 × n matrix of one decision (about 30 MB). [new]
7. **Rust seam**: window and cross-section kernels and reductions sit behind Arrow input and output (2.4), so when ADR 0054's trigger holds (most of a workload's time in one Numba kernel, and the owner judges it too slow), they are swapped (ADR-0016, bit-identity test). [the P11 measurement is the new basis]
P11 (a) is measured before M3a fixes the structure (decision 2026-11-04, #175): reduced to the M1 columns at the end of M2 (T2.24) and in full at the end of M3a (T3.18), both in the early-measurement table above. Both run on the plan of 5.3.2 (shared terms, decision rows only; T1.7a) but before T6.26's column pruning and batched reductions, so they show the cost of the structure as the engine first implements it. The engine measurement of (a)(b)(c) with the design means is T6.26 (P11 on synthetic `full`), and with its result this table's first numbers are re-written; no gate is set (ADR 0054 [#725 Decisions 257]).

**Prototype results (2026-10-28, summary `docs/design/prototype-results.md`)**: P5 on a Mac mini M4 cold p50 5.8 s / p95 6.8 s, warm 1.6 / 3.7 s, USS 2.1 GiB (laptop M1 cold p95 19.6 s); P11 (a) 50 s, (b) 34 s, (c) 30 s (simplified version), USS ≤ 1.35 GiB; with daily ffill, financials alone would have been 194.6 GB (computed); computing only decision rows = full computation (2,190,000 values bit-identical); output hashes identical across two machines and cold/warm; hand calculations 32/32. Design means added: **8. Skipping by row-group statistics** — the min/max statistics of Parquet row groups are used so that chunks of other markets and years are not read (P11 (a) 122 s → 45 s). Measurement rule added: macOS cannot read the USS of other processes, so the summed tree USS of P7 is summed from **the measuring rig inside each worker process** reporting its own USS. The synthetic generator (4.8) adds the shapes missing in the comparison with real data: `obs_status` no_trade and missing, `symbol_change` and `announced` events, and the `full` scale matched to real data (about 27,000 instruments, about 60 million daily bar rows).

**P11 fixed fixture**: so that the comparison target does not move, the workload is pinned in a file — `bench/fixtures/p11.json` (made by T2.24): a DAG of 300 factors (generator seed recording 40 base columns, each with the dataset it comes from, the window length distribution {5, 20, 63, 126, 252}, the rank and composite ratios, and the node sharing rate), composite weights, period 1995–2024, label `fwd_return` 1 month, a 3-point grid (`n ∈ {20, 50, 100}`), walk-forward `train="10y", test="1y", step="1y"`, scenarios off. `fixture_sha256` = the sha256 of that file.
**P11 stage budget** (target, revised after measurement): (a) 300 s = opening and column reads 30 + shared-term computation (decision rows only) 180 + IC and quantile reduction per decision 60 + saving 30; (b) 60 s = Pipeline (not an L1 hit) 35 + composite score and targets 5 + state machine 15 + saving 5; (c) 180 s = reuse of (b)'s Pipeline 0 + 3-point grid linear runs 3 × 20 + fold IS slicing 5 + stitched OOS state machine 20 + the linear run run alongside 15 + saving and hashing 10 + slack 70. If a stage exceeds 2 times its first number: profile → the cause written in an ADR; the Rust seam (2.4) only by ADR 0054's trigger.
**Correctness evidence**: the bench JSON includes `fixture_sha256`, per-stage times, USS and the **canonical output value hashes** (`outputs{file: value_sha256}`; the same hash as 5.9.2). Accepted only if the output hashes of a run without cache (cold) and with cache (warm) are identical (fast yet the same result).

**Prototype summary and engine evidence** (design review D14, 2026-11-04): the prototype results above are a summary of throwaway code whose raw JSON is not in the repository; they set no gate and are not cited as engine evidence. Engine evidence is a result JSON under `bench/results/` from T1.24 on: code pinned by the git sha in its name, a synthetic fixture pinned by `snapshot_id` and `fixture_sha256` (4.8), the environment below, per-stage measurements and the canonical output value hashes. A comparison with another engine is reported only for the same semantics, costs and outputs (the 8.1 equal-semantics rule); no budget or sentence assumes that NexusBT is faster before such a measurement.

**Resource fields of the result JSON** (D14, 2026-11-04; the budgets and the tables above are unchanged). Besides the fields of the measurement rules, each workload reports:
- `environment{}`: Python version, OS and version, CPU model, memory size, the sha256 of `uv.lock`, `workers`, `[compute] threads`, `[ml] threads` (summary figures only, no host or user names, 2.7);
- `io{bytes_read, bytes_written_outputs, bytes_written_cache}`: bytes read from the snapshot and the cache, bytes written to the run folder and to the cache;
- `peak_intermediate_bytes`: the largest number of bytes the engine's own intermediate arrays (column blocks, term arrays, reductions) held at once, from the planner's allocation accounting; it is the copy and intermediate-array cost, beside the process-level `rss_max` and `uss_max`;
- `cache{l0_bytes, l1_bytes, l0_hits, l0_misses, l1_hits, l1_misses}`: the cache size on disk after the run and the hit and miss counts (the counts behind `cache_state`; P2b's hit rate is computed from them);
- `fits{n, fit_seconds, hash_seconds}` and `replays`: the number of model fits (the rows of `fits.parquet`, 5.3.11) with the sums of its `fit_seconds` and `hash_seconds` columns (hashing against fitting, 5.3.11 Cost; #270), and the number of state-machine passes (the main run, each scenario, each grid point and each fold or path pass, 6.3a);
- `stage_seconds.record`: writing the run record (manifest, events, derived tables, index; 5.9) apart from computing, so the record cost of a small default run (P5) and of a nested validation run (P11 (c)) can be compared.
A field the milestone cannot measure yet (no cache before T6.4 and T6.12, no fits before M2) is `null`, never 0.

**CI performance smoke test** (GitHub runner, synthetic `small`): P2-small ≤ 3 s, P5-small ≤ 20 s. It is for catching a 5x regression and is not a gate.

---

## 8. Tests (comparison, hand calculation, properties and metamorphic relations, TDS conformance)

### 8.1 The zipline comparison suite (`tests/parity/`) [R-50, R-55, P10, D7, 11.8]

- **Environment**: `tests/parity/pyproject.toml` + its own `uv.lock`. Python 3.12, `zipline-reloaded==3.2.1`, `pandas==2.3.3`, the rest as the lock sets it. It is not mixed with the engine environment. Run: `uv run --project tests/parity python -m parity.run --suite L1 --fixture small`. Results are written as Parquet, and the pytest of the engine-side `tests/parity_compare/` reads and compares them (the engine side does not import zipline) [R-64].
- **Scope** [SC-12, A-57]: the zipline comparison is a test that shows "the same value when the meaning is the same". It compares price terms, adjustment, masks and classification (L1) and backtests of the preset `zipline` (L2). **Our daily-bar OHLC adverse-side rules (5.6.4) differ in meaning from zipline, so they are not compared and are tested only with hand-calculated golden cases (G-02~G-09, G-31~G-33).** The order types themselves (limit, stop, stop-limit, day/GTC) are compared in L2-d **with a preset that imitates zipline's trigger behaviour** (the "comparison test with zipline" of user decision 9.0 is kept within this scope). The evidence file carries the sentence "parity does not imply realism" [blueprint 12.6].
- **Data**: CI = synthetic `small` (the version with prices rounded to 3 decimals). Manual on the compute machine = a subset of a real snapshot chosen by principle (not put in the repository; evidence is summary figures only, 2.7): 50 instruments × 5 years per market, filled for each category (split ≥1, reverse split ≥1, cash dividend ≥1, delisted during the period, halt ≥1, no events) from the front of the `sha256(instrument_id)` order — no selection fitted to instruments [design 4.8, no-ticker-fitted-rules].
- **The only conversion point**: `parity.make_bundle(snapshot_path, market) -> bundle_name` (TDS package → zipline bundle; it uses the same mapping table as `tds export zipline-bundle` of TDS 9.8, and calls that export once it exists). zipline sid = the sort order of that market's `instrument_id` (so ordinal ties come out the same). Instruments whose prices go beyond the price ×1000 uint32 range (about 4,290,000) are not dropped but held with a **per-instrument price scale** (×1/10ⁿ, undone by a test-side `InMemoryDailyBarReader` subclass), and the list of scaled instruments is written in the evidence (reported as a limit of the comparison scope). splits ratio = `1/ρ`; dividends are given as (ex_date, record_date, declared_date, pay_date, amount) and zipline computes `1 − D/previous close`.
- **Time alignment**: zipline `EquityPricingLoader` shifts by one day → the output for zipline date D = NexusBT market-axis row `prev_session(D)`. Each market is run separately [11.8].
- The `InMemoryDailyBarReader` bug is worked around with a test-side subclass. zipline's known bugs are not taken as the answer: differences are written in `tests/parity/KNOWN_DIFFERENCES.md` [R-55 fix]. When they differ, the hand calculation (T-25) decides (the same rule as 3.4). Differences v3.1 knows: missing values under Filter `~` (we: unknown, zipline: true) and under a comparison such as `close > X` (we: unknown, zipline: false; ADR 0045) — L1-d compares only cells without missing values, and so does L1-a for a Filter, and missing cells are tested by T-24; tie-breaking of `nx.top`, `Factor.top`, `rank("ordinal")` (the comparison uses `tiebreak = "id"`); `VWAP` with a NaN close and a volume in the window (we: the row is out of both sums, 3.4; zipline: its volume stays in the denominator) — the `VWAP` comparison compares only windows without such rows, and those windows are tested by T-25 [#278]. Differences found by L1 (2026-11-19, #252, ADR 0045): (A) the day before an ex-date — zipline's value for D already carries the adjustments effective at D, and the engine row `prev_session(D)` is fixed at its own row (ADR 0044) before them; on a row whose next session is an instrument's ex-date, a price-level value (SMA, EWMA, VWAP, `close.latest`, the close window) is compared as the engine's × that session's price factor `m` = zipline (the split factor only for the cases read without dividends), a ratio as it is. (B) terms not known at the row — in row t's window the engine uses the version of an event known at `cutoff(t)`: none before its terms are published (5.3.4), and for an amended or cancelled event the version known then (5.3.3a); zipline has no knowledge time and applies the final version from the ex-date. A cell whose window holds the ex-date of an event not yet final at `cutoff(t)` is not compared. The test fixes, for each case, the number of cells (A) compared through `m`, (B) left out and the cells compared, from the corporate actions and the calendar; the evidence carries them. Differences found by L1-d (2026-11-20, #253, ADR 0046): `quantiles` (we: the mean-rank bucket of 5.4.3, 1..q; zipline: `pandas.qcut` on the values, 0..q−1, an error when two edges are equal), `percentile_between` (we: the mean-rank percentile of 5.4.2; zipline: the values between numpy's linear percentiles), `zscore` (we: the 5.3.10 order of addition; zipline: numpy's), a grouped `rank` (zipline: NaN for a whole group with a NaN cell; both sides pass `mask=x.notnan()`), a Filter outside its mask (we: unknown, 3.3; zipline: false; compared as the set of true cells) and an instrument on its last session (in the engine's cross-section, not in zipline's). The test computes from the engine's input the cells where zipline's rule on zipline's cross-section answers differently (for `rank(groupby=)`, the cells whose rank in their group differs), with the near ties the input's last bits can reorder, leaves them out and fixes their number; ties at a bucket edge or a bound are tested by the unit tests of 5.4.2 and 5.4.3, and the quantile hand calculation is V31-58.

| Stage | Comparison | Tolerance | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| L1-a price terms | `close.latest`, `Returns(2/21/252)`, `SMA(20/200)`, `EWMA`, `AverageDollarVolume(63)`, `AnnualizedVolatility`, `VWAP`, `RSI`, 3 user CustomFactors (12-1 momentum over 253 rows, SMA ratio, threshold Filter `close > X`) — **default inputs on both sides** (our default for return series = `close_tr` ↔ zipline `USEquityPricing.close`; windows that contain dividends included) | `|a−b| ≤ 1e-9 + 1e-9·|b|` | The threshold Filter tests the adjustment reference point (C2). Level factors (`SMA`, `VWAP`) are compared with the same input stated explicitly |
| L1-b adjustment | windows of ±5 sessions before and after splits, reverse splits and dividends | same | `compat.zipline.USEquityPricing.close` ↔ `Price.close_tr` |
| L1-c point-in-time fundamentals | removed 2026-11-24 (#752) with `Fund` | — | — |
| L1-d masks and classification | `top(N, mask=)`, `rank(ordinal, groupby=)`, `zscore`, `percentile_between`, `quantiles` | sets and ranks exact; `zscore` values `|a−b| ≤ 1e-9 + 1e-9·|b|` (ADR 0046) | |
| L2-a backtest, zero costs | month-end top-N equal weight, preset zipline + `information_lag_sessions=1` (zipline's `before_trading_start` has data up to the previous day), sizing price = close of the decision day, fill at the next day's close, slippage 0, commission 0 | daily NAV relative 1e-8, fill quantities exact | |
| L2-b costs on | FixedBasisPointsSlippage(5, 0.1), PerShare(0.001), including cases where the cap binds | NAV relative 1e-6, quantities exact | the preset implements the 10% same-bar cap |
| L2-c corporate actions | holdings through a split, a cash dividend (pay date) and a delisting | valuation absolute 0.01 (currency) | delisting = last price (preset) |
| L2-e long–short (preset) | month-end top-N long + bottom-N short equal weight, zero costs, zipline negative holdings | daily NAV relative 1e-8, fill quantities exact | windows that include short dividend payments |
| L2-d order types (preset semantics) | in the preset `zipline`, limit, stop and stop-limit buys and sells, day/GTC, partial-fill carry-over: trigger session, fill quantity, fill price | quantities and sessions exact, price relative 1e-9 | trigger = that bar's close alone (5.6.15, settled by T3.15b from the zipline code). Our OHLC rules are not tested here |
| L3 data round trip | export → bundle → zipline reader values | same (rounded inputs) | list of rows outside uint32 |

- **Evidence**: `tests/parity/evidence/<YYYYMMDD>-<sha>-<suite>-<fixture>.json` = `{suite, zipline_version, fixture_semantic_hash, cases: [{id, metric, n, max_abs, max_rel, tolerance, pass}], environment}`. Committed at the end of each milestone (like the reference producer's contract RESULTS) [design 4.8].
- Mixed markets (a sum of sleeves) have no zipline comparison. "The combined result = the sum of single-market runs (valued at exchange rates)" is checked by the property test M-SLEEVE (premise: `market_local`, no cross-market terms, 8.3) [design risk 13, SC-04].
- There is no second comparison engine (vectorbt or the like) (zipline is the only reference engine, 9.0 Q35). The canonical answers are the hand-calculated golden cases of 8.2, not `handcalc.py`.

### 8.2 Hand-calculated golden cases (`tests/golden/`) [11.8 M0, M3, M4]
- A case = `tests/golden/cases/<id>.yaml`: a description, a synthetic `tiny` event list (a table set by hand), the input (either a canonical target given directly to test execution only, or terms given to test the time rules), **expected values written out as literal numbers for each session and phase** (order events, fill quantity and price, costs (minor units), cash, holdings, `decision_value`, `nav_economic`, bridge rows).
- **No second engine**: v2's "independent hand-calculation engine `handcalc.py`" meant writing the general state machine once more, so its errors correlate and it makes M0 heavy. In v3 the expected values are **per-session arithmetic for each case** (the numbers in the yaml + a check of a few lines next to the case file, `tests/golden/check/<id>.py`: arithmetic and rounding only, no loops, classes or engine imports). `tests/golden/test_handcalc_self.py::test_no_general_loops` checks via the AST that the check files have no `for`/`while`/`class` and do not import `nexusbt`. A person checks a few numbers of each case by hand and leaves them in a yaml comment (this is the truly independent verification).
- **Place and protection**: a case is written by another session in the **golden ticket right before** the implementation ticket that uses it (chapter 10, e.g. T3.Ga) and merged first. An implementation PR cannot change `tests/golden/cases/**` or `check/**` (`.github/CODEOWNERS` = the user + `tests/repo/test_golden_guard.py` rejects changes to those paths in the PR diff; if the spec is wrong, "spec deviation" + the golden ticket is reopened).
