Time and Pipeline golden cases T-01~T-12: T-01~T-06 removed 2026-11-24 (#752) with the fundamentals, panel, classification and universe kinds they tested, T-07 hand calculation of windows before and after splits and reverse splits, T-08 the `close_tr` window of a dividend whose amount comes late, T-09 symbol reuse (`instrument_symbols`), T-10 alignment of a start date that falls on a holiday, T-11 ties in `instrument_id` order (under `tiebreak = "id"`; the default hash is T-27), T-12 `Returns(232).shift(21)` = 253-row window `close[-22]/close[0]−1` = `P[t−21]/P[t−252]−1`; the three forms are equal when no cell of those rows is late, and with a cell that became available after `ready(t−21)` and by `ready(t)`, the `shift` form is NaN where the window forms read the cell (5.1.2) [C7]. T-13 and T-14 removed 2026-11-24 (#752) with `share_counts` and `classifications`, T-15 a `price_factor` null event makes the earlier rows of the window NaN (4.6), T-16 label `ts_label_start`, `ts_label_end` and purging at the end of a segment (5.8), T-17 removed 2026-11-24 (#752) with `eligibility_daily`.

T-24 three-valued Filter — with close 12,000 / 8,000 / NaN (halted), `close > 10000` = true/false/unknown, `~(close > 10000)` = false/true/**unknown (finally false; true in v3)**, `(close > 10000) | close.isnan()` = true/false/true; `(close > 10000).all(2)` is unknown for true and unknown, false if there is a false; unknown in `.as_factor()` → NaN; missing in `nx.Item.sector.eq(1)` → unknown; `(~Trad.halted).fillna(True)`; T-25 hand calculation of built-in factors (`tests/golden/factors/`): `SMA(3)` 1, NaN, 3 → 2.0; `EWMA(3, decay=0.5)` 1, 2, 3 → 2.428571; 1, NaN, 3 → NaN; `MaxDrawdown(4)` 10, 12, 9, 11 → 0.25; `Returns(3)` 100, NaN, 110 → 0.10; a large value 50 outside the window does not enter `MaxDrawdown(3)` (nautilus#4333); `VWAP(3)` close 10, NaN, 12 with volume 100, 300, 300 → 11.5 (pairs only, not 4600/700), no finite close or the only pair's volume 0 → NaN [#278] — 5-row cases with NaN positions for all 12 kinds (the three return-series kernels of 3.4, #725 Decisions 246, get theirs in the same form before they are built); T-26 percentile composite — f1: A 10, B 20, C NaN, D 30 / f2: A 3, B 1, C 2, D NaN (higher is better for both): `pct_rank(na="drop")` f1 = 0, 50, NaN, 100; `"bottom"` f1 = 33.33, 66.67, 0, 100; `composite({f1: 1, f2: 1}, na="middle")` = A 50, B 0, C 50, D 100; T-27 tie-breaking — `KR:T00001`, `T00002`, `T00003` with equal scores: `tiebreak="hash"` order T00002, T00001, T00003 (key = the first 8 bytes of `sha256("nexusbt-tiebreak-v1" ‖ 0x1F ‖ id)`: 2629704763309498885 < 4315078529452203467 < 8949987214584180479; the same at every decision and clock — v3.1's decision salt is dropped), `"id"` order T00001, T00002, T00003; T-28 market-level series — for a KR sleeve, `Market.series("IDX:KOSPI_TR").latest` is the index close at decision d; under `KR_close`, `Market.series("IDX:SPX_TR")` is the US close of d−1, and putting it in a window is E17 [V31-13].

T-18 rectangle lookup — a row announced in advance with a future effect does not hide the current row (5.3.6 examples 1~3), T-19 removed 2026-11-24 (#752) with `classifications`, T-20 a late bar (`ts_end+PT5M`) and `ready_delay`: the bar stays in its own row and the decision uses it from the first row whose `ready` reaches its `ts_available` (5.1.2), T-21 economic label 100 → dividend 10 → 100 = 10% (5.8) and its time-order cases below, T-22 purging of a delisting label whose recovery is disclosed after the segment end (`ts_label_available`), T-23 the decision dates of `every(5)` do not depend on the start date (3.7).


**T-21 cases** (the time order of 5.8's `fwd_return`) [2026-11-04]: 1 share, zero costs, pre-withholding, entry price 100, a cash dividend `D` = 10 whose ex-date is session e and whose terms are available before e unless the row says otherwise. The last column is v3.4's product formula, which reinvested at the close of e even when the share was sold before it; it must not appear.

| case | path | `R` | 1-share accounting value | `ts_label_available` | v3.4 product |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| (a) | e before the exit session, close of e 100, exit open 100 | n = 1.1 → 110/100 − 1 = 10% | – (cash 10 kept, not reinvested) | ≥ the available time of the close of e | 10% |
| (b) | exit at the open of e 90, close of e 100 | (90 + 10)/100 − 1 = 0% | 90 + receivable 10 = 100 → 0% | the exit open's | 1.1 × 0.9 − 1 = −1% |
| (c) | as (b), close of e 120 | 0% (the close of e is not read) | 0% | the exit open's | (130/120) × 0.9 − 1 = −2.5% |
| (d) | `label_entry = "close"`: entry close 100, exit at the close of e 95 | (95 + 10)/100 − 1 = 5% | 95 + 10 = 105 → 5% | the close of e's | (105/95) × (95/100) − 1 = 5% |
| (e) | as (b), terms of `D` available 3 sessions after e | 0% | 0% | the terms' `ts_terms_available` (3 sessions after e) | −1% |
| (f) | a 2:1 split (`q_e` = 2) and no dividend, exit at the open of e 45 | n = 2 → 90/100 − 1 = −10% | 2 × 45 = 90 → −10% | the exit open's | −10% |

Changing the high, low or close of e in (b), (c), (e) and (f) leaves `R` and `ts_label_available` unchanged. Tested by T2.7 and T2.G.

### 8.3 Property tests (`tests/property/`, hypothesis)

| id | property | generation |
|---|---|---|
| P-CHUNK | all node values bit-identical with chunk sizes of 1 year / 3 years / everything [INV-03] | synthetic small and its late-bar variant (4.8 rule 3a), random graphs |
| P-PERM | the same results with the instrument column order shuffled [INV-04] | |
| P-CACHE | cache hit = miss [INV-05] | |
| P-FUT | between a snapshot with the data after time τ deleted (rows with `ts_available > τ`: bars, event versions (`ts_known > τ`), delisting recoveries, FX, value-table rows) and the original snapshot, the targets and orders of decisions with `decision_ts ≤ τ` are the same; deleting an event version whose terms come late (an amount amendment) also leaves earlier decisions the same; two snapshots equal in available information and different only in after-the-fact amounts give the same orders (and the same `nav_decision`) on every session, and differ only in the report series (`nav_economic` and what is built from it); with an `nx.ml.factor` node (#270), a snapshot with rows added after τ in the same year partition gives the same `fit_id`, seed, predictions and targets for every decision at or before τ, and one feature or label value of a training sample corrected gives a different `fit_id`, so the earlier fit is not reused (its predictions may change; they are equal only when the model is insensitive to that value, which the test does not assume either way) [INV-01, INV-02, 5.3.11] | random τ; includes the fixed case G-42 |
| P-ADJ | registered-kernel shortcut = reference (random events and scalars, relative 1e-12) [INV-11] | |
| P-NAV | accounting reconciles every session, money in integer minor units [INV-06] | random targets and events |
| P-BRIDGE | every session, the sum of the NAV and P&L bridge rows = the NAV change (difference 0) [INV-06]; it closes even with splits, dividends and distributions put at random on days without trades (halts), with ledger deposits and withdrawals (V31-19), with short and long–short holdings (borrow cost, short dividends), and with cash interest and negative-cash obligations | random targets, events, costs, flows, shorts |
| P-QTY | after every order and every event `requested = filled + cancelled + remaining`, no EXPIRED exit orders [INV-18]; exact ratios (`ratio_num/den` = 1/3, 1/7, 21/20, 129/100) applied to integer-multiple holdings give fractional-share cash 0; the same for short holdings; an event whose exact ratio is missing, invalid or disagrees with its f64 column is handled as 5.6.1 says (#188); covers ≤ `|q|` | random orders, splits, OCO, shorts |
| P-RESUME | checkpoint and restart at a random session = in one go [INV-07, INV-10] | |
| P-PARITY13 | screen target = backtest target at 20 random decisions [INV-08] | |
| P-STATE | order state transitions stay within the table of 5.6.2 | |
| P-DET | the same input twice → the same physical hash of the output [INV-09]. run in **two new processes** with different `PYTHONHASHSEED`. Parallel order (decision 2026-11-04, #152): `workers = 1` and `workers = 2`, and a cold and a warm cache, give the same predictions, `fits.parquet` rows, selected grid points, path tables and output hashes for a CPCV run with a learned factor and a grid (5.3.8) | |
| P-STAT | PSR, DSR, PBO = independent test values (paper examples, hand-made matrices); bad inputs (N<2, T<2S) → n/a | |
| P-INCOMPLETE | a CPCV path or a stitched walk-forward series with a missing fold (a fit not made for one fold's test range) is reported `incomplete_path` with its evaluation period and `n_valid`/`n_total`, never as a complete OOS record: no metric over the dates that remained is its result, it is not in the path distribution, and the representative number of a CPCV run is the median over complete paths, never the best path (6.3a, #216) | random splits and folds whose training labels hold one class |
| P-IS-STRICT | CPCV with a `grid` and a learned node, strict IS scores (6.3a, #215). (1) Perturbing only the labels of one split's held-out decisions leaves that split's training sample keys, the `pred_sha256` of its fits, its IS predictions, IS scores and chosen parameter unchanged (the labels are a column independent of prices; the `fit_id` of its fits is unchanged too, since their inputs are, 5.3.11); the OOS profit and loss of its test range is not required to be unchanged, and with `is_scores="approximate"` the same perturbation is allowed to change them (the fixed example G-116 does). (2) A training block's IS returns equal those of a linear run started with capital K and an empty book at the block's first decision with the same grid point and the split's predictions, whatever was traded in the test groups before it: replacing the targets of every earlier test group by cash changes no IS return (no carried account state). (3) Changing prices inside a test group **does** change the value of a feature at a later training decision whose window reaches into that group, and may change that block's IS scores: the test asserts that the feature value changed, so that it tells a legitimate later market feature built from test-period prices from a forbidden carried account state, and a strict mode that cut market data at the block start fails it | random `n_groups`, k, refit schedules and grids; a model that predicts the mean of its training labels; a feature with a window longer than a group |
| P-LOOK | research-record properties (6.2, 6.4, #269): (i) a second run on a preregistered segment (look number n ≥ 2) is never labelled independent; (ii) a run whose conditions differ from the preregistration is not labelled independent; (iii) an exact rerun adds no look (it keeps the look number and label of the run it reproduces) | random sequences of preregistrations, runs, reruns, changed conditions and extended end dates on synthetic small |
| P-LIVE | no `fill` with sessions beyond the watermark; in the order **`live → fill → live` (same decision)** and in random orders, one order batch per decision, no duplicate fills [INV-19]; `live` twice on the same snapshot and book gives the same `sheet_id` and no new event; **a fill against a superseded sheet is refused** (E35) in any order of `live`, a corrected snapshot and `record_fills` (`tests/property/test_live.py::test_P_LIVE_superseded_sheet_refused`); **a corrected fill counts once**: the account equals the one built from the last row of each correction chain, whatever the chain length and when the CSV is loaded twice (`::test_P_LIVE_corrected_fill_counts_once`) (5.9.6 rules 1 and 2) | random call orders, snapshot corrections and correction chains |
| P-BOOK | forced termination at every commit boundary of a ledger transaction (fragment write, checkpoint, HEAD, status) → reopening gives one valid state; two writers → one gets E35 [INV-19] | termination injected at each boundary |
| P-KERNEL | for each registered kernel, fast kernel = reference kernel (bits in the same environment), reference = the explicit left-fold definition (ADR-0019 A); for the transcendental class both paths use the same elementwise function [INV-13] | random windows and NaN positions |
| P-START | shifting the start date by 1, 21 or 252 sessions leaves the term values (registered-kernel bits) and canonical targets of the overlapping decisions the same (including `every(n)` — calendar anchor) [INV-14] | random graphs |
| P-DERIVED | `derive_tables(canonical events)` = the stored `positions`, `daily`, `metrics`, `attribution`, `exposures` [INV-16] | random strategies |
| P-RECON | after a paper ledger runs for n sessions, `reconcile` = yes; changing one ledger event gives the first difference at that date and phase [INV-10] | |
| P-FILLCAUSAL | (i) between the original snapshot and one where, at a random session t, volume, orders and the state before the phase are fixed and only that day's H, L, C (and all data after t) are changed, the quantity, `fill_price` and cost components (minor units) of open-phase fills (`at=open` market, limit and stop set at the open) are the same; intraday-phase fills are the same when only `C_t` is changed. (ii) For an **isolated order** with no other orders and ample cash, increasing `volume_t` does not decrease the fill quantity (monotone non-decreasing), the fill quantity is at most `floor_lot(η·ADV_t)` for every `volume_t`, and the fill price does not depend on `volume_t` (the total cost changes with the quantity, so it is not compared) [INV-01]. v3.1's "increasing `volume_t` does not increase the fill quantity" contradicted the second line of G-56 (up to 50,000 at 1,000,000), so a correct implementation failed it | random sessions and variants |
| P-SLEEVE-ISO | with targets fixed, replaying each sleeve separately = replaying them together (except cross-market tax payments), scenario column batches = separately [INV-21] | random targets |
| P-GRID | every limit price, stop price, exit price and computed U, D is on that session's grid; adding ±1 ULP to bar prices leaves the fill results the same; the order of pairs across price-band boundaries is preserved in minimum-unit integers, and off-grid observed bars are not moved [INV-20] | random prices and orders |
| P-SHORT-OFF | (i) **same-build differential**: running the example strategies and random long strategies with `[shorting] enabled = false` and with `enabled = true` + no negative targets + default exposure (`max_gross ≥ 1`, `max_net ⊇ [0, 1]`) gives the same value hash for every output table; (ii) 0 short events (`borrow_fee`, `short_rebate`, `dividend_short`), `side ∈ {sell_short, buy_to_cover}` and `reserved_minor ≠ 0` in an off run; (iii) **baseline**: the off run matches the **value hashes** (not physical bytes) of the four core tables (`targets`, `orders`, `account_events`, `daily`) that T4.17 recorded before the short-sale tickets — the baseline files are a golden-protected path, updated only by a ticket that changes the schema, with a reason in the PR + the user-approval label `baseline-update`; (iv) the independent long golden cases G-01~G-66 pass [INV-22]. v3.1's "all output bytes = a fixed hash file" was dropped because it collided with the legitimate schema and report changes of later tickets | example strategies, random long strategies |
| P-MARGIN-OFF | (i) **baseline**: the value hashes of the four core tables (`targets`, `orders`, `account_events`, `daily`) of a `[account] type = "cash"` (default) run equal the baseline from before T4.20 (the same protected path and `baseline-update` label as P-SHORT-OFF); (ii) 0 `margin_interest`, `margin_call`, `forced_liquidation` events in a cash-account run, 0 negative cash after fills (except obligation `shortfall`); (iii) **same-build differential**: a long-only strategy with `type = "margin"` + `initial_margin = 1.0` + `debit_rate_bps = 0` + a `maintenance_margin` that never triggers has the same value hash for every output table as the cash account (buying power = E − R(1) = cash) | example strategies, random long strategies |
| P-TRADES | `Σ realised per trade + unrealised of open trades = period P&L` (costs, distributions, borrow included), a flipping fill is split into a closing share and an opening share, MAE and MFE use only prices after entry, without the extremes of the entry and exit bars | random orders, splits, shorts |
| P-CACHE-INCR | on a new snapshot where only the last year partition changed, the result of L0 and L1 hits = computing without cache; with an amendment of a past partition or of an old event, misses from that year on [INV-05]; v3.2 cases: the overlapping window of January rows sees an amendment of the previous year's partition, an amendment of a version in the middle of a chain, an amendment that changes only the publication time, amendments of `instruments` and `calendar_sessions`, a snapshot where only the watermark changed keeps its L0 hits, a wrong `value_sha256` (H004) does not reuse another block, a partial L1 cover does not fill missing rows — the whole result (hit vs miss) is compared, not the keys; axis cases (user decision 2026-11-04), each checking that exactly the years of the 5.3.9 invalidation table miss and every other year hits: tomorrow's session added (the current year only), a new name listed (the years its listing period touches), an old listing date corrected (the years the old and the new period touch), a session row of year Y corrected (Y and Y+1), a new snapshot that changes `calendar_version` on every past row and nothing else (no year misses: every year keeps its L0 and L1 hits); L1 cases (user decision 2026-11-04): after tomorrow's session is added, the L1 hit rate is > 0 — term values whose input rows lie only in past years hit, the others miss, and the result equals computing without cache; a session row of a past year Y corrected misses exactly the L1 entries whose input rows reach Y or Y+1; with no `session_ord` column, one session added in a past year Y: among the L1 entries whose input rows start after Y+1, only the nodes that read the absolute `session_ord` (an `nx.every` node and every node downstream of one) miss, and the others hit | random amendment positions, random listing periods |
| P-CACHE-FIT | (user decision 2026-11-04) the same data and the same learned factor (`nx.ml.factor`) evaluated with two different excluded-sample sets (two CPCV combinations whose test blocks, purging and embargo differ), each with the L1 cache on (warm, filled by the other set) and off: the four runs give the predictions and validation numbers of their own set, equal between cache on and off, and cold and warm caches give the same `fit_id` and predictions (#270); two runs that differ only in `run_seed`, with a model whose predictions depend on `random_state`, give different predictions, each equal to its own cache-off run; a node downstream of the learned factor misses with it, and a node without a learned input keeps its hits [5.3.9, 5.3.11] | a deterministic toy model with `random_state`, random test-block choices |
| P-RISK-OFF | (i) between a run without account rules and a run with an account rule that is always m = 1 (`drawdown_brake(off=0.99, on=0.98)` on data where it does not trigger), the value hashes of all tables outside INV-23's exclusion list (`risk_state`, the `account:*` rows of `waterfall`, `graph_hash`, `settings_hash`, `run_id`, the report's "risk order" line) are the same; (ii) without account rules, `final_weight ≡ target_weight` (bits) and the account layer is not called; (iii) `top(n, weight=w, max_weight=c)` = `top(n), sizing=nx.size.w(max_weight=c)` (bits); (iv) together with `[shorting] enabled = false`, the INV-22 baseline holds [INV-23] | example and random long strategies |
| P-SIZE-PURE | sizers and market rules do not receive the account: randomly changing the account state (cash, holdings, NAV) leaves `target_weight` bit-identical; per side of the sizer output `Σ|w| ≤ 1`, after modifiers `Σ|w| ≤ min(1, max_gross)` and `Σw⁺ + c·Σ|w⁻| ≤ 1` [INV-08] | random selections and windows |
| P-RISK-SHRINK | after every risk rule (built-in and random user rules) `|w_after| ≤ |w_before|`, the same sign, the reduced share goes to cash (no redistribution); bit-identical for the same order | random rule lists |
| P-WF-STITCH | for a strategy with nothing to choose (no `grid`, no ML), the stitched OOS series and all table values of `method="walk_forward"` are bit-identical to `backtest(start=t_1, method="linear")` (starting with capital K at the first test decision `t_1`) | example strategies |
| P-SPLIT-DET | the split list and `split_hash` are the same in two new processes; in no fold does training contain a sample caught by the purge condition (`max(ts_label_end, ts_label_available)` overlaps the test block, whose end is the latest such time over all its samples, 6.3a) or by the embargo; CPCV path count = `k·C(n_groups,k)/n_groups`. Required cases (user decision 2026-11-04, #149), each stated by `decision_ts`, label intervals, publication times and the market calendar, never by row counts: (0) an earlier test decision whose label ends after the last test decision's label, and a training sample that overlaps only the earlier label is purged; (1) an earlier test decision whose label is published after the last one's (a delisting recovery published late); (2) a label that ends before the block's latest `ts_label_end` but whose `ts_label_available` is after it; (3) different holding periods in one block (triple-barrier stops at different times); (4) several instruments at one `decision_ts`, some with late labels, where every sample of that `decision_ts` is on the same side, training or test; (5) a training label interval that touches either end of the block exactly, which is purged (both ends included); (6) an embargo counted in market sessions from the last test decision's `decision_ts` when the next decision falls after a market holiday | random `n_groups`, k, decision grids, late-disclosed labels; cases (0)–(6) as fixed examples |
| P-ML-REPRO | with `[ml] threads = 1`, `pred_sha256` is the same for each training in `rerun`; in every method (walk-forward, CPCV, segments) the training samples have no labels after τ (label availability time + embargo ≤ τ, forward only); CPCV training samples have no decisions of that split's test blocks; shifting the start date leaves the training at the same τ the same (INV-14) | a deterministic toy model (a test linear regression with only `fit/predict`) |
| P-SKIP | per decision, `universe count = selected + skips (selection stage) + out of rank` and `target count = order intents + skips (execution stage) + suppression events` match exactly; 0 skips without a reason | random missing values and halts |
| P-TDS | the output of every preset and `degrade` combination of `make_snapshot` passes `tds check --level full` with the declared profile; the `capabilities` and `fallbacks` of a snapshot opened by the engine equal the tables of 4.3 and 4.5 [INV-17] | |

**Metamorphic tests** (`tests/property/test_metamorphic.py`) [SC-04, INV-15]. Each relation has a **premise**, and the test keeps both random graphs that satisfy the premise (cases where it holds) and fixed counterexamples that deliberately break the premise (confirming that the relation breaks):

| id | transformation | premise | relation |
|---|---|---|---|
| M-SCALE | all raw prices of one instrument ×k, volume ×1/k, factors unchanged | every term of the graph is homogeneous of degree 0 in that instrument (returns, ratios and their ranks), and there are no price-level thresholds, price-level ranks or costs (minimum commission) | degree-0 homogeneous terms, selection and target weights invariant, degree-1 homogeneous terms ×k. Counterexample: the threshold `close > X` |
| M-DUMMY | add an instrument that enters the universe at no decision | that instrument enters **the mask of no cross-sectional operation** of the graph (every cross-sectional operation has a mask no larger than the universe) | the result table invariant, every column and row bit for bit; each count of `term_diagnostics` (5.4.1) unchanged or one more, the added instrument's own cell at that term and session. Counterexample: a universe on top of a `zscore` without a mask |
| M-SPLIT-NEUTRAL | add a fake split event with ratio 1.0 | – | all results invariant |
| M-CCY | `base_ccy` KRW ↔ USD in a strategy with only a US sleeve | no currency-unit terms in the decision inputs (no `.to(ccy)`) | USD-based returns the same, the KRW combined return = the USD return + the FX and cross terms of currency attribution (5.6.9) |
| M-SLEEVE | a combined KR+US run vs two single-sleeve runs | `clock = "market_local"`, no cross-market terms (`across_markets`, `.to(ccy)`) | combined NAV = the sum of the single NAVs valued at FX (currency minor units). Counterexample: `KR_close` + a cross-market rank |
| M-START | the same as P-START | target-state recursion off | |

### 8.4 Look-ahead and hygiene test list [R-51, R-52]
`tests/unit/test_determinism_rules.py`: 0 wall-clock, random and `uuid4` calls in decision-path modules, 0 places where iteration over an unsorted `set` decides event order (AST). `tests/unit/test_leaks.py`: negative shift (E1 static), **an injected future-reading source** (a test reader passes the whole-period array of the snapshot in through a closure, and a function reads values after the decision date → probe E1; `::test_probe_catches_injected_future_reader`), `np.roll(close, -5)` that wraps only inside the window (it does not read the future, so **E1 must not be raised**; `::test_bounded_roll_not_flagged`), a function that uses a whole-period mean precomputed in a global (E24 + probe + L1 off), changing the constant global `THRESHOLD` (the cache key changes, no E24), a label input (E2), a survivorship-biased list (E4), mixing currencies (E5), segment exposure records (E10), a function that uses state outside the window (global accumulation → E32 start-date probe), the priority of probe date selection (6.5 a/b/c), a `backfilled` dataset used (E30).

**G-TIME-LATE** (a late bar; the visibility rule of 5.1.2 and 5.3.1) [decision 2026-11-04]: market KR whose bars have the column rule (`{"column": "ts_available"}`) and no `ready_delay`, so `Δ = 0` (`ready(t) = ts_close(t)`), instruments A and B, a decision at each session close, orders at the next session open. The bar of A at session s (close 18) has `ts_available = ready(s+2)`; every other bar is on time, and B closes 12.2 on every session. Strategy: `SMA(3)` of `close`, `top(1)`, the run starting at session s. The last column is what a rule that reads the bar as soon as some snapshot holds it would give; it must not appear.

| row | A term value `close` | A `SMA(3)` | B `SMA(3)` | target | order intents | look-ahead A `SMA(3)` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| s−2 | 10 | – | – | – | – | – |
| s−1 | 11 | – | – | – | – | – |
| s | NaN (`LateBar` 1) | nanmean(10, 11) = 10.5 | 12.2 | B | buy B at the open of s+1 | 13 (A) |
| s+1 | 13 | nanmean(11, 13) = 12 | 12.2 | B | none | 14 (A) |
| s+2 | 14 | (18 + 13 + 14)/3 = 15 | 12.2 | A | sell B, buy A at the open of s+3 | 15 (A) |

Rerunning with a snapshot published after s+2 gives the same rows s and s+1, bit for bit (INV-02). A reader with reference time `ready(s+3)` — the report's `LateBar` list and `reconcile` — shows the cell (s, A) as stored: close 18 with `ts_available = ready(s+2)`. Tested by stage (decision 2026-11-10, #356): T1.1b (an opened snapshot keeps each bar's `ts_available`), T1.3 (`ready(t)` and the `LateBar` count), T1.4 (the row), T1.9 (A's `SMA(3)`), T1.11 (the target) and T3.4 (the order intents).

**Newest data before a sheet** (5.9.6 rule 4) [decision 2026-11-03]: a synthetic snapshot (4.8) of market KR whose watermark `complete_through_session` is session d and whose `bars` and `calendar_sessions` cover d, while the coverage `by_market.KR.last` of `tradability_daily` stops at session d−2. Strategy A uses tradability rules; strategy B reads only prices; both decide at the close of d. `nexusbt live A --book paper:t` emits no sheet, leaves the book's `state_version` unchanged and exits 3 with E19 "tradability_daily for KR ends at session d−2, before decision session d in snapshot {id}"; B on the same snapshot gets its sheet. **Value-table variant** [decision 2026-11-04, #192; `eligibility_daily` and `fundamentals_state` removed 2026-11-24, #752]: on a snapshot of the same market and watermark whose `values` is processed through session d−2 (`coverage.by_market.KR.processed_through_session`; the generator's `behind={"values": 2}` of 4.8 once it writes that dataset) and whose `corporate_actions` is processed through d with its last event weeks before d, strategy C reads `nx.Item.roa` and decides at the close of d: `nexusbt live C --book paper:t` emits no sheet and exits 3 with the E19 "values for KR ends at session d−2, before decision session d in snapshot {id}" and below it "  read by the strategy: nx.Item.roa"; `corporate_actions` blocks no strategy, and B still gets its sheet. With the field removed from every dataset's `tds.json` entry by the test (a TDS v1.4 package written before it; the generator always writes it, 4.8), C gets its sheet, as rule 4 did before, and one E30 line names every dataset C reads without a row per session for KR, `values` among them (F-17). Tested by T5.12: `tests/unit/api/test_live_checks.py::test_newest_data_per_dataset_E19`, `::test_newest_data_names_every_missing_dataset`, `::test_newest_data_event_dataset_E19_names_its_items`, `::test_newest_data_event_dataset_current_without_an_event`, `::test_newest_data_without_processed_through_E30`.

**Real-ledger situation table** (5.9.7, OI-46) [decision 2026-11-11, #162]: each row is a situation the engine claims to handle, and its evidence is a session of the owner's real ledger on which the situation occurred, reconciled with `reconcile` (5.9.7). The table is the criterion, not a fixed length of ledger: the ledger covers the rows it can, and a row with no such session is recorded as "not observed" with the reason. For a `manual:` book the `reconcile` report compares holdings and fill prices (5.9.7 (4)), and such a book records fills, rejections and corrections only (5.9.6). So the last column says where the actual value comes from: "reconcile" is that report; "statement" is an amount the report does not compare, which the owner reads from the account's own statement against the event the model books, recording the difference as a number. The ledger, the snapshot, the statement and their rows stay outside the repository (2.7); the repository holds this table and, per row, summary numbers only (T6.29).

| Row | Situation | Rule | What the model books | Compared with |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| LV-01 | a split of a held instrument | 5.6.10 | the holding quantity after the effective session | the actual holding (reconcile) |
| LV-02 | a cash dividend whose pay date was estimated or declared (#752) | 5.6.11 | the receivable and its pay session | the cash received and the session it arrived (statement) |
| LV-03 | a delisting with a terminal value | 5.6.13 | the terminal entitlement | the cash received for the removed holding (statement) |
| LV-04 | a halt on an execution session | 5.6.5 | the `blocked` order and its carry-over | the actual fills of that session and the next (reconcile) |
| LV-05 | a partial fill under the volume cap | 5.6.6 | the filled and carried-over quantities | the actual fills (reconcile) |
| LV-06 | a price-limit day | 5.6.5 | the block or the fill at the limit | the actual fill of that session (reconcile) |
| LV-07 | a corrected corporate action | 5.3.3a (7) | the correction entry (`action_correction`) | the adjustment the account received (statement) |
| LV-08 | a short position with borrow cost | 5.6.3 (accrual) | the `borrow_fee` over the nights held | the charge to the account (statement) |

A row whose session has a fill also reports the fill-vs-decision basis points against the model's own reference: the next open, with the order time, type and quantity matched to the actual order, so that the overnight move is not read as execution cost.

### 8.4a TDS conformance tests (`tds/tests/`, package `nexusbt-tds`)
- For each check code there are `tds/conformance/<code>/fail/` (a small package that raises only that code) and `pass/`, and `test_conformance.py::test_<code>` checks fail → exactly that code, pass → 0 errors (TDS 8.5). The packages are made by the generator script `tds/conformance/build.py`, which pins their hashes (no hand-edited files).
- `test_cli_exit_codes` (0/1/2/64/66/70), `test_jsonl_output_schema`, `test_value_hash_ignores_physical_choices` (changing compression, row group or dictionary gives the same value and semantic hashes), `test_partition_columns_from_path`, `test_vectors_match_published` (`tds/conformance/hash-vectors.json`; the test vectors are language-independent hand-written byte strings and their sha256), `test_availability_change_changes_semantic`, `test_profile_three_results` (declared / check level / not_checked), `test_M012_path_escape`, `test_M013_duplicate_path`, `test_M014_unknown_required_rule`, `test_experimental_schemas_flagged`.
- Engine side: `tests/unit/data/test_open_snapshot.py` checks that the E14 message contains the TDS code and (when present) the `producer.hints` sentence, and no producer ticket number.

### 8.4b Error layer tests (`tests/errors/`)
- `layers.toml`: one layer per error code (+ condition id) (or two in order, such as `data → your code`). The same as the layer lines of the 6.6 table.
- `cases/<code>-<condition>.py`: the smallest code that raises that error (synthetic `tiny`). `test_every_code_has_layer_case` (every code and condition has a case), `test_layer_line_matches_table`, `test_layer_tail_fields` (the tail per layer: data = snapshot, dataset, column, producer; your code = file:line, function; config = table, key, value; engine bug = the `nexusbt bugreport` command), `test_fix_line_present`.
- `test_no_internal_terms.py`: 0 engine module or class names outside the public name table (3.1) in every case sentence of the layers other than engine bug.
- `test_user_exception_note.py`: `KeyError`, `IndexError` and the like inside a user function keep their original class and traceback, and get the layer and fix lines attached through `add_note` (R-42).

### 8.5 Documentation example tests [R-53, R-45]
The ```python blocks of README, `docs/` and `examples/` that carry the `<!-- test -->` mark are run by `tests/docs/test_examples.py` on the synthetic `small` snapshot. At each milestone, that milestone's examples (chapter 10 "commands to show") join this test. The **English quick start** (`docs/quickstart.md`) is a document that follows two CSVs (small hand-written files) → `tds import csv` → `tds check` → `evaluate` → a saved run → `rerun` without knowledge of the private producer, and `::test_quickstart_csv_to_rerun_en` runs it as it is.


- **Output compared too** (absorbed from ml4t, TD-ML4T-03): fails when the text of the `<!-- output -->` block next to a `<!-- test -->` block differs from the run output (fields that change, such as times and paths, are replaced by fixed placeholders).
- **Templates**: for each of the 22 templates of `nexusbt new strategy --template T` (3.10) (the test of each template is the acceptance test of the ticket that turns on its feature: `buffer-hold` T7.1, `walk-forward-cpcv` and `ml-factor` T6.27, `zipline-port` T5.19), `tests/docs/test_templates.py::test_template_runs[T]` runs `backtest --preview` and `screen` on the `init --demo` snapshot and compares them with the output blocks.
- **The 30-minute path**: `tests/docs/test_first_30_minutes.py` runs, in a clean virtual environment, the five commands `uv init quant && cd quant && uv add nexusbt` (the first line runs as one command even without a project; CI, before publishing on PyPI, uses `uv add nexusbt --find-links dist/` — the same clean wheel as the release gate T6.15) → `nexusbt init --demo` → `nexusbt new strategy momo --template monthly-topn` → `nexusbt backtest momo.py --preview` → `nexusbt screen momo.py` in turn, and checks that each command ends without error and that the total is under 2 minutes (CI runner). The 30 minutes a person measures is the pre-release user test (T5.18 Done means).
- **zipline → NexusBT mapping table**: the table of `docs/migrate-from-zipline.md` (English canonical + Korean) (what bundle, `initialize`, `handle_data`, `context`, `schedule_function`, `order_target_percent`, `attach_pipeline`, `pipeline_output`, `run_algorithm` and `CustomFactor` each become) and the `zipline-port` template, a runnable example with the same content; every code snippet in the NexusBT column of the table is `<!-- test -->`.
- **Tool cards: one plain explanation format** (design review D19, #219): every diagnostic of the 6.3 table and every `method` of 6.3a has a card `docs/guide/tools/<id>.md` (English canonical) with `docs/guide/tools/<id>.ko.md`, in seven numbered parts with these headings in this order: (1) the question it answers and the trap it guards against; (2) when to use it and when it does not fit; (3) the data, comparison set and conditions it needs; (4) how the data is split into training, selection and evaluation (a table or figure of the decision dates of the part 5 example); (5) a minimal run on the synthetic `small` snapshot (`<!-- test -->` with its `<!-- output -->` block once the feature exists); (6) how to read the numbers and figures and what may and may not be concluded, quoting the first-screen line exactly as 3.9 prints it; (7) what to try next, the computation cost and the limits that remain. Plain words and one concrete example come first; formulas and advanced conditions sit after them in a closed expandable part (`<details>`). A card uses the terms of 3.9, 6.3 and the guide (3.10), so Python, CLI, notebook and report say the same thing; no card names a pass threshold, a "safe" badge or a general probability of overfitting. Parts resting on a decision not yet in the spec say so with the issue (none at present: #149 and #215, which the `cpcv` card waited on, are in 6.3a, and that card names the `strict`, `approximate` and `slice` IS scores); the failure markers and the primary and reference labels are those of 6.3a (#216). `tests/docs/test_tool_cards.py::test_card[<id>]` checks the seven headings in order in both languages, the run of part 5 and the quoted line of part 6; `::test_every_tool_has_a_card_row` checks that the table below has one row per 6.3 row and per 6.3a method. The ticket in the table writes the card in the pull request that turns on the tool, and its Acceptance names `test_card[<id>]`.

| Card id | Tool | Ticket |
|---|---|---|
| `linear` | `method="linear"` | T3.13a |
| `holdout` | segments, `splits.holdout()`, `nx.preregister` | T2.18 |
| `walk-forward` | `method="walk_forward"` | T6.20 |
| `cpcv` | `method="cpcv"` and the CPCV path distribution | T6.21 |
| `pbo` | PBO (CSCV), `method="cscv"`, `sweep` | T6.8 |
| `psr` | PSR(0) | T6.8 |
| `dsr` | DSR | T6.8 |
| `min-trl` | MinTRL | T3.20 |
| `lo-sharpe` | Lo Sharpe | T3.20 |
| `ic-newey-west` | IC Newey–West t | T2.8 |
| `bhy` | BHY p | T2.16 |
| `scenarios` | cost sensitivity, scenarios, capacity (capacity says E18 until T6.14) | T3.13b |
| `offsets` | offsets | T6.18 |
| `log-loss-brier` | log-loss, Brier | T6.24 |
| `spa` | SPA / White reality check | none in v1 ("not supported in v1"); the v1.x ticket of 12.1 RS-03 |

- **Worked lessons** (design review D19, #219): six small comparisons, each a script `examples/lessons/<id>.py` and a page `docs/guide/lessons/<id>.md` (English canonical, with `<id>.ko.md`) that shows the code, the decision dates of each split as a table, the output block and how to read it. They run on the synthetic `small` snapshot (4.8), whose prices are independent uniform steps per instrument: no factor has a planted edge there, so any skill a lesson's flawed variant shows is the trap itself. Each lesson states its assumptions, seed and data next to its output; its "expected observation" is the direction the lesson teaches under those assumptions, not a claim that one tool is better in general, and the exact numbers are whatever the fixed seed prints (the output block, compared by the rule above). `tests/docs/test_lessons.py::test_lesson[<id>]` runs the script, compares its output block and checks the engine signals in the table. The code is a draft that has not been run (there is no engine yet); the owning ticket writes and runs it.

| Lesson id | What is compared | Data and seed | Expected observation | Engine signals checked | Ticket |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `leaky-training` | a model fitted outside the engine on every label of the period, loaded with `nx.ml.pretrained(training_cutoff=end)`, against `nx.ml.factor(train="rolling")` trained forward-only (5.3.11) | `small`, KR, monthly decisions, `seed=11` | the outside model fails before the run (E48); with `allow_overlap=True` it runs, every overlapping decision is marked (E49) and the IC on the marked decisions is above that of the forward-only factor (the outside model was fitted on those labels), whose IC stays near 0 | E48, then E49 with the marked count and denominator; `ml fits: forward-only` on the forward-only run | T6.27 |
| `best-of-many` | the best of 20 settings of a momentum lookback chosen on `train`, against the same choice on the `test` segment never used to choose (6.1) | `small`, KR+US, `seed=12`, `grid` of 20 lookbacks | the chosen setting's train Sharpe is the highest of the 20 and its `test` Sharpe falls back toward that of the other settings; on the train `grid`, PBO is near or above one half and DSR is below PSR(0), as expected when no setting has an edge | the comparison-set line with N = 20, PBO (CSCV) and DSR lines, the evaluation label of the `test` run | T6.27 |
| `preprocessing-window` | a feature scaled by each instrument's mean over the whole period (read through a global array) against the same feature scaled by a trailing window (`SMA`) | `small`, KR, monthly decisions, `seed=13` | the whole-period version shows a large IC (it knows where each price will return to), the trailing version an IC near 0 | E24 on the global read, then E1 from the truncation probe naming the scaling node; no E1 on the trailing version | T6.27 |
| `periodic-reselection` | choosing the lookback once on all of D and backtesting D with it, against `method="walk_forward"` with the same `grid` (re-chosen each window) | `small`, KR, `seed=14` | the once-on-all-data result is better than the stitched walk-forward series; the walk-forward chosen value changes between windows (selection stability) | the walk-forward fold table (chosen value per fold, `n_purged`, `n_embargoed`), the selection-stability line, the linear line beside it with its period | T6.27 |
| `cost-change` | one monthly top-50 strategy against the same rule rebalanced weekly, each at cost 0x, 1x and 2x | `small`, KR, `seed=15` | net CAGR falls as cost rises, and faster for the weekly version in proportion to its turnover; the chosen rule is not re-chosen by the scenario | the `cost sensitivity` line and turnover line of both runs; scenarios add no candidate (6.2) | T3.18 |
| `daily-watch-vs-good-day` | watching a paper book every day (`status.json`, `reconcile`) and checking a preregistered criterion once at a fixed date, against running the `test` segment every month and declaring at the first month the Sharpe clears a threshold | `small`, KR, a strategy with no edge, `seed=16`; the declaration rate over 2,000 synthetic return series drawn with `seed=16` | the daily status lines report the book and decide nothing; one fixed check declares a pass at about its nominal rate, the stop-at-the-first-good-month rule declares several times more often, each rate printed with its Monte Carlo standard error. The page and the output say: "the rate comparison is a plain simulation over 2,000 synthetic return series, not an engine feature; the engine's evidence is the look numbers and the label only" | look numbers 1 to 12 and the label "exploratory on reused final data (seen m times before)" on the monthly reruns; the preregistration line on the fixed check | T5.6 |

- **Use scenarios** (design review D19, #219): nine kinds of user, from the plain call to the expert setting, each with criteria a test checks on the synthetic `small` snapshot (`tests/docs/test_scenarios.py::test_scenario[<n>]`) and the documentation ticket whose Acceptance names it. A user who stops before any final test is a normal user: no scenario requires a research object, a hypothesis document, a preregistration or an unused holdout unless it is about that tool. Parts resting on a decision not yet in the spec say so with the issue; that spec edit adds them to the row.

| # | Scenario | Criteria | Ticket |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A fixed RSI or moving-average rule, linear | a strategy using only `SMA` and `RSI` runs with `nx.backtest(strategy, start=, end=)` and no other argument or object; giving `study=` changes no target, order, fill or NAV (it only groups records); the first screen carries the `data`, `period`, `execution`, `costs` and `assumptions` lines, the attempts line and `evaluation research data`, and the `hypothetical exits` line when the run had one (5.6.1, #150) | T3.18 |
| 2 | A factor with `evaluate` | the README section "Evaluate one factor" prints the IC mean, its Newey–West t with L, the `label` line (which return, over what interval, when it was known) and the BHY line with the cohort's m; a factor below `[gates] ic_t` still converts with `.to_strategy()` and its backtest runs (gates are display only) | T2.14 |
| 3 | A linear `grid` or an external optimizer | a `grid` of k points records k candidates and one selection event; a point that raised an error is a `failed` run counted in the denominator of `with returns a/b`; the chosen point's line says `research data`; a user loop calling `nx.backtest` five times records five candidates and no selection event, and the line says "recorded by the engine" (6.2; protocols for external optimizers are v1.x, 12.1 DR16-1) | T6.27 |
| 4 | Rule tuning or an ML factor with walk-forward | changing only the labels of decisions inside test window k leaves unchanged every fit, in-sample score and chosen parameter made before the first moment such a label is usable for training (its `max(ts_label_end, ts_label_available)` plus the embargo, in market sessions; 6.3a, #271); a fit or a selection made at or after that moment under the declared refit policy may differ, and the test checks both halves: unchanged before, a difference allowed after and never earlier; changing the label of the last decision of window k, whose usable moment is after the window, changes no fit inside window k; an `nx.ml.factor` takes a factor as input with no `evaluate` run of it | T6.27 |
| 5 | An allocation strategy (`erc-vol-target` template) | changing returns after a decision leaves that decision's weights unchanged (the covariance window ends at the decision, 5.5.1a); traded amounts and costs at a decision follow from the holdings carried from the previous decision; the linear account starts empty with capital K (6.3a state table) | T3.18 |
| 6 | An event strategy (trial (c), `event-driven-earnings`) | a decision after an event is an ordinary decision with an `nx.Item.<name>.age()` Filter (3.7; `on_event` and G-108 removed 2026-11-24, #752); a violation no reduction can repair is recorded `constraint_residual` (5.5.2b, #151); the strategy state record of one book is never read by another book or another strategy (5.5.3, #152) | T5.18 |
| 7 | CPCV or CSCV | the report gives the path count, the cohort's N and the observation count on separate lines, and N does not grow with the paths (6.2); `method="cscv"` without a `grid` is E16; path rows carry the 6.3a interpretation sentence and the best path is never the result line; purging covers every label of a test block (6.3a, #149); with a `grid` and an ML factor the IS scores are strict by default, and changing only the labels of a test group does not change the parameter chosen for the split that tests it, even after those labels are usable for training (the split's exclusion mask); with `is_scores="approximate"` the first screen says that the IS scores are sliced from a full-period run and are approximate; with no `grid` and no ML factor nothing is selected and nothing changes (6.3a, #215) | T6.27 |
| 8 | Missing conditions, an external model, reused data | a run with T < 30 shows `DSR n/a (T < 30)` and goes on; a pretrained model's cutoff is shown as declared, not audited (ADR-0041), with E49 marks when overlap is allowed; a rerun of a seen `test` segment shows "exploratory on reused final data (seen m times before)"; a path with a fold whose fit was not made shows `incomplete_path` with its dates and `n_valid`/`n_total`, not a Sharpe over the dates that remained, and a period held in cash for lack of a signal counts as cash return, not as a failure (6.3a, #216) | T6.27 |
| 9 | Reading a result in Python, CLI, HTML and CSV | for a `method="cpcv"` run, `Run`, the CLI first screen, `report.html` and the CSV exports label the same numbers as the requested evaluation and as the linear reference, with the method and period on each line: `Run.primary`, `Run.metrics` as the reference, `Run.diagnostics`, `Run.provenance`, the `evaluation_context` block of `metrics.json` and the `role` column of a CSV name the same split, and `metrics.json` says which numbers are the CPCV evaluation and which the linear reference (6.3a, #216) | T6.27 |

The scenarios do not stand in for a person: the strategy-writing trial of T5.18 records, for each step, whether the guide (3.10) was used and whether the person could say what the output shows and what it cannot, which is how the understandability of the guidance is reviewed.
---

## 9. The order of building with plinth (including the TDS conformance checker package) [R-65]

The engine is built with the user's plinth loop (`plinth/2.7.1/docs/how-to/run-a-project.md`). One rule of plinth — **every "done" is something a person can see and confirm directly** — is carried by every ticket of this spec as its "Done means" line.

| plinth step | In NexusBT | Who | This document |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Start | `/plinth:new-project <owner>/<name> --archetype=library` (**public repository**) → merge the first PR → token, account and the four manual steps → check `LICENSE` (Apache-2.0) and `NOTICE` | user | 9.1 |
| 2 Gather the thinking | `CONTEXT.md` (terms) and the first ADRs in one PR | agent writes, user merges | 9.2, 9.3 |
| 3 Plan the big work | put this spec into the repository as `docs/spec/engine-spec-v3.4.md` and TDS as `docs/tds/tds-v1.4.md` (both public editions without private comments, personal information and paths, 2.7), one plan issue with `/to-spec`, the chapter 10 tickets as issues with `/to-tickets` | user runs the commands | 9.4, chapter 10 |
| 4 Build one ticket | `/clear` → `/implement #N` (tdd + code-review), one ticket per session | user runs the commands, agent implements | 9.5 |
| 5 Confirm what the checks cannot see | required checks green + the ticket's "Done means" seen with one's own eyes through `/run` | user | 9.6, chapter 10 "commands to show" per milestone |
| 6 Merge | the user says "merge" | user | |
| 7 Breakage | `diagnosing-bugs`: reproduction command → failing test → fix | agent | 9.7 |
| 8 Stop and come back | state in issues and PRs, `/handoff` | both | 9.8 |
| 9 Stay healthy | dependency updates one at a time, `/plinth:floor-check` | user | 9.8 |

### 9.1 Step 1: the repository and the required checks

**Create** (typed by the user):

```text
/plinth:new-project <owner>/nexusbt --archetype=library
```

- Create it **public** (user decision 2026-10-27, 2.1). plinth 2.7.1 `new-project.sh` defaults to public (`visibility=public`) and only a private repository takes `--private`, so there is no separate public flag [local plinth 2.7.1 `scripts/new-project.sh` lines 5, 85 and 236 checked, 2026-10-29]. After creating it, check that `gh repo view <owner>/nexusbt --json visibility` is `PUBLIC`.
- The first PR adds `LICENSE` (the full Apache-2.0 text), `NOTICE`, `license = "Apache-2.0"` in `pyproject.toml`, and "engine and data standard are public; no data ships in this repository" in the repository description.

- `library` archetype: the container image check (`image`) is not required (the engine is not a service). The CLI is also an entry point of the same package (`[project.scripts] nexusbt = "nexusbt.cli.main:main"`).
- Merge the first PR → `git switch main && git pull` → agent token (no workflow permission) → agent account → the four manual steps. Among them, "real data outside the agent session": the agent develops and tests only with synthetic TDS packages (4.8). Measurements that use the real export (`bench/`) and manual comparison runs happen only when the user runs them on the compute machine, and the committed evidence is summary figures only (2.7).

**The required checks plinth provides** (as they are; the points the engine must follow):

| Check | What the engine keeps | Local command |
|---|---|---|
| `ci / pr-title` | PR title = ticket title (Conventional Commits, not ending in `(#N)`) | – |
| `ci / lint` | ruff, `uv.lock` in agreement | `uv run ruff check . && uv run ruff format --check .` |
| `ci / typecheck` | mypy. `[tool.mypy] strict = true` (src); Numba functions get their types from the stub `nexusbt/compute/_jit.pyi` | `uv run mypy .` |
| `ci / test` | `uv run pytest` runs the **default markers** (unit, golden, property `ci` profile, docs, repository hygiene 2.7) (`addopts` deselects the markers parity, perf_smoke, examples and slow, which run as separate jobs; `tds` is not a marker but the separate job `tds / conformance`). Coverage also tests the `.py_func` of Numba kernels so that their lines are counted | `uv run pytest` |
| `ci / build` | wheel and sdist of the two distributions (`nexusbt`, `nexusbt-tds`); after install, `import nexusbt as nx`, `import nexusbt_tds`, `tds --help` | `uv build --all-packages` |
| `ci / secrets`, `ci / deps` | no secrets, no vulnerable dependencies. Because the repository is public, no personal paths or hosts either (`test_public_hygiene` of 2.7 blocks them separately) | – |
| `ci / diff-size` | one PR ≤ 400 lines (documents and lock excluded). **The basis of the ticket size rule** (chapter 10). `[tool.*]`, check settings and deleting tests need administrator approval | `git diff --stat main` |
| `ci / floor-check`, CodeQL | keep the template floor | `/plinth:floor-check` |

**Checks the engine adds** (jobs added next to the plinth-calling job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; the agent token cannot change workflows, so the user pushes them or approves them with the user account — ticket T0.10; after they are added, the user puts them in the ruleset's required list):

| Check name | Content | Local command | Required from |
|---|---|---|---|
| `engine / parity` | L1 and L2 on synthetic small in the `tests/parity` environment (Python 3.12, zipline-reloaded 3.2.1) → the engine-side comparison pytest | `uv sync --project tests/parity --locked && uv run --project tests/parity python -m parity.run --suite all --fixture small && uv run pytest -m parity` | end of M1 (L1), end of M3 (L2) |
| `engine / property-deep` | hypothesis `deep` profile (e.g. 500 cases) | `uv run pytest -m property --hypothesis-profile=deep --hypothesis-show-statistics` | M1 |
| `engine / perf-smoke` | the CI performance smoke tests of chapter 7 | `uv run pytest -m perf_smoke` | M3 |
| `engine / docs-examples` | 8.5 (also in the default `ci / test`, but the full run of `examples/` is here) | `uv run pytest tests/docs -m examples` | M2 |
| `tds / conformance` | the own tests of the **TDS conformance checker package** (`tds/`) (8.4a: pass and fail packages per code), and `tds check --level full` on synthetic `tiny` and `small` and every `degrade` combination | `uv run --package nexusbt-tds pytest tds/tests && uv run python -m nexusbt.testing.synth --preset small --out .scratch/snap --validate` | M0 (end of T0.14c; the `full` level at the end of M1) |
| `tds / schemas` | `tds/schemas/**.json` and the TDS document tables are the same (the document tables are generated from the schema files; this catches hand-edited differences); checks for violations of the TDS version rules (10.2): no added required columns or type changes within the same MAJOR, only `stable` schemas carry a compatibility promise | `uv run --package nexusbt-tds python -m nexusbt_tds.schemas.verify` | M0 |
| `engine / golden-guard` | implementation PRs do not change `tests/golden/cases/**` or `check/**` (allowed only for golden ticket PRs titled `test(golden): …`) | `uv run pytest tests/repo/test_golden_guard.py` | M0 |
| `engine / release` | install only the two wheels in a clean virtual environment (outside the working folder) and run the English quick start and the public examples; the independent producer test package (`tds/conformance/producers/csv-minimal`) passes `full` | `uv run python -m tests.repo.release_check` | M6 |

Manual on the compute machine (not a required check; summary evidence committed at the end of a milestone): full performance `uv run python bench/run.py --all --snapshot <id>`, real-data comparison `uv run --project tests/parity python -m parity.run --suite all --fixture real:<id>`.

pytest markers: `unit`, `golden`, `property`, `docs`, `examples`, `parity`, `perf_smoke`, `slow`, `repo`.

A property test too slow to run in the `ci` profile within `ci / test`'s time limit carries `slow` as well as `property`: the default run leaves it out, and `engine / property-deep` (`-m property`) runs it, with the number of cases the test sets for itself (decision 2026-11-20, #254). That job prints each property test's case counts (`--hypothesis-show-statistics`).

**Operating the TDS package** (versioned separately): `tds/` is a uv workspace member in the same repository as the engine, but its version, change log (`tds/CHANGELOG.md`) and tags are separate. The release states are **draft → RC → 1.0** (tags `tds-v1.0.0-rc.N` → `tds-v1.0.0`; the `tds_version` string stays `MAJOR.MINOR`, `"1.0"`). Conditions for graduating to 1.0 (TDS 10.5): one independent producer (it may go through the import tool) and one producer export pass `full`, the engine reads both and the same example runs, and the hash test vectors agree between the two implementations. PRs that change the TDS documents, schemas or checker use the title scope `tds` (e.g. `feat(tds): …`) and raise `tds_version` according to the MINOR/MAJOR rules of TDS 10.2. The engine writes the version range of `nexusbt-tds` (`>=1.0,<2`) as its dependency. The reference producer also runs the `full` check right after export with the same package (`pip install nexusbt-tds` or a repository path). In `pyproject.toml`, `addopts = "-m 'not parity and not perf_smoke and not examples and not slow' --hypothesis-profile=ci"`.

### 9.2 Step 2: the glossary (`CONTEXT.md`)

The glossary is [`GLOSSARY.md`](../../GLOSSARY.md), translated in [`GLOSSARY.ko.md`](../../GLOSSARY.ko.md); `CONTEXT.md` and `CONTEXT.ko.md`, the names this spec uses, are symbolic links to them. It explains in plain words the terms 1.2 defines and the other words of this project; where the two differ, 1.2 wins. A term is added or changed there when it is settled, and a term whose change would change a rule is changed in this spec first.

### 9.3 Step 2: the decision records (`docs/adr/`)

The decision records are the files of [`docs/adr/`](../adr/), one per decision, numbered in order. Each file's Status line says whether the decision stands or which record superseded it, and a record that amends another names it.

### 9.4 Step 3: the plan issue and the tickets

1. The first M0 ticket (T0.1) puts this spec in as `docs/spec/engine-spec-v3.4.md` and TDS v1.4 as `docs/tds/tds-v1.4.md` (the content as it is; only the name strings adjusted to the repository name; public editions without private comments, TDS chapter 11, the user's personal information, hosts and data paths, 2.7).
2. User: `claude plugin install mattpocock-skills-extra@plinth` (once) → new session → `/to-spec docs/spec/engine-spec-v3.4.md`. One plan issue ("spec: NexusBT v1 (spec v3.4)") is created. Body = the scope of chapter 1, the milestone table of chapter 10, the open items of chapter 12, and a link to the spec file.
3. `/to-tickets` on that plan issue. The tickets of chapter 10 are already cut, so they become issues as they are: title = ticket title, body = Context / Done means / Acceptance tests / Spec section, labels = `task` and the triage label (no milestone or module labels: the milestone tag under the body's `## Parent`, such as `M1`, names the milestone, which a ticket id's prefix does not always match), "Blocked by #N" = the issue number of the dependency ticket. If a ticket has to be split again, keep the same form (the 400-line rule). Before making the issues, run `tests/repo/test_ticket_graph.py` once (it reads the chapter 10 table of this spec and checks that the dependency graph is acyclic and that each ticket's "Blocked by" is in the same or an earlier milestone) (made by T0.12).
4. No GitHub milestones are created. The milestone tracking issue is the plan issue itself: its `## Milestone status` table says, for each milestone, which tickets and pull requests are done and when its "end conditions" (chapter 10) and "commands to show" were met. Wherever this spec sends figures or a checklist "into the milestone tracking issue", they go into that table's row for the milestone.
5. Producer-side work is handled separately in the producer repository (not this engine repository).

### 9.5 Step 4: `/implement #N` rules (what the agent keeps)

- After `/clear`, one ticket per session. At the start, read only `CONTEXT.md`, the ticket and the spec sections the ticket points to.
- Tests first (tdd): create the test names of the ticket's "Acceptance tests" as they are, see them fail, then implement. Golden cases (`tests/golden/cases/**`) are the ones the preceding golden ticket already merged, and they are **not changed** (8.2); if a case differs from the spec, stop and write "spec deviation".
- Deep modules: change or use only the public interfaces of 2.4. Do not make public a name that is not in the public API (chapter 3). Do not import the `_` internals of another module (import rule test).
- If something has to differ from the spec, stop implementing, write it in the PR description as "spec deviation", and submit an ADR draft with it. Do not make it green by widening tolerances or skipping tests.
- PR description: evidence for "Done means" (commands and part of the output), which review ran and the findings of the fresh-context review as it reported them (a new subagent or session given only the diff, the issue's acceptance criteria and the Code Review Rules of `AGENTS.md`; `/implement`'s own `/code-review` is briefed by the session that wrote the change, so it is not that review), what could not be confirmed, the `Assisted-by` line.
- If it looks like it will go over 400 lines, split the ticket into several PRs, each branched from `main` after the previous one merged (none is stacked on an unmerged PR; 10.0's ticket rules) — record the split, dated, in the issue body's `## Decisions`.

### 9.6 Steps 5~6: "green + seen with one's own eyes"
- Green = the plinth required checks + the `engine / *` checks made required by then.
- Seen = the user (or `/run`) runs the ticket's "Done means" commands and the output matches the description. At the end of a milestone, run the "commands to show" of chapter 10 and merge the PR that commits the evidence files (comparison JSON, performance JSON).
- Default targets of `/run`: `uv run nexusbt doctor` (without data, it points to the synthetic snapshot), and per milestone `uv run python examples/m<k>_demo.py`.

### 9.7 Step 7: when something breaks (`diagnosing-bugs`)
1. Reproduce: the smallest synthetic snapshot — `make_snapshot(tmp, preset="tiny", events=[...], degrade=[...])` + a few lines of strategy. If it happens only on real data, look at the relevant rows with `nexusbt explain` and in the run folder's `orders.parquet` and `account_events.parquet`, and move **only the shape** into tiny synthetic events (no real values or instrument codes in tests, 2.7). For a data format problem, first `tds check --level full`.
2. Failing test: as a hand-calculated case (`tests/golden/cases/`) or a unit test.
3. Fix: the test passes and the other tests stay as they are. Fixes that widen tolerances or `KNOWN_DIFFERENCES.md` are not accepted without an ADR.
4. For a zipline comparison difference, first see whether it is in the "known differences table"; if not, check the time alignment (8.1), the rounded inputs and the sid order. Differences between our OHLC rules and zipline are not bugs (scope of 8.1).

### 9.8 Steps 8~9: stopping, coming back, health
- Before stopping: the remaining work in the description of the PR in progress, the milestone's row of the milestone tracking issue updated (9.4), decisions in ADRs, `/handoff`.
- Sources for the answer to "Where is this project, and what's next?" when coming back: the milestone tracking issue, the "Blocked by" of open tickets, for each requirement its ticket in appendix B, that ticket's issue in the plan issue's ticket map (9.4) and, in that issue, its state and the PR its `## Outcome` names, `bench/results/`, `tests/parity/evidence/`.
- Health: numpy, numba, pyarrow and polars are tied to each other, so one per PR (check the numpy range numba supports). The pinned zipline is the comparison reference, so it is not upgraded (if it is: an ADR + the comparison evidence again). After a plinth template update, `/plinth:floor-check`.

## 11. Extension points (E4 and E5, designed now and not implemented) [R-46, R-48, D6, design 4.6]

v1 places only the boundaries below (Protocols, data types, table names) in `nx.bridge`. There is no implementation, and calling them in v1 is `CapabilityError` (E18). The test `tests/unit/bridge/test_capabilities.py` (ticket T6.17) checks this. The TDS tick, quote, futures, options and crypto schemas this chapter uses are `status: experimental` in TDS v1.1 (outside the version-compatibility promise; promoted to stable once lossless import and replay fixed cases exist).

### 11.1 Execution backend boundary

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class Capabilities:
    asset_classes: frozenset[str]          # v1 panel: {"equity"}
    bar_freqs: frozenset[str]              # v1: {"1d"}
    order_types: frozenset[str]            # v1: {"market","limit","stop","stop_limit"}
    intraday_sequencing: bool              # v1: False (daily bars: the adverse side is assumed)
    book_depth: int                        # v1: 0
    margin: bool                           # v1: True (off by default in the config: [account] type = "cash", 5.6.7a); the cash account has no margin loan for longs
    short_selling: bool                    # v1: True (off by default in the config)
    fractional_qty: bool                   # v1: False

class ExecutionBackend(Protocol):
    name: str                              # "panel" (v1), "nautilus" (E4)
    def capabilities(self) -> Capabilities: ...
    def run(self, orders: Iterable[OrderIntentBatch], market: MarketView,
            config: SimConfig) -> Iterable[FillEvent]: ...   # fill events only
```

- **The engine is the accounting canon**: a backend emits **fill events only** (order status transitions, fill quantity, price, liquidity side). The `session_start` event (5.6.3(1)), tradability blocks (5.6.5), sellable quantity and short collateral (5.6.7), costs (5.6.8), cash allocation, corporate actions, dividends and delistings, and NAV are done by the engine's `accounting` with the same code. Why: the NautilusTrader core has no dividend, split or delisting handling (0 hits searching `crates/`), no price-band check and no trading calendar, and it has a history of bugs filling buys without enough cash (NT#5047, NT#5119), so filling in accounting inside the backend would make two copies. Nautilus is used with its accounting switched off by `calculate_account_state=False` [est.]. The connection tests include reproduction cases of NT#5047, #5054 and #5461 (E4).

- `nx.backtest(..., engine: Literal["panel"] = "panel")`. Any other value is E18. `engine="auto"` is not made (fill semantics are not changed silently) [C10, R-48].
- The features a strategy requires (asset class, bar frequency, order types, intraday sequence) are compared with `Capabilities`, and when one is missing the run stops with E18 before it starts.
- Every backend takes the same `TargetFrame` input and produces the same canonical columns of `orders.parquet` and `account_events.parquet` and the same manifest [R-48].

### 11.2 NautilusTrader connection (E4)
- Only through the optional dependency `nexusbt[nautilus]`, used by importing it as a library (LGPL-3.0; no source copied, 2.1), with the version pinned. Only the 128-bit high-precision wheels are used (the 64-bit `Money` cap is about 9,200,000,000 won) — the connection tests include "a KRW account of 1,000,000,000,000 won" [design 4.6, risk 4].
- The conversion table uses TDS 9.3 (the reverse direction is `tds export nautilus-catalog`): `instruments` → `Equity`/`FuturesContract`/`OptionContract`/`CryptoPerpetual`/`CurrencyPair`; `bars(freq<1d)` → `Bar` (`ts_end` = the closing time). Tick and book kinds have no TDS schema: such kinds may be added later, equities first (4.1) [#725 Decisions 252, 253].
- One Nautilus `Strategy` evaluates the NexusBT Pipeline incrementally at each timer (decision clock) and goes `TargetFrame` → order intents → execution algorithm (immediate/TWAP). The user does not see Nautilus configuration objects.
- **Rust core comparison** [user decision 2026-10-28]: E4 intraday and tick execution (minute and tick event processing, order book state, `intraday-path-v1`) is designed as a Rust core (PyO3/maturin) on the 2.4 seam, and at that point it is compared with this Nautilus bridge on the same fixed cases (accounting ground-truth data, reproductions of NT#5047, #5054 and #5461) to choose one. The daily v1 path keeps the Python/Numba reference as canonical.
- Daily equities stay with the v1 panel backend (Nautilus has no next-bar-open fill mode by default). The cross test matches NAV with zero costs and fills at the close.
- When minute or tick data exist, the intraday sequence is judged with that data: fill model `intraday-path-v1` (walks the minute bars in time order and applies the 5.6.4 rules to each minute bar, with the same order state machine). Queue and latency defaults are conservative, and imitating limit orders with bars gives an optimism warning [research 2.5].

### 11.3 Asset data types (E4 and E5)

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class FutureSpec:  root: str; ts_expiry: datetime; multiplier: float; settlement: Literal["cash","physical"]; first_notice: date | None
@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class OptionSpec:  underlying_instrument_id: str; strike: float; right: Literal["call","put"]
                   exercise_style: Literal["american","european"]; ts_expiry: datetime; multiplier: float; settlement: Literal["cash","physical"]
@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class CryptoSpec:  base: str; quote: str; venue: str; qty_step: float; price_step: float
```

- TDS holds no schema or profile for these assets: the drafts that named their tables and profiles were removed 2026-11-24 (#752); such kinds may be added later, equities first [#725 Decisions 252, 253]. Tick and quote times are to be in ns, prices int64 fixed-point (TDS K2, K7) [TDS-D4]. Only provider Greeks and IV are inputs; values recomputed by the engine are not inputs.
- Futures: continuous-contract prices are **for signal computation only**; fills and P&L use the actual individual contracts. The strategy states the roll with `roll.mapping` (when: last_trade / first_notice / open_interest / volume) and `roll.normalization` (how: backwards_ratio / panama / none) [research 2.7].
- Options: fills by default buy = ask, sell = bid, and the mid price is an explicit optimistic option; early exercise for American style only [research 2.6]. In E5, Nautilus and LEAN are compared on small accounting, exercise and margin ground-truth data before one is chosen [Q16].
- Crypto: calendar `CRYPTO-UTC` (session = one UTC day, minute bars by bar index), fractional quantities (`fractional_qty`). The decision clock is `clock = "utc:HH:MM"` (the name is only reserved, E18 in v1) [SC-25]. The market rules pack (4.7) holds that market's rules (no price limits, 24/7).
- Plugin points (later): Python entry points `nexusbt.datasets`, `nexusbt.costs` and `nexusbt.factors`, these three kinds only. Plugin names and versions go into the structure hash and the cache keys. Execution backends are not plugins but only code in the engine repository (one canon for fill semantics) [blueprint 12.4].
- Duration windows (`Returns("30d")`): added on top of row-count windows by a "time range → row range" conversion (R-47). No per-asset arguments are added to the `Strategy` or term API [R-46].

### 11.4 Broker ledger boundary (after E3) [R-49, Q11]

```python
class Book(Protocol):
    book_id: str
    def state(self) -> AccountState: ...
    def submit(self, orders: Orders) -> SubmissionReceipt: ...       # a paper ledger only records
    def fills(self, since: datetime) -> pa.Table: ...                # canonical columns of orders.parquet
```

Real broker adapters (`nx.book("kis:<account>")` and the like) are opened by a separate ADR after they pass reconciliation and idempotency tests (M5 level). Keys come only from environment variables or the OS keychain (2.6). A broker's paper account is only a connection smoke test, not a verification standard (5.9.7). On a broker ledger, `reconcile` becomes a TCA report.

## 12. What remains open (the recommended defaults keep work from stopping)

> **2026-10-28 all closed.** 0 open, 0 user decisions (●). 2026-11-04: OI-54 (the short-side bound of `ca_exit_lo`, #150) opened with its default; 1 open, 0 ●. the three user questions raised by the fourth review round (f64 ratios, `kelly`, the direction of ML training inside CPCV) and economic-event investing were closed by user decision the same day (header) — no new open items [R4F-01~03]. the user decisions on the round 4 topics (CPCV v1, minimum ML factor v1, validation accounts, one entry point) are written in the header as decided, and there are no new open items; the points for the fourth review round to judge are in `review/packet-r4.md` and are not open items (work proceeds with the designer's defaults). the four user questions raised by the third review round were closed by user decision the same day (OI-50~OI-53, decided). What the engine has to do is decided or applied, and the data-side work (OI-01·02·03·37) moved to the producer roadmap. Taxes, like commissions, are written by the user in the config file (OI-18, 5.6.11a).

This combines O1~O11 of the v1 spec, blueprint OQ-1~OQ-15 and TDS Q1~Q16 into one; v3 corrected it with the results of the three-reviewer review (`review/consensus.md` chapters 3 and 5) and added OI-44~OI-49 (numbered `OI-xx`, the original number in parentheses). "Status": **applied** = the recommended default is already applied in this spec, **decided** = the user decided, **moved** = closed in the producer roadmap, **closed** = settled by the three-reviewer review without a user decision, **open** = work proceeds with the default but someone has to close it. A ● in the "User" column means a user decision is needed (the default keeps implementation from stopping).

### 12.1 Deferred in v3.1~v3.4 (not open items; no user decision needed)

**Target column**: `v1.x` = small releases after the v1 release, `E4`/`E5` = the intraday and derivatives stages (chapter 11), `next TDS MINOR` = the standard side, `dropped` = not reopened, `solved in v1` = after the deferral a v1 ticket already took it on (⚑ = a row whose deferral reason contradicted that v1 ticket; the reason column of this table is kept as a record).

These are what the teardown of existing engines (`teardown/MATRIX.md` chapter 2) decided not to put into v3.1. They are not open questions but **settled deferrals** (0 open / 0 ● in the table above stand; OI-54, opened 2026-11-04, is the one open item since). When one is reopened, a ticket is made under its number and the semantic contract and fixed cases are written first (the 10.1 "Later" rule).
